### 527. Assert the deployed hook registration SET matches the merge-source, not only each path's form
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 369, Task 445

**Description**: Extend the hook-registration lint so it asserts the deployed hook registration SET against the merge-source, not only the FORM of each individual command path, and wire the resulting gate into verify-deploy.sh.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh to RESULT=landed_verify_clean. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** or lua/**; both are permitted in this description.

--- DEPENDENCIES: TWO EDGES ---

1. orphan_sweep_for_removed_merge_target_entries (task 369). That record's acceptance criterion 3 requires the registration-identity notion to be defined in exactly ONE place consumed by both its sweep and this lint, with a test asserting the two agree on a path outside hooks/. This lint must CONSUME that settled identity notion rather than define a second one, so 369 must land first.
2. gate8_expected_vs_new_failure_distinction (task 445) -- a territory edge on verify-deploy.sh's gate surface, which both records touch.

--- DIAGNOSIS IS COMPLETE: DO NOT RE-DERIVE IT ---

GOAL. scripts/lint/lint-hook-registration-paths.sh checks the FORM of each hook command path -- whether a registration uses the fixed "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR"-prefixed shape or the stale CWD-relative one. It does not check the SET. Three drift shapes therefore pass it silently, all three observed in the field:

(a) a registration for a hook script that no source surface declares anywhere;
(b) the same hook script registered under two different matcher strings for one event, so a single notification fires it twice;
(c) a registration whose hook script file does not exist on disk at all, failing open behind its own `2>/dev/null || echo '{}'`.

MEASURED FIELD INSTANCES as of 2026-11-07, across the repos this loader deploys into:
- Logos/Theory carried all three: shape (a) (Stop|* registrations of tts-notify.sh and a bare wezterm-notify.sh, neither declared by any source surface), shape (b) (two Notification blocks, matchers permission_prompt|idle_prompt|elicitation_dialog and permission_prompt|elicitation_dialog, both registering tts-notify.sh, so one permission event fired two announcements), and shape (c) (wezterm-clear-status.sh, whose file is absent).
- ModelBuilder and Logos/Website carry the same shape (a) and (b) pair.
- theorem_proving_in_lean4 carries shape (c) twice (claude-stop-notify.sh, wezterm-preflight-status.sh).
- Fourteen other deployed repos are clean, which is what makes a set assertion useful rather than noisy: the drift is real but rare, so a gate on it will not fire constantly.

FIX. Extend the lint to compare the deployed (event, matcher, hook-identity) set against the union of what the merge-source and root-files surfaces declare, and report (a), (b) and (c) as distinct findings with distinct remedies. Then wire it into verify-deploy.sh's gate suite so the drift class fails a check instead of accumulating.

--- MANDATORY CONSTRAINTS ---

- Consume the single shared identity notion the orphan-sweep record establishes. Defining a second notion here is the specific failure the first dependency edge exists to prevent -- the hook-path lint and the loader's own hook_identity() have a DOCUMENTED historical divergence (the lint keyed coverage on any invoked script path, hook_identity() recognized only paths under hooks/), and resolving it in one place is that record's criterion, not this one's to re-litigate.
- A hand-written custom hook -- a command not recognizable as a source-store hook invocation -- is NEVER a finding. The carve-out is mandatory, not best-effort. A lint that reports a user's own hook registration as drift is worse than no lint.
- Anchor detection on the "command" JSON key, never a text grep over the file. A naive .claude/hooks/ grep matches root-files/settings.json's own _comment field -- a false-positive hazard already recorded by this lint's prior research.
- Shape (b) must be reported even when both registrations are individually well-formed. Form-correctness is exactly why it passes today.
- env, permissions, statusLine and every non-hooks key are out of scope for this lint; it must not report or touch them.

--- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ---

1. Each of shapes (a), (b) and (c) produces a distinct finding with its own remedy text, demonstrated against a faithful copy of a real drifted deploy, not only a synthetic fixture.
2. A hand-written hook command survives the lint with zero findings, with a dedicated regression case.
3. A clean deploy produces zero findings -- verified against several of the fourteen clean repos, so the gate is not noisy by construction.
4. The identity notion is imported from its single shared definition; a test asserts this lint and the loader's sweep agree on a path outside hooks/.
5. The gate is wired into verify-deploy.sh and a deliberately drifted fixture fails it.
6. Pre-existing lint and verify-deploy tests still pass; a newly red pre-existing test is treated as caused by this task.

### 526. Announce completion and input-needed over TTS from the source store, with a cooldown
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 435

**Description**: Make every repo the loader deploys into announce the two things that actually matter -- work finished, and input needed -- and make repeat announcements harmless by construction. Today neither fires.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** and agent-system/extensions/nvim/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh to RESULT=landed_verify_clean. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** or lua/**; both are permitted in this description.

--- DEPENDENCY: TERRITORY, NOT SUBJECT ---

This task depends on the open record reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class (task 435) as a TERRITORY edge, not a subject edge. That record's file_scope covers the directory roots agent-system/extensions/core/hooks/, core/merge-sources/, core/root-files/, core/scripts/ and agent-system/extensions/nvim/context/ -- every one of which this task edits specific files inside. Per context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md, shared file territory is a reason to add the edge and serialize, never to exclude. Task 435 is a prose-rewording sweep with no TTS subject overlap, so the edge exists purely to serialize two writers over the same roots.

--- DIAGNOSIS IS COMPLETE: DO NOT RE-DERIVE IT ---

Everything below is established fact. Research should confirm the current file state and design the fix, not re-diagnose the defects.

DEFECT 1, a documented-but-unimplemented behavior. hooks/claude-stop-notify.sh's no-workflow-marker branch fires only the WezTerm color, with the inline comment "Fire needs_input wezterm color only (no TTS for non-lifecycle stops)". But scripts/lifecycle-notify.sh's own header decision table promises the opposite: "orchestrate final completion: orchestrate-active cleared by Stage 8 -> subsequent Stop hook fires TTS" and "orchestrate paused/blocked: ... Stop hook fires TTS". Two files in the same source store state contradictory contracts, and the implemented side is the silent one, so an /orchestrate run that finishes never announces.

FIX: call hooks/tts-notify.sh from that no-marker branch, after the existing wezterm-notify.sh call. The existing subagent-detection guard (agent_id present in the Stop stdin JSON) and the global workflow-active-marker suppression loop MUST both be preserved exactly as they are -- they are what keeps a mid-orchestrate pause from overwriting an in-progress tab color, and the marker suppression is deliberately global across sessions (see that file's own header for the rationale, which this task must not weaken).

DEFECT 2, a trigger that cannot fire. The canonical Notification matcher is permission_prompt|elicitation_dialog. The operator runs with skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt: true in the user-level settings and in bypass-permissions mode, so permission prompts essentially never occur and that matcher is effectively dead. idle_prompt -- the actionable notification type meaning Claude has been waiting 60s or more for user input -- was removed from the matcher at some earlier point, which is precisely the "a question needs answering" case the operator wants announced, and it now has no trigger at all.

FIX: widen the matcher to permission_prompt|idle_prompt|elicitation_dialog in BOTH surfaces that declare it, merge-sources/settings-hooks.json and root-files/settings.json. Both must change together; changing only one leaves a fresh deploy and a resynced deploy disagreeing.

DEFECT 3, the reason widening the matcher is not safe on its own. idle_prompt re-fires on a repeating cadence while Claude waits, so the matcher widening above would by itself produce a repeating announcement rather than one.

MEASURED EVIDENCE from a repo that was accidentally running with idle_prompt registered (Logos/Theory, whose specs/tmp/claude-tts-notify.log holds 1329 bare "Tab N" lines against 21 lifecycle lines): of the last 199 inter-announcement intervals, 65 fell in the 10-70 second band -- the idle re-fire cadence -- and 11 fell under 2 seconds, those being one event matching two overlapping Notification blocks at once.

FIX: add a cooldown/dedup stamp to hooks/tts-notify.sh, keyed by WEZTERM_PANE so concurrent panes never suppress each other, that drops a repeat announcement inside a short window. This is the load-bearing change: with the cooldown in place, an idle re-fire, a Stop and a Notification coinciding, or any future double registration all degrade to a single utterance instead of a burst. Keep the existing TTS_ENABLED and PIPER_VOICE env contract and the non-blocking backgrounded piper | paplay invocation shape. The stamp must live somewhere a repo without a specs/ directory can still write, or fail open silently exactly as the current log write does.

--- REQUIRED BEHAVIOR, NOT OPTIONAL POLISH ---

The two cases must be distinguishable by ear. A completion and an input-needed prompt currently both render as the bare string "Tab N". Give the Stop-branch utterance its own word (for example "Tab N done") while the input-needed utterance stays "Tab N", and keep the existing --lifecycle STATUS mode's "Tab N STATUS" vocabulary (researching/researched/planning/planned/implementing/completed/blocked) unchanged.

--- DOCUMENTATION RECONCILIATION, IN SCOPE AND NOT DEFERRABLE ---

agent-system/extensions/nvim/context/project/neovim/guides/tts-stt-integration.md currently asserts "The Stop hook does NOT trigger TTS. This eliminates random 'Tab N' announcements during mid-workflow orchestrator pauses." and carries a Notification Event Types table listing only permission_prompt and elicitation_dialog. Both become false with this task and must be restated to the implemented behavior, including the cooldown and the distinguishable utterances.

scripts/lifecycle-notify.sh's header decision table must be reconciled in the same pass -- it is the file that was already correct about the intent, so the reconciliation is to make its description match what the code now does and to drop any claim that is still untrue afterwards.

--- SCOPE DECISION ALREADY SETTLED BY THE OPERATOR ---

Not to be revisited by research or planning: the Neovim extension loader owns TTS. Do NOT add a TTS hook to the user-level ~/.dotfiles/config/claude/settings.json, even though hooks is an enforced key there and it would reach repos with no deployed .claude/ tree. Those repos stay silent by choice.

--- NON-GOALS, OWNED ELSEWHERE ---

Do not absorb these and do not edit their files:
- The loader-side inability to delete a stale hook registration, and the one-shot remediation of the repos currently carrying stale registrations, are owned by the open orphan-sweep record (orphan_sweep_for_removed_merge_target_entries, task 369).
- Asserting the deployed registration SET against the merge-source is owned by the hook_registration_set_lint_assertion record.

This task changes only what the source store declares and what the hooks do.

--- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ---

1. An /orchestrate run reaching completion produces exactly one audible announcement, distinguishable by ear from an input-needed announcement.
2. An idle_prompt re-fire inside the cooldown window produces no second announcement; one outside it does. Demonstrated against the real notification cadence, not only a synthetic repeat.
3. A mid-orchestrate pause still produces no Stop-branch announcement: the workflow-active-marker suppression and the subagent agent_id guard both still hold, with a regression check for each.
4. merge-sources/settings-hooks.json and root-files/settings.json declare the identical Notification matcher; a check asserts they agree.
5. tts-stt-integration.md and lifecycle-notify.sh's header contain no claim contradicted by the landed code.
6. TTS_ENABLED=0 still fully disables every path, including the new Stop-branch call.

### 525. Repair the always-failing extensions-directory fallback in the task-type detection suite
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Parent**: Task 418

**Description**: Repair the always-failing extensions-directory fallback in the task-type detection suite

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/**; both are permitted in this description.

--- MEASURED EVIDENCE ---

scripts/tests/test-task-type-detect.sh resolves its real-manifest root in two branches. The first detects the source-store shape directly and is correct. The second -- the fallback, reached whenever the first does not match, which is precisely when the suite runs from the DEPLOYED copy -- cannot ever succeed:

    REAL_EXT_ROOT="$(common_test_resolve_candidate "real extensions directory" \
      "$(cd "$SCRIPT_DIR/../.." && pwd)/extensions")" || exit 2

common_test_resolve_candidate (scripts/lib/common.sh) selects the first candidate satisfying
[ -f "$_common_candidate" ]. The single candidate passed here is an extensions DIRECTORY, so the
[ -f ] test fails against it unconditionally, the helper falls through to its own not-found error,
and the suite exits 2 before reaching the real-manifest case the variable exists to serve.

Confirmed structurally: the helper's loop body tests [ -f ] and nothing else; the candidate path
ends in a directory component by construction. Confirmed historically: this branch is byte-identical
before and after the subject-resolution migration that surfaced it, so the migration neither caused
nor touched it. The branch was deliberately left on the older candidate helper by that migration's
own plan, which is why the mismatch survived.

--- THE CONSEQUENCE ---

Gate 8 runs the deployed copy, which is exactly the shape that takes the failing branch. The
real-manifest case guarded by REAL_EXT_ROOT -- the one asserting a bare "literature" mention no
longer force-routes to meta -- therefore never runs under Gate 8. This is a silent coverage hole,
not merely a red suite: the assertion that regression test exists to make is not being made.

--- REQUIRED DIRECTION ---

Resolve a DIRECTORY with a directory-appropriate test rather than passing one to a file-only
helper. Research must settle which of these the tree's own conventions favour, with evidence
rather than preference:

- A directory-mode selector on the shared helper, if one already exists or if other callers need it.
- A plain inline [ -d ] check in this suite, if this is the only site that resolves a directory.

Prefer whichever option leaves exactly one home for the behaviour. If the shared helper gains a
directory mode, research must enumerate every existing common_test_resolve_candidate call site and
confirm none of them silently changes meaning.

Research must also settle whether other suites pass a directory to this file-only helper. A single
grep over the call sites answers it, and an unfixed sibling would be the same silent hole.

--- VERIFICATION ---

- A fixture proving the fallback branch resolves the real extensions directory when it is taken --
  exercised by running the suite from the DEPLOYED copy, which is the shape that takes it. A fixture
  that only ever runs the source-store shape cannot reach the branch under repair and does not count
  as evidence.
- A fixture proving the first branch still wins when the source-store shape matches, so the repair
  does not silently reroute the source-store path through the fallback.
- The real-manifest assertion (bare "literature" no longer force-routes to meta) is observed actually
  executing under the deployed shape, not merely not-failing. A skipped assertion and a passing one
  are indistinguishable in the suite's current output, which is how this hole stayed open.
- Final phase declares the complete gate set with an explicit source-store TARGET (the bare form
  silently skips the source-store-only gates).

Known-unrelated pre-existing failures: Gate 3 lean4 doc-lint missing index.json entry; Gate 20
eager-load budget over baseline; Gate 22 shellcheck SC2155 in the lean extension's
lean-plan-compliance-check.sh; Gate 24 two /ide references in the memory extension's docs; Gate 8
suites test-detect-noop-bash.sh, test-four-tier-conflict.sh, test-guard-git-push.sh. Do not
attribute these to this work and do not fix them here.
--- AUDIT DISPOSITION (mechanically corroborated overlaps, each disposed of rather than inherited) ---

The proposal audit returned two mechanically corroborated overlaps against open records. Both are
coarse-declaration artifacts, not genuine duplicates, and neither owns the defect above:

- Record 435 (reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class), verdict subset-of-open, shared paths
  scripts/lib/common.sh and scripts/tests/test-task-type-detect.sh. That record's file_scope declares
  whole DIRECTORIES (agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/ among roughly eighty entries), so it
  mechanically contains every file under them. Its subject is rewording prose that names three deleted
  lifecycle commands, per site and per class. It does not touch candidate resolution, directory-versus-file
  tests, or REAL_EXT_ROOT. The overlap is an artifact of directory-level declaration.

- Record 448 (contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations), verdict duplicate-of-open, shared path
  scripts/tests/test-task-type-detect.sh. That record likewise declares scripts/tests/ as a directory.
  Its subject is containing a regression-anchor source-store mutation so a concurrent session cannot
  observe or inherit it. That is a test-isolation concern about deliberate mutation; this record is a
  resolution-helper type mismatch. Disjoint mechanisms.

Both records were open and not_started at this record's creation. If either is dispatched concurrently
with this one, the shared file is scripts/tests/test-task-type-detect.sh and the two must be serialized
rather than run in parallel; add a dependencies[] edge at that point rather than widening either scope.

### 524. Rule on a staged-vs-working-tree divergence signal for a pathspec commit, and name the partial-stage trap in the staging-scope standard
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 461, Task 484, Task 501
- **Parent**: Task 418

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation, session sess_1794023900_f4ac5a. User-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only.

CITATIONS: #418:iss_1794001639995_QvyIy9 (the near-miss itself, severity costly); #418:iss_1794001112756_1CPE72 (the partial-stage attempt and its motivation); #418:iss_1794001757595_GJUSiy (a live concurrent sibling writer observed in the same working tree during the same dispatch); #418:iss_1793989293251_3pfJ9z (a plan-time territory collision with a concurrent sibling over a shared file). None of the four carried a suggested_channel hint; routing them to the agent-system channel is the orchestrator's own classification, on the grounds that the hazard lives in shared commit tooling and its governing standard. Severity of the primary entry: costly.

PROVENANCE: every file, line number, and quoted string below was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-11-06), not copied from the cited entries. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narrative itself is CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited entries; the mechanism it turns on was independently re-confirmed against the script, as recorded below.

LIVE SYMPTOM. A hand-built partial patch was staged with `git apply --cached` against agent-system/extensions/core/manifest.json, specifically so that one task's own two hunks would be committed and a concurrent sibling's uncommitted lib/gate-store-fixture-lib.sh provides.scripts line would be left untouched. The subsequent `git commit -- <pathspec>` nevertheless committed the WORKING-TREE content for that path, sweeping the sibling's unrelated uncommitted line into the commit. It was caught immediately via `git show --stat` and corrected with a second narrow commit removing that line from HEAD, after which the sibling's line was restored into the working tree uncommitted so their in-progress work survived exactly as found. No work was lost. The report is the near-miss and the standing unsoundness, not a live breakage.

ROOT CAUSE IS KNOWN GIT SEMANTICS, ALREADY DOCUMENTED IN THE SCRIPT. scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh's header carries a dedicated block (the "Git semantics discovered empirically while diagnosing V8" note, around lines 259-270) stating that `git commit -- <pathspec>` RE-READS the current working-tree content fresh for each named path AT COMMIT TIME and does not commit whatever `git add` most recently staged. The index is therefore not the authority for a pathspec commit, and any partial stage of a named path is discarded by the commit that follows. The defect is not that this is unknown; it is that nothing refuses, warns about, or documents the partial-stage idiom that the semantics silently defeat.

WHY NO EXISTING GATE CATCHES IT -- VERIFIED, AND THE CRUX TO RULE ON. The V8 post-commit integrity gate (header around lines 164-207; implementation around lines 780-828) takes its PRE-COMMIT INTENT SNAPSHOT as `git hash-object -- "$v8_f"` of each enumerated path's WORKING-TREE content, with an explicit "absent" sentinel when the file is not on disk. V8's operative definition of intent IS therefore the working tree. In the cited incident the working tree held the sibling's line, the commit landed exactly that content, and V8 compared a MATCH -- correctly, by its own contract. V8 structurally cannot see index-vs-working-tree divergence because it never reads the index. The V7 verification loop does read the index, but only for PRESENCE of a path, never for its content. So no gate in the script notices that the caller staged a subset and git committed the superset, and the script exits 0 with nothing printed.

THE QUESTION TO RULE ON, NOT A PRESCRIPTION. A defensible outcome here is option (2) -- no code change, documentation only. Do not assume a code change is required.
  (1) Add a PRE-COMMIT divergence check inside git-commit-scoped.sh: for each surviving positive pathspec, compare the staged content against the working-tree content (per-file index blob vs. `git hash-object`, or `git diff --cached --name-only -- <p>` against `git diff --name-only -- <p>`) and REFUSE, or WARN, when they differ. Refusal is the stronger posture but will also fire on the ordinary and entirely legitimate case of a file edited further after it was staged; decide whether that case is a real population in this pipeline or only an artifact of hand workflows, and record the evidence for whichever answer is given.
  (2) Change nothing in the script and treat sub-file partial staging as simply UNSUPPORTED, documented as such -- the script's contract then being stated plainly as "for every named path you get the working tree as it stands at commit time".
  (3) A narrower middle: detect only the specific shape where the index holds a STRICT SUBSET of the working-tree changes for a named path (the partial-stage signature) and warn on that alone, leaving a plain edited-after-staging file unflagged.
Options (1) and (3) both interact with V8: if a pre-commit divergence signal exists, V8's own snapshot choice becomes a deliberate, stated decision rather than an unexamined one, and the header must then say which authority each gate consults and why they differ.

DOCUMENTATION OBLIGATION, INDEPENDENT OF WHICH OPTION WINS. context/standards/git-staging-scope.md's "Concurrency Qualifier: Targeted Staging Is Necessary But Not Sufficient" section (around lines 223-258) states that "Path granularity is the file -- an explicit-path `git add` cannot subdivide a file by author", then routes the reader to the two sanctioned remedies (the first-claim contended-path lease inside git-commit-scoped.sh, and per-dispatch working-tree isolation). It does NOT name the partial-stage attempt anywhere. A reader who has just been told that granularity is the file will reach for `git apply --cached` as the obvious sub-file workaround, and that reach is silently unsound under `git commit -- <pathspec>`. The section MUST name the trap explicitly: sub-file staging does not survive a pathspec commit, so it is not an available remedy, and the lease and tree isolation remain the only sanctioned ones.

WHY THIS IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL. The same dispatch separately observed a concurrent sibling editing scripts/verify-deploy.sh live, mid-dispatch, in the same working tree (#418:iss_1794001757595_GJUSiy), and a plan-time territory collision with a concurrent sibling over a shared file (#418:iss_1793989293251_3pfJ9z). Concurrent writers in one shared working tree are this repository's normal operating condition, not an exceptional state, which is precisely what makes a silently-unsound narrowing idiom worth closing or naming.

DO NOT RE-LITIGATE any of the following: V5's contended-path lease and its first-claim posture; V8's existence, its working-tree snapshot being valuable on its own mechanism-agnostic terms, or its dedicated exit code 5; the prohibition on a bare `git commit`; the settled bare-directory-pathspec policy and its lint. Note also that the incident's own commit was a HAND-RUN `git commit --`, not a git-commit-scoped.sh invocation, so "the script was not involved" is a point for the planner to weigh, not a reason to close the record -- the standard it governs is read by the humans and agents who run git by hand.

DEPENDENCY RATIONALE (file territory, not a sequencing preference). Three open records already declare scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh and scripts/tests/test-git-commit-scoped.sh in their file_scope: task 461 (the V7 refusal of a positive directory pathspec for a `git rm --cached` untracking), whose declared three-file scope is identical to this record's; task 484 (collapsing per-path scope-excursion issue rows); and task 501 (cycle-postflight containment predicate parity). Because this repository runs concurrent writers in one shared working tree -- the very hazard this record reports -- the overlap is sequenced by dependency edges rather than left unordered. Tasks 430, 446 and 486 additionally declare context/standards/git-staging-scope.md but none of the two scripts; that overlap is documentation-only and is deliberately left unsequenced rather than chained, to avoid over-constraining the backlog.

PRIOR ART -- DISPOSED OF EXPLICITLY AT CREATION TIME, DO NOT REDISCOVER OR RE-LITIGATE.
  - Task 374 (completed) is the closest prior art and shares this record's exact three-file scope: it diagnosed and fixed the silent staging drop in git-commit-scoped.sh, and its product IS the V8 gate. It does NOT cover this case, and that distinction is the whole substance of this record. V8's intent authority is the WORKING TREE (`git hash-object` of each enumerated path), so it catches a divergence arising BETWEEN its snapshot and the commit -- a concurrent writer changing the file after staging -- and compares a correct MATCH when the index held a narrower subset than the working tree all along. Read that record's outcome before planning, then state in the plan which authority each gate consults and why.
  - Task 461 (open) declares a scope identical to this record's three files, for a different defect: the V7 refusal of a positive directory pathspec for a `git rm --cached` untracking whose content is still on disk. Sequenced here by dependency edge.
  - Task 484 (open) and task 501 (open) both declare git-commit-scoped.sh and its suite -- 484 collapsing per-path scope-excursion issue rows, 501 giving the cycle-postflight containment site predicate parity. Both sequenced by dependency edge for file territory, not for any logical ordering between the subjects.
  - Tasks 430, 446 and 486 (all open) declare context/standards/git-staging-scope.md but neither script. Documentation-only overlap on one shared file, deliberately left unsequenced.
  - Task 435 (open) declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/ and agent-system/extensions/core/context/ as whole directories for a repo-wide prose reword; that declaration overlaps essentially every agent-system record and is left unsequenced on the same grounds the rest of the backlog leaves it unsequenced.
  - Closed and settled -- do not reopen: task 302 (the scoped-commit directory pathspec and first-claim task lease), task 199 (the concurrent-dispatch isolation posture decision record, which context/standards/git-staging-scope.md already cites as the home of both sanctioned remedies), task 277 (an unresolvable pathspec made a hard error), task 304 (an out-of-repository pathspec aborting the whole commit, V9), task 226 (staged deletions dropped by the V2 gate).
  - The creation-time backlog audit returned a cluster of roughly 0.4-to-0.5-scored duplicate-of-open and covered-by-closed verdicts carrying mechanical_class=none against unrelated records. Those are uncorroborated Pass-2 shortlist entries, inflated by this description's length -- the exact condition task 491 (open) exists to fix. They were checked, not accepted.
  - Task 448 (open) declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/ as a whole directory, which covers this record's test suite but neither of its other two files. Its subject -- containing a test harness's deliberate-break mutation of the shared source store -- is unrelated to this one. A single-file overlap inside a broad directory declaration, deliberately left unsequenced: the dependency edges above already serialize this record against every open task that names git-commit-scoped.sh itself.

### 523. Make Gate 27 MISSING_DECLARATION count call sites, not whole-file substring mentions
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 448
- **Parent**: Task 418

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation, session sess_1794023900_f4ac5a. User-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only.

CITATION: #418:iss_1794024837652_7HqnmR. The entry carried no suggested_channel hint; routing it to the agent-system channel is the orchestrator's own classification, on the grounds that the defect is in a lint this system owns rather than in any task's work product. Severity: recurring minor -- a workaround exists and nothing is blocked, but it fired three separate times inside a single migration phase, each time costing a diagnosis pass.

PROVENANCE: every file, line number, and quoted string below was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-11-06), not copied from the cited entry. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing.

LIVE SYMPTOM. Gate 27's MISSING_DECLARATION check reports a violation against a suite that is in fact fully compliant, whenever a header comment or explanatory prose in that suite names the resolver helper by its literal identifier without an equal number of mentions of the declarer helper. Observed three times inside one migration phase, on scripts/tests/test-force-phases.sh, scripts/tests/test-lint-json-channel-discipline.sh, and scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh.

ROOT CAUSE, VERIFIED AGAINST THE SCRIPT. scripts/lint/lint-subject-resolution-declaration.sh's classify_file(), around lines 232-239:

    resolve_subject_count="$(grep -c 'common_test_resolve_subject' "$f" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    declare_subject_count="$(grep -c 'common_test_declare_subject' "$f" 2>/dev/null || true)"
    ...
    if [[ "$resolve_subject_count" -gt "$declare_subject_count" ]]; then
        hits="$hits MISSING_DECLARATION"
    fi

Both figures are whole-file, unanchored `grep -c` SUBSTRING counts over every line of the file -- comments, heredoc bodies, and string literals included, with no call-shape anchoring and no comment stripping. Any line that names the resolver identifier without also naming the declarer identifier adds one to the resolve side only, and a single such line is enough to make a compliant file count resolve > declare.

LATENT FRAGILITY, VERIFIED LIVE -- cited because it shows the trap is not confined to the three files already reworded. scripts/tests/test-dispatch-metrics.sh has four genuine common_test_resolve_subject call sites (lines 47, 51, 55, 59) and four genuine common_test_declare_subject call sites (lines 50, 54, 58, 62), yet the lint counts 5 and 5 -- balanced only because its line 43 comment happens to name BOTH identifiers on one line. Had that comment named only the resolver, which is the natural phrasing for explaining why lib/common.sh is sourced, the file would count 5 against 4 and the lint would flag a suite that is entirely correct. The same accidental balance holds in scripts/tests/test-common-lib.sh, test-lint-test-harness-adoption.sh, and test-validate-state.sh, each of which also carries a comment mentioning the identifiers.

THE WORKAROUND IN PLACE IS THE DEFECT BEING REPORTED. The fix applied at the time was to reword prose so it says "the shared resolve-subject helper" rather than naming the function. Five suites now carry such de-identified prose: test-force-phases.sh (lines 25, 50, 90), test-lint-json-channel-discipline.sh (line 18), test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh (line 9, hyphenated as "resolve-subject-helper"), test-orchestrate-suggest-next-batch.sh (line 68), and test-reconcile-demote-stranded.sh (line 33). The cited entry attributes three of these to this workaround; the other two may predate it for unrelated reasons, so re-derive the set by grep rather than trusting this enumeration. Either way the standing cost is the same: the next author who writes the identifier in a comment, which is the natural thing to do, trips a gate for no reason.

SCOPE -- rule on the counting predicate explicitly and record the reasoning. Candidate directions, offered as starting points and NOT as a prescription:
  (1) Strip comment lines before counting. Cheap and closes the observed cases, but a heredoc-embedded fixture or a string literal naming the identifier still counts.
  (2) Anchor on a call-shaped pattern (the identifier at a command or command-substitution position, followed by whitespace or a quote) rather than a bare substring. This closes the heredoc and string-literal cases too.
  (3) Both: count only lines that are neither comments nor inside a quoted fixture body AND that match a call shape.
Whichever is chosen, the KNOWN LIMITATION header block (around lines 42-51) MUST be rewritten to state what the new predicate can and cannot see. It currently claims file-level regex matching as its precedent and names the limitations that follow from that; it must not keep claiming a limitation it no longer has, nor quietly retain one it still does.

DO NOT RE-LITIGATE any of the following -- they are settled and out of scope: the three bad shapes themselves (HAND_DUP, INLINE_LOOP, UNMIGRATED_IMPLICIT_ORDER); the Layer-2 file allowlist mechanism and its existing permanent entries; the deliberately narrow scripts/tests/ scan corpus and the documented reason it does not use lint_corpus_scan_collect_files's shared-corpus path; and the mandatory-declaration contract itself. Only check 3's counting predicate is in question.

VERIFICATION OBLIGATION.
  - scripts/tests/test-lint-subject-resolution-declaration.sh Case 3 (around lines 110-133) already seeds a genuine missing-declaration file and asserts a non-zero exit whose message names the seeded file. That case MUST stay green: the fix must not blind the check to the real defect it exists to catch.
  - Add a new case that is RED before the fix and GREEN after: a fully compliant suite whose comment names the resolver identifier without naming the declarer. This is the regression anchor for the false positive.
  - Gate 27 in scripts/verify-deploy.sh (around line 1980) runs this lint with --verbose over the source store and must stay green.
  - Restoring the de-identified prose in the five suites above to name the identifier directly is the natural end-to-end confirmation, and is what actually removes the standing trap for the next author. Decide whether to do that here or to leave it as follow-up, and say which.

DOCUMENTATION. context/standards/shell-script-testing.md (around lines 153-168) states the mandatory-declaration contract and describes this lint as detecting "a common_test_resolve_subject call with no paired common_test_declare_subject". That description is currently aspirational rather than accurate -- the implementation counts mentions, not calls. If the predicate becomes call-shaped, the description becomes true as written; if it stays count-based, the doc must say so plainly. Either way the doc must not leave a reader believing comments are exempt from the count when they are not.

DEPENDENCY RATIONALE (file territory, not a sequencing preference). Task 448 declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lint/ and agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/ as whole directories in its file_scope, plus context/standards/shell-script-testing.md -- every file this record edits falls inside that declaration. This repository runs concurrent writers in one shared working tree, so an unordered overlap is sequenced by a dependency edge rather than left to chance.

PRIOR ART -- DISPOSED OF EXPLICITLY AT CREATION TIME, DO NOT REDISCOVER OR RE-LITIGATE.
  - Task 418 (completed) is this record's parent. It created this lint, the explicit common_test_resolve_subject/common_test_declare_subject pair, the mandatory-declaration contract, and Gate 27 itself -- and it applied the prose-rewording workaround described above. The defect reported here is in what that work shipped, not a gap it left unexplored. The relationship is parent_task, not duplication.
  - Task 448 (open) declares scripts/lint/ and scripts/tests/ as whole directories plus context/standards/shell-script-testing.md. Its subject is containing a test harness's deliberate-break mutation of the shared source store -- a different defect in overlapping territory. Sequenced here by dependency edge; nothing in its scope is re-litigated.
  - Task 445 (open) and task 511 (open) both declare context/standards/shell-script-testing.md. 445 is the Gate 8 expected-versus-new failure distinction; 511 is an enumeration grep sweeping a gitignored venv. Both are documentation-only overlaps on one shared file and are deliberately left unsequenced rather than chained, to avoid over-constraining the backlog.
  - Task 435 (open) declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/ and agent-system/extensions/core/context/ as whole directories, for a repo-wide per-class prose reword of three deleted lifecycle command names. That declaration overlaps essentially every agent-system record and is deliberately left unsequenced on the same grounds the rest of the backlog leaves it unsequenced.
  - The creation-time backlog audit returned a cluster of roughly 0.5-scored duplicate-of-open and covered-by-closed verdicts carrying mechanical_class=none against unrelated records. Those are uncorroborated Pass-2 shortlist entries, inflated by this description's length -- the exact condition task 491 (open) exists to fix. They were checked, not accepted, and are not evidence of duplication.

### 520. Emit deploy ordering in implement dispatch body
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate batch over eight tasks (408, 464, 465, 466, 478, 489, 493, 495), session sess_1793993117_98582a. User-approved at the orchestrate-lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. SEVERITY: costly. All eight tasks in the batch reached [COMPLETED]; this defect caused no failure, it cost cycles.

## The defect

Implement-phase dispatch bodies carry no deploy-ordering instruction, so an implementation that edits the source store, redeploys, and then lands a further source edit leaves the deployed tree stale and is refused by the completion-deploy gate.

MEASURED: task 465 lost two full orchestration cycles. Its implement dispatch made source-store edits, redeployed via deploy-headless.sh, then landed a further source edit (a manifest.json registration) AFTER the deploy. orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh's completion-deploy gate refused the completion write with exit 6 ("modified_files overlap agent-system/extensions/** and the deploy is stale") on two consecutive cycles, each deferring to the next cycle's Inter-Cycle Redeploy Checkpoint. The task reached [COMPLETED] only when the checkpoint's own post-deploy reconcile promoted it.

## The workaround that proved the fix

The orchestrate lead hand-patched a "DEPLOY ORDERING" paragraph into every subsequent implement dispatch prompt in the batch, and no later task hit the gate. That instruction belongs in the dispatch body orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh emits for every implement-phase dispatch, not in an ad-hoc addendum a human has to remember per dispatch.

## Scope

Add a deploy-ordering instruction to the implement-phase dispatch body emitted by agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh, stating that every source-store edit must precede the final deploy-headless.sh run and that no source-store edit may land after it. Cover the emitted instruction with a test in agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh.

## Constraints

- Emit the instruction for implement-phase dispatches only, not research or plan phases.
- Do NOT change the completion-deploy gate. The gate is correct; this task removes the cause of its firing, not the gate.

## Distinct from the already-adjudicated gate-side remedies

Verified by reading the records, not assumed. Three prior records attack the same cost from the gate/trigger/recovery side and leave the prevention side untouched:

- Task 442 (`deploy_before_implement_postflight_to_avoid_cycle_cost`, completed) evaluated a pre-postflight-sweep deploy trigger and deliberately left it UNBUILT, recording that decision in agent-system/extensions/core/context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md. No gate logic or trigger predicate changed.
- Task 507 (`revisit_pre_sweep_deploy_trigger_ruling`, open) re-adjudicates that declined ruling against measured whole-cycle gate cost. Its file_scope is batch-orchestration-guardrails.md only.
- Task 367 (`reduce_deploy_staleness_completion_round_trip`, completed) widened the Inter-Cycle Redeploy Checkpoint's post-deploy reconcile sweep so a refused task cannot stay invisible to the self-heal mechanism. The gate itself is unchanged.

This task acts on the PREVENTION side -- what the dispatch builder tells the implementing agent -- and touches none of those three files. It is complementary to task 507, not a substitute for it, and neither blocks the other.

### 519. Infra exempt transport error dispatch deaths
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate batch over eight tasks (408, 464, 465, 466, 478, 489, 493, 495), session sess_1793993117_98582a. User-approved at the orchestrate-lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. SEVERITY: blocking. All eight tasks in the batch reached [COMPLETED]; this defect caused no failure, it cost cycles.

## The defect

A dispatch killed mid-flight by a transport error is charged as a genuine work cycle and reported as verdict `failed`, even though its work is demonstrably recoverable.

MEASURED: three plan-phase dispatches (tasks 408, 464, 478) were killed by `API Error: Unable to connect to API (ESERVFAIL)`. Each had already written `.return-meta.json` with `status: "in_progress"` but had not yet written `.orchestrator-handoff.json`. orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh therefore declined return-meta recovery with RECOVERY_DECLINED / STATUS_IN_PROGRESS, charged the cycle (`transport_error=true, meta_touched=true`), and emitted verdict `failed`. All three were then rescued by the separate stall-reprompt path, which re-dispatched at the same `dispatch_seq` and succeeded on the FIRST retry.

## The premise to adjudicate

The decline message asserts that the occurrence "is not evidence of recoverable, work-intact failure". Three first-retry successes in a single batch are direct counter-evidence to that premise. The decline, not the transport error, is what cost the cycles. That premise is the most likely thing to fix.

## Scope

Decide and implement the correct remedy. Either side, or both, is in scope:

- Postflight side: treat the conjunction `transport_error=true` + `.return-meta.json status=in_progress` + no `.orchestrator-handoff.json` as an infra-exempt DEFER (uncharged cycle, non-`failed` verdict) rather than a charged work cycle with a `failed` verdict.
- Agent side: have planner-agent write a terminal-status `.return-meta.json` earlier, or write the handoff incrementally, so a transport death leaves a readable terminal record behind.

## Hard constraints

- MUST NOT weaken the handoff-staleness gate, which is working as designed.
- MUST NOT make a genuinely-stalled dispatch (no transport error) look recoverable. Any exemption must key on real transport-error evidence, never on `status=in_progress` alone -- otherwise it reopens exactly the stall-vs-agent-fault conflation that prior work closed.

## What the closed record already settled, and what it deliberately did not

Verified by reading the records, not assumed:

- Task 426 (`recovery_declined_cannot_tell_stall_from_agent_fault`, completed) fixed the RECOVERY_DECLINED arm's two recording bugs and added arm-local stall/strand corroboration to its message and to issue-record.sh's `--tags-json`. Its own completion summary states this was done "without touching either canonical signal's own trigger expression". The trigger, the cycle charge, and the `failed` verdict are therefore untouched by that record -- they are this task's subject.
- Task 428 (`enforce_planner_agent_terminal_write`, completed) examined the terminal-write gap and resolved documentation-only: it recorded in agent-system/extensions/core/docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md's Outcome Channels section that RECOVERY_DECLINED detection is the sole, detection-only enforcement of the terminal write on the /orchestrate direct-dispatch path. That establishes the detector's ROLE; it does not address whether the decline's premise is correct under transport-error conditions.
- Task 258 (`fix_postflight_recovery_decline_attribution`, completed) fixed WHO the defect is attributed to, not WHETHER the cycle is charged.

Research should start by reading those three records' artifacts before re-deriving any of the above.

## Durable evidence anchors

- specs/events.jsonl: evt_1793996242895_2qx9GM, evt_1793996246440_SEHzq3, evt_1793996249954_Roptu4
- issue-log entries: 408/iss_1793996242954_ouf9ST, 464/iss_1793996246495_y5MWCS, 478/iss_1793996250009_5hs0ww
- detecting site: skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md:cycle-postflight-recovery-declined

## Overlapping open file territory

Several open tasks declare overlapping file_scope on orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh and its test suite (notably 501 `cycle_postflight_containment_parity`, 472 `handoff_validator_crash_and_defect_record_gap`, 476, 448). No dependency edge is asserted here because none of them blocks this work; the overlap is recorded so batch admission orders rather than parallelizes them.

### 518. State the blockers[] admission contract and continuation-on-partial requirement at every handoff-authoring site
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: orchestrator
- **Dependencies**: Task 472, Task 435

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

## Goal

Every handoff-authoring site must state two facts it currently states nowhere: that the base orchestrate engine LENGTH-COUNTS `blockers[]` and therefore admits no non-blocking entry, and that a continuation pointer is required when `status = "partial"`. A resolved retrospective note placed in `blockers[]` must be recognizable as the defect it is at the moment of authoring, not after it has already stranded a task.

## Provenance

Observed in session sess_1793988163_b25d4a, 2026-11-06, on task 418 (explicit_lint_fixture_test_subject_resolution). Phases 1-3 completed cleanly, were committed, and verified green. The implement dispatch's wrap-up then wrote a handoff that hard-blocked the task from any further dispatch. The defect was in the ARTIFACT, not in the implementation:

  1. The wrap-up placed an already-RESOLVED retrospective note into `blockers[]`. The note concerned transient API transport instability and its own text ended "No blocker prevents resuming phases 4-8 in a normal dispatch". Per docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md's `blockers` field definition, the base engine "only ever reads `blockers | length`, never individual" field content -- so a self-described non-blocker is structurally indistinguishable from a live one.
  2. The same write omitted `continuation_path`, which the validator expects when status is `partial`.

The orchestrator's own postflight independently raised HANDOFF_VALIDATION_FAILED and attributed it to agent-system/extensions/core/agents/general-implementation-agent.md.

## The gate, and its asymmetry

scripts/orchestrate-triage-classify.sh's `partial` branch (verified 2026-11-06, the `elif $status == "partial"` block) evaluates in this order:

  - `if $cont` -> group "implement" (routes to implement EVEN WITH blockers present)
  - `elif ($bc > 0)` -> group "needs_human"
  - `else` -> group "implement"

With `cont=false` and `bc=1`, only needs_human is reachable. Note the asymmetry the authoring sites never mention: a continuation pointer ALONE would have routed to implement with the blocker entry still present. Two independent omissions were each individually sufficient to avoid the strand, and the authoring instruction makes neither salient.

## MEASURED SCOPE -- the gap is systemic, not one agent's

The change request scoped this to general-implementation-agent.md. Measurement (2026-11-06) shows the gap is universal and the instruction is duplicated verbatim:

  - 43 files under agent-system/extensions/*/ carry the identical boilerplate paragraph beginning "`summary` and `blockers` are BOTH required top-level fields." Enumerated: core/agents/{general-implementation,planner,reviser}-agent.md plus 40 extension agents across books, cslib, email, epidemiology, filetypes, founder, latex, lean, nix, nvim, present, python, rust, typst, web, z3.
  - ZERO of them contain the string `continuation_path`. Verified by grep across every handoff-writing agent including general-research-agent.md.
  - ZERO of them state that `blockers[]` is length-counted, or that an entry is admissible only when progress is genuinely prevented.
  - What the paragraph DOES say is entirely about not OMITTING the fields: "`[]` is normal and expected on a clean researched/planned/implemented return. The handoff validator FAILS on either one missing, so write both every time." It is an anti-omission instruction with no admission criterion, which is the precise hole that admits a resolved note.

## A CONTRADICTION in the shared hard-mode contract

context/contracts/wrap-up.md (the H9 contract, `--hard`-only by its own header, loaded by ~48 agents) is the one place that discusses a blocker-bearing partial, and it instructs the exact shape that strands a task. Its "`blocked` vs. `partial`-with-`blockers`" subsection reads: "Use `partial` with a populated `blockers[]` entry AND NO `continuation_path` when some phases completed successfully but a specific remaining phase is blocked by a cause outside the agent's control".

For the case that subsection describes -- a genuinely external, unclosable blocker -- needs_human is the INTENDED routing, and the subsection even says so ("stops same-run redispatch ... routes the task to a visible human-escalation row"). So this is not simply wrong. But it is the only prose an agent has to pattern-match against, it never states that the omission of the pointer is what does the routing, and it was not loaded at all in the observed run (standard mode never loads this file). An agent in standard mode has no guidance whatsoever; an agent in hard mode has guidance that reads as a template for "blockers plus no continuation".

Also note: `summary` is the correct home for a resolved retrospective note. The engine does not count it.

## WHAT TASK 472 ALREADY OWNS -- read before planning, do not re-litigate

472 (handoff_validator_crash_and_defect_record_gap, not_started) is the sibling record and already owns two items that would otherwise be in scope here. This record is deliberately scoped to the DELTA.

  - 472 acceptance item 3 owns "The blockers element shape is stated at the authoring site(s), alongside the existing `artifacts[]` object-shape sentence." That is element SHAPE -- object versus bare string. THIS record is about ADMISSION SEMANTICS: whether an entry belongs in the array at all. Both are holes in the same paragraph and the fixes land in the same sentences, which is why the dependency edge below exists.
  - 472 acceptance item 4 owns "The Check 5 partial/continuation question has an explicit recorded verdict, and the validator, the schema document and the authoring instruction state the same thing", and its body instructs the implementer to establish whether the advisory WARN was a deliberate ruling. THE MECHANICAL-GUARD QUESTION FOR continuation-on-partial IS THEREFORE 472'S TO RULE ON, NOT THIS RECORD'S. Do not independently decide to make validate-handoff.sh's Check 5 blocking; read 472's landed verdict and conform the prose to it.
  - 472 names only THREE authoring sites (core/agents/general-implementation-agent.md, lean/agents/lean-implementation-agent.md, lean/agents/lean-implementation-hard-agent.md). As scoped it would fix 3 of the 43 sites carrying the paragraph. The remaining 40 are this record's territory.
  - 472 does NOT include context/contracts/wrap-up.md in its file_scope at all, so the contradiction above is unowned.

## CANDIDATE APPROACHES -- weigh, do not assume

Presented as candidates with tradeoffs. The plan must choose and record why.

(A) PROSE AT EVERY SITE. Extend the existing boilerplate paragraph in all 43 files with an admission criterion ("an entry here asserts that progress is PREVENTED; the engine counts entries and does not read them, so a resolved or informational note belongs in `summary`") and a continuation-on-partial sentence.
    + Reaches every authoring site, including standard mode where no contract file loads.
    + Matches the precedent 472 cites: the closed record pin_handoff_artifacts_element_shape fixed the adjacent `artifacts[]` hole by pinning prose at the authoring sites.
    - 43-file edit, which is the kind of duplicated-prose sweep that keeps recurring. 435 (reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class) is already an in-flight sweep over this same corpus for a different reason.

(B) CENTRALIZE, THEN POINT. Move the handoff-authoring paragraph into one shared context file loaded in BOTH modes (wrap-up.md cannot serve: it is `--hard`-only) and replace the 43 copies with a reference. context/contracts/phase-closure.md is already cited by wrap-up.md as "the universal recording site ... loaded in both modes" and is a candidate host.
    + Removes the duplication that is the root cause of this class of gap recurring, and fixes the next such gap once instead of 43 times.
    - Larger blast radius; changes what every handoff-writing agent loads. Must verify the host file is genuinely loaded on every handoff-writing dispatch (research, plan, implement, standard and hard) before relying on it -- an unverified assumption here silently removes the instruction from some sites.

(C) MECHANICAL GUARD ON ADMISSION. Have validate-handoff.sh (or hooks/validate-handoff-location.sh, which already gates every handoff Write/Edit) reject a `blockers[]` entry that is self-evidently non-blocking, or require a continuation pointer whenever blockers are present on a `partial`.
    + Catches the defect at write time regardless of what prose the agent loaded, which is the only approach that does not depend on the agent having read anything.
    - "Self-evidently non-blocking" is not mechanically decidable from entry text; a guard would have to key on something coarser. The continuation-pointer half of this is 472's call per the note above. A guard that forces a continuation pointer whenever blockers exist would also break the legitimate external-blocker case wrap-up.md describes, where needs_human IS the wanted outcome.
    NOTE the existing enforcement asymmetry worth stating in whichever direction is chosen: hooks/validate-handoff-location.sh rejects an ABSENT `blockers` key with exit 2 at write time, but nothing checks what an entry asserts.

(D) COMPLEMENT, NOT ALTERNATIVE. (A or B) for the admission criterion, plus whatever 472 rules for the continuation pointer. Probably the intended shape, but the plan must say so explicitly rather than inherit it.

Also in scope either way: resolve the wrap-up.md subsection so it states WHY the pointer's absence routes to human escalation, rather than presenting "blockers and no continuation_path" as a bare template.

## Acceptance

  1. Every handoff-authoring site reachable by a standard-mode implement dispatch states that `blockers[]` is length-counted by the engine and that an entry asserts progress is prevented, with `summary` named as the home for resolved or informational notes. "Every site" means all 43 measured files, or a verified-loaded shared host plus references, per the chosen approach.
  2. The continuation-on-partial requirement is stated at the authoring sites, conforming to 472's recorded Check 5 verdict rather than re-deciding it.
  3. context/contracts/wrap-up.md's "`blocked` vs. `partial`-with-`blockers`" subsection explains the routing consequence of omitting the continuation pointer instead of presenting the shape as a template.
  4. A fixture test asserts the admission criterion is enforced, if and only if approach (C) is adopted for it; if the chosen approach is prose-only, record explicitly that no mechanical check is added and why.
  5. The asymmetry is stated once at whichever site is authoritative: a continuation pointer routes a blocker-bearing partial to implement, its absence routes to human escalation.

## Backlog reconciliation

Audited against the full backlog corpus via audit-task-proposals.sh before creation. Recall-biased by design; returned covered-by-closed 73, duplicate-of-open 14, overlaps-open 3, scope-blind 41, subset-of-open 1. The discriminations that matter:

  - 472 -- the sibling record. NOT a duplicate but a genuine partial overlap, handled by scoping this record to the delta and by the serializing edge. See the dedicated section above for the item-by-item split. If an operator prefers, the two could be merged; they were kept separate because 472 is a validator-crash-and-recorder-attribution record whose prose items are secondary, while this record's whole subject is the authoring contract across 43 sites.
  - 435 -- shares the entire agent corpus (it declares core/agents/ and 16 extension agents/ directories wholesale) for an unrelated prose-rewording sweep. Concurrent edits to the same 43 files are a live conflict risk. Serializing edge declared. 472 declares the same edge for the same reason.
  - 430, 446, 486, 515, 517 -- each shares only general-implementation-agent.md and concerns phase-commit staging, file_scope reconciliation, or rebase-wait guidance. Different subjects; rejected as covering. No edges declared: single-file overlap on a large file, and over-serializing on it would stall this record behind five unrelated records.
  - 378 -- reviser-agent handoff contract contradiction, defect class HANDOFF_STALE_OR_ABSENT (a writer emitting NO handoff). This record is about a handoff that was written and admitted a bad entry. Rejected as covering; already an edge-predecessor of 472, so it is serialized transitively.

Dependency edges are SERIALIZING, on shared file territory and on 472's Check 5 ruling:

  - 472 -- shares the authoring sites and owns the element-shape and Check 5 items this record must conform to rather than duplicate. This is the one edge with a genuine logical component: acceptance item 2 cannot be discharged before 472's verdict lands.
  - 435 -- shares the whole agent corpus for an unrelated sweep.

## Why this is worth fixing

The observed run lost nothing recoverable -- phases 1-3 were committed and green -- but the task could not be re-dispatched by any ordinary path, and the only thing wrong was two fields in a wrap-up artifact. The cost is paid by an operator diagnosing a strand by hand, and the trigger is an instruction paragraph that tells 43 agents to always write a field while telling none of them what the field means.

### 517. Require a phase commit to regenerate and stage the derived surfaces it stales
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 455, Task 515

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/core/**` in THIS repository (never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Re-derive the store rather than trusting this note: `source_dir` lives PER-EXTENSION at `.extensions.<name>.source_dir` in a consumer repo's `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, NOT as a top-level key. Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via `deploy-headless.sh`. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under `agent-system/**`; both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in `specs/**`.

ORIGIN: conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an `/orchestrate` invocation over task 867 in `~/Projects/BimodalLogic`, session `sess_1793993028_251d86`, user-approved at the orchestrate-lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN BimodalLogic, per the mis-siting correction under #390. The identifier 867 is a BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository.

CITATIONS -- FOUR RECORDED RECURRENCES across Phases 21, 22.1 and 22.2 of one task, each reproducible against the consumer repository where they were observed (all class `minor`):

  for id in iss_1793914994233_yAr1ND iss_1793993819933_l3QK5V iss_1793996881076_bMWXPp; do
    jq -c --arg id "$id" 'select(.entry_id==$id)' \
      ~/Projects/BimodalLogic/specs/867_formalize_gabbay_separation_ztime/issues.jsonl
  done

========================================================================
THE OBSERVATION, stated honestly: adding or editing a Lean module desynchronizes two sets of pinned counts -- the generated per-directory inventory blocks in `README.md`, `FormalSystem/README.md` and `FormalSystem/Metalogic/README.md` (reddening `check-module-invariants.sh`'s INV check until `--emit-inventory` is re-run), and the FormalSystem file/line counts pinned in `typst/generated/status.typ` (firing the pre-commit `typst-sync-check.sh --counts-only` gate). BOTH were repaired through their sanctioned entry points on each of the four occasions. NOTHING IS BROKEN IN EITHER SCRIPT and no work was wrong. This is a standing, predictable per-phase cost, and the deliverable is guidance, not a repair.

=== THIS RECORD OWNS THE EXECUTION HALF ONLY. READ THIS BEFORE SCOPING ANYTHING ===
There are two distinct halves to this friction and this repository already has an open record for the other one. Do not merge them and do not duplicate it.
  - DECLARATION HALF (NOT THIS RECORD): whether the generated surfaces appear in a plan's declared `file_scope` / Files-to-modify rows at all. `scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` ALREADY carries a `Generated-surface appending` stage whose documented job is exactly this -- it appends the status-table typst file and every markdown README whose `<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: inventory -->` block covers a directory on the edited file's ancestor chain, gated behind a `generated_surfaces_available()` predicate so it stays inert in a repository carrying neither generator. #455 owns determining why that stage did not cover its own cited case, and #446 owns the plan-to-file_scope reconciliation gap beneath it. DO NOT EDIT `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` OR ITS TEST FROM THIS RECORD.
  - EXECUTION HALF (THIS RECORD): declaring a path does not regenerate the file at it. Even with the surfaces correctly declared, nothing in the phase-checkpoint or commit protocol tells the implementing agent to RE-RUN the generators and STAGE their output WITHIN the module-adding phase. That is the gap these four recurrences sit in, and the requested change is to that guidance.

EVIDENCE THAT THE EXECUTION HALF IS GENUINELY UNCOVERED, re-derived at creation time (re-derive before editing; line numbers move):
- `scripts/lib/derived-artifact-probe-lib.sh` is the single home of a repo-declared, build-free derived-artifact staleness probe, driven by a hand-authored `derived_artifact_probes` key in a consumer repo's `.claude-extensions.json` (`component`, `check_command`, `derived_output_dirs`). Its consumers are `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` and `command-gate-out.sh` -- BOTH implement-POSTFLIGHT engines. The probe therefore runs only AFTER every phase commit of the plan has already landed, which is structurally too late to stop a phase commit from desynchronizing a pinned count.
- The originating consumer repository's own `.claude-extensions.json` carries NO `derived_artifact_probes` key at all (verified absent at creation time with `jq -r '.derived_artifact_probes // "ABSENT"'`). So in the repository where all four recurrences happened, even the postflight probe never fired. Research must decide whether that absence is a separate consumer-repo gap to be recorded locally there, or evidence that the declaration mechanism is too obscure to be discovered -- and say which.
- `context/checkpoints/checkpoint-commit.md`'s step 1 (`Determine Targeted Scope`) computes what to stage from the plan file and the agent's self-reported `modified_files`. A regenerated inventory block is in neither unless the generator has already been run, so the staging step cannot rescue an unrun generator.

=== THE DECISION THIS RECORD OWES, with alternatives on their own merits ===
  (a) A PHASE-LEVEL OBLIGATION IN THE COMMIT PROTOCOL. State in `checkpoints/checkpoint-commit.md` (and mirror into `agents/general-implementation-agent.md`'s phase-commit prose) that when a phase's own edits touch a declared derived-output surface, the phase MUST re-run that surface's sanctioned regeneration entry point and stage the result in the SAME phase commit. Most direct, and keeps the obligation where the staging decision is actually made -- but it is prose-only, so nothing detects a phase that skips it.
  (b) HOIST THE EXISTING PROBE TO PHASE-COMMIT TIME. Call `derived-artifact-probe-lib.sh`'s probe from the phase-commit path as well as from implement-postflight, so a stale derived surface is flagged at the commit that staled it rather than at the end of the plan. Mechanical rather than prose, and reuses a built, tested mechanism instead of adding one -- but the probe is explicitly build-free and fail-open, it only DETECTS staleness, and it still requires a declaration the originating repository does not carry.
  (c) BOTH, WITH (a) AS THE RULE AND (b) AS ITS BACKSTOP. Likely the right answer, but say so as a ruling with the cost acknowledged rather than defaulting into it.
Whichever is chosen: the obligation must be expressed in terms of DECLARED derived surfaces, never by naming one repository's generator scripts in a core file. Core must not acquire knowledge of `check-module-invariants.sh` or `typst-sync-check.sh` beyond what `plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` already carries behind its availability predicate.

=== HARD CONSTRAINTS ===
- Stay INERT in a repository that declares no derived surfaces. A repository with no `derived_artifact_probes` key and no generator scripts must see no new obligation and no new failure.
- Do NOT make this a reason to run a full build at every phase commit. The existing probe is deliberately build-free; preserve that property in anything added.
- Do not reach across the repository boundary. The remedies specific to the originating repository -- its generator invocations, its `nolints.json`, its maintenance docs -- are not agent-system files. If the ruling concludes part of the answer belongs in a consumer-repo directive (including declaring `derived_artifact_probes` there), the correct outcome is to say so and have a record created in that repository, not to edit it from here.
- Resist scope growth. This is the MINOR item of its batch, distinguished only by recurring four times. If the honest answer is "(a) alone, three sentences of prose", that is a complete and successful outcome.

=== VERIFY ===
- If (b) is chosen in any form: extend the probe library's own tests to pin that it is callable from the phase-commit path and that it stays fail-open and build-free. Pin the inert case -- a repository declaring no probes gets no new behaviour.
- If (a) alone is chosen: pin that `scripts/lint/lint-contract-compliance.sh` (or whichever lint governs the checkpoint corpus) still passes, and that the new obligation does not self-trip any verbatim-fragment check.
- Re-derive the four-recurrence claim from the cited entry ids rather than trusting the count in this description.
- Use the scoped test narrowing, not the full aggregator, while other sessions are active.

=== TERRITORY EDGES, declared with rationale rather than as bare numbers ===

It shares NO files with this record, so this is an ordering edge, not a contention edge -- this record's guidance must agree with whatever #455 lands about which surfaces a plan declares. It in turn follows #446. Its own citation predates these four recurrences, which are evidence the friction persisted after it was filed.

SERIALIZATION-only, operator-liftable with `update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove` if research confirms this record's change lands entirely in `checkpoints/checkpoint-commit.md`.
