### 516. Stop the Lean plan compliance check failing on not-yet-reached end-state deliverables
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: lean-extension
- **Dependencies**: Task 504, Task 515, Task 493

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/lean/**` and `agent-system/extensions/core/**` in THIS repository (never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Re-derive the store rather than trusting this note: `source_dir` lives PER-EXTENSION at `.extensions.<name>.source_dir` in a consumer repo's `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, NOT as a top-level key. Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via `deploy-headless.sh`. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under `agent-system/**`; both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in `specs/**`.

ORIGIN: conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an `/orchestrate` invocation over task 867 in `~/Projects/BimodalLogic`, session `sess_1793993028_251d86`, user-approved at the orchestrate-lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN BimodalLogic, per the mis-siting correction under #390. The identifier 867 is a BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository.

CITATION, reproducible against the consumer repository where it was observed -- `BimodalLogic:867:iss_1793993819916_UOHnrR` (class `costly`):

  jq -c 'select(.entry_id=="iss_1793993819916_UOHnrR")' \
    ~/Projects/BimodalLogic/specs/867_formalize_gabbay_separation_ztime/issues.jsonl

========================================================================
THE DEFECT, in one sentence: `scripts/lean-plan-compliance-check.sh` returns `COMPLIANCE_CHECK: failed` whenever a plan's MISSING list consists of the task's END-STATE DELIVERABLES -- declarations that only the plan's FINAL phases produce and that no earlier phase can possibly reach -- because its single global `**Goal Polarity**` field cannot express a plan-end-state goal, and so cannot distinguish "this declaration is missing because the plan diverged" from "this declaration is missing because its phase has not run yet".

MEASURED, carried-unverified from the cited entry. At Phase 22.1 of 28 the ENTIRE missing list was `sepZ`, `sepZ_sepClauses`, `separationOver_ztime`, `trZ`, `openTruthAt_iff_trZ`, `openValidIn_ztime_iff_trZ`, `plusTruthAt_sepZ_iff` -- every one of them produced by Phases 25-26. The verdict was IDENTICAL against two different plan revisions of the same task, which is the evidence that this is a property of the script and not of either plan. A gate that is red by construction for 24 of a 28-phase plan's phases teaches implementers to ignore it, which is the real cost.

=== VERIFY THE MECHANISM FIRST, do not trust this summary ===
In the source store, `extensions/lean/scripts/lean-plan-compliance-check.sh` resolves `**Goal Polarity**` with a single `grep -oP` over the whole plan file, lowercases it, and then branches three ways (re-derive; line numbers move):
- polarity set and NOT `refutation` -> `COMPLIANCE_CHECK: skipped`, with a message conceding that "a mixed or otherwise non-refutation polarity cannot be expressed by this single global field";
- polarity `refutation` -> every harvested name must be ABSENT;
- polarity absent (the default) -> MUST-EXIST: every harvested name must be PRESENT, and any `[MISSING]` fails the whole check.
The field is therefore already admitted to be an inexpressive single global switch. This record is the third call on that inexpressiveness, after the keyword-exclusion and colon-optional narrowings the script's own header records -- which is itself evidence about where the contract belongs.

=== THE DECISION THIS RECORD OWES, with alternatives recorded on their own merits ===
  (a) PER-PHASE REACHABILITY. Teach the check which phase introduces each goal name, so a name whose introducing phase has not run is reported as `[PENDING]` and does not fail the check. Strongest verdict, and the only one that distinguishes real divergence from normal progress -- but it needs a per-goal phase attribution the plan format does not carry today, so it implies a `plan-format.md` change and a new authoring obligation on every Lean plan.
  (b) A PLAN-END-STATE POLARITY VALUE. Add a third polarity (e.g. `end-state`) meaning "these names are expected only once the final phase lands", under which a MISSING list is reported but not failed before the plan is complete. Cheapest, and expressible in the existing single global field -- but it buys leniency by discarding the divergence signal for the whole plan, so a genuinely diverged plan also stops failing.
  (c) GATE THE CHECK ON PLAN COMPLETION. Run the MUST-EXIST branch only at the plan's final phase and `skipped` before it. Preserves the signal exactly where it is sound and needs no format change -- but it means the check contributes nothing for most of a long plan's life, which may be the honest answer rather than a defect.
Weigh (b) and (c) against the `skipped` precedent the script ALREADY sets for an inexpressible polarity: that precedent chose honest abstention over a wrong verdict, which is an argument for (c). Weigh (a) against the authoring cost it pushes onto every plan. Whichever is chosen, "strip the end-state names out of the Goals block" must NOT be the settled answer -- that is the same readability-destroying workaround pattern a sibling record already rejected for this script.

=== SCOPE BOUNDARY, binding ===
This record owns ONLY the phase-reachability-versus-divergence question. It does NOT own WHICH backticked tokens in the `**Goals**` block count as goal declarations -- that is #504's territory (over-broad harvest picking up prose binder names and pre-existing cross-referenced lemmas). The two are genuinely disjoint: narrowing the harvest to leading identifiers would NOT fix this defect, because every name in the measured missing list above IS a leading identifier and a genuine plan deliverable. #504 must land first (edge declared below) so this record reasons over a settled name set.

=== VERIFICATION ===
Extend `scripts/tests/test-lean-plan-compliance-check.sh` and the `scripts/tests/fixtures/challenge/` fixture set rather than replacing them. The minimum bar is a regression fixture that is a multi-phase plan whose Goals block declares end-state deliverables, evaluated at a non-final phase, which the check does NOT fail on -- plus a companion fixture of a genuinely DIVERGED plan at the same phase which it still DOES fail on. Without that second fixture the change is indistinguishable from disabling the check. The script's own header warns that two Goals-block readers must not drift apart on which header shapes they accept; find the second reader (`scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` is the likely one) before changing either.

=== TERRITORY EDGES, declared with rationale rather than as bare numbers ===
- #504 (`lean_goals_block_harvest_contract`, not_started): SEMANTIC predecessor, declares all three of this record's primary files. Owns the harvest contract this record's verdict logic sits downstream of. Not liftable.
- #493 (`plan_format_tier_justification_fixes`, researching): declares `core/context/formats/plan-format.md`, which options (a) and (b) above would also edit. SERIALIZATION-only but currently IN FLIGHT -- reconcile by hand rather than assuming the file is free.
- The predecessor named in this record's `dependencies` alongside those two shares `lean/agents/lean-implementation-agent.md` (the check's invocation site) and is SERIALIZATION-only, operator-liftable with `update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove` if research confirms this record does not need to touch that agent file at all.
- #813 and #824 (in the originating repository's numbering, migrated or not) own the Challenge block's precedence/snapshot-prerequisite rules and the fixture-dependent gate statement. Do not edit those contracts here.
- #385 (`lean4_plan_with_zero_lean_goals`, completed) and #360 (`validate_carried_unverified_names_a_source`, not_started) are adjacent; read #385's artifacts, since a zero-goals plan and an all-pending-goals plan are near neighbours in this script's branch structure.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED against the full open backlog and the closed corpus via `scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh` (verdict document recorded on this entry's own `audit_verdict` field). ### 515. Rebase the bounded-wait guidance on the measured foreground-backgrounding ceiling
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and `agent-system/extensions/lean/**` in THIS repository (never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`; a hand-authored file under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration, so the edit appears to succeed and has no lasting effect). Re-derive the store rather than trusting this note: `source_dir` lives PER-EXTENSION at `.extensions.<name>.source_dir` in a consumer repo's `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json`, NOT as a top-level key, so a top-level `jq -r '.source_dir'` returning null must not be read as the key being absent. Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via `deploy-headless.sh`. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under `agent-system/**` (`rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`, `rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in `specs/**`.

ORIGIN: conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an `/orchestrate` invocation over task 867 in `~/Projects/BimodalLogic`, session `sess_1793993028_251d86`, user-approved at the orchestrate-lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN BimodalLogic: an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store, per the mis-siting correction recorded under this repo's #390 and the authoring hole under #391. The identifier 867 is a BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository.

CITATION, reproducible against the consumer repository where it was observed -- `BimodalLogic:867:iss_1793996881041_SDZX8Y` (class `costly`):

  jq -c 'select(.entry_id=="iss_1793996881041_SDZX8Y")' \
    ~/Projects/BimodalLogic/specs/867_formalize_gabbay_separation_ztime/issues.jsonl

========================================================================
THE DEFECT, in one sentence: the mandated single-call bounded-waiter idiom is built on a 600-second foreground Bash ceiling that this harness does not honour. The MEASURED behaviour is that a foreground Bash call is forcibly backgrounded once it exceeds roughly 180 seconds, REGARDLESS of the value passed in that call's own `timeout` parameter. The idiom therefore cannot complete in one call for a full `lake build` or a multi-gate sweep, and the guidance's central remedy -- "raise the Bash tool's own `timeout` parameter to `600000`" -- is inert.

MEASURED CONSEQUENCE, carried-unverified from the cited entry and not re-measurable in this repository: this stalled dispatch 17 of the originating task in the very run that produced this record. The agent ended its turn on an unresolved background wait, landing Phase 22.1's commit but NONE of its wrap-up artifacts, and the orchestrator had to spend its one sanctioned stall re-prompt to recover. This failure mode will stall every long Lean dispatch the same way until the guidance is corrected.

=== FIRST OBLIGATION: RE-MEASURE THE CEILING BEFORE EDITING ANYTHING ===
The ~180s figure is CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited issue entry. The whole deliverable is a numeric model, so research MUST establish the real number first-hand rather than transcribing it: issue a foreground Bash call of known duration with the tool `timeout` parameter raised well above it, and record at what elapsed time the harness takes the call away. Do this at several durations to find the actual boundary and to confirm the parameter is genuinely ignored rather than merely mis-set. If the measured boundary turns out to be the documented 600s after all, close this record as no-change with the measurement recorded -- that is a successful outcome here, not a failure. Record the harness version alongside the number, since this is a harness behaviour and not a property of this repository.

=== WHAT THE GUIDANCE CURRENTLY SAYS (re-derive; line numbers move) ===
- `context/patterns/external-process-wait.md` Rule 1 makes raising the tool `timeout` parameter to `600000` a REQUIRED step, calls the inner bound "inert unless this parameter is raised FROM that default", and carries a "Why 540 < 600" paragraph explaining a 60-second margin beneath a 600s ceiling. Its sub-case split (3a "fits under the 600s ceiling", 3b "cannot fit under the 600s ceiling at all") is defined entirely in terms of that ceiling.
- `context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md`'s `### Sizing a Wait Near the Per-Call Ceiling` prescribes a sequence of separate bounded calls "each sized safely under the ceiling", and states that the tool-`timeout`-parameter pairing applies PER CALL, again at `600000`.
- Both numbers are wrong if the measurement confirms ~180s, and so is the 3a/3b sub-case boundary derived from them: under a 180s ceiling, essentially every real `lake build` falls in 3b, which inverts which branch is the common case.

=== THE WORKING REMEDY TO PROMOTE INTO THE GUIDANCE ===
The re-prompted agent in the originating run resolved it this way, and it is the shape worth making canonical for the sub-ceiling-impossible case: detach the build writing a SENTINEL FILE on completion, then resolve it with repeated foreground polls each sized comfortably under the measured ceiling. Note this is NOT a return to the sentinel-poll antipattern `bounded-build-waiter.md`'s `## The Defect` section opens with: that defect was an UNBOUNDED `until grep -q` loop whose sentinel became unwritable when the guarded command died, and a name-matching liveness test that matched itself. A bounded sequence of short foreground polls that ALSO checks writer liveness by captured PID (`kill -0 "$pid"`) is a different construct. The record must say explicitly why the sentinel is safe here and how writer death is still detected, or it will read as licensing the very shape the file forbids.

=== PROPAGATION OBLIGATION -- the expensive half of this record ===
`bounded-build-waiter.md` is a GENERATED-COPY SOURCE, not an `@`-import: an `@`-reference inside an agent body does NOT auto-resolve when a subagent is spawned, so each in-scope agent body carries a LITERAL copy of the canonical bullet pair from the file's `### Copy this exact text` block, and `scripts/lint/lint-agent-contracts.sh` Check H keeps every copy in sync by comparing it against that source. Check K separately reads four anchor lines (`NOTIFICATION_PHRASE`, `BACKGROUND_PHRASE_1`, `BACKGROUND_PHRASE_2`, `PROHIBITIVE_MARKER_PATTERN`) from the file at runtime. A creation-time read counted 29 files under `agent-system/extensions/*/agents/` carrying the MUST bullet verbatim; re-derive the set rather than trusting that count:

  cd agent-system/extensions && grep -rln 'bounded-build-waiter.md.*canonical idiom VERBATIM' --include='*.md' .
  cd agent-system && grep -rln '600000' --include='*.md' extensions/

IF the copied bullet text changes, every carrier must change in the SAME change and the lint must still pass. PREFER A CHANGE THAT LEAVES THE COPIED BULLET BYTE-IDENTICAL and say so explicitly if achieved -- the bullet forbids `run_in_background`/`Monitor` and turn-ending on an unresolved wait, none of which the numeric correction need touch. The 600000-bearing prose lives in the surrounding pattern files and in `lean/rules/lean4.md`, `lean/context/project/lean4/operations/long-builds.md`, `core/context/contracts/batch-gate-stage.md`, `core/context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md`, `core/skills/skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md` and `core/agents/general-implementation-agent.md`; a correction confined to those is a far smaller change than a bullet rewrite.

=== DO NOT RE-LITIGATE (each has a recorded ruling section in the file) ===
- Hard variants do not inherit the contract; research agents are out of this mechanism's scope; Rule 5 does not propagate. Note them if relevant, do not fold them in.
- Do NOT weaken the livelock protection detachment exists to provide: a killed Lean build caches no artifact, so retries restart at the same module. The correct shape keeps detachment and fixes the WAIT step.
- Preserve the canonical idiom's liveness primitive: a captured `pid=$!` and `kill -0 "$pid"`, never `ps | grep` or `pgrep -f` (a PreToolUse hook, `hooks/validate-bounded-waiter.sh`, blocks the name-match-inside-a-loop conjunction, and `kill -0`'s presence grants no exemption).

=== VERIFY ===
- `scripts/lint/lint-agent-contracts.sh` passes, Checks H and K included. If the copied bullet is unchanged, PIN that it is byte-identical rather than asserting it.
- `scripts/tests/test-lint-agent-contracts.sh`, `-heartbeat.sh`, `-handoff-section.sh`, `-await-notification.sh` and any `validate-bounded-waiter` test stay green.
- The newly documented poll idiom is exercised end to end at least once against a real long command, not only described.
- Use the scoped commit narrowing, not the full aggregator, while other sessions are active.

=== TERRITORY EDGES, declared with rationale rather than as bare numbers ===
- #454 (`same_turn_gate_runs_foreground`, not_started) declares EXACTLY this record's two pattern files. It is NOT a duplicate -- it adds a result-needed-this-turn criterion and a fourth shape to the Shape Taxonomy -- but its own prescribed remedy for "cannot fit the ceiling AND the result is needed this turn" is, in its words, "raise the timeout per the existing guidance", which this record establishes is inert. THIS RECORD MUST LAND FIRST so #454 reasons from a corrected premise; an edge has been added onto #454 making it follow this record.
- #439 (`audit_cross_extension_bounded_wait_duplication`, not_started) shares `bounded-build-waiter.md` and `lint-agent-contracts.sh`. It rules on PROPAGATION ARCHITECTURE (one canonical source versus 29 literal copies), a different question from this record's numeric correction. An edge has been added onto #439 making it follow this record, so its audit operates on corrected text.
- #412 (lean tier vocabulary, not_started) shares the two lean implementation agents; #409, #418, #420 share `batch-orchestration-guardrails.md`; #430 shares `general-implementation-agent.md`; #431 shares `lint-agent-contracts.sh`; #435 declares `core/context/` coarsely. All SERIALIZATION-only and operator-liftable with `update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove` once an operator confirms the named files are not contested.
- #376, #356, #392, #172, #236, #237, #345, #347, #354, #175 (all completed) previously touched this corpus. #392 in particular landed "the tool-timeout half of the bounded-wait idiom" -- the very instruction this record corrects -- so read its artifacts before concluding the 600000 figure was never deliberate.

### 514. Convergence guard blind to zero-dispatch cycles
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: orchestrator
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage, Channel (b), of an orchestration run; user-approved task creation for a confirmed defect. Durable records: issue-log entry iss_1793992157216_ZCSwKL (class "orchestrator convergence guard blind spot", severity costly, resolution open) in specs/445_gate8_expected_vs_new_failure_distinction/issues.jsonl, and metadata.detected_defects[0] (class ORCHESTRATOR_CONVERGENCE_GUARD_BLIND_SPOT) in specs/.orchestration/.return-meta-multi-sess_1793990805_500dd7.json.

DEFECT. In orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh, the block commented "Convergence guard" tests `${#probed_dispatch[@]} -eq 0 && ${#eligible_tasks[@]} -gt 0`; on the else branch it resets `.consecutive_no_dispatch_cycles` to 0, and only at a streak of 3 does it set stop_reason="convergence_guard". The predicate is wrong on two counts:

(1) WRONG ARRAY. `probed_dispatch` is not the cycle's emitted dispatch set. It is the output of the read-only lock PROBE loop (`task-lock.sh check`), and it is then narrowed twice more -- first to `probed_dispatch_post_h1` (H1-refused candidates moved to blocked[]), then by the live per-task loop, whose only `out_dispatch_rows+=` site is at the very END of the loop body. Every `continue` before that point drops a task from the emitted `dispatch[]` while leaving it counted in `probed_dispatch`. Those downstream exclusion paths are: project_name unresolvable in state.json; "locked by another session at ACQUIRE time"; task-lock.sh acquire errored; orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh failed; identity-seq mismatch; and the identical-dispatch halt. The acquire-time lock failure is the dominant one and is structurally invisible to the guard, because the guard's evidence is the earlier read-only `check`.

(2) WRONG SITING relative to its own stated purpose. The guard's stop_message blames "a tie-breaker defect ... or an unexpected file_scope_collision/session_active chain", but the admission defer that implements those (`if [ "${admit_decision[$t]:-defer}" = "defer" ]` -> out_deferred_rows, `continue`) runs ABOVE the guard and correctly leaves those tasks out of `probed_dispatch`. So the guard covers the class it names while being blind to the class that actually stalls a run.

CONSEQUENCE: on any cycle where at least one candidate survives the probe but every survivor is excluded downstream, the else branch fires and resets the counter to 0 even though `dispatch[]` is emitted empty. The counter can never reach 3, so stop_reason="convergence_guard" is unreachable for this entire class of stall and the loop spins until an operator kills it.

NOT COVERED ELSEWHERE: the no-eligible circuit breaker (stop_reason="no_eligible_stuck" / "max_cycles") is computed inside the eligibility loop from `any_stuck` and cannot cover this case -- in this class of stall the candidates ARE eligible and are excluded later, at admission/acquire. The identical-dispatch convergence guard is a different mechanism (it compares dispatch CONTENT across cycles and fires only once a dispatch has actually been built).

OBSERVED EVIDENCE (session sess_1793990805_500dd7, batch 418,431,435,445). Cycles 3 and 4 were byte-identically zero-dispatch with no stop verdict emitted: #431 deferred on a __WHOLE_TREE__ file-scope collision with out-of-batch non-terminal #409; #445 deferred by the self-modification tie-breaker in favor of #418; #418 and #435 both locked by a concurrent live session. `consecutive_no_dispatch_cycles` still read 0 after cycle 4, and the script's own stderr reported "Contended-path manifest: 83 path(s) contended across 2 dispatched task(s) this cycle" -- that manifest is built over `probed_dispatch_post_h1`, so it is direct confirmation that 2 tasks were still counted as dispatching while `dispatch[]` emitted empty. The run had to be terminated by operator judgment.

VERIFIED AT TASK CREATION: the guard block reads as described in the source store; the deployed copy .claude/scripts/orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh is byte-identical to the source-store copy (so the repository's current core/lean deploy staleness does not affect this file). `__WHOLE_TREE__` does not appear in orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh at all -- it lives in scripts/lib/file-scope-overlap.sh and reaches this script only through the upstream admission computation.

REMEDY DIRECTIONS (to evaluate, not prescriptive). (a) Re-site the guard below the live per-task loop so it runs after every admission/acquire/build exclusion has had its say. (b) Or keep it where it is for the increment-side cheapness but change the predicate to count the finally-emitted `out_dispatch_rows`, so the counter reflects what the cycle actually dispatched. Either way: preserve the legitimate reset path (a cycle that genuinely dispatches must still clear the counter); decide deliberately whether an aux-only composition counts as a dispatch for this guard, and keep that decision consistent with the EXISTING deliberate exclusion of aux-only compositions from the plan_cache write guard (documented in this script's own header field-list comment and at the plan_cache write site) -- that exclusion is load-bearing and must not be broken as a side effect; refresh the now-misleading stop_message so it names the acquire-time and build-failure exclusions it would newly cover; and add regression coverage in tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh for the specific shape "probe non-empty, emitted dispatch[] empty, counter must increment".

SCOPE NOTE: the declared file_scope overlaps #420 (same 3 paths) and #487 (2 paths, orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh and its test suite). These are distinct defects, not duplicates; the overlap is a batching/serialization concern and the admission gate's file-scope collision deferral will serialize them mechanically at dispatch time.

### 513. Mechanically detect a plan phase that closed without its own commit
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]

**Description**: ONE gap: the phase-checkpoint protocol's one-commit-per-phase rule is a behavioral requirement
with NO mechanical check anywhere. A phase can close without its own commit and nothing notices --
the deviation surfaces only if the implementing agent volunteers it to its own issue log. The
deliverable is the DETECTION, not a change to the rule.

Its `what_happened` field, verbatim:

  "Phases 18 and 19 both land in ElimHard.lean and were committed together rather than one commit
  per phase. Phase 18 was build-verified green on its own (scoped guarded build, exit 0) before
  Phase 19 began, but no commit was fired at that boundary, so the two cannot now be separated
  without rewriting the file. Deviation from the phase-checkpoint protocol's one-commit-per-phase
  rule."

SILENT is the operative word: Phase 18 was green on its own and a commit was owed at that
boundary; none fired; no gate, script or postflight check registered anything.

=== THE RULE THIS CHECKS ALREADY EXISTS AND IS NOT IN SCOPE TO CHANGE ===

`context/standards/git-staging-scope.md`'s `### implement` section states it outright: "A plan
phase closes with exactly one commit." `context/contracts/phase-closure.md`'s "Marker/commit
synchrony is bidirectional" section states both directions of the companion marker requirement
(promotion-on-commit, and no-promotion-without-evidence) and records that the over-claim incident
"was caught only because the orchestrator cross-checked the marker count against the working
tree" -- i.e. by an ad hoc human cross-check, not by a mechanism.

THE OBSERVED SHAPE IS A THIRD ONE THAT NEITHER DIRECTION COVERS, and saying so is the point of
this record. Promotion-on-commit requires a phase's marker promotion to ride in the same commit as
that phase's final work; a commit carrying phase 18's promotion AND phase 19's work satisfies that
sentence literally while still violating one-commit-per-phase. No existing contract sentence,
script or test in this source store addresses phase COALESCING. Verified by reading the store:
`skill_corroborate_phase_counts` counts phase headings only, and no script correlates a phase
heading with the existence of that phase's own commit.

=== RECURRENCE -- MEASURED IN THE CONSUMER REPO, RE-DERIVABLE, AND FAR BROADER THAN THE SELF-REPORT ===

The deviation is routine rather than exceptional, and in its commonest form it self-declares in
the commit message. Measured 2026-11-06 in `~/Projects/BimodalLogic` (NOT in this store -- this
store's own history has its own, different population, and the check must be validated against
both). Re-derive rather than trusting these figures:

  git -C ~/Projects/BimodalLogic log --oneline --all --grep="phase [0-9]\+-[0-9]\+" | wc -l
  git -C ~/Projects/BimodalLogic log --format=%s --all --grep="phase [0-9]\+-[0-9]\+" \
    | grep -oE '^task [0-9]+' | sort -u | wc -l

110 commits across 82 DISTINCT tasks carry a coalesced `phase N-M` message, against 6697 total
`phase` commits -- about 1.6%. The originating entry's commit is `8bd45b6ea` ("task 867 phase
18-19: ..."). Coalescing is not limited to adjacent pairs: `9c212620c` coalesces three phases
(`phase 2-4`) and `0688a7a3c` coalesces five (`phase 2-6`). Exactly ONE of those 110 produced an
issue-log entry -- the one cited above. The self-report is the outlier, not the violation. That is
the whole argument for a mechanism, and the reason the per-instance `minor` severity must not be
read as "rare".

=== TWO SHAPES, AND AN HONEST STATEMENT OF WHAT A CHEAP CHECK CATCHES ===

  SHAPE 1, DECLARED. The commit message itself names a range (`task {N} phase 18-19: ...`),
  deviating from the `task {N} phase {P}: {phase_name}` convention in `rules/git-workflow.md`.
  Detectable by reading messages alone -- the signal is already sitting in the data and nothing
  reads it. All 110 measured instances are this shape.

  SHAPE 2, UNDECLARED AND STRICTLY WORSE. A commit names ONE phase while carrying two phases'
  work, or a phase marked `[COMPLETED]` has no `phase {P}` commit at all. A message scan cannot
  see this; it needs the completed-phase markers in the plan cross-checked against the set of
  per-phase commits for that task. Research MUST state which shapes the delivered check covers,
  and MUST NOT let Shape 1 coverage be reported as coverage of the rule.

A plausible economical design, TO BE CONFIRMED OR REJECTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: the ingredients
already exist and are already called at postflight. `skill_corroborate_phase_counts` (defined in
`scripts/skill-base.sh`, tested by `scripts/tests/test-corroborate-phase-counts.sh`) already
counts conforming phase headings in a plan, and `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh` already
counts a task's commits in a dispatch window via `git log --since` scoped to the task directory
(its `stall_commit_count` derivation). A per-phase commit-existence cross-check -- for every phase
heading marked complete, assert a commit matching `task {N} phase {P}` exists -- reuses both and
catches Shape 2 as well as Shape 1.

=== WHERE THE CHECK BELONGS -- TO BE DECIDED, WITH THE TERRITORY CONSTRAINT STATED ===

  (a) `skill_corroborate_phase_counts` in `scripts/skill-base.sh`. PREFERRED STARTING HYPOTHESIS:
      it already owns plan-phase-heading accounting and already has a dedicated test suite, so the
      check lands beside the data it needs.
  (b) `scripts/orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`. The natural surfacing point, but the MOST
      CONTENDED file in this store -- open records #472, #476 and #501 all claim it and its test
      suite, and #501 already carries nine dependency edges. If research concludes a call-site
      edit there is unavoidable, declare the edges on those contenders AT THAT POINT rather than
      editing concurrently; per the batching guardrail an unordered overlap gets an edge, never a
      bare exclusion. Prefer a design where the function's existing output line carries the new
      finding so NO postflight edit is needed -- confirm whether that is possible before assuming
      it.
  (c) The phase-closure contract's own gate sequence. Prose only; carries no detection by itself,
      so it is a complement to (a) or (b), never a substitute.

Prefer the cheapest site that catches both shapes. A detection firing only at task postflight is
still worth building even though it cannot un-coalesce an already-landed commit: the measured cost
of the observed instance is irreversibility (the two phases "cannot now be separated without
rewriting the file"), so the realistic value is a named, counted deviation the next dispatch and
the operator can see -- NOT prevention. Do not design for a blocking refusal at the phase-commit
boundary unless research shows it is as cheap AND cannot strand verified-green work; stranding
green work is a worse failure than the deviation it would prevent.

=== TERRITORY -- WHY THE DEPENDENCY EDGES EXIST ===

This record's declared files are claimed by four open records, so it SEQUENCES rather than
excludes (batching guardrail):
  - `scripts/skill-base.sh` and `context/contracts/phase-closure.md` -- claimed by #446 (close the
    plan-to-file_scope reconciliation gap and make a containment drop non-advisory).
  - `scripts/tests/test-corroborate-phase-counts.sh` -- claimed by #418 (make the
    lint-fixture-test family subject resolution explicit and self-declaring), which is `planned`
    and will move that suite's fixture conventions; authoring a new case before that lands means
    writing it twice.
  - `context/contracts/phase-closure.md` -- additionally claimed by #449 (site the Batch Gate
    Deferral directive where it is read before the phase's verification is launched) and #479
    (require every task to name a landing target).
Read each one's CURRENT text before editing a shared file; do not reintroduce or re-correct
anything they fix. If the implementer finds the `phase-closure.md` edit can be deferred to a
follow-up without losing the third-direction statement, the edges carried solely for that file
(#449, #479) may be dropped at that point, with the reasoning recorded.

=== THE NEAREST ADJACENT MECHANISM, AND WHY IT DOES NOT COVER THIS ===

A closed record already surfaces a per-cycle scoped commit that was ATTEMPTED AND FAILED
(`git-commit-scoped.sh` exiting a loud, attributable code at the cycle-postflight commit call
site) into the dispatched task's own status. That is the opposite input: a commit that fired and
returned an error, which the system already sees. This record's shape is a commit that was never
attempted at all, so no exit code, no error and no issue entry exists to surface -- the boundary
simply passed in silence. A reader who finds that record should not read it as coverage here.

=== NON-GOALS ===

(1) NO change to the one-commit-per-phase rule itself, in either
    `context/standards/git-staging-scope.md` or `rules/git-workflow.md`. The rule is correct; it
    is unchecked. Adding the check must not restate, relax or re-derive the rule.
(2) No history rewrite to separate already-coalesced commits, in the originating task or in any of
    the 81 other affected tasks in that repository. History rewrites are barred while other
    writers are live (`rules/git-workflow.md`).
(3) No retroactive issue-log backfill for the 109 unreported instances.
(4) No change to the marker-promotion contract's two existing directions, and no change to the
    `[PARTIAL]`/`[BLOCKED]` status machinery.
(5) NOT the adjacent generated-surface question. The per-phase generated-surface regeneration
    obligation and the Batch Gate Deferral narrowing are owned by #449, which already carries the
    sibling citation from this same consumer-repo issue log (`{867,
    iss_1793914994233_yAr1ND}`) in its FOLDED IN section. This record owns commit-boundary
    ACCOUNTING only; do not write into #449's subject.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) A check exists that, given a task with a coalesced phase commit, names the deviation
    mechanically -- without any agent self-report.
(2) Run against `~/Projects/BimodalLogic`'s existing history, the check reports that repository's
    measured population (re-derived at implementation time rather than trusted from this record),
    and does NOT fire on a conforming `task {N} phase {P}:` commit nor on the sanctioned
    per-objective substep form `task {N} phase {P}.{O}: {objective}`.
(3) The delivered coverage is stated explicitly against Shape 1 and Shape 2, with any uncovered
    shape named rather than left implied.
(4) If a blocking refusal is introduced anywhere, the research record states why it cannot strand
    verified-green work.
(5) A case in the chosen site's existing test suite (EXTEND it, do not replace existing cases)
    that fixtures a coalesced phase boundary and FAILS against the unmodified code before the fix
    lands -- the suites are green while the gap is live, so a case that cannot fail first proves
    nothing.
(6) The deploy verification this store already requires reports clean afterwards.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

Every edited file lies outside `specs/**`, so its prose and any new message string must carry no
task-number references (`rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`) and no record-versioning
language (`rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md`). Durable anchors that MAY be cited
freely: the file names (`skill-base.sh`, `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh`, `phase-closure.md`,
`git-staging-scope.md`), the function name `skill_corroborate_phase_counts`, the variable name
`stall_commit_count`, the commit-message conventions `task {N} phase {P}: {phase_name}` and
`task {N} phase {P}.{O}: {objective}`, and section headings. The originating task number, the
issue entry_id, the adjacent record numbers, the specific commit shas and the measured counts may
NOT appear in any deliverable -- they live in this record only.

=== SITING NOTE -- WHY THIS RECORD IS HERE AND NOT IN THE CONSUMER REPO ===

The defect was observed in `~/Projects/BimodalLogic`, but its edit targets resolve only under this
source store. That consumer repository carries only a deployed, disposable `.claude/` tree
regenerated from here (`rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`), and it already carries a
frozen-record tracker counting 32 non-terminal `meta` records that name source-store paths and
therefore cannot be worked there -- a dispatch picking one up there can read nothing it is meant
to edit and commit nothing it manages to write. A sibling record filed there from this same issue
log was abandoned on that exact ground, with its work folded into #449 here. This record is
therefore filed at the store from the outset rather than filed there and migrated.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised by /orchestrate's conclusion stage, channel (b) agent-system changes, user-approved at the
orchestrate-lead level for session `sess_1793989212_34b9e4` while orchestrating the originating
task in the consumer repository. Originating entry cited verbatim above; it records
`suggested_channel: null`, so the channel classification is the conclusion stage's own.

### 512. Builtin command exemption for dead command lint
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [PLANNED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [512_builtin_command_exemption_for_dead_command_lint/reports/01_builtin-command-exemption-mechanism.md]
- **Plan**: [512_builtin_command_exemption_for_dead_command_lint/plans/01_builtin-command-exemption-marker.md]

**Description**: verify-deploy gate 24 reports two findings against lines that are correct and must keep working: memory-setup.md and memory-troubleshooting.md each recommend Claude Code native /ide command to connect an Obsidian vault. The lint derives its valid-command set solely by enumerating agent-system/extensions/*/commands/<name>.md, so a host-provided built-in can never be valid and is flagged forever. Decide and implement the exemption mechanism. The constraint that makes this a task rather than a one-line fix: lint-dead-command-recommendations.sh own header documents zero-tolerance, no allowlist and nothing is ever hardcoded here as a deliberate decision with stated rationale, so any built-in allowlist must amend that recorded decision rather than quietly contradict it, and no authoritative set of Claude Code built-in commands exists anywhere in this repository to scope or maintain one against. Candidate approaches to weigh: amend the recorded policy and add a narrowly-scoped built-in set with a named maintenance owner; add an inline per-line exemption marker following the established task-ref-ok and version-ok substring-marker idiom, which keeps the lint free of any hardcoded list; or rule the findings permanently accepted and teach the gate to report them separately from genuine violations. Rewording the two documents to drop the Run /<name> recommendation shape was considered and rejected as the primary fix: it clears the finding without addressing the mechanism, and the next document that correctly recommends a built-in trips the same false positive. SCOPE BOUNDARY: this record owns the lint and its test suite only. The prose naming the three deleted lifecycle commands is owned by reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class, which declares agent-system/extensions/memory/context/ in its own file_scope; that record also covers the separate, genuine defect at memory-setup.md where a numbered connection-test step recommends an unrunnable /research invocation with a --remember flag that does not exist. Do not edit those documents from here. SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/** never a deployed .claude/** tree. Evidence: the batch gate run of the orchestration that filed this record measured 25 confirmed verify-deploy findings, of which these two persisted after every other attributable finding was cleared.

### 509. Make a redeploy phase declarable without polluting file_scope, and bar a pre-redeploy phase from asserting a deployed-vs-source drift gate
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: file-scope-lifecycle
- **Dependencies**: Task 446, Task 452

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate invocation that drove seven tasks to completion, session sess_1793947646_0784d9. User-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. All three citing entries come from one task, which hit both halves of this defect inside a single plan.

CITATIONS -- specs/494_phase_commit_task_own_artifact_scope_carveout/issues.jsonl:
  {task 494, iss_1793948939447_o36RLL} -- class "scope declaration", severity minor, plan phase. Half (i).
  {task 494, iss_1793959179573_3BHjT4} -- class "gate collision", severity minor, implement phase. Half (ii).
  {task 494, iss_1793961716350_rmdGtR} -- class "gate collision", severity minor, implement phase. Supporting evidence for half (ii); its Batch-Gate-Deferral half belongs to an existing record, named under TERRITORY below.

SEVERITY: minor per the entries. Both halves are plan-format and harvest-contract gaps, not script bugs -- state them as such and resist the pull toward a code fix that pins a rule nobody wrote down.

=== HALF (i): AN HONEST REDEPLOY PHASE POLLUTES ITS OWN file_scope ===

As recorded: "The redeploy phase must declare the regenerated deployed .claude/** paths in its Files to modify block for honesty, which makes them land in this task's harvested file_scope alongside the __WHOLE_TREE__ sentinel. A future consumer of that file_scope could misread those three .claude/** entries as declared hand-edit targets, which rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md forbids. Confirmed by a harvest dry-run: file_scope ends up carrying both the source-store triple and its deployed mirror."

The bind is real in both directions and must not be resolved by making the declaration dishonest. A redeploy phase genuinely does change those paths, and a Files-to-modify block that omits them understates what the phase does. But file_scope is simultaneously the phase-commit containment input and the batch-admission territory contract between concurrently dispatched tasks, and a deployed .claude/** path appearing there reads as a declared hand-edit target -- the exact thing the source-store/deploy boundary rule forbids.

THE ASK for half (i) -- decide and implement one rule, recording the reasoning:
  (1) Distinguish the two kinds of entry at the harvest, so a deploy-OUTPUT path is recorded as such rather than as a declared edit target. Weigh what every downstream consumer does with the field before choosing a representation; a shape change to file_scope is not free.
  (2) Keep file_scope as a flat list of hand-edit targets only, and give the redeploy phase a different, stated way to be honest about its regenerated output -- for instance declaring the deploy as its product in prose while the Files-to-modify block names only the source-store paths and the sentinel, with the convention written down so the next author does not re-derive it from a refusal.
  (3) Rule that the __WHOLE_TREE__ sentinel already carries this and the enumerated .claude/** paths are redundant -- if so, say so in the plan format AND make the harvest drop them, so honesty and the field's meaning stop pulling against each other.
Whichever is chosen, the rule must be stated where a plan author reads it before writing a redeploy phase, not only where a harvest implementer reads it.

=== HALF (ii): A PRE-REDEPLOY PHASE CANNOT ASSERT A DRIFT GATE ===

As recorded: "Phase 3's own verification criterion demanded a clean verify-deploy.sh --skip-slow --only-gate 3,4 pass, but gate 3 (doc-lint) structurally cannot pass until Phase 4's redeploy: it reports deployed-vs-source drift for Phase 1/2's already-committed, not-yet-deployed script changes, plus an index-entries.json line-count mismatch for this phase's own doc edit whose fix script refuses to run outside a deployed tree. Gate 4 (task-reference lint) passes cleanly now. Deferred to Phase 4's redeploy and full gate run, recorded as a deviation in the phase-3 progress file and annotated in the plan."

This is a SELF-DEFEATING criterion, not a flaky gate: the phase's own committed-but-not-yet-deployed work is what makes the gate red, so the criterion is unsatisfiable by construction for every phase that precedes the plan's own redeploy phase. A plan can be internally correct in every other respect and still contain a criterion no implementer can discharge, which forces a recorded deviation on a phase that in fact passed everything it could.

WHY THE EXISTING BASELINE-RELATIVE RECORD DOES NOT COVER IT: the open record baseline_relative_gate_criteria requires a baseline-relative verdict, and its WHEN clause binds "whenever a phase's verification criterion invokes a gate whose scope exceeds the phase's edit scope AND that gate is not green at the task's starting commit". Verified by direct read of that record at creation time. Here the gate may well be green at the starting commit -- it goes red BECAUSE of this task's own in-flight, correctly-committed, not-yet-deployed changes. The trigger is different, so the rule does not reach the case. This half is the SIBLING rule, to be sited beside it rather than folded into it, and this record must be read against whatever that record actually lands rather than against its description.

THE ASK for half (ii) -- add the sibling rule to plan-format.md, sited with the baseline-relative criterion rule and the existing rule on naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria, and propagate it to the planner's own contract. The rule must state at least:
  - WHEN it binds: a phase whose verification criterion invokes a gate that compares the deployed tree against the source store, where the same plan contains a later redeploy phase.
  - WHAT the criterion must say instead: a verdict the phase can actually discharge -- its own scoped suites, plus the drift gate deferred by name to the redeploy phase -- never an absolute clean pass on a drift gate that the phase's own undeployed work makes red.
  - WHERE the drift gate IS legitimately asserted: at or after the redeploy phase, which is also the phase that can run a fix script requiring a deployed tree.
  - That a deferral stated up front in the plan is not a deviation. The cited incident had to record a deviation for a phase that passed everything it could, which mislabels a correct outcome.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do not weaken gate 3. It reported real drift correctly; the defect is a criterion that demanded an impossible result from it.
- Do not make the plan format longer than it has to be. It is already a long standard whose requirements compete for an author's attention -- site the new rule as a sibling of the existing criterion rules and reuse their framing instead of restating it.
- Any mechanical enforcement is a ruling to make, not an assumption: decide whether validate-artifact.sh can flag a pre-redeploy drift-gate criterion without false-flagging the legitimate post-redeploy assertion, and record the ruling either way. An advisory rule is an acceptable outcome if a check would misfire.
- Half (i) must not be resolved by making a declaration dishonest, and must not silently change what any existing file_scope consumer receives.

VERIFY: for half (i), a harvest dry-run over a plan containing a redeploy phase, pinned in tests/test-plan-file-scope-harvest.sh, showing the chosen representation and showing that a non-redeploy plan's harvest is byte-for-byte unchanged. For half (ii), the documentary check plus whatever validate-artifact.sh ruling lands; if a check is built, pin both the flagged pre-redeploy case and the unflagged post-redeploy case.

TERRITORY EDGES -- both declared files are SHARED FILE TERRITORY, so edges are declared rather than raced:
- close_the_plan_to_file_scope_reconciliation_gap declares plan-file-scope-harvest.sh, its test, and plan-format.md, and owns the plan-to-file_scope reconciliation this record's half (i) modifies. Filed as a dependency.
- baseline_relative_gate_criteria declares plan-format.md and planner-agent.md and owns the criterion-rule section half (ii) extends. Filed as a dependency.
- Also declaring plan-format.md, with no edge filed because the sections are disjoint -- re-check at plan time and add an edge if they have converged (plan_cross_phase_line_budget_consistency also declares validate-artifact.sh): plan_format_tier_justification_fixes, plan_cross_phase_line_budget_consistency, validate_carried_unverified_names_a_source, cross_task_traceability_and_changelog_verdict, and lean_goals_block_harvest_contract.
- hoist_batch_gate_deferral_directive owns the third citation's other half -- the conflict between a plan's literal instruction to run the full gate set and a dispatch-level deferral directive added after the plan was authored. That half is NOT this record's subject; it is cited here only as corroborating evidence that this plan's gate criteria were unsatisfiable as written.

MECHANICAL OVERLAP FROM BROAD-PREFIX DECLARATIONS, no edge filed: reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class declares whole directory prefixes (core/agents/, core/commands/, core/context/, core/hooks/, core/index-entries.json, core/manifest.json) and contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations declares core/scripts/tests/ and core/scripts/lint/ as prefixes, so the directory-prefix overlap predicate reports both against most agent-system records, including every record created in this batch. No dependency edge is filed, matching how the rest of this backlog treats those two blanket declarations. Re-check at admission time and file an edge if either has narrowed to a concrete file this record also touches.

    {task 29, iss_1793951759427_sMN8fo} -- the provenance half, quoted below

SEVERITY: costly in aggregate, though each entry is minor. RECURRING across three independent tasks within one batch.

=== SCOPE NOTE: THIS RECORD IS THE UNCOVERED REMAINDER ===

A duplicate check was performed against the open backlog at creation time, using the ownership map an existing record (cycle_postflight_containment_parity) already states for this problem family. That map is accurate and is reproduced here so this record is not re-litigated:
- feed_undeclared_implement_paths_into_file_scope owns the GENERAL in-dispatch file_scope extension channel.
- manifest_registration_dropped_from_phase_commit owns the EXTENSION-MANIFEST half, including the option of treating the touched extension's own manifest.json as implicitly in scope when the same phase adds a file under that extension's scripts/ tree. Its three options -- implicit widening, a pre-commit obligation, or subsumption by the general mechanism -- are the vocabulary this record's half (1) must choose from rather than reinvent.
- close_the_plan_to_file_scope_reconciliation_gap owns the plan-to-file_scope harvest reconciliation and makes a containment drop non-advisory.
- collapse_perpath_scope_excursion_issue_rows_into_o owns the per-path issue-row fan-out that inflates these counts.
- cycle_postflight_containment_parity owns the cycle-postflight site and the recovery commit.
- The completed record phase_commit_task_own_artifact_scope_carveout landed the ADJACENT case -- admitting a task's own artifact directory as an implicit containment member, as a per-site splice.
- The completed record allowlist_infrastructure_paths_in_scoped_commits landed the shared engine-written-state allowlist that the three containment sites consult.

What is NOT owned by any of the above, and is this record's subject:

HALF (1) -- WHERE DOES SHARED EXTENSION METADATA BELONG? The dominant path in the citations above is index-entries.json, in three different extensions, NOT manifest.json. The manifest half's narrow option is keyed on a phase adding a file under that extension's scripts/ tree; index-entries.json is written by scripts/generate-context-line-counts.sh --write as a repo-wide line_count sync over CONTEXT files, so that key does not reach it. The obvious candidate home is the engine-written-state allowlist, and the question is genuinely open because that library's own admission bar appears to refuse it. Verified by direct read of agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lib/engine-written-state-paths-lib.sh at creation time, the bar requires a candidate to be BOTH (a) engine-written on behalf of every/many tasks, not authored by one task's own dispatch, AND (b) never legitimately declarable in any one task's own file_scope. Shared extension metadata satisfies (a) and appears to FAIL (b): a task that adds a context file or a script legitimately declares that extension's index-entries.json or manifest.json in its own file_scope, and several open records do exactly that. The same header's rejected-candidates list refuses task-owned specs/ paths on precisely this reasoning. So decide, with the reasoning recorded:
  (1a) Admit agent-system/extensions/*/index-entries.json (and manifest.json, if the manifest-half record's ruling has not already placed it) to the allowlist, and AMEND the stated admission bar to say why a path that is sometimes legitimately declared still belongs -- do not add a member while leaving a bar that forbids it, which would leave the next author with a contradictory rule.
  (1b) Leave the allowlist alone and resolve this at the sync tool instead: have the tool declare what it touched so the obligation surfaces before the commit rather than as a drop.
  (1c) Rule that the general in-dispatch extension channel subsumes it, record that explicitly, and close half (1) rather than leaving it open.
Read the manifest-half record's ruling first if it has landed; this record's half (1) must agree with it or state why it diverges.

HALF (2) -- THE PROVENANCE HAZARD, unowned by anything above. Quoted from {task 29, iss_1793951759427_sMN8fo}: a commit "staged the WHOLE agent-system/extensions/core/index-entries.json file via a jq-rewrite-then-commit, which incidentally also committed three unrelated line_count corrections (plan-format.md 882->910, progress-file.md 270->268, metadata-file-return.md 147->143) that reflected OTHER agents' edits to those files, not mine. Verified all three values are objectively correct (wc -l matches). No content harm; only a provenance/attribution imprecision -- those corrections landed in this task's commit instead of their own authors'." The entry's own lesson: "never jq-rewrite-and-commit an entire shared index file while other writers may be concurrently dirtying unrelated entries in it; prefer a narrower, single-entry jq filter applied immediately before commit." Verified at creation time by direct read: generate-context-line-counts.sh --write "rewrite[s] each index-entries.json in place with corrected line_count values", iterating every extension directory, with no flag to narrow which extension or which entries it writes. sync-manifest-scripts.sh --write is NOT whole-document -- its own header documents a deliberately surgical, line-oriented rewrite of the provides.scripts array block only, precisely to avoid reformatting two manifests that are not jq-canonical elsewhere in the file -- but it too iterates every extension directory with no narrowing flag, so a per-task invocation can still insert entries into an extension a concurrent sibling owns. Two further consequences of the same driver were observed in the same batch and are recorded here as context: one task had to hand-revert foreign hunks a repo-wide tool wrote into a concurrent sibling's territory, and another had a sibling's foreign hunk swept into its own commit anyway.
  THE ASK for half (2): give the repo-wide --write sync tools a narrowing mode -- correct and write only the entries the caller names (or only the entries whose source files the caller touched) -- so a per-task invocation cannot rewrite, stage, or sweep a concurrent sibling's unrelated entries. The unnarrowed whole-corpus --write stays available for a deliberate repo-wide sweep run on its own. Apply the same narrowing to sync-manifest-scripts.sh --write, whose per-block rewrite is already surgical but whose extension iteration is not narrowed; keep its surgical line-oriented mechanics intact rather than replacing them with a jq round-trip.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT weaken the containment predicate for a genuine excursion. Drop-and-warn for an out-of-territory source edit is correct and must survive intact, pinned by a test.
- Do NOT make any containment check fatal. Fail-open on a missing, empty, or malformed file_scope must stay.
- Preserve the allowlist library's fail-open posture: a failed source, or an empty/unparseable result, must never narrow a site's admitted scope below today's hardcoded two-literal fallback.
- The library is sourced, never executed; keep it setting no shell options a caller inherits.
- --check mode of both sync tools must keep reporting the whole corpus. Narrowing is a --write concern only; a narrowed check would hide real drift.
- If half (1) lands as (1a), do not hand-copy the literal path list into any consumer; the library is the one place the set is defined.

VERIFY: extend tests/test-engine-written-state-paths-lib.sh for the chosen half-(1) ruling and for the unchanged fail-open fallback. For half (2), pin: a narrowed --write correcting only the named entry and leaving a concurrently-wrong sibling entry untouched; an unnarrowed --write still correcting the whole corpus; --check unchanged in both modes; and a malformed narrowing argument refused rather than silently widening to the whole corpus. generate-context-line-counts.sh has no dedicated suite today -- create one, or extend the nearest existing suite and say which, rather than leaving half (2) unpinned.

TERRITORY: this record deliberately declares the allowlist library and the two sync tools, NOT git-commit-phase.sh or git-commit-scoped.sh -- those two files are declared by five other open records between them, and the completed allowlist record already wired all three containment sites to consult the library, so the library is the available lever. If the chosen ruling turns out to require editing a containment site directly, declare the edge to the records holding it and widen the declaration then; do not race it.

DEPENDENCY EDGE: manifest_registration_dropped_from_phase_commit. Its ruling is the direct input to half (1), and a divergent answer here would leave two incompatible rules for one mechanism.

MECHANICAL OVERLAP FROM BROAD-PREFIX DECLARATIONS, no edge filed: reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class declares whole directory prefixes (core/agents/, core/commands/, core/context/, core/hooks/, core/index-entries.json, core/manifest.json) and contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations declares core/scripts/tests/ and core/scripts/lint/ as prefixes, so the directory-prefix overlap predicate reports both against most agent-system records, including every record created in this batch. Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate invocation that drove seven tasks to completion, session sess_1793947646_0784d9. User-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only.

VISIBLE HINT OVERRIDE, recorded so a future reader can audit it: the citing entry's own suggested_channel is fix_now. The orchestrate lead overrode it to the agent-system channel and said so to the user when seeking approval. The reason is that there is no local fix to make -- the gate behaved correctly, the work was correct and complete, and the Inter-Cycle Redeploy Checkpoint discharged the deferral exactly as documented. What remains is a recorded cost/benefit trade to re-examine, not a bug to repair.

CITATIONS -- {task 419, iss_1793958119931_beLFwQ} in specs/419_enforce_state_write_convention_python_channel/issues.jsonl (class "stale deploy or source-store boundary", severity costly, implement phase, resolution worked_around).

SEVERITY: costly. One full extra orchestration cycle per affected batch.

MEASURED EVIDENCE, as recorded: a postflight completion write was refused by the completion-deploy gate with exit 6, because the deployed .claude/ tree was stale relative to the source store at completion time. The work itself was correct and complete. The status landed one cycle later, when orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh's Inter-Cycle Redeploy Checkpoint deployed and auto-reconciled it -- observed working exactly as documented, commit 7f3cf35b1. The batch this occurred in drove seven tasks through to completion, and one whole cycle of it existed only to discharge the gate.

THE DURABLE ANCHOR TO AMEND: context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md already carries a subsection titled "A Pre-Postflight-Sweep Deploy Trigger: Evaluated, Intentionally Not Built", which records this exact trade as considered and declined. Verified present by direct read at creation time. Its four recorded grounds are: (a) the concurrency objection narrows at the Move 2/Move 3 boundary, since Move 3 runs only after every Move 2 Agent call has returned, leaving only the cross-session mutex race that both existing trigger sites already accept; (b) the structural cost of a third genuine caller of the deploy/regeneration script -- a caller that scripts/tests/test-lint-deploy-caller-wrap.sh mechanically asserts does not exist, and which would require re-threading the baseline-relative branches, confirmation/attribution filters, idempotence guard and durable ledger onto a new call site; (c) the bounded cost being removed, characterized there as "one additional, largely empty cycle round trip"; (d) the verdict -- judged SAFE at that boundary, left unimplemented on cost/benefit grounds. The subsection closes by asking that "A later pass re-examining this question should re-derive this reasoning rather than re-propose the trigger without it." This task IS that later pass.

THE ASK -- this is an ADJUDICATION, not a build order:
(1) Re-derive each of the four recorded grounds against the current tree, not against the record. Ground (b) in particular names a specific mechanical assertion and a specific set of branches to re-thread; confirm that the caller-wrap assertion and each named mechanism still exist and still have the shape the record assumes, and report any that have moved.
(2) Price ground (c) against the measured evidence above. The record characterizes the removed cost as "one additional, largely empty cycle round trip" -- a qualitative estimate made before this measurement existed. State what a cycle of a seven-task batch actually costs, how often the gate fires (derive the frequency from the orchestration logs and the issue corpus rather than asserting it), and whether a largely-empty cycle is still a fair description.
(3) Rule: either REAFFIRM the existing decision with the new figure cited in the subsection, or SUPERSEDE it. Preserve the existing reasoning in either case -- an amendment note recording that this decision was amended, dated, is the sanctioned form (rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md exempts exactly that, and forbids rewriting the subsection as a draft lineage). Do not delete the four grounds; a future reader must be able to see what was weighed.

IF THE RULING IS SUPERSEDE: the implementation is NOT in this task's scope and this task must not attempt it. The trigger's call site would land in orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh and would have to widen test-lint-deploy-caller-wrap.sh's asserted caller set -- territory declared by four other open records. Record the ruling, name the implementation targets and the mechanisms needing re-threading, and the follow-on implementation record is created from the ruling.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do not weaken or bypass the completion-deploy gate. It refused correctly; a stale deployed tree at completion time is a real condition and the gate is the thing that catches it.
- Do not remove or duplicate the Inter-Cycle Redeploy Checkpoint. It worked as documented, and the deferral path it provides is the current, functioning answer.
- Any figure this task's artifacts quote must name its source. The frequency claim in (2) must be derived and shown, not asserted.

VERIFY: the deliverable is a ruling in prose, so verification is documentary -- the amended subsection states the ruling, cites the measured figure with its source, retains the four original grounds, and carries a dated amendment note if the decision changed. Run the documentation gates over the touched file rather than claiming a code-level check this task does not make.

TERRITORY EDGES -- context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md is SHARED FILE TERRITORY, so edges are declared rather than raced (that same document's own rule: shared territory is serialized with an edge, never a bare exclusion). Two open records declare it: explicit_lint_fixture_test_subject_resolution and warn_plan_size_exceeds_cycle_budget. The latter also declares orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh, which a supersede ruling would eventually touch. Both edges are filed as dependencies.

### 506. Stop the bounded-waiter hook refusing a write whose prose documents the anti-pattern the hook enforces
- **Status**: [PLANNED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [506_bounded_waiter_hook_fires_on_documenting_prose/reports/01_heredoc-aware-hook-detector.md]
- **Plan**: [506_bounded_waiter_hook_fires_on_documenting_prose/plans/01_heredoc-aware-waiter-detector.md]

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate invocation that drove seven tasks to completion, session sess_1793947646_0784d9. User-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only.

CITATIONS -- {task 415, iss_1793949006050_VDJRUK} in specs/415_exact_pid_process_check_standard/issues.jsonl (class "tooling friction", severity minor, plan phase, suggested_channel null).

SEVERITY: minor per the entry, but self-reinforcing: the content class that trips the hook is exactly the content class that teaches the rule the hook enforces, so the obstruction recurs every time the standard is extended or restated.

LIVE SYMPTOM, as recorded: writing a plan file via a Bash heredoc was refused by the PreToolUse hook hooks/validate-bounded-waiter.sh. The plan's PROSE quoted the process-name-match anti-pattern it was documenting, and the hook's textual shape detection fired on the heredoc command text. Worked around by using the Write tool instead. The same dispatch's implement phase -- authoring a standard whose whole subject is this anti-pattern -- had to be warned in advance to author with Write/Edit rather than a shell heredoc, and that hazard was carried as a risk row in the plan. The hook's own header already concedes the governing principle: "A false positive here is treated as worse than no hook."

ROOT CAUSE (verified by direct read of the source store at creation time; line numbers move, re-derive before editing): the hook blocks on a three-leg conjunction over the raw Bash command text -- (a) an `until` or `while` keyword, (b) a `sleep` call, (c) a name-match liveness test. A heredoc that writes a document ABOUT the forbidden shape puts all three legs into the command text as data, not as a command the shell will run as control flow. A grep of the hook for heredoc, here-doc, prose, or comment handling returns nothing: there is no redirection-aware or quoted-data-aware exclusion anywhere in it, so the detector cannot presently distinguish a live waiter from a file being written that describes one.

THE ASK -- close the false-positive surface without weakening the block on a live waiter. Decide and implement ONE coherent rule, recording the reasoning:
(1) Make the detector heredoc-aware: strip heredoc bodies (and other data-only regions the hook can identify reliably, such as a single-quoted argument to a file-writing redirection) from the text the three-leg conjunction is tested against, then apply the existing conjunction unchanged to what remains. Weigh honestly: shell heredoc quoting has enough variants (<<, <<-, quoted vs unquoted delimiters, nested cases) that a parser pretending to be complete is its own hazard; a deliberately conservative stripper that bails to today's behaviour on anything it cannot parse confidently is the safer shape.
(2) Narrow leg (a) or leg (b) so a data-bearing command cannot satisfy the conjunction -- for instance, requiring the loop keyword to appear in command position rather than anywhere in the text. Weigh against the hook's existing bare-word ruling, which is deliberately stricter than a flag-narrowed match and must not be silently relaxed.
(3) Neither: declare that authoring documentation about this anti-pattern must use Write/Edit, and site that instruction where an agent reads it BEFORE it writes such a file rather than discovering it from a refusal. If this is the ruling, record it in the hook's own header AND in the pattern document the hook enforces, and make the refusal message say so explicitly, so the next author is redirected in one step instead of debugging the block.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- The hook must keep blocking a genuine live self-match waiter, pinned by a test. The motivating incident (a name-matching wait loop that matched its own command line and never resolved) must stay refused.
- Keep the fail-OPEN posture on every internal error: a broken guard that blocks every Bash call in the repo is worse than no guard.
- Keep blocking via exit code 2 plus a stderr message; never switch to permissionDecision deny, which is documented-buggy for allow-listed Bash tool calls.
- Keep the hook registered BARE in settings.json: a `2>/dev/null || echo '{}'` wrapper converts exit 2 into exit 0 and silently disables the block.
- Do not introduce a shared library between this hook and its pattern document; the header records that the drift path there is deliberately closed by duplication.

VERIFY: extend agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-validate-bounded-waiter.sh with fixtures pinning (a) a heredoc writing prose that quotes all three legs -- ALLOWED, (b) the motivating live waiter -- still BLOCKED, (c) a one-shot name-match conditional outside a loop -- still allowed, (d) a bare name-match and a bare process-list-pipe-grep -- still allowed, and (e) malformed or truncated heredoc input -- fails open to today's verdict rather than erroring.

TERRITORY: hooks/validate-bounded-waiter.sh and tests/test-validate-bounded-waiter.sh are declared by no other open record -- this task holds them alone.

ORDERING NOTE, NOT A DEPENDENCY: the open record same_turn_gate_runs_foreground delivers guidance prose into context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md and context/patterns/external-process-wait.md -- precisely the documentation class that trips this hook. No file overlap exists, so no dependency edge is filed; if both are admitted to one batch, land this one first, and until it lands, author that prose with Write/Edit.

MECHANICAL OVERLAP FROM BROAD-PREFIX DECLARATIONS, no edge filed: reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class declares whole directory prefixes (core/agents/, core/commands/, core/context/, core/hooks/, core/index-entries.json, core/manifest.json) and contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations declares core/scripts/tests/ and core/scripts/lint/ as prefixes, so the directory-prefix overlap predicate reports both against most agent-system records, including every record created in this batch. No dependency edge is filed, matching how the rest of this backlog treats those two blanket declarations. Re-check at admission time and file an edge if either has narrowed to a concrete file this record also touches.
