**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate invocation that drove seven tasks to completion, session sess_1793947646_0784d9. User-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. The originating entry's own suggested_channel hint was agent_system; this routing agrees with the hint.

CITATIONS -- {task 385, iss_1793948998156_m8PsvT} in specs/385_lean4_plan_with_zero_lean_goals/issues.jsonl (class "missing cheap verification tier", severity minor, plan phase, resolution worked_around, evidence_path agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lib/suite-selection-lib.sh).

SEVERITY: minor per the entry, but structurally broad: it affects covering-suite selection for every non-core extension, not one plan.

LIVE SYMPTOM (verified by direct read of the source store at creation time; line numbers move, re-derive before editing): suite-selection-lib.sh maps touched paths against exactly ONE scripts tree -- agent-system/extensions/core/scripts when sourced from the source store, or the flat merged .claude/scripts tree when sourced from a deploy. Its own header states the limitation explicitly (around lines 41-47): "Extending the mapping to reason about a SOURCE-STORE path belonging to a different extension while still un-deployed is explicitly out of scope", justified at the time by a measurement that "0 of 19 non-core suites were found to match the deployed-vs-source hazard this library also classifies -- a core-only phenomenon today". The cited plan edited agent-system/extensions/lean/scripts/** in two separate phases; suite_selection_select returned the __FULL_SWEEP__ sentinel for those paths rather than naming the obvious paired suite test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh. Worked around by hand-naming that suite's direct invocation as the covering check in each Tier Justification instead of a run-all.sh --only selection. Two separate tasks in the same batch hit this and applied the same hand workaround.

WHY THE RECORDED OUT-OF-SCOPE NOTE DOES NOT DISPOSE OF IT: the note scopes out the deployed-vs-source CLASSIFICATION hazard for non-core suites (the source-only / source-first / deployed-first signal), and the 0-of-19 measurement is about that hazard. It does not establish that non-core source-store paths have no covering suite to select -- the cited incident is a direct counterexample, since test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh is a real, named, cheap covering suite that the selector declined to name. Re-derive the 0-of-19 figure before relying on it; it is CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the library header here.

THE ASK -- make the mapping resolve a covering suite for a source-store path under agent-system/extensions/<ext>/scripts/** for any <ext>, not core alone, and decide explicitly what happens to the deployed-vs-source classification for those suites:
(1) Extend the path-to-suite mapping to walk every extension's source scripts/ and tests/ tree, so a touched non-core script resolves to its own extension's paired suite. The deployed-flat case must keep behaving exactly as it does today (one merged tree, where core-only and whole-tree already coincide).
(2) Rule on the classification half: either extend the source-only / source-first / deployed-first signal to non-core suites too, or state in the header WHY the classification legitimately stays core-only while the SELECTION does not, and re-measure the figure the current note rests on rather than carrying it forward unexamined.
(3) Keep __FULL_SWEEP__ as the honest answer where no suite covers a path. The defect is a false sweep escalation for a path that DOES have a covering suite, never the sentinel itself.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- The library is sourced, never executed; it must keep setting no shell options a caller inherits and exporting nothing a caller must guess at.
- Do not narrow what the selector admits today. A path that resolves to a suite now must resolve to the same suite after the change.
- Preserve the deployed-mode dual behaviour documented in run-all.sh's own source-store/deployed note; do not fork that convention.

VERIFY: extend agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-run-all-selection.sh (the existing suite for this library -- there is no test-suite-selection-lib.sh) with fixtures pinning (a) a touched agent-system/extensions/<non-core-ext>/scripts/ path resolving to that extension's own paired suite, (b) a genuinely uncovered path still returning __FULL_SWEEP__, (c) deployed-mode selection unchanged, and (d) the chosen classification ruling. Run the suite from both a source-store and a deployed vantage point.

TERRITORY: agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lib/suite-selection-lib.sh and tests/test-run-all-selection.sh are declared by no other open record -- this task holds them alone.

ORDERING NOTE, NOT A DEPENDENCY: the open record plan_format_tier_justification_fixes weighs requiring a plan author to RUN suite_selection_select and quote its actual output rather than asserting a covering suite. That requirement becomes materially better once this defect is fixed and materially worse before it -- mandating measurement from a selector that falsely escalates non-core paths would push authors toward full sweeps. No file overlap exists (that record declares only context/formats/plan-format.md), so no dependency edge is filed; if both are admitted to one batch, land this one first.

MECHANICAL OVERLAP FROM BROAD-PREFIX DECLARATIONS, no edge filed: reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class declares whole directory prefixes (core/agents/, core/commands/, core/context/, core/hooks/, core/index-entries.json, core/manifest.json) and contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations declares core/scripts/tests/ and core/scripts/lint/ as prefixes, so the directory-prefix overlap predicate reports both against most agent-system records, including every record created in this batch. META TASK. ONE defect, ONE record: `lean-plan-compliance-check.sh` and the contract
`context/formats/plan-format.md` documents disagree about what a plan's `**Goals**` block
contributes as goal declarations. The script and the document cannot both be right; this record
settles WHICH, deliberately.

MEASURED {871, iss_1793913951568_HzkOMJ} (class `tooling-contract`, phase `plan`). The script
harvests EVERY backticked token in the Goals block, not only the leading identifier that
plan-format.md's `## Lean Challenge Statements` section describes. Binder names cited in
descriptive prose (`P`, `hP`) and cross-referenced PRE-EXISTING lemmas
(`plusValidIn_reflectTime`, `lcN_plusValidZTime`) were harvested as goal declarations. Two of them
can never resolve as declarations introduced by the plan, so the Final Verification spot-check
would have failed permanently, not transiently. The affected plan had to work around it by
stripping every backtick from its Goals prose -- a workaround that degrades the plan's own
readability to satisfy a harvester, and that the next plan author will not know to apply.

VERIFY THE MECHANISM FIRST, do not trust this summary. In the source store's
`scripts/lean-plan-compliance-check.sh` the harvest is a `sed` range over `/^\*\*Goals\*\*/` to
the next `/^\*\*[^G]/` piped through a backticked-token extraction, followed by a per-name
declaration grep across the resolved Lean source roots. The script's own header already records
two prior narrowings of the same harvest (a keyword exclusion list, so a backticked `sorry` or
`box` in prose is no longer harvested; and a colon-optional Goals-range fix) -- so this is the
THIRD narrowing of one over-broad harvest, which is itself evidence about where the contract
should live. The header also warns that two Goals-block readers must not drift apart on which
header shapes they accept; find the second reader before changing either.

THE DECISION THIS RECORD OWES, with the rejected alternative recorded on its own merits:

  (a) Narrow the SCRIPT to the documented contract -- harvest only the leading identifier of each
      Goals entry -- making descriptive backticks in Goals prose safe again.
  (b) Widen the DOCUMENT to state the script's actual behavior, i.e. make "no backticks in Goals
      prose except goal identifiers" an explicit, discoverable authoring rule rather than folklore
      a plan author discovers by a permanent verification failure.

Weigh (a) against the keyword-exclusion precedent already in the script: that precedent chose to
narrow the harvest rather than to constrain prose, which is an argument for (a). Weigh (b) against
the risk that a leading-identifier rule mis-parses the multi-line and parenthesized Goals shapes
the test fixtures already cover (`plan_r19_multiline_paren_goals.md`, `plan_r18_paren_goals.md`,
`plan_list_item_goals.md`, `plan_goals_preamble.md`). Whichever is chosen, the stripped-backticks
workaround must NOT be the settled answer.

VERIFICATION: extend `scripts/tests/test-lean-plan-compliance-check.sh` and the
`scripts/tests/fixtures/challenge/` fixture set rather than replacing them. The minimum bar is a
regression fixture whose Goals block cites a pre-existing lemma and a binder name in prose and
which the check does not fail on.

SCOPE BOUNDARY. Two adjacent open records own the Challenge block's OTHER contracts and must not
be edited here: #813 (precedence and snapshot-prerequisite rules for a pinned Challenge block) and
#824 (making a fixture-dependent gate statement expressible under `lean-challenge-snapshot.sh`).
This record owns only the Goals-block harvest-versus-contract disagreement.

EDIT TARGET, binding. Per `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md` this repository's `.claude/`
tree is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from a source store; a hand-authored
file under `.claude/**` is silently wiped by the next regeneration -- the edit appears to succeed

META TASK. ONE documentation defect, ONE record: `/revise` is a user-facing slash command that a
dispatched implementation agent cannot invoke, and that unavailability is undocumented at the point
of use. An agent that needs a sibling task's DESCRIPTION updated therefore has no documented route,
and finds the sanctioned one only by reading `skill-reviser`'s own internals.

MEASURED {884, iss_1793913745294_dZNuX5} (class `tooling`, phase `implement`): a dispatched
implementation agent needed another task's description updated, could not invoke `/revise N`, and
used the `state-write.sh` jq object-merge fallback instead (object-merge on `description` and
`last_updated`, with `--session-id`, `--argjson num N`, and `--regen-todo`). THE WORK WAS CORRECT:
that fallback is the identical mechanical path `skill-reviser`'s own Stage 7 uses for a description
update, and the plan had named it as the fallback. The defect is purely that an agent had to
rediscover it.

WHAT TO VERIFY BEFORE WRITING ANYTHING. Establish, and state in the report, which of these the
system actually enforces -- they imply different wording and possibly different surfaces:
  (i) `/revise` is categorically unavailable to a dispatched subagent (the same measured
      tool-withholding that applies to `AskUserQuestion`, documented in
      `docs/reference/standards/agent-frontmatter-standard.md`'s "Tool Withholding from Dispatched
      Subagents" section);
  (ii) it is available in principle but prohibited by contract;
  (iii) it is a Skill-tool invocation that would succeed, and the observed unavailability was
        narrower than the entry's own wording.
Note that `commands/revise.md` and `skills/skill-reviser/SKILL.md` are NOT currently marked
user-only -- `skill-tag` is the system's existing precedent for a user-only marking, so compare
against it rather than inventing a new convention.

THE DELIVERABLE is documentation and, if (i) or (ii) holds, a named route at the point of use:
state the unavailability where an agent will meet it (`commands/revise.md` and
`skills/skill-reviser/SKILL.md`), and name the sanctioned description-update route for a dispatched
agent -- with the exact `state-write.sh` invocation shape, since an agent reconstructing a jq
object-merge by hand against the single mutex-guarded state writer is precisely the kind of
improvisation this system elsewhere refuses to leave to chance.
`context/standards/postflight-tool-restrictions.md` is one candidate home for the general
"what a dispatched agent cannot invoke" statement; weigh it against stating the restriction only on
the two `/revise` surfaces, and record the rejected alternative.

SCOPE BOUNDARY. Do not change `skill-reviser`'s own revision workflow, and do not add a new
agent-invokable description-update command: the mechanical path already exists and is correct. This
record's deliverable is that the path is DISCOVERABLE from where an agent looks for it.

META TASK. ONE gap, ONE record: the research phase has no obligation to enumerate the GENERATED
doc-sync companion surface a `.lean` edit forces, so that surface is discovered at plan time -- by
running the file-scope harvester against a draft plan -- or later, by a gate firing
mid-implementation. Two entries from the same task record the same cause.

MEASURED {871, iss_1793913951546_jLdhm5} (class `scope-discovery`, phase `plan`): "The research
report did not cover the generated doc-sync surface at all." Running
`plan-file-scope-harvest.sh` against the DRAFT plan surfaced four companion paths the harvester
augments for any `.lean` edit -- `typst/generated/status.typ` plus three registered README
inventory blocks -- each separately gated by `typst-sync-check.sh` and
`check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory --check`. They had to be retrofitted into a late
phase as explicit regeneration tasks, declared files, verification criteria, and two Risks rows.

MEASURED {871, iss_1793927811619_YMJoHw} (class `scope-delta`, phase `implement`): a phase's Scope
Hypothesis predicted zero mentions of a renamed construct in any README; grep found a fourth
surface inside a GENERATED inventory block. Inspection of
`scripts/check-module-invariants.sh`'s `render_block` established that the Description column is
PRESERVED from the existing block (only Lines are refreshed), so that cell is hand-authored and a
hand edit SURVIVES regeneration. That is a non-obvious fact about a generated surface, and the
scope hypothesis predicted its opposite.

WHY THESE ARE ONE GAP. The harvester's own header (`scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh`,
"Generated-surface appending") already documents the augmentation, its `.lean` trigger, its
capability guard (both generator scripts must exist under the resolved repo root, else the whole
addition is inert), and the fact that a plan omitting these "either blocks on the pre-commit
`typst-sync-check.sh` gate or fails `check-module-invariants.sh`'s INV check mid-implementation --
this has happened twice independently." The capability probe that decides whether the augmentation
applies is therefore ALREADY mechanized and already repo-agnostic. Nothing in the research-phase
contract consults it, so the research report can be complete on its own terms and still hand the
planner a surface inventory that is short by four paths.

THE DELIVERABLE is the research-phase contract: `context/processes/research-workflow.md` and the
research agent's own instructions should require a report whose scope inventory names the
generated/derived companion surface for every source file class it expects to be edited, together
with which generator owns each surface and whether any column or region of it is hand-authored
rather than regenerated. Decide deliberately whether the obligation is discharged by prose, by
invoking the existing harvester capability probe at research time, or by both; record the rejected
alternative on its own merits. A research phase cannot be asked to enumerate a repository-specific
generator set from nothing -- so weigh resolving it from the repository's own declarations
(`context/standards/repo-level-declarations.md` is the existing surface) against reusing the
harvester's probe directly.

SCOPE BOUNDARIES, binding. This record does NOT own the harvester's augmentation SET, nor the
containment behavior of a path it fails to augment: open record #839 owns both (its Item 1 settles
whether Scope Hypothesis contingency paths become a second harvest source, and its Item 1 Extended
owns the companion-surface rule keyed on "the phase touches any `.lean` file"). Do not change
`plan-file-scope-harvest.sh`'s augmentation set here. This record also does not own the LEAN
extension's plan-authoring prose record (#785) or its `plan-authoring-gate-collisions.md`
(#785, #840) -- if the `render_block` Description-preservation fact needs a durable home in a
lean-specific pattern note rather than in the core research contract, say so and propose adding it
to that record instead of writing into its declared files.

NOT a repair of this repository's generators. `scripts/check-module-invariants.sh`,
`scripts/typst-sync-check.sh`, `typst/generated/status.typ` and every inventory block are correct
as they stand; the Description-column preservation is deliberate behavior to be DOCUMENTED, not
changed.

### 501. Give the cycle-postflight containment site predicate parity and a guarded recovery route for a dropped path
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: file-scope-lifecycle
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 430, Task 435, Task 448, Task 461, Task 472, Task 476, Task 484, Task 486

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve the extension's own source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json (that file carries NO top-level source_dir, only per-extension entries) and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate invocation over task 328, session sess_1793904029_00a39c. User-approved at the lead level.

SEVERITY: minor per drop, but the aggregate effect is that a legitimately-discovered path is first silently omitted from a cycle commit and then recovered by a commit that carries NO containment guarantee at all.

=== SCOPE NOTE: THIS RECORD IS THE UNCOVERED REMAINDER, NOT THE GENERAL PROBLEM ===

The originating change request was framed as the general file_scope-staleness problem with two candidate resolutions (a sanctioned in-dispatch file_scope extension path, versus a containment predicate that accepts a newly-created file under an already-declared parent). A duplicate check performed at creation time established that BOTH framings are already owned, and that three of the four issue ids the request cited are already cited by an existing record:

- #430 (feed_undeclared_implement_paths_into_file_scope) owns the general in-dispatch extension channel, including the proposed_file_scope-extended-to-implement option, the auto-merge-versus-operator-confirmation ruling, and the concurrent-territory-contract constraint. It already cites iss_1792191089627_HbXkDD.
- #486 (stop a required extension manifest.json registration being dropped) owns the extension-manifest half, including the "treat the touched extension's own manifest.json as implicitly in scope" option. It already cites iss_1793841635583_gpNhE6, iss_1793847274112_byw6XR and iss_1793848518784_26KAZz, and it already records the typst-phase recurrence.
- #446 owns the plan-to-file_scope harvest reconciliation and the unread-warning half; #484 owns the per-path issue-row fan-out that produces the large "scope excursion" counts; #494 owns the own-artifact-directory member at the phase-commit site; completed #469 landed the shared engine-written-state allowlist.

No new record was created for the general problem. What remains genuinely unowned is below: it is specific to the CYCLE-POSTFLIGHT containment site and to the recovery commit, neither of which any record above names.

=== EVIDENCE (this run, cycle 1, dispatch_seq 24) ===

orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh emitted EXCURSION DROPPED for two paths and reported "2 of 11 reported modified_files entries were dropped from this cycle's commit as file_scope excursions":

    agent-system/extensions/literature/manifest.json
    agent-system/extensions/literature/scripts/lib/literature_search_pylib.py

Both were real, chartered work: the phase extracted a shared python library and registered it in that extension's manifest. The dispatch recovered them in a separate labeled follow-up commit, ff880f77259fffd64836f373350710e0b5d1d7b0 ("task 328 phase 11: collapse literature-search.sh's self-internal repetition (follow-up commit)"), which carries exactly those two files and 334 insertions.

SCOPING ACCURACY, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVERSTATED LATER: the 127 entries of class "scope excursion" in specs/328_systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency/issues.jsonl are CUMULATIVE across that task's whole history, not from this run. In this run only cycle 1 hit the condition; cycles 2 and 3 both reported clean containment. This is a recurring structural gap, not an every-phase failure.

=== MECHANICS VERIFIED IN THE SOURCE STORE AT CREATION TIME (re-derive before editing; line numbers move) ===

FINDING 1 -- THE DROP FIRED AT A SITE NO OPEN RECORD OWNS. context/standards/git-staging-scope.md's "Three Containment sites, not one" section (around line 342) names site 1 as orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh's WORK (h)/(i) filter, site 2 as git-commit-phase.sh's Phase-Commit Containment Self-Check, and site 3 as V10 inside git-commit-scoped.sh. The EXCURSION DROPPED string exists at exactly one place in the corpus, orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh (WORK (i), around line 1954), so the observed drop is a SITE 1 event. Every open record listed in the scope note above declares git-commit-phase.sh (site 2) and/or git-commit-scoped.sh (site 3) in its file_scope; NONE declares orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh. #486's file_scope is exactly four paths -- git-commit-phase.sh, its test, general-implementation-agent.md, git-staging-scope.md -- and its VERIFY section extends only test-git-commit-phase.sh. So whatever manifest-registration rule #486 lands will NOT reach the site where this drop actually happened.

FINDING 2 -- THE TWO SITES DO NOT RUN THE SAME PREDICATE, THOUGH THE STANDARD SAYS THEY DO. git-staging-scope.md calls it "the same Containment predicate ... checked at three independent sites". It is not the same predicate:
- Site 1 (orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh, around lines 1689-1690) tests `$p == $f or ($p | startswith($f))`. Bare startswith, with no path-boundary check and no trailing-slash normalization.
- Site 2 (git-commit-phase.sh's is_contained(), around lines 272-305) normalizes a trailing-slash entry down to a bare directory via a `while [ "${entry%/}" != "$entry" ]` loop and then composes a `"${entry}/*"` glob.
Two consequences follow, in OPPOSITE directions, and both are live today. Site 1 OVER-matches: a declared entry `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts` admits `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts-archive/x.sh`, because startswith has no `/` boundary. Site 1 also does not share site 2's trailing-slash normalization, so the two sites can disagree about the same path against the same declaration. Task 328's own declaration is the worked example: it holds `agent-system/extensions/literature/scripts/tests/` (trailing slash, matched at site 1 only by accident of startswith) and `agent-system/extensions/literature/scripts/literature-search.sh`, but neither `.../scripts/lib/` nor `.../manifest.json` -- so the two dropped paths are genuine non-members at both sites, while the predicates' agreement on OTHER paths is coincidental rather than guaranteed.

FINDING 3 -- THE RECOVERY ROUTE HAS NO CONTAINMENT GUARANTEE. Site 3 (V10 inside git-commit-scoped.sh) is `--task <N>` opt-in only. Its own header (around lines 461-472) states that `containment_scope_arr` is left EMPTY -- "the single disable switch every downstream V10 check consults, never a second flag" -- on any of: --task omitted; no specs/state.json; the task's entry not found; a missing, empty, null or malformed file_scope; or an internal resolution error. Both recovered paths are absent from task 328's declared file_scope (128 entries, checked at creation time, neither path present), yet both landed in ff880f772. So the follow-up commit reached HEAD past all three sites. Establish WHICH mechanism allowed it -- candidates to TEST, not to assume: --task was omitted on the recovery call, so V10 was disabled outright; or --task was supplied and V10's drop-and-warn posture with exit code 7 ("Observability, not a refusal", per that script's header around line 82) still let the commit stand; or the recovery used a route that does not pass through git-commit-scoped.sh at all. Record the verdict. The structural point survives whichever answer lands: the only route available for recovering a path the containment predicate correctly dropped is a commit that re-asserts no containment.

=== REQUIRED OUTCOMES -- decide and implement ONE coherent rule for each, recording the reasoning ===

1. PREDICATE PARITY AT SITE 1. Site 1 and site 2 test a candidate path by the same rule, with a path-boundary-correct containment test and trailing-slash normalization, so the two sites cannot disagree about the same path against the same declaration. Decide WHERE that rule lives: a shared library function both sites source (the precedent is scripts/lib/engine-written-state-paths-lib.sh, which all three sites already share for the allowlist), or a corrected per-site implementation. Weigh honestly -- the allowlist was consolidated into one library precisely because it had been hand-copied at three sites, and this is the same shape of problem one layer down; but a shared function must preserve each site's own fail-open posture, which differs in detail.

2. SITE 1 INHERITS WHATEVER RULE THE EXTENSION-MANIFEST RECORD LANDS. Once #486 settles its manifest-registration question at site 2, site 1 must reach the same verdict rather than keeping the old behaviour. Decide whether that inheritance is structural (because outcome 1 put the rule in one shared place) or a second explicit edit here. If #486 rules for its own option (3) -- close without a rule, on the grounding that #430's general mechanism subsumes it -- record that site 1 needs nothing on this axis and say so explicitly.

3. A GUARDED RECOVERY ROUTE. Give a dispatch one sanctioned way to land a path the predicate correctly dropped, in which the declaration is extended through the existing mutex-guarded state-write.sh path and the extension is itself recorded, so the recovery commit is checked by the same predicate as the commit that dropped the path. This is the half of the originating change request that #430 does NOT own: #430 decides whether and how a dispatch PROPOSES an addition to its own file_scope; this decides what a dispatch may legitimately COMMIT in the window before that proposal has merged. If the ruling is that the two are inseparable and the recovery route is wholly #430's territory, record that and reduce this record to outcomes 1, 2 and 4 rather than leaving the question open.

4. THE STANDARD STOPS MISDESCRIBING ITSELF. git-staging-scope.md's "Three Containment sites, not one" section no longer claims the three sites run the same predicate when they do not, and records whatever parity outcome 1 lands plus the recovery route outcome 3 lands.

=== HARD CONSTRAINTS ===
- The containment check exists to stop a concurrently dispatched session's uncommitted edits being swept into the wrong task's commit (git-staging-scope.md's "Concurrency Qualifier: Targeted Staging Is Necessary But Not Sufficient" section, around lines 223-243, records that hazard as observed in production). NO outcome here may weaken it. In particular, fixing site 1's over-matching must TIGHTEN that site, and a guarded recovery route must not become a general bypass.
- Do NOT make any site fatal. Fail-open on a missing, empty or malformed file_scope is deliberate at all three sites and must stay, as must site 1's detection-only, no-exit-code posture for the WORK (h) advisory itself.
- Do NOT weaken drop-and-warn for a genuine excursion. An out-of-territory source edit must still be dropped, warned, and recorded, pinned by a test.
- Do NOT re-introduce or disturb site 2's trailing-slash normalization or the shared engine-written-state allowlist; both are present and working.
- Do NOT widen a task's declared file_scope by hand as the fix, and do not treat the per-task widening used as a workaround elsewhere as a precedent.
- Any state.json write goes through the mutex-guarded state-write.sh path, never a second writer.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===
- A fixture in test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh proves site 1 and site 2 agree on a shared battery of (declaration, candidate path) pairs, including the trailing-slash directory entry, the boundary case where a declared entry is a string prefix of a NON-child sibling path, and a newly created file under a declared parent directory.
- A fixture proves a genuinely out-of-territory path is still dropped, still warned, and still recorded at site 1.
- A fixture preserves fail-open for a missing, empty and malformed file_scope at site 1.
- The verdict on how ff880f772 landed two non-member paths past all three sites is recorded, with whatever follow-on it implies either fixed here or filed.
- git-staging-scope.md's three-sites section is truthful about the predicates and carries the recovery-route ruling.

=== TERRITORY EDGES -- SEQUENCE, DO NOT PARALLELIZE ===
Dependency edges are declared rather than racing shared files (context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md: shared territory is serialized with an edge, never a bare exclusion).
- #486 and #430: this record's outcome 2 consumes #486's ruling, which in turn depends on #430's general mechanism. Both must settle first; neither reaches site 1.
- #461 and #484: both declare git-commit-scoped.sh (#484 also its test), the surface outcome 3 touches. #484 additionally rewrites the per-path issue-record emission that site 1's sibling sites call.
- #494: the nearest neighbour in SHAPE -- it propagates an own-artifact-directory member that sites 1 and 3 already have to site 2 which never received it. This record is the mirror image, propagating to site 1 what sites 2 and 3 have. Neither subsumes the other and their file_scopes are disjoint, but read it before scoping: its account of the three-site propagation gap is the same account, in the opposite direction.
- #435 (82-path lifecycle-prose sweep) is a strict SUPERSET of this record's declared scope: every path here is already in its file_scope. #448, #472, #476 and in-flight #328 each declare test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh and/or test-git-commit-scoped.sh.

=== BACKLOG AUDIT PERFORMED AT CREATION TIME (scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh, full corpus) ===
The matcher's highest-scoring mechanically-overlapping open records were #486 (semantic 0.69), #494 (0.64), #484 (0.57), #430 (0.51) and #446 (0.50) -- precisely the records read and disclaimed in the scope note above. Ruling rendered by the creating surface, not by the script (which never decides duplication): NOT a duplicate. For #486, #494, #430 and #446 the ONLY mechanically shared path is context/standards/git-staging-scope.md, the standard every containment record necessarily amends; none of the four declares orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh, the site where the cited drop fired. #494 is the closest in shape and is the mirror image rather than the same work: it carries a member sites 1 and 3 already have to site 2, where this record carries to site 1 what sites 2 and 3 have. Every remaining non-trivial verdict was a territory overlap on git-commit-scoped.sh, its suite, or test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh, and is discharged by a dependency edge above rather than a merge. The matcher additionally emitted 41 scope-blind degradations (corpus records declaring no file_scope, emitted unconditionally per that script's documented noise trade-off) and a long tail of semantic-only verdicts in the 0.40-0.53 band against substantively unrelated records; neither class was treated as a duplication signal.

### 500. Dependency-edge backfill is unreachable for a not-started task
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 430, Task 446, Task 448

**Description**: `update-task-status.sh --depends-on-add=<json-array>` cannot fire on a task whose status is `not_started` -- which is the single most common status for a task that needs an edge added after the fact. Restore reachability without weakening any of the flag's existing guarantees.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/core/**`, never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. Redeploy afterwards via the bounded-wait idiom (`timeout 540 bash .claude/scripts/deploy-headless.sh`, Bash tool timeout raised to 600000), never a hand-patch of a deployed file.

== THE DEFECT, MEASURED ==

The flag carries a STATUS-NO-OP RESTRICTION, stated in the script's own header: it is valid ONLY on a call whose `operation:target_status` pair resolves to the task's own CURRENT status, so that it rides the existing no-op-replay branch inside `update_state_json()` and never performs a real status transition in the same call. A violating call is a hard validation error.

`map_status()` has twelve arms. They emit exactly: `researching` (from `preflight:research` and from `postflight:needs_research`), `planning`, `implementing`, `researched`, `planned`, `completed` (from both `postflight:implement` and `postflight:pr_ready`), `pr_ready`, `hold`, `partial`, `blocked`. No arm emits `not_started`, and there is no `operation:target_status` pair that could produce it -- `not_started` is the pre-lifecycle state, so no operation yields it.

Therefore the precondition is unsatisfiable for any `not_started` task, and the flag is unreachable for them. Reproduced live:

```
Error: --depends-on-add requires operation:target_status to resolve to task 421's CURRENT
status (a no-op replay) -- got current='not_started', resolved target='researching'.
```

`update-task-status.sh` was confirmed to be the only sanctioned edge-add path -- there is no `manage-dependencies.sh` or equivalent -- so a `not_started` task's edge cannot be backfilled by any sanctioned means. Hand-editing `state.json` is not an acceptable workaround (it bypasses the write-time cycle refusal described below).

== WHY THE RESTRICTION EXISTS, AND WHY THAT MAKES THIS A GAP RATHER THAN A DESIGN CHOICE ==

The header states the rationale: an edge add between two already-existing tasks is phase-independent -- it has nothing to do with either task's lifecycle phase -- so there is no natural `operation:target_status` pair meaning "just add this edge," and replaying the task's own current status is the existing vehicle for "touch this entry without moving its phase." The flag reuses that vehicle rather than inventing a second one.

That reasoning is sound, and the gap follows from it rather than contradicting it: the chosen vehicle happens not to exist for the one status where backfill is most often needed. The restriction's intent (never couple an edge add with a real transition) is fully compatible with admitting `not_started`; only the mechanism is missing.

== TWO LIVE EDGES ARE CURRENTLY UNADDABLE BECAUSE OF THIS ==

Both arose during this task's creation session and both remain outstanding:

1. An edge making task 421 (`retarget_distill_meta_as_evidence_consumer`, `not_started`) depend on the task that relocates the three vault-independent distill telemetry sub-modes into core. The owner ruled RELOCATE FIRST, so 421's retargeting work must land at the new core location rather than in a tree about to be retired. The ruling currently survives only as prose in the relocation task's description, so nothing mechanically prevents 421 from being dispatched first and writing its work into the wrong tree.

2. An edge making task 438 (`dependency_edge_rationale_semantic_check`, `not_started`) depend on THIS task. See the ordering note below.

Both are real regressions in the backlog's machine-readable ordering, not hypotheticals.

== OPEN DESIGN QUESTION, DELIBERATELY NOT PRE-DECIDED ==

Rule on this during research/plan and record the verdict with its reason. Three candidate shapes:

(a) ADD A NO-OP-ONLY PAIR THAT RESOLVES TO `not_started`. Preserves the existing "ride the no-op replay" design exactly; the change is confined to `map_status()` plus whatever validates the operation vocabulary. Cost: it mints an `operation:target_status` token that names a status no real transition produces, which risks reading as a settable lifecycle target and being misused as one. If chosen, the arm must be unmistakably no-op-only and must be refused for any call that is not an edge-only call.

(b) ADMIT `--depends-on-add` AS A STANDALONE OPERATION with no `operation`/`target_status` positional arguments at all. This is the most faithful to the header's own observation that an edge add is phase-independent, and it removes the need for any synthetic status token. Cost: it is precisely the "second vehicle" the header declined to invent, so choosing it means overturning a stated design decision -- do that explicitly and record why, rather than silently.

(c) LEAVE THE FLAG AND MOVE EDGE-ADD FOR `not_started` TASKS TO A DIFFERENT SURFACE. Cheapest to reason about, but it splits one primitive across two entry points, which is the duplicate-mechanism outcome this codebase's consolidation bias argues against. Probably the weakest option; state why if rejected.

Weigh (a) against (b) on the number of call sites affected and on which leaves the primitive easier to describe in one sentence. A prose-only or documentation-only outcome is NOT acceptable here -- the flag is genuinely unreachable, so something must change in the script.

== GUARANTEES THAT MUST SURVIVE UNCHANGED ==

Whichever shape is chosen, every one of these existing behaviours must be preserved and covered by the test suite:

- NO TRANSITION EVER ACCOMPANIES AN EDGE ADD. This is the restriction's actual purpose and is non-negotiable.
- MANDATORY WRITE-TIME CYCLE REFUSAL. Before any write, and before a `--dry-run` preview returns, the script builds a scratch copy of `state.json` with the candidate edge applied and runs `validate-state.sh --deep` against it, reusing that script's existing Kahn-algorithm cycle detector rather than transcribing a second one. This is a reuse requirement with a fail-closed guard; do not reimplement it and do not let a new admission path bypass it.
- VALUE VALIDATION: the value must parse as a JSON array of NUMBERS, not strings; a malformed value is a hard validation error (exit 1), never a silent no-op.
- SELF-EDGE REFUSAL: the dependent's own number must not appear in the list.
- MISSING-PREDECESSOR REFUSAL: every listed predecessor must already exist in `active_projects`, refused by number if not.
- UNION-MERGE IDEMPOTENCE: a predecessor already present in `dependencies[]` is not an error; a repeated call is idempotent by construction.
- DRAIN CONFIRMATION STAYS THE CALLER'S RESPONSIBILITY. The script deliberately does not invoke `orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh --dry-run` itself, because only the caller knows the full intended batch. Do not add an auto-invocation.

== ORDERING AGAINST TASK 438, STATED DELIBERATELY ==

Task 438 (`dependency_edge_rationale_semantic_check`) shares `update-task-status.sh` and is the ONLY record contending on this same flag: its own open design question contemplates a per-edge rationale discriminator that `--depends-on-add` "would then have to accept."

This task deliberately does NOT declare a dependency on 438. The correct order is the reverse -- the admission gap first, the rationale schema second -- for two reasons. First, 438's open question is materially easier to answer against a flag that can actually fire on a `not_started` task, which is the status most edges are backfilled onto. Second, blocking a confined admission fix behind 438's own five-deep dependency chain would over-serialize it for no benefit.

So 438 should gain an edge onto THIS task once this task lands. That edge is itself currently unaddable for exactly the reason this task exists (438 is `not_started`), which makes adding it a natural end-to-end verification -- see acceptance below.

== WHY THE DECLARED EDGES ARE WHAT THEY ARE ==

Shared file territory means a declared edge and a serialized sequence, never a bare exclusion:
- 328 (`systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency`) is currently `implementing` and shares `test-update-task-status.sh`. A live writer, so this edge is not theoretical.
- 448 (`contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations`) shares `test-update-task-status.sh`.
- 430 (`feed_undeclared_implement_paths_into_file_scope`) and 446 (`close_the_plan_to_file_scope_reconciliation_gap`) each share `update-task-status.sh`. Both touch the file's `file_scope`-feeding behaviour rather than its edge-admission logic, so the overlap is unordered -- hence an edge and a sequence, with the direction chosen conservatively (this task after theirs).

== BACKLOG-RECONCILIATION RULINGS ALREADY MADE AT THE CONFIRMATION GATE ==

Recorded here so research does not revisit them:
- `duplicate-of-open #430` (semantic 53%, mechanical overlap) and `duplicate-of-open #446` (semantic 48%, mechanical overlap): NOT duplicates. Both are mechanically corroborated only because they share `update-task-status.sh`; 430 concerns feeding undeclared implement paths into `file_scope` and 446 concerns plan-to-`file_scope` reconciliation. Neither touches the `--depends-on-add` admission precondition. They ARE genuine serialization contenders and carry declared edges above.
- `duplicate-of-open #450, #360, #472` (semantic 44-51%, all `mechanical_class: none`): NOT duplicates, semantic noise.
- `subset-of-open #435` (semantic 24%, mechanical subset): low-confidence, no edge declared; 435's own declared scope does not include either of this task's two files.

== ACCEPTANCE ==

1. `--depends-on-add` succeeds against a `not_started` task in a fixture, adding the edge and leaving the task's status still `not_started`.
2. The same call still refuses, with its existing error text, any attempt to combine an edge add with a real status transition.
3. The write-time cycle refusal still fires on a candidate edge that would create a cycle, including on a `not_started` target, and still fails closed when `validate-state.sh` cannot be run.
4. Malformed value, self-edge, and missing-predecessor refusals all still exit 1 with their existing messages; a repeated call is still idempotent.
5. END-TO-END, ON THE TWO REAL EDGES THIS GAP BLOCKED: after the fix, add the 421 edge onto the telemetry-sub-mode relocation task, and add 438's edge onto this task. Both must succeed and leave both tasks' statuses untouched. Report the resulting `dependencies[]` arrays.
6. `validate-state.sh` passes over the resulting `state.json` with zero failures.
7. Re-verify every quoted error string, header sentence, and `map_status()` arm cited in this description against the tree as it then stands rather than trusting this description.

== NON-GOALS ==

- Do not add the per-edge semantic/serialization rationale discriminator -- that is 438's work.
- Do not change `dependencies[]`'s flat integer-array schema.
- Do not add an auto-invoked drain check.
- Do not touch the status vocabulary in `state-management.md`'s transition model beyond whatever the chosen shape strictly requires.

### 499. Retarget the store-coupled distill sub-modes and retire the memory extension
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: Task 496, Task 497, Task 498

**Description**: Retarget the two store-coupled `/distill` sub-modes onto the hosted store, drop the six that the hosted store already performs, remove core's now-orphaned callers, and retire the memory extension.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/**`, never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via `deploy-headless.sh`.

== PART A: RETARGET TWO SUB-MODES ==

`--revise` (correlates deviation/blocker evidence to stored knowledge, classifying each correlated item corroborated / contradicted / gap) and `--learn` (retroactive batch harvest across already-archived tasks whose candidates were never harvested) are store-coupled and must be retargeted onto hosted knowledge objects rather than relocated or deleted.

Carry forward: the three-strikes threshold before anything counts as contradicted or a gap; the interactive selection gate (neither may ever run under `--auto`); the first-class degraded path when the event store is empty (`total_events: 0` is a NORMAL outcome, not an error). Note that `--revise`'s event-to-OTel session join degrades on this machine because OTel is not enabled -- it must announce that explicitly, not silently.

The `--revise` classification outcomes that previously tombstoned or updated a local record must be rethought against a store with NO DELETE API: a contradicted item cannot be tombstoned. Decide and record what replaces it -- most likely writing a superseding note that carries the correction, since the hosted store performs its own conflict surfacing and stale-position re-confirmation.

== PART B: DROP SIX SUB-MODES ==

Delete `report`, `--purge`, `--merge`, `--compress`, `--refine` and `--gc` outright. The hosted store already performs all six server-side: overnight consolidation and compaction, staleness re-confirmation of load-bearing positions, and active conflict detection between a person's own notes. This was observed directly in a session briefing reporting 2372 knowledge objects compacted into 5 structures, a stale-position re-confirmation prompt, and 10 open conflicts awaiting resolution. Porting them would duplicate a service the store provides.

Record this reasoning in the retirement documentation, because "we deleted six maintenance modes" is otherwise indistinguishable from a capability regression.

== PART C: REMOVE ORPHANED CORE CALLERS ==

Three memory-coupled pieces live in CORE, not in the memory extension, and would be left dangling by a naive uninstall:
- `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/memory-retrieve.sh` -- superseded by the sapience retrieval script.
- `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/memory-harvest.sh` -- already uncalled by any skill or command (its own header says so); delete it.
- `agent-system/extensions/core/skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md`'s HarvestMemories stage -- reads candidates from `state.json`, dedups against the local index, writes record files and regenerates local indices. Retarget it to the sapience write path or remove it, and update the archival flow's accounting of what it touches.

== PART D: RETIRE THE EXTENSION ==

Remove the memory extension from the source store and from `.claude-extensions.json`, and update the sapience extension's own documentation so it describes the single remaining knowledge path rather than the split-by-altitude arrangement it currently documents.

== HARD SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT ==

`.memory/` STAYS ON DISK, READ-ONLY, THROUGHOUT. Do not delete it as part of retirement, and do not attempt to bulk-import it. There is no re-import path into the hosted store and no delete API, so a bad import cannot be undone -- an import that turns out wrong is permanent. The local records remain readable for as long as the hosted corpus has not accumulated an equivalent. Deleting `.memory/` is a separate, later, owner-initiated decision and is explicitly out of scope here.

Note also that the vault data was never tracked in the extension's own installed-file manifest, so an ordinary uninstall leaves it in place by default. That is the desired behaviour; do not "fix" it.

== RELATION TO EXISTING RECORDS ==

`superset-of-open` of 421 by mechanical scope -- the same relocation relation the sub-mode promotion task carries, covered by the declared edges and by 421's re-sequencing behind that promotion task.

== VERIFICATION ==

- `--revise` and `--learn` operate against hosted knowledge objects and take their stated degraded paths when the event store is empty and when OTel is absent.
- No remaining reference anywhere in the source store points at the memory extension, at `memory-retrieve.sh`, or at `memory-harvest.sh`.
- A full deploy from a clean tree succeeds with the memory extension absent.
- An `/orchestrate` dispatch still receives repo-scoped injected context (from the sapience retrieval path) and still degrades to empty context on failure.
- `/todo` archival completes without the HarvestMemories stage erroring.
- `.memory/` is present and unmodified at the end of the task.
- Email triage Stage 7 still accumulates preferences correctly (guaranteed by the dependency on the email tally substrate task).

### 498. Replace the email preference harvest tally substrate
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: Task 496

**Description**: Port `skill-email-cleanup`'s Stage 7 sender/domain preference harvest off the local knowledge vault, so the memory extension can be retired without breaking email triage. This is the hardest break in the migration and it BLOCKS retirement.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/email/**`, never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via `deploy-headless.sh`.

== WHAT CURRENTLY DEPENDS ON THE LOCAL VAULT ==

Stage 7 of `skill-email-cleanup` is live, executable prose, not a design sketch. It calls `email-preference-harvest.sh dedup .memory/memory-index.json "email/preferences/${ACCOUNT}/${KEY}"` to decide CREATE vs EXTEND vs UPDATE, then applies tally arithmetic via that script's `tally-op` subcommand, gates on its `threshold` subcommand, and resolves mixed senders via `dominant`. It depends on four things the local store provides and the hosted store does not:

1. NAMESPACE-SCOPED TALLY ARITHMETIC -- CREATE once, then UPDATE/EXTEND an accumulating per-sender action count, never one record per message.
2. EXACT-KEY DEDUP FOR RESERVED NAMESPACES -- an exact hit/miss lookup on the key `email/preferences/{account}/{key}`, not a fuzzy or semantic match.
3. ARCHIVE-SCOPE TALLY ISOLATION -- an archive-scope pass records its delta in a separate sub-section and must never merge into the inbox-scope tally.
4. A READABLE INDEX -- the ground-truth tally is read back from the record body before each update.

The hosted store offers none of these. Its write tool is a freeform note with no tally arithmetic, no exact-key namespace dedup, and no local index to read back; and as established in the sapience per-repo storage task, there is no delete API, so a mis-accumulated tally cannot be removed.

== WHAT THIS TASK MUST SETTLE ==

The ruling is deliberately NOT pre-made and is the first thing to decide at plan time: whether the tally substrate becomes (a) a small local state file owned by the email extension itself, with the hosted store receiving only the settled preference as a durable note, or (b) a fully hosted representation read back through repo-filtered search. Argue it both ways and record the verdict with its reason.

Considerations for (a): tally accumulation is high-frequency, low-value intermediate state -- exactly what should NOT go to a store with permanent, undeletable, unreviewed writes. The hosted store's noise gate would also judge most intermediate tally updates operational chatter. Considerations for (b): a second local store is the substrate the migration exists to remove, and reintroducing one under a different name needs justifying.

Whichever is chosen, preserve: the exact-key (not fuzzy) dedup semantics for the reserved namespace, archive-scope isolation as a first-class separate accumulator, the evidentiary threshold gate (uniform-batch this round, or rolling N>=3 at >=80% against the post-update tally), the mixed-sender branch that splits or declines rather than averaging into a false scalar action, and the single consolidated never-silent approval gate.

== CONSTRAINTS ==

- Stage 7 must keep firing ONLY after Stage 6, reading ONLY the Stage 6 executed diff -- never the Stage 2 candidate manifest and never an approved-but-not-yet-executed manifest. The migration must not relax this.
- `.memory/` is READ-ONLY for the duration of the whole migration. This task may read the existing tallies to carry them forward; it must not write to them.
- The deterministic subcommands of `email-preference-harvest.sh` (`normalize`, `freemail`, `identity`, `tally-op`, `dominant`, `threshold`) are pure and should be REUSED unchanged wherever possible. Only `dedup`'s substrate changes. Do not fork the tally arithmetic.

== VERIFICATION ==

- A uniform-batch pass over a fixture produces a CREATE, and a second pass over the same sender produces an EXTEND or UPDATE with a correctly accumulated count -- never a second parallel record.
- An archive-scope pass's delta lands in the archive accumulator and does not perturb the inbox tally.
- A mixed-sender group with no uniform majority either splits by token into distinct keys or declines to aggregate, and never averages.
- A sender below both evidentiary bars is tallied but NOT presented as a strong preference.
- The existing tallies in `.memory/` are carried forward without loss, and `.memory/` is not written to.

---

### 497. Promote the three vault-independent distill telemetry sub-modes into core
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: Task 435, Task 328

**Description**: Relocate the `--meta`, `--dream` and `--review` sub-modes out of the memory extension into core, so they survive the memory extension's retirement.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/core/**` and `agent-system/extensions/memory/**`, never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via `deploy-headless.sh`.

== WHY THESE THREE AND NOT THE OTHER SIX ==

`/distill` carries nine sub-modes serving two unrelated jobs. Six are knowledge-store maintenance (`report`, `--purge`, `--merge`, `--compress`, `--refine`, `--gc`). Two are store-coupled (`--revise` correlates evidence to stored knowledge; `--learn` harvests into the store). These THREE touch no store at all: they read `specs/events.jsonl`, `~/.claude/history.jsonl`, session transcripts and OTel, and they report or propose. They live under the memory extension only by accident of when they were built.

The relocation is MECHANICAL, not a rewrite. Their mandated data access path, `events-query.sh`, ALREADY lives in core at `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/events-query.sh` and was verified working (it returns `{"total_events": 7586, ...}` against this repo). No dependency untangling is required.

A second benefit: promotion frees them from the knowledge-store `validate-on-read` precondition they are currently gated behind via the Shared Sub-Mode Skeleton's Edge Case Checks. None of the three needs it.

== WHAT EACH SUB-MODE IS FOR, AND THE SIGNAL CONFIRMING IT IS WORTH KEEPING ==

`--meta`: surfaces recurring (three-strikes) friction pointing at a named skill, hook, rule or checkpoint, then delegates task creation to `meta-builder-agent`. Current signal in this repo's `specs/events.jsonl` (7586 events: 83 deviation, 2 blocker, 32 system_defect) shows unambiguous clusters -- 14 occurrences of "handoff mtime N predates this dispatch window"; 5 of handoff `dispatch_status` off-schema (the writer emits a key named `dispatch_status` instead of the schema-required `status`, with values including `"completed"`, `"weird_status"` and empty); 3 `dispatch_seq` mismatches (1 vs 2, 19 vs 18, 4 vs 7); several dispatches that narrated completed work but wrote no handoff, no `.return-meta.json` and no report artifact; and one record reading "research dispatch narrated 4 source-store file modifications but reported modified_files: [] -- the scoped commit would have stranded them", which is a near-data-loss defect. The handoff-schema cluster alone clears the three-strikes threshold several times over and is currently unread by anything.

`--dream`: clusters the user's own prompt history (`~/.claude/history.jsonl`, 31666 prompts total, 3135 for this repo, sliced by each line's `project` field) and surfaces recurring themes that no stored knowledge or open task covers. The dominant uncovered cluster in recent history is the user hand-auditing batch dependencies before dispatching -- repeated requests of the form "are there any natural dependencies missing", "are the appropriate dependencies in place for me to resume", "can I now run this batch", "if some tasks should revise others, make that explicit". Five instances inside roughly 400 prompts, with no command covering it. That is exactly the shape `--dream` exists to surface and funnel to `--meta`.

`--review "<question>"`: strictly read-only ad hoc inquiry spanning all four source tiers, answering in prose with per-tier evidence citations. It never writes and never proposes; findings funnel to `--meta`, `--revise` or the write path.

== CAVEATS TO CARRY INTO THE RELOCATED SPECIFICATIONS ==

- OTel IS NOT ENABLED on this machine: there is no `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TELEMETRY` in the environment or in any settings file. So `--review`'s Tier 1 is unavailable and must take its stated degraded path ("OTel not enabled for this session/repo -- outcome evidence unavailable for this question"), announced explicitly, never silently. `--meta` and `--dream` do not use OTel and are unaffected.
- The `reflection` event type has exactly ONE record in this repo's event store and no live producer (see `context/formats/events-format.md`). Anything keyed on reflections is effectively dead signal; the relocated specifications must not present it as a live input.
- `--review` and `--dream` carry a sanctioned MANDATORY STOP exemption because neither mutates. Preserve those exemptions as explicitly STATED, not silently dropped -- the existing specifications are deliberate about this and the relocation must not quietly lose it.

== RELATION TO EXISTING RECORDS, ALREADY RULED ==

This task is a `superset-of-open` of 421 (`retarget_distill_meta_as_evidence_consumer`) by mechanical scope: 421's own two declared files are a subset of this task's. The ruling, made by the owner at the confirmation gate, is RELOCATE FIRST: this task moves the three sub-modes into core as a pure relocation, and 421 is re-sequenced to depend on this task so its retargeting work (issues.jsonl ingestion, matcher consumption, consolidation channel, per-invocation cap) lands at the new core location rather than being written into a tree about to be retired. 421's own `file_scope` will need updating to the new core paths as part of that re-sequencing.

This task is also a `subset-of-open` of 435 (`reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class`), which declares the broader `agent-system/extensions/memory/context/` and `agent-system/extensions/memory/skills/` directories. Edge declared. 328 is declared because it is currently `implementing` across `agent-system/extensions/memory/commands/` and `agent-system/extensions/memory/skills/`, which contains this task's `skill-distill/SKILL.md` edit.

Three further `duplicate-of-open` verdicts (459, 451, 360) were ruled NOT duplicates at the confirmation gate: all three are `mechanical_class: none` at 41-45% semantic similarity, and none concerns sub-mode relocation.

== EXPLICIT NON-GOALS ==

- Do not retarget, redesign or extend any of the three sub-modes. This is relocation. 421 owns `--meta`'s retargeting.
- Do not relocate `--revise` or `--learn` -- they are store-coupled and belong to the retirement task.
- Do not delete the six store-maintenance sub-modes here; that is the retirement task's work.

== VERIFICATION ==

- Each of the three sub-modes is invocable after relocation and produces its documented output shape against this repo's real `events.jsonl` and `history.jsonl`.
- `--meta` run against this repo surfaces the handoff-schema cluster described above, demonstrating the signal path works end to end at the new location.
- `--review` with OTel absent takes the stated degraded path and says so explicitly.
- No remaining reference anywhere in the source store points at the old memory-extension paths for these three files.
- The relocated specifications' MANDATORY STOP exemptions for `--review` and `--dream` are still explicitly stated.

### 496. Add per-repo storage and injection to the sapience extension
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: Task 360, Task 449, Task 450, Task 487, Task 488

**Description**: Give the sapience extension repo-scoped knowledge storage and per-dispatch context injection, so it can replace the memory extension's local vault substrate. This is the essential piece of the migration -- the other three tasks in this batch depend on it.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/sapience/**` and `agent-system/extensions/core/**`, never a deployed `.claude/**` tree, which is a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). Resolve `source_dir` from `<project-root>/.claude-extensions.json` and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via `deploy-headless.sh`.

== THE SCOPING MECHANISM IS ALREADY SETTLED, AND IT IS NOT THE OBVIOUS ONE ==

Per-repo scoping works ONLY via a repo tag embedded in the KO's `provenance` string, filtered on read by `/v1/search`'s `source=` substring parameter. This was established empirically against the live host; do not re-derive it, and do not substitute the `project` field.

What was measured:
- A KO written with `provenance` containing `[repo:nvim]` is returned by `source=[repo:nvim]` and by `source=repo:nvim`, and is NOT returned by `source=[repo:bimodal]`. The filter acts as a hard pre-filter that dominates relevance ranking -- an unrelated query still returned only the repo-tagged row.
- The `project` field is a DEAD END. `/v1/search` silently ignores `&project=`: identical 8-row result sets for a real slug, another repo's slug, and a nonexistent slug. `/v1/sf1/handoff?project=X` merely echoes the slug back with `project_known: false` and returns cross-project KOs even for a bogus project. `/v1/sf1/state` returns 404. `/v1/projects` returns 403 `{"code": "company_only"}` -- project surfaces are a paid team feature on this account.
- `source_loc` (the field the `sapience_remember` MCP tool's own `source` argument populates) is NOT matched by the `source=` filter and is not returned in search results. A probe written with `source_loc: repo:/home/benjamin/.config/nvim` was not findable by that value; a probe with the tag in `provenance` was.
- CONSEQUENCE: the `sapience_remember` MCP tool CANNOT be used for tagged writes, because `mcp.py`'s `remember_provenance()` hardcodes provenance to `"<Name> via Claude Code session (MCP)"` with no injectable repo marker (an unknown `SAPIENCE_CLIENT` collapses to the label `AI`, so it cannot be smuggled in that way either). Tagged writes must go to `POST /ingest-kos` directly, which accepts an arbitrary `provenance` string verbatim.

== DELIVERABLE 1: sapience-retrieve.sh ==

A retrieval script calling `GET /v1/search?q=<keywords>&source=[repo:<slug>]&limit=5`, emitting the SAME `<memory-context>` envelope `memory-retrieve.sh` emits today, so the dispatch-side contract is unchanged. Preserve that script's existing budget discipline: 2000-token budget, 5-entry cap. Derive the repo slug from `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` (basename), so the same script works unmodified in every repo the system deploys into. Reuse `memory-retrieve.sh`'s existing keyword-extraction stage (lowercase, split on non-alpha, stop-word filter, length>3, top 10) rather than inventing a second one -- only the corpus lookup changes, not the query derivation.

Measured latency: 0.22s average over five cold queries against the live host, so per-dispatch injection is viable. The response's `meter` field is informational (`tokens_read`/`tokens_held`), not a search quota.

== DELIVERABLE 2: the dispatch swap ==

Swap the retrieval call in at `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh`, at the existing memory-retrieval call site in its Stage 3.5 `memory_context` block (currently a `bash "${SKILL_REPO_ROOT}/.claude/scripts/memory-retrieve.sh" "$description" "$task_type" "$memory_arg3" 2>/dev/null || memory_context=""` form, guarded by the `--clean` flag check).

PRESERVE THE FAILURE-SWALLOWING WRAPPER. The `2>/dev/null) || memory_context=""` shape means a failure degrades to no injection rather than a broken dispatch. This matters MORE after the swap than before it, because the new call is a network round-trip rather than a local jq pass -- an outage must not break every dispatch in a batch. Also preserve the `--clean` suppression semantics unchanged.

== DELIVERABLE 3: sapience-remember.sh ==

A write script calling `POST /ingest-kos` with the repo-tagged provenance AND `explicit: true`.

`explicit: true` IS MANDATORY, not an optimization. Measured: a realistically-worded probe note was silently discarded by the server's noise gate -- the response was `{"ok": true, "written": 0, "gated": 1}`, with `ok: true` despite nothing being written. The same note with `explicit: true` wrote successfully (`written: 1`). Without the flag, task findings would be lost invisibly, and the success-shaped response makes the loss undetectable from the caller side. The script MUST additionally treat `gated >= 1` as a loud, reported outcome rather than a silent success, so a future gate change cannot reintroduce silent loss.

== CONSTRAINTS AND KNOWN HAZARDS TO RECORD IN THE DESIGN ==

- THERE IS NO DELETE API. `DELETE /v1/ko/<hash>`, `DELETE /v1/kos/<hash>` and `POST /v1/ko/<hash>/delete` all return 404. Writes are permanent; deletion is web-app-only. This is the principal risk of any per-dispatch write path and MUST shape how much is written per task -- prefer few, high-value, well-formed writes at a task boundary over a write per decision.
- Writes through this path bypass the `auto_approve: false` review gate entirely (that gate governs only the transcript-extraction path), so every write is permanent and unreviewed. State this in the extension's own documentation.
- The transcript-extraction path CANNOT be repo-tagged, so it will keep writing untagged KOs that repo-filtered reads never see. The vault therefore accumulates two differently-shaped populations. Document this asymmetry rather than attempting to fix it.
- Two disposable probe KOs written during the research that established the above could not be deleted via API and need manual removal at app.spnc.ai: hashes `806180dc43c5cb34` ("sapience source_loc filter probe") and `9ba2b479b5e4a7e2` ("sapience provenance tagging probe"). Note this as a manual cleanup item; it is not implementation work.

== WHY THE DEPENDENCY EDGES EXIST ==

`orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh` is shared file territory with four non-terminal records (360, 449, 450, 487). Per the batching rule, shared territory means a declared edge and a serialized sequence, never a bare exclusion. 488 is declared because it owns `agent-system/extensions/sapience/settings-fragment.json` and `review-gate-boundary.md`, which this task's documentation deliverable touches.

== BACKLOG AUDIT DISPOSITION (adjudicated at the confirmation gate) ==

The audit's `duplicate-of-open #421` verdict is `mechanical_class: none` at 41% semantic similarity and was ruled NOT a duplicate: 421 concerns evidence consumption, not context injection. The `overlaps-open` verdicts against 360, 435, 449, 450 and 487 are real and are discharged by the declared dependency edges above.

== VERIFICATION ==

- A tagged write followed by a repo-filtered read returns that KO, and a read filtered to a DIFFERENT repo tag does not.
- A write whose response carries `gated >= 1` is reported as a failure by the script, not swallowed.
- With the retrieval endpoint unreachable (simulated), a dispatch still builds and runs with an empty `memory_context`, exactly as the current `|| memory_context=""` behaviour provides.
- `--clean` still suppresses injection after the swap.
- The emitted `<memory-context>` block is byte-comparable in shape to the one `memory-retrieve.sh` produces for an equivalent corpus, so no consumer needs changing.
