### 492. Make backlog-audit corroboration computable and gate the confirm prompt on it
- **Effort**: small
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 491

**Description**: Key the backlog-audit confirmation prompt to corroboration rather than to the verdict label, and define that bar once instead of re-deriving it at each consumer.

--- PRECONDITION: RE-MEASURE BEFORE IMPLEMENTING ---

This task was filed from evidence gathered against the PRE-FIX scorer. Before implementing anything, re-run audit-task-proposals.sh against the corrected scorer and record the new numbers. If the corrected scorer alone drops the verdict volume to the point where the prompt no longer misfires on genuinely-new proposals, then outcomes 2 and 3 below shrink to a documentation change (state the bar explicitly) and the consumer edits may be unnecessary. Do NOT implement the full change on the strength of the measurements quoted here. The dependency on the scorer task exists for exactly this reason. Outcome 1 is exempt from this precondition: it is a wiring defect, not a calibration question.

THE DEFECT, INDEPENDENT OF SCORER CALIBRATION:
Each consumer filters the audit's verdict set by LABEL alone -- it drops `genuinely-new` and `scope-blind` and prompts if anything at all remains:
  commands/task.md:181    backlog_verdicts=$(... select(.verdict != "genuinely-new" and .verdict != "scope-blind") ...)
  commands/review.md:519  the same jq predicate, verbatim
  commands/errors.md:145  the same rule stated as prose
Nothing in any of the three keys on whether the verdict carries mechanical corroboration. That contradicts the script's own contract: the audit-task-proposals.sh header states Pass 2 is "a recall-biased, over-inclusive SHORTLIST" and that a verdict emitted purely from Pass 2 with no mechanical corroboration "is not a certified fact". The consumers nonetheless treat such a verdict as prompt-worthy, which is a design mismatch that a better-calibrated scorer does not by itself repair.

It also contradicts the consumers' own stated intent. Each site carries the instruction that when the filtered set is empty the prompt must be skipped entirely, because the check "must never add friction to the common genuinely-new case". With a label-only filter over a deliberately lenient threshold, the filtered set is almost never empty, so the escape hatch never fires.

OBSERVED (pre-fix scorer, 2026-11-05): two proposals that were in fact genuinely new drew 56 and 20 non-genuinely-new, non-scope-blind verdicts respectively. Every one had `shared_paths: null` and `mechanical_class: "none"` -- zero mechanical corroboration. Both prompts were overridden on inspection. A gate that is correct to override on essentially every firing trains the override rather than the check.

COMPANION DEFECT, AND THE REASON THE FILTER FIRES ON EVERYTHING: Pass 1 never runs at any call site.
audit-task-proposals.sh accepts `--file-scope JSON_ARRAY` (usage at :94, :130; parsed at :158), and the mechanical file-footprint comparison is the pass the design treats as authoritative corroboration. NO CALLER PASSES IT. Every invocation sends only --title/--description/--id:
  commands/review.md:517
  commands/task.md:179
  commands/errors.md
  skills/skill-fix-it/SKILL.md
  skills/skill-spawn/SKILL.md
  context/workflows/meta-interview.md (two invocations)
With no proposal file_scope supplied, the mechanical pass has nothing to compare, so it can only ever return `mechanical_class: "none"` and `shared_paths: null`. Every verdict the system produces is therefore semantic-only BY CONSTRUCTION, which is also exactly why a corroboration-based filter would currently filter nothing: corroboration is never computed.

Demonstrated: an audit run for a proposal to fix the --depends-on-add not_started gap reported zero corroborated verdicts, even though task 500 (edge_backfill_unreachable_for_not_started) declares precisely scripts/update-task-status.sh and scripts/tests/test-update-task-status.sh -- a dead-on footprint match that Pass 1 would have caught had it been given the proposal's scope. The duplicate was found only by a manual file_scope query. This is the near-duplicate-with-shared-paths failure the script exists to catch, missed because its authoritative pass was never fed.

NOT IN SCOPE: the hook hooks/validate-task-creation-audit.sh is correct as written and must not change. It enforces only that a task-creating state write RECORDED an audit (--audit-verdict / --audit-override), and deliberately does not parse verdicts. The prompting decision is the command layer's, which is where this task operates.

--- REQUIRED OUTCOMES ---

1. Pass the proposal's file_scope to the audit at EVERY call site listed above, so the mechanical pass can actually run. This is a prerequisite for outcomes 2 and 3: a corroboration-based bar is meaningless while corroboration is structurally uncomputable. This outcome is NOT contingent on the re-measurement precondition -- it is a wiring defect that holds regardless of how the semantic scorer is calibrated, and it should be fixed even if every other outcome here is dropped. Where a call site does not yet know the proposal's paths, decide whether to derive them or to record explicitly that the call is semantic-only.

2. Define the prompt-worthiness bar once, in context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md, as a named rule the consumers reference. The bar must distinguish a mechanically corroborated verdict (mechanical_class exact/subset/superset/overlap, or non-null shared_paths) from a Pass-2-only verdict, consistent with the script header's own statement about what each is worth. Decide and record whether a Pass-2-only verdict should prompt at a higher semantic bar, be reported without prompting, or be summarized as a count.

3. Have commands/task.md, commands/review.md, and commands/errors.md reference that single rule instead of each carrying its own copy of the predicate. This mirrors the consolidation already done for topic assignment, where context/patterns/topic-assignment-pattern.md replaced several inline copies; the same duplication is re-forming here across three sites.

4. Preserve the corroborated path's behavior exactly. A verdict with a real file-footprint class must still prompt, and `covered-by-closed` must still name the discharging artifact's path rather than a bare archived task number.

5. Keep the recall-biased posture intact. The point is to change what the CONSUMER does with an over-inclusive shortlist, not to narrow what the audit detects. Do not raise SEMANTIC_THRESHOLD as part of this task; that constant belongs to the scorer task.

6. Reconcile docs/reference/standards/multi-task-creation-standard.md if it restates the label-only filter, so the standard and the pattern doc agree.

STILL UNFILED, deliberately: the size of the audit_verdict blob persisted into state.json (one task's entry added roughly 970 lines / 97 verdict rows pre-fix). It is downstream of the scorer fix in the same way this task is -- measure the post-fix blob before deciding what is worth persisting, and file it then if it still warrants a task.

SOURCE STORE: all edits land in agent-system/extensions/core/ in this repo, never in a deployed .claude/** tree -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md.

---

### 491. Fix the Pass-2 semantic scorer whose normalization inflates against verbose corpus records
- **Effort**: small
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328

**Description**: Fix the Pass-2 semantic scorer in audit-task-proposals.sh, whose normalization inflates scores against verbose corpus records and so fires duplicate-of-open on proposals that share only house boilerplate.

THE DEFECT, VERIFIED (2026-11-05, measured with the script's own tokenizer):
`token_overlap_score($ta; $tb)` (scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh, in JQ_PROGRAM_BODY) computes `$inter / ([$la, $lb] | min)` -- the overlap coefficient normalized by the SMALLER token set. Because corpus records in this repo carry long, structured descriptions, the smaller side is almost always the incoming proposal, so the score becomes "what fraction of MY tokens appear anywhere in that record". A verbose record therefore matches nearly every proposal.

Measured on one real pair (proposal "make generate-task-order.sh read active_goal" vs record 390 migrate_mis_sited_agent_system_tasks):
  proposal unique tokens: 195
  record unique tokens:   933
  shared tokens:          110
  score as implemented (/ min):  0.564  -- clears SEMANTIC_THRESHOLD 0.4, emits duplicate-of-open
  Jaccard (/ union):             0.108  -- far below 0.4
The 110 shared tokens are house style, not subject matter: agent-system, extensions, core, scripts, state, json, specs, write, and similar. The two tasks have no subject overlap and no shared file_scope.

OBSERVED BLAST RADIUS: that proposal drew 56 non-genuinely-new, non-scope-blind verdicts, every one with `shared_paths: null` and `mechanical_class: "none"` (i.e. zero mechanical corroboration). Top semantic matches included consent_gated_git_push (0.63) and guard_latex_builds_against_vimtex_watcher (0.574), neither related to a Goal-line reader. The proposal was in fact genuinely new.

CONSTRAINT FROM THE EXISTING DESIGN: the SEMANTIC_THRESHOLD comment block states the constant is deliberately lenient per the False-Negative Posture in context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md, and that it must be tuned "ONLY after re-measuring both error costs, never to quiet down a noisy run". This task must therefore change the METRIC and re-measure, not merely lower the constant. The recall-biased posture itself is sound and should be preserved: a missed near-duplicate is the expensive error.

--- REQUIRED OUTCOMES ---

1. Replace or correct the normalization so score no longer depends on corpus-record verbosity. Evaluate at least Jaccard (intersection over union) and proposal-side normalization, and state why the chosen metric is right. The asymmetry, not the constant, is the defect.

2. Reduce boilerplate inflation. The current stopword list covers English function words but none of this repo's structural vocabulary. Consider document-frequency weighting across the corpus, or stripping structural scaffolding (section banners such as the REQUIRED OUTCOMES header, path fragments, fenced blocks) before tokenizing. Do not hand-extend the stopword list with repo nouns as the whole fix -- that does not generalize to other deploys.

3. Re-measure SEMANTIC_THRESHOLD against the new metric and record both error costs, per the existing comment block's own instruction. Update the comment block and the False-Negative Posture section of context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md (section at :104) so the stated rationale matches the implemented metric.

4. Regression coverage in scripts/tests/test-audit-task-proposals.sh: a proposal paired with a long, unrelated corpus record that shares only structural vocabulary must NOT clear the threshold. Build the fixture from the measured pair above so the test fails against the current scorer.

5. Preserve the mechanically-corroborated path exactly. Verdicts backed by a real file-footprint class (exact/subset/superset/overlap) must keep firing regardless of semantic score; only Pass-2-only verdicts are in scope for this change.

OUT OF SCOPE (deliberately, decided at creation):

1. The consumer-side confirm contract -- the label-only verdict filter in commands/task.md, commands/review.md and commands/errors.md, which prompts on any non-genuinely-new verdict regardless of corroboration. Now filed as its own task (492), which depends on THIS task and carries a precondition to re-measure against the corrected scorer before implementing. If the scorer fix here drops verdict volume enough that the prompt stops misfiring, that task shrinks to a documentation change.

2. The size of the audit_verdict blob persisted into state.json -- one task entry added roughly 970 lines / 97 verdict rows under the current scorer. Still unfiled on purpose: it is downstream of this fix, so measure the post-fix blob before deciding what is worth persisting, and file it then if it still warrants a task.

SOURCE STORE: all edits land in agent-system/extensions/core/ in this repo, never in a deployed .claude/** tree -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md.

### 490. Make generate-task-order.sh read active_goal so the field stops being a dead write
- **Effort**: small
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328

**Description**: Make generate-task-order.sh read `active_goal` from state.json so the field stops being a dead write: it is declared, documented, validated, and written, but no consumer ever reads it.

THE GAP, VERIFIED (2026-11-05):
- Declared: context/schemas/state-schema.json:164 defines `active_goal`.
- Documented: context/reference/state-management-schema.md:77 describes it as a free-prose statement of the repository's current goal.
- Validated: scripts/validate-state.sh:858 lists `active_goal` among recognized widened fields, so writing it passes validation cleanly.
- Written: commands/review.md:734-740 (Section 6.7.3) writes `.active_goal` via `state-write.sh ... --regen-todo`, explicitly so the TODO.md regeneration happens inside the same mutex as the write that triggered it.
- NEVER READ: scripts/generate-task-order.sh resolves the Goal line only from (a) an explicit `--goal TEXT` override (GOAL_OVERRIDE, lines ~92, 110-112, 1040-1042) or (b) read_existing_goal() (lines ~909-931), which greps the existing `**Goal**: ` line back out of TODO.md. `grep -n active_goal scripts/generate-task-order.sh` returns nothing.

REPRODUCED: in a consumer repo, writing active_goal via `state-write.sh ... --regen-todo` succeeded (state.json updated) but TODO.md was left byte-identical with no Goal line, because TODO.md had no prior Goal line for read_existing_goal() to recover. The line rendered only after manually passing `--goal "$(jq -r .active_goal specs/state.json)"` to `generate-task-order.sh --update-todo`. Any caller that sets active_goal expecting it to render hits the same silent no-op; /review Section 6.7.3 is the one in-tree caller that does.

--- REQUIRED OUTCOMES ---

1. generate-task-order.sh resolves the Goal line from a defined precedence: explicit `--goal` override first, then state.json `active_goal`, then the existing TODO.md Goal line (current read_existing_goal behavior), then omit the line entirely. The existing `--goal` and preserve-across-regeneration behaviors must not regress.

2. Decide and record the conflict rule between a state.json `active_goal` and a hand-edited TODO.md Goal line. The preserve-from-TODO behavior exists so regeneration does not clobber a human edit; giving active_goal higher precedence means a stale active_goal silently overwrites hand edits. State the chosen rule and its rationale rather than leaving it implicit in the code.

3. context/formats/task-order-format.md documents the full precedence. Its Goal Statement section currently documents only the preserve-from-TODO and `--goal` behaviors; the Generation and Generation Algorithm sections, which enumerate what the script reads, also need the new source listed.

4. A regression case in scripts/tests/test-generate-task-order.sh: with `active_goal` set in state.json and no Goal line present in TODO.md, `--update-todo` must emit the Goal line. That is the exact condition which silently no-ops today, so the test must fail against the current script.

5. Confirm whether commands/review.md Section 6.7.3 needs any change once the reader exists. Its `--regen-todo` rationale becomes accurate rather than aspirational, so a comment or no change may suffice. Do not widen scope to other callers unless they are found to write active_goal.

SOURCE STORE: all edits land in agent-system/extensions/core/ in this repo, never in a deployed .claude/** tree -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md.

- **Effort**: small

- **Task Type**: meta

- **Research**: [488_mcp_write_tool_grant_ownership_scope/reports/01_mcp-ownership-scope-verdict.md]

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout 600000). Re-derive every quoted clause from the source store, never from a deployed copy. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** .

FINDING (recorded during the sapience extension build, issues.jsonl class scope-mismatch, severity minor): the sapience extension's settings-fragment.json grants four MCP write tools (sapience_remember, sapience_wrapup, sapience_handoff, sapience_adddoc) at THIS repository's project scope. That closes the autonomous-dispatch permission blocker inside this repo and is correct for that purpose. But context/standards/mcp-server-ownership.md's own rule states that permission grants for a given MCP server belong with the entity that OWNS the server registration -- here ~/.dotfiles/config/claude/settings.json, whose keys are reset from the dotfiles repo by home.activation.claudeSettings on every home-manager switch. The two placements are in tension and the tension is currently unresolved rather than decided.

Note the enforcement asymmetry that makes this non-obvious: the dotfiles copy is machine-wide and nix-enforced (so an extension CANNOT durably write it -- an edit there would be reverted on the next switch), while the repo-scoped fragment is the only placement an extension can actually own. A verdict of 'the repo-scoped fragment is correct' is therefore legitimate; it just has to be STATED rather than left as an apparent standards violation.

DECIDE, do not assume: (a) whether mcp-server-ownership.md's rule should be amended to carve out the nix-enforced-settings case explicitly, or whether the sapience grant should move to ~/.dotfiles and the extension stop granting it; (b) whether a repo-scoped write-tool grant for a server the repo does not own needs any additional operator-visible disclosure. Reason about the security posture of the narrower-scope placement on its own merits, not only about which file the standard currently names.

ACCEPTANCE: mcp-server-ownership.md states a verdict covering the nix-enforced-settings case; the sapience extension's grant placement matches that verdict; verify-deploy.sh gate set green after redeploy.

### 487. Stop the identical-dispatch convergence guard halting a task whose previous cycle closed a phase
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: orchestrator
- **Dependencies**: Task 449

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over task 328, session sess_1793890953_7cb51f. User-approved at the lead level.

SEVERITY: costly. OBSERVED FIRST-HAND. There is NO issues.jsonl citation for this record -- the orchestrate lead's own observation during the originating run, recorded verbatim below, IS the evidence. Do not go looking for a citation that does not exist.

LIVE SYMPTOM: the identical-dispatch convergence guard halts a task on cycle 2 even when cycle 1 closed a real plan phase, capping every /orchestrate invocation at exactly one phase of progress.

EVIDENCE (session sess_1793890953_7cb51f, task 328):
- Cycle 1 dispatched general-implementation-agent for task 328 at dispatch_seq 22. It closed Phase 10 of specs/328_systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency/plans/03_markdown-corpus-consolidation.md, advancing that plan 9/20 -> 10/20 phases, committed as 7a47a6a57 and f41aecf54.
- Cycle 2's orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh then composed dispatch 24, found its content byte-identical to dispatch 22's, reported `IDENTICAL DISPATCH ... (streak=2)`, and issued `IDENTICAL DISPATCH HALT: task #328 ... halting this task for the rest of the run`.
- The guard's own stated premise -- that re-issuing an identical dispatch means no progress is being made -- was therefore FALSE here: progress was made AND committed between the two compositions.

ROOT CAUSE, VERIFIED IN THE SOURCE STORE (re-derive before editing; line numbers move):
- The composed dispatch body's ONLY phase-progress interpolation is the `PHASES COMPLETED: <n> of <m>` line inside orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh's `## Phase Mission` section (around line 617), and that entire section is gated on `[ -n "$phase_number" ]` (around line 604).
- `--phase-number` is set by orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh at ONE site only (around line 2633): `[ -n "${h1_next_phase[$t]:-}" ] && build_args+=(--phase-number "${h1_next_phase[$t]}")`, whose own comment states it is "only ever set for a hard-mode implement candidate".
- Therefore an ordinary (non-hard-mode) implement dispatch carries NO phase-progress content whatsoever. Two consecutive cycles for the same task, phase and plan hash identically no matter how many phases landed in between.
- cycle_plan_dispatch_hash() (orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh, around line 2160 onward) strips exactly three per-cycle-varying lines before hashing: `- dispatch_seq: N`, `- dispatch_start_ts: N`, and the Issue Log's `--what-happened "..." [--phase P] [--dispatch-seq N] \` boilerplate line. Its own header comment asserts that "a real behavioral change to the dispatch (a different phase_number, a different territory payload, a different focus string) changes some OTHER line and therefore still changes the digest". For a non-hard-mode implement dispatch that assertion does not hold: closing a phase changes no line of the body. Correcting that comment is part of this work, not an afterthought.

SCOPE -- decide and implement, with the reasoning recorded. The three candidate directions below are the originating lead's own framing and are NOT a prescription; they are not mutually exclusive:
(a) Include phase progress in the dispatch content the guard hashes -- emit the completed-phase count, or the next-unstarted-phase identity, into the dispatch body for every implement dispatch rather than only a hard-mode one. Note the STANDING OBLIGATION recorded in cycle_plan_dispatch_hash()'s own header: any new per-cycle-varying content added to a composed dispatch file MUST be reconciled with the strip pattern in the SAME change, or the halt silently goes inert. Phase progress is per-PROGRESS-varying, not per-cycle-varying, so it must NOT be stripped -- state that distinction explicitly wherever it is added, as the existing DECIDED AND RECORDED rulings in that header do.
(b) Have the guard consult an independent progress signal before halting -- the committed phase count, or `phases_completed` from the handoff file, which orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh already reads (around lines 322-331). A streak would then be charged only when the hash matches AND no phase advanced. Compare with the deploy_pending streak-FREEZE backstop immediately above the halt block: that is the established in-codebase precedent for "do not charge churn when an independent signal explains the repetition", and it freezes rather than suppresses, so a pre-existing genuine streak survives the interruption intact.
(c) Both.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- The guard MUST still fire for genuine non-convergence. A task that truly re-issues the same dispatch with nothing landing must still halt; this record must not neuter the guard.
- Preserve the deploy_pending streak-freeze semantics and the hash-degradation posture (sha256sum unavailable, or the just-written dispatch file unreadable, leaves the streak at 0 and disables the guard for the run with a named stderr notice -- never fatal, never a silent skip).
- Preserve the two DECIDED AND RECORDED non-strip rulings in cycle_plan_dispatch_hash()'s header (the `## Batch Gate Deferral` and `## Outstanding Gate Finding` sections stay unstripped), or restate them with fresh justification if the chosen mechanism genuinely requires revisiting them.
- The halt's existing clean back-out must survive intact: pre-dispatch status restore via state-write.sh, task-lock release, and no budget charge, no --flush-seq, no pending_dispatch record, no dispatch row for the halted task.

VERIFY: a regression test MUST pin that a cycle which closed a phase is NOT halted by this guard, and a companion fixture MUST pin that a cycle which closed nothing still IS halted. Site both alongside the existing coverage in agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh, whose "Group 27" fixtures already own this guard -- its Case F pins the strip-pattern-drift failure mode that once left the halt inert, and is the model to follow.

COST OF LEAVING IT: every /orchestrate N over a multi-phase plan yields at most one phase of progress. The plan cited above alone has 10 phases remaining -- ten separate invocations instead of progress within one run.

TERRITORY EDGES -- orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh and orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh are SHARED FILE TERRITORY with several open records, so an edge is declared rather than racing them (context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md):
- hoist_batch_gate_deferral_directive: its option (1) re-emits `## Batch Gate Deferral` at a different position in the SAME composed dispatch body this record adds content to, and the hash normalizer carries an explicit DECIDED AND RECORDED ruling about that very section. Dependency edge declared: these two must be ordered, not raced.
- Also writing one or both of these two scripts; no ordering declared here, but re-check at admission: warn_plan_size_exceeds_cycle_budget, validate_settled_route_before_dispatch, advise_on_dirty_file_scope_at_admission, validate_carried_unverified_names_a_source, snapshot_lean_challenge_at_plan_time.

### 486. Stop a required extension manifest.json registration being dropped from a phase commit as a scope excursion
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: file-scope-lifecycle
- **Dependencies**: Task 430, Task 446, Task 484, Task 494

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over task 328, session sess_1793890953_7cb51f. User-approved at the lead level.

SEVERITY: costly. RECURRING -- three separate firings within one task.

CITATIONS -- specs/328_systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency/issues.jsonl:
  iss_1793841635583_gpNhE6 (dispatch_seq 18, Phase 7 -- 47 of 48 migrated test files dropped)
  iss_1793847274112_byw6XR (dispatch_seq 20, Phase 8 -- 51 touched paths dropped)
  iss_1793848518784_26KAZz (dispatch_seq 21, Phase 9 -- 2 paths dropped, one of them manifest.json)

LIVE SYMPTOM: git-commit-phase.sh's Phase-Commit Containment Self-Check drops a legitimately-required manifest.json registration from a phase-closing commit. A phase that extracts a new shared shell library into agent-system/extensions/<ext>/scripts/lib/ must ALSO register that lib in that extension's manifest.json provides.scripts -- scripts/tests/test-manifest-scripts-sync.sh enforces that pairing mechanically. But the containment predicate reads the manifest.json path as a file_scope excursion and drops it, so the phase closes with an incomplete commit and has to pay a separate labeled follow-up commit to recover. Observed in Phase 9 (the install-extension / uninstall-extension pair) and again, identically, in Phase 10 (the typst pair).

WHAT IS ALREADY FIXED -- DO NOT RE-FIX IT (verified in the source store while creating this record; re-derive before editing, line numbers move):
- All three cited entries attribute the drop to a DOUBLE-SLASH glob defect: a trailing-slash file_scope directory entry composing "${entry}/*" into "...tests//*", which never matches a real single-slash child path. THAT DEFECT IS CLOSED. agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/git-commit-phase.sh's is_contained() (around lines 272-305) normalizes a trailing-slash entry down to a bare directory via a `while [ "${entry%/}" != "$entry" ]` loop before composing any glob, and its own comment documents exactly the failure mode those citations describe.
- An engine-written-paths allowlist is also in place: is_contained()'s file_scope array is pre-seeded from lib/engine-written-state-paths-lib.sh, with a hardcoded ("specs/TODO.md", "specs/state.json") pair as the fallback on a source failure.
The citations are therefore retained as the evidence of RECURRENCE and of cost, NOT as a live description of the mechanism. Read them for the cost account, not for the diagnosis.

THE ACTUAL OPEN QUESTION -- a manifest.json registration is a STRUCTURALLY DERIVABLE obligation, not an unforeseeable newly-discovered path. When a phase adds a file under agent-system/extensions/<ext>/scripts/, that extension's own manifest.json registration is required BY A TEST, in the same change. Decide and implement ONE coherent rule, with the reasoning recorded:
(1) Make the containment predicate treat the touched extension's own manifest.json as implicitly in scope whenever the same phase adds or removes a file under that same extension's scripts/ tree. Narrow, mechanically derivable, and prevents the FIRST drop rather than only the repeat. Weigh against: it widens declared territory without review, and file_scope is also the batch admission gate's conflict-detection input and the territory contract between concurrently dispatched tasks.
(2) Surface the obligation as a PRE-COMMIT requirement instead of an implicit widening -- a phase that adds a script under an extension without also touching that extension's manifest.json is told so before the commit fires, leaving the declaration honest and the operator in the loop.
(3) Neither, on the grounding that the general declaration-staleness feedback mechanism (see the territory edges below) subsumes this case once it lands. If this is the ruling, record it explicitly and close this record rather than leaving it open.

NOTE ON THE TASK-LOCAL WORKAROUND: the symptom was worked around for one task alone by widening that task's own declared file_scope (commit 059e41377, which added agent-system/extensions/core/manifest.json and agent-system/extensions/typst/manifest.json to it). That workaround is per-task and does not generalize -- the next task that extracts a lib under a different extension hits the same drop with no declaration covering it. Do not mistake the widened file_scope for a fix.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT weaken the containment predicate for a genuine excursion. Drop-and-warn for an out-of-territory source edit is correct and must survive intact, pinned by a test.
- Do NOT make the check fatal. Fail-open on a missing/empty/malformed file_scope, and never refusing a whole commit over this guard, are both deliberate and must stay.
- Any state.json write must go through the mutex-guarded state-write.sh path, never a second writer.
- Do NOT re-introduce the trailing-slash normalization or the engine-written-paths allowlist; both are already present and must be left working.

VERIFY: extend agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-git-commit-phase.sh with fixtures pinning (a) the chosen rule's behaviour for a phase that adds a new script under an extension plus that extension's manifest.json, (b) a genuinely out-of-territory path STILL dropped with its warning and its issue-record.sh call, (c) fail-open behaviour on absent/empty/malformed file_scope unchanged, and (d) the trailing-slash normalization still working.

TERRITORY EDGES -- git-commit-phase.sh is SHARED FILE TERRITORY with three open records, so dependency edges are declared rather than racing them (context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md: shared territory is serialized with an edge, never a bare exclusion):
- feed_undeclared_implement_paths_into_file_scope: the general form of this exact problem -- feeding observed-but-undeclared implement paths back into the declaration. If its general mechanism lands first, THIS record may reduce to option (3) above, or to a narrow prevention rule layered on top. Read its description before scoping; it already carries a verified account of git-commit-phase.sh's drop site and of the proposed_file_scope channel.
- close_the_plan_to_file_scope_reconciliation_gap: owns the plan-to-file_scope harvest path (plan-file-scope-harvest.sh, skill-base.sh) and makes a containment drop non-advisory. A manifest.json registration is exactly the kind of path a plan's "Files to modify" block may or may not name, so the two interact directly.
- collapse_perpath_scope_excursion_issue_rows_into_one: rewrites the per-path issue-record.sh emission inside the very loop this record changes.

### 484. Collapse per-path scope excursion issue rows into one record per drop event
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: file-scope-lifecycle
- **Dependencies**: Task 430, Task 446, Task 469

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system change request) of an /orchestrate invocation, session sess_1793838084_ef69bd. User-approved at the lead level.

=== THE PROBLEM: THE CLASS DESTROYS ITS OWN AGGREGATE SIGNAL ===

123 entries of class "scope excursion" were aggregated from a single /orchestrate invocation -- by far the largest class in the run, more than five times every other class in that aggregate combined. CITATIONS: {task 429, iss_1793840234975_Bu5d3D}, plus 122 entries on task 328 spanning iss_1792181960729_J5c0JK through iss_1793848445269_5W27NH.

At that volume no operator reads the class, and a genuine excursion is indistinguishable from the noise. That is a defect in the RECORDING mechanism, independent of whether any individual drop was correct.

=== MECHANISM, VERIFIED IN THE SOURCE STORE DURING TASK CREATION (re-derive before editing; line numbers move) ===

The class has exactly TWO emitters, and BOTH call issue-record.sh once PER DROPPED PATH from inside a per-path loop:

  1. scripts/git-commit-phase.sh, around line 286, inside `for f in "${files_touched[@]}"` --
     one warn + one issue-record.sh call per uncontained path.
  2. scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh, around line 655, inside the per-pathspec V10 loop --
     same shape, textually distinct message.

Consequence: ONE drop event over a phase that legitimately touched twenty migrated test suites writes TWENTY near-identical issue rows that differ only in the quoted path. The 122 entries on task 328 are not 122 independent incidents; they are a small number of multi-file drop events, fanned out one row per path. A third site, orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh's WORK (h)/(i) "EXCURSION DROPPED" filter, prints per-path notices to stderr but does NOT call issue-record.sh -- confirm whether that asymmetry is deliberate before changing it.

=== REQUIRED OUTCOMES ===

1. A single drop event produces a single issue-log record that enumerates the dropped paths, rather than N records each naming one path. Decide the representation (a paths array in the record, a count plus a bounded sample, or grouping at aggregate time) -- research/plan owns the verdict. Do NOT assume the answer, and do NOT suppress information: the full set of dropped paths must remain recoverable from the record, per issue-log.md's no-suppression posture.

2. Decide whether the collapse belongs at the emitter (git-commit-phase.sh and git-commit-scoped.sh each build one record before writing), in issue-record.sh itself (a batch/multi-path call shape), or at read time in issue-grouping.sh / orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh. Note that a read-time-only fix leaves the raw log just as large; an emitter-only fix duplicates the new call shape at two sites. Justify the choice against both.

3. The scope-excursion severity stays `minor` and the posture stays drop-and-warn, never refuse. This task changes how a drop is RECORDED, not whether a drop happens and not what is dropped.

=== EXPLICIT NON-GOALS -- these are owned by existing open tasks, do not re-do or pre-empt them ===

- Whether the containment predicate is too eager, or a task's declared file_scope is too narrow, and the feedback loop that would widen file_scope from observed implement paths: owned by #430 and #446. This task takes NO position on the calibration verdict.
- Allowlisting engine-written or registry infrastructure paths at the three Containment sites: owned by #469, whose own required outcome 2 already asks which further paths belong on the list. One concrete case observed in this run belongs to that task, not this one: the postflight EXCURSION DROPPED path left agent-system/extensions/core/manifest.json uncommitted as an excursion, when registering a newly added script in its extension manifest is the ordinary, expected consequence of adding one. Carry that observation to #469 as evidence; do not fix it here.
- Class-vocabulary normalization and the aggregate's malformed repeated-flag call: owned by #477.

=== SERIALIZATION NOTE ===

file_scope here overlaps #430, #446 and #469 on git-commit-phase.sh, git-commit-scoped.sh and their suites. Those tasks edit the containment PREDICATE and its allowlists; this one edits the RECORDING call beside it. The overlap is real and the work should be serialized behind them rather than run concurrently -- landing the calibration fixes first also shrinks the drop volume this task is measured against.

=== VERIFICATION ===

- test-git-commit-phase.sh and test-git-commit-scoped.sh cover the new record shape: a multi-path drop asserts exactly one issue row naming every dropped path.
- A repo-wide check that no other site emits class "scope excursion" per-path.
- Existing scope-excursion rows in specs/**/issues.jsonl remain parseable by issue-grouping.sh and orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh after the change; a schema change must not strand the existing corpus.

### 483. Document that an extension lifecycle hook can never fire without a declared task_type
- **Status**: [PLANNED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [483_document_hook_task_type_prerequisite/reports/01_hook-task-type-prerequisite.md]
- **Plan**: [483_document_hook_task_type_prerequisite/plans/01_hook-task-type-prerequisite.md]

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: `agent-system/extensions/core/` (never `.claude/**`, a disposable deploy artifact regenerated by deploy-headless.sh -- see `rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md`). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS. No task-number references in any file landing under `agent-system/**` (`rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`); they are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

## Goal

Document, in the extension-development guidance, that an extension lifecycle hook declared in `manifest.json`'s top-level `hooks` object can NEVER fire for an extension that declares no `task_type`. This is a DOCUMENTATION task only. Do not change the hook-resolution mechanism; no caller has asked for a new cross-cutting seam, and adding one would be a separate design decision.

## The verified mechanism (independently re-derived twice from source, not inferred)

- `skill_get_extension_dir()` in `agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/skill-base.sh` (the gate is at the `ext_task_type` comparison, currently line 178) gates on `[ -n "$ext_task_type" ] && [ "$ext_task_type" = "$task_type" ]` and returns the FIRST matching extension. A manifest with no `task_type` fails the `-n` test unconditionally, so its extension directory is never resolved.
- All four `skill_run_extension_hook` call sites in the same file (preflight, context_injection, verification, postflight -- currently lines 478, 541, 761, 1146) pass the CURRENT dispatched task's own `task_type`.
- Consequence: the `nvim` and `nix` `context_injection` hooks work ONLY because each extension declares its own matching `task_type`, and each therefore fires only on its own domain's tasks. The mechanism is per-domain, never cross-cutting.
- There is therefore NO extension-declarable seam for cross-cutting, task-type-independent pre-dispatch context injection. The one real instance of that shape -- the `<memory-context>` block -- is a hardcoded `memory-retrieve.sh` call inside core's own `orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh`, not an extension hook. Replicating that shape is a CORE change, not an extension change.
- Post-task observers are also the wrong tool for pre-work orientation: they fire only after a task reaches a resting state.

## Why this is worth documenting

A task description in this repo asserted the opposite premise outright: it instructed an implementer to use `hooks.context_injection` for a cross-cutting purpose and cited the nvim and nix extensions as precedent ("the mechanism already exercised by the nix and nvim extensions"). That premise is false for any extension forbidden to declare a `task_type`. It cost a full research round to refute and forced a planned deliverable (`scripts/sapience-context.sh`) to be dropped as dead code before it shipped.

Issue-log evidence, from `specs/482_create_sapience_extension/issues.jsonl`:
- task 482, entry_id `iss_1793844574027_V05zIp` -- class `dispatch-premise-incorrect`, severity `costly`: the gap-3 dispatch guidance is structurally unreachable.
- task 482, entry_id `iss_1793845054770_eL3BT2` -- class `scope-deviation`, severity `minor`: the plan drops the dispatch-named deliverable after independently re-verifying the refutation.

## Current state of the guidance (verified, do not re-derive from scratch)

Two files need coordinated edits; neither currently states the constraint:

1. `agent-system/extensions/core/docs/guides/creating-extensions.md`
   - Its `## Lifecycle Hooks` section (currently ~line 688) documents the `hooks` schema, the hooks-vs-`provides.hooks` distinction, basename path resolution, the 5 positional arguments, and the non-blocking exit-code contract. It says hooks are "per-extension, not per-repo" but never states that a declared `task_type` is a PREREQUISITE for any hook to run at all.
   - Its `### Observers vs. Lifecycle Hooks` section (currently ~line 880) DOES already state that lifecycle hooks are "resolved by manifest `task_type` string equality against a single-valued top-level `task_type` field". That statement is correct but is framed as a comparison against the observer seam and scoped to why hooks cannot serve a TOPIC-GROUPED seam. It never states the stronger and more commonly needed consequence: no `task_type` at all means no hook ever fires.
2. `agent-system/extensions/core/context/guides/extension-development.md`
   - Its manifest field table (currently ~line 79) documents `task_type` as optional: "omit for resource-only extensions".
   - Its resource-only-extension prose (currently ~line 256) tells authors outright that extensions providing only shared resources "can omit `task_type` entirely", with no warning that a `hooks` declaration in such a manifest is dead on arrival.

This partial, non-load-bearing coverage is precisely what let the false premise look plausible: an author reading the Hook Schema section, or reading the resource-only-extension permission, finds nothing that contradicts it.

## Acceptance criteria

1. `creating-extensions.md`'s `## Lifecycle Hooks` section states the prerequisite explicitly and prominently (not buried in the observer comparison): a lifecycle hook fires only for a task whose `task_type` string-equals the declaring extension's own top-level `task_type`; an extension with no `task_type` has no firing path for any of its four hooks.
2. It names `skill_get_extension_dir()` in `scripts/skill-base.sh` and quotes the exact gate expression as the mechanical source of truth, so a future reader can verify the claim against source rather than trusting the prose.
3. It states that nvim and nix are PER-DOMAIN precedents, not cross-cutting ones -- each fires only on its own domain's tasks -- so that citing them as precedent for a cross-cutting hook is visibly wrong.
4. It records that the only real cross-cutting pre-dispatch injection in the system is the hardcoded `memory-retrieve.sh` / `<memory-context>` call in `scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh`, and that replicating that shape is a core change, not an extension change. It states plainly that no extension-declarable cross-cutting seam exists.
5. It notes that post-task observers do not solve pre-work orientation either, since they fire only after a task reaches a resting state. Keep this brief and cross-reference the existing observer sections rather than duplicating them.
6. `extension-development.md`'s resource-only-extension prose (and/or its `task_type` field-table row) carries a pointer warning that omitting `task_type` also forfeits all lifecycle hooks, linking to the `creating-extensions.md` section above.
7. No duplication of the existing `### Observers vs. Lifecycle Hooks` content -- extend or cross-reference it; do not fork a second account of the same resolution rule that can drift.
8. Redeploy so the statement reaches `.claude/`, and confirm the deployed copies match the source store.

## Scope boundaries

- Documentation only. `skill-base.sh` is READ for verification and is NOT edited.
- Do not add, propose, or design a new cross-cutting hook seam.
- Do not change the manifest schema, the hook resolution order, or the non-blocking exit-code contract.

## Backlog-reconciliation ruling (recorded at creation)

The creation-time audit returned a recall-biased shortlist; the full verdict document is recorded on this entry's `audit_verdict` field. Two candidates had mechanical corroboration and were ruled on explicitly:

- #327 `repair_extension_lifecycle_hook_mechanism` (closed, `covered-by-closed`, semantic 0.70, mechanical `overlap`): NOT covering. That task repaired the resolver, the return-code channel, and the uninvoked verification stage, and it authored the `## Lifecycle Hooks` and `### Observers vs. Lifecycle Hooks` prose that exists today. The specific statement this task adds -- that a missing `task_type` means no hook can ever fire, and that no cross-cutting seam exists -- is absent from that prose, as verified by reading the current source file. This is additive content in a file a closed task previously edited, not a redo of it.
- #328 `systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency` (open, `subset-of-open`, mechanical `subset`): shares both target files only because its `file_scope` includes the broad `agent-system/extensions/core/docs/` and `.../context/` directory prefixes. Its acceptance is net corpus-size reduction driven by `script-inventory.sh` ranking, a different subject. Overlap is directory-prefix breadth, not subject overlap. Sequence after #328 if a conflict materializes; no dependency edge is asserted now.
- #29 `generate_mcp_json_from_extension_manifests` (open, mechanical `overlap` on `extension-development.md`): deploy-engine and MCP-registration work on the same file, unrelated subject. No edge.

Every other `duplicate-of-open` verdict in the document carries `mechanical_class: "none"` with semantic scores at or below 0.58 -- Pass-2-only shortlist noise, which `audit-task-proposals.sh` documents as a non-certified disposition for a caller to rule on. Ruled genuinely-new.

### 481. Rule on and migrate the 32-record mis-sited agent-system task delta accumulated since the first migration record
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 390

**Description**: GOAL. The mis-sited-agent-system-task migration owned by nvim #390 covers EIGHT BimodalLogic records. Thirty-two more non-terminal `task_type: meta` records have accumulated in BimodalLogic's task space since #390 was authored on 2026-10-11, all filing work against the shared source store under ~/.config/nvim/agent-system/**. This record rules on each of the 32 and then migrates or retires them accordingly.

READ #390 IN FULL BEFORE ACTING, AND REUSE ITS CONSTRAINTS BY REFERENCE RATHER THAN RESTATING THEM. #390 carries nine numbered constraints that are the authored contract for HOW a record is moved: (1) renumbering, not copying, since the two repositories keep independent numbering spaces; (2) descriptions preserved verbatim including every iss_* citation id; (3) citations must stay resolvable cross-repo; (4) terminal disposition for each vacated BimodalLogic entry, each with a durable pointer to where the work went; (5) measured git-trackability of the vacated directories; (6) deriving a file_scope for records that declare none; (7) rewriting each file_scope to source-store paths only, which is NOT a defect repair; (8) resolving a dangling dependency edge to an archived task; (9) supplying titles where they are null. Do not re-derive or duplicate these. Where this record needs them, cite them as the predecessor record's numbered constraints.

#390'S OWN MEASUREMENTS ARE STALE AND MUST NOT BE INHERITED. Its task-directory emptiness readings, file_scope contents and record counts were taken 2026-10-11, nearly a month before this record was filed. Re-measure everything this record acts on.

=== THE 32 RECORDS, AND WHY THEY SPLIT FOUR WAYS ===

Measured 2026-11-04 against BimodalLogic's specs/state.json; re-derive every figure before acting. #390 names 767, 768, 769, 770, 730, 741, 752 to move plus 753 as transplant-then-abandon. The remaining 32 non-terminal `meta` records are: 729, 751, 772, 774, 777, 779, 780, 783, 784, 785, 810, 813, 814, 816, 822, 823, 824, 825, 826, 827, 834, 835, 839, 840, 841, 842, 843, 845, 847, 848, 857, 858.

They do not take one uniform treatment. Classified by declared file_scope:

GROUP A -- 19 declare ONLY agent-system/** source-store paths and are unambiguously mis-sited: 785, 810, 813, 822, 823, 824, 825, 826, 827, 834, 835, 839, 840, 841, 842, 843, 845, 857, 858. These are the straightforward moves under #390's constraints.

GROUP B -- 5 are MIXED, declaring both agent-system/** and BimodalLogic-local paths: 772, 777, 783, 814, 816. A mixed record cannot simply move. Either its BimodalLogic-local obligations are split into a separate local record, or the migrated record must state how a cross-repo file_scope is declared at all. That second option is the open question nvim #401 cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness already owns, so CROSS-REFERENCE #401 AND DO NOT RE-DECIDE IT HERE; if the ruling for a mixed record turns on #401's unanswered question, say so and leave that record pending rather than forcing a disposition.

GROUP C -- 3 declare only BimodalLogic-local or .claude/** paths: 751, 774, 784. These are candidates to STAY. But a .claude/** path is a deploy-artifact path whose real edit target is the source store, so each record's declared scope must be re-read before its siting is settled; a .claude/** declaration is weak evidence of local siting, not proof of it.

GROUP D -- 5 declare NO file_scope at all: 729, 779, 780, 847, 848. This is exactly the condition #390's constraint (6) handles for its own two no-scope records, so reuse that constraint's derive-a-file_scope procedure rather than inventing a second one.

Verify the exact membership of every group against the tree; the four counts above sum to 32 but the classification was made from declared file_scope alone and a re-read may move a record between groups.

=== PHASE 1 IS THE RULING PASS, AND IT GATES EVERYTHING ELSE ===

The per-record covered-versus-not ruling is this record's first phase and no record may be moved, folded or abandoned before it is done. Each of the 32 gets a STATED DISPOSITION with its grounds: `move` (no nvim record covers it), `fold-into-#NNN` (an open nvim record covers it; transplant anything the absorbing record lacks first, per the pattern #390 already uses for its own transplant-then-abandon case), or `abandon-as-covered` (a closed nvim record already covered it). A BULK VERDICT OVER A GROUP IS NOT ACCEPTABLE OUTPUT -- the ruling is per record, by name, each with its grounds.

THE EVIDENCE AVAILABLE, AND ITS MEASURED LIMITS. audit-task-proposals.sh was run over all 40 BimodalLogic meta records against nvim's full corpus (115 active including 44 completed, plus 332 archived). The mechanical Pass-1 file_scope-overlap output was written to a session-scoped scratchpad path that will be gone by the time this record runs, so re-run the audit rather than depending on it. Two limits are load-bearing and must shape how the ruling pass uses the tool:

  (a) MECHANICAL OVERLAP IS NOT COVERAGE. Every one of the 28 source-store-scoped records overlaps some open nvim record, but nvim #328 systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency and #435 reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class appear in nearly every shortlist because they are broad-territory sweep records. An overlap verdict naming only a sweep record is not evidence of coverage.

  (b) THE SEMANTIC PASS IS NOISE AT THIS CORPUS SIZE. One proposal drew 110 verdicts. Per context/patterns/backlog-reconciliation.md the script never decides duplication and Pass 2 is an explicitly recall-biased shortlist; at this corpus size it shortlists nearly the whole backlog. The ruling must be made by reading the candidate records, using the shortlist only to bound what gets read.

TWO TOOLING CONSTRAINTS THE RULING PASS MUST RESPECT, BOTH MEASURED. audit-task-proposals.sh dies with `jq: Argument list too long` on batches above roughly two proposals, because these descriptions are very large -- that failure is nvim #858 state_write_audit_verdict_argv_overflow, ALREADY OPEN, so cross-reference it and chunk the audit rather than treating it as a new defect to fix here. And truncating descriptions to fit degrades Pass-2 recall, so truncation is NOT a sound workaround for a ruling pass whose false-abandonment cost is losing a real record: truncation was used only to obtain the mechanical overlap figures cited above, never for a disposition.

=== RECONCILE THE LOCAL COUNTERPART, DO NOT DUPLICATE IT ===

BimodalLogic #784 retire_migrated_source_store_bound_records is status BLOCKED and is the retire-side counterpart on the BimodalLogic side. It sits in Group C of the 32 above, so it is simultaneously a record this task rules on AND a record whose own subject matter overlaps #390's constraint (4) on terminal dispositions for vacated entries. Reconcile the two rather than migrating #784 blindly or duplicating its content; if #784's blocker is what prevents its own disposition, record that and leave it blocked.

=== ADJACENT RECORDS TO CROSS-REFERENCE WITHOUT ABSORBING ===

nvim #391 agent_system_task_siting_and_target_root owns the authoring hole that files these records into the wrong repository in the first place. THIS RECORD IS REMEDIATION, NOT PREVENTION, AND MUST SAY SO: it clears an accumulated backlog and does nothing to stop the next one, which is #391's job. nvim #401 owns cross-repo overlap and dependency blindness (see Group B above). nvim #457 repo_specific_content_in_shared_source_store owns the converse defect -- repo-specific content leaking INTO the shared store -- and is the record that an /orchestrate conclusion-stage dispatch recently discovered had contradicted its own siting assumption.

=== PROVENANCE ===

Raised by the repository owner immediately after an /orchestrate conclusion-stage channel-(b) dispatch folded five proposals into four existing nvim records (#452, #455, #360, #450) and reported that its own siting assumption had been contradicted by #457. The cross-repo siting confusion is therefore recurring in live runs rather than being a historical artifact, which is the argument for ruling on the delta now rather than letting it grow a third time.

=== DEPENDENCY NOTE ===

dependencies: [390] is a user ruling recorded for shared-file-territory serialization: both this record and #390 write ~/.config/nvim/specs/state.json, and the edge serializes them rather than racing a concurrent writer on one file. It carries NO claim that this record needs #390's conclusions.

=== DELIVERABLE HYGIENE ===

This record's own description lives in specs/** and may cite task numbers freely. Any file this record causes to land under agent-system/** must carry no task-number references (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md) and no record-versioning language (rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md). Note that this record's primary deliverable is task-space mutation (moving and retiring records), not source-store prose, so it may land no agent-system/** files at all.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

(1) Every one of the 32 records carries a stated disposition with its grounds -- move, fold-into-a-named-nvim-record, or abandon-as-covered -- and no disposition rests on a sweep-record overlap or on a truncated-description semantic verdict alone.
(2) Group membership was re-verified against the tree rather than inherited from this description, and any record that moved between groups is named with the reason.
(3) Each moved record was moved under the predecessor record's nine numbered constraints, cited rather than restated, with its citation ids intact and its vacated BimodalLogic entry given a terminal disposition carrying a durable pointer.
(4) Each Group B mixed record either had its local obligations split out or is recorded as pending on #401's unanswered question; none was force-fitted to a disposition.
(5) BimodalLogic #784 was reconciled with constraint (4) rather than duplicated or blindly migrated.
(6) The record states explicitly that it is remediation and that prevention belongs to #391.
(7) The audit was chunked rather than run in batches large enough to trip the argv overflow, and #858 is cross-referenced rather than re-filed.

### 480. Advisory detector for research tasks stranded at RESEARCHED with artifacts but no named landing target
- **Effort**: low
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 479

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout 600000). The originating repo's .claude/ currently reports STALE against its source store, so do NOT read a deployed file as authoritative -- re-derive every line number and every quoted clause from the source store before citing it. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

=== WHAT THIS BUILDS ===

A periodic check that flags any task resting at [RESEARCHED] which carries report or probe artifacts but names NO LANDING TARGET -- surfaced the way the backlog audit surfaces duplicate proposals: advisory and loud, listing each stranded task together with WHAT IT WOULD LOSE ON ARCHIVAL (the specific report and probe paths that leave version control when /todo moves the task directory into the gitignored specs/archive/).

The output's job is to make the loss concrete before it happens. "Task X is stranded" is a line nobody acts on; "Task X is stranded; archiving it removes these 3 files from version control, including a probe no Lake target reaches" is.

=== ADVISORY, NEVER BLOCKING -- THIS IS A HARD CONSTRAINT ===

A stranded task is an OBSERVATION ABOUT THE BACKLOG, not a failure of any build. The check must never fail a build, never block an admission, never gate a commit, and never change an exit code that something else consumes. Ship it advisory, state the promotion criterion if one is even conceivable, and say explicitly in the deliverable that promotion to blocking is NOT part of this change. A loud advisory that an operator reads is worth more than a gate an operator disables.

=== DEFINITION DEPENDENCY ===

"Names no landing target" is defined by the task_landing_target_requirement record, not here. That record defines a landing target as a durable artifact outside specs/ -- a library module, a theorem-index or ledger row, a document or manual section, or a tracked probe under specs/evidence/ -- and establishes both where a task records one and what a research dispatch must emit. This detector DETECTS AGAINST THAT DEFINITION, so it cannot be specified before that record lands. The dependency edge is a genuine content dependency, not a serialization edge: building the detector first would mean inventing a second, drifting definition of the thing being detected.

=== WIRING ===

Wire the check where the other repo-health probes are wired, rather than inventing a new invocation surface. Candidates to evaluate, in writing, before choosing:
  - scripts/validate-state.sh, which already carries a numbered sequence of state.json-level checks and already distinguishes advisory findings from failures. Its per-check conventions are the ones to follow.
  - scripts/assess-repo-health.sh, which emits the repository_health object consumed by /todo's "Sync Repository Metrics" stage. A stranded-task count is plausibly a repository_health metric; note that assess-repo-health.sh's header records `manageable` and `concerning` as deliberately-reserved status values for a future graded metric, so decide whether a stranded count is that metric or a separate advisory entirely.
Rule on which surface owns the check and why; do not wire it into both.

Note the natural adjacency to /todo: archival is the moment the loss becomes real, so an advisory surfaced at /todo time has the best chance of being acted on. The task-abandon-mode.md pattern already states the related obligation for abandonment -- that a guard citing a file by its task-directory path "must be re-pointed, or the file promoted into specs/evidence/, before the task is archived". Re-read that section and make the new check consistent with it rather than duplicating it.

=== FILE SCOPE (re-derive before editing; the planner may narrow or widen with cause) ===

  agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/check-stranded-research.sh   -- new: the detector itself
  agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/validate-state.sh            -- candidate wiring site
  agent-system/extensions/core/context/standards/task-creation-standard.md  -- where the detector's relationship to the landing-target definition is recorded

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

- A task at [RESEARCHED] with report or probe artifacts and no landing target is flagged, by number and name, with the concrete list of paths it would lose on archival.
- A task at [RESEARCHED] that names a landing target is NOT flagged; a task at any other status is not flagged.
- The check's exit code is unaffected by any finding it emits, demonstrated by a fixture test in both the fires and does-not-fire directions.
- The wiring-site decision is recorded with its reasoning, and the check is wired to exactly one surface.
- Run against the real backlog and report the flag count; if it is large, that is a finding about the backlog, not a defect in the check.
- The new script is shellcheck clean per context/standards/shell-strict-mode.md, no task-number references and no record-versioning language land under agent-system/**, and the tree is redeployed and no longer STALE.

### 479. Require every task to name a landing target: a creation-time gate on report-only task descriptions, and a research-phase completion contract naming where findings land
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 360

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout 600000). The originating repo's .claude/ currently reports STALE against its source store, so do NOT read a deployed file as authoritative -- re-derive every line number and every quoted clause from the source store before citing it. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

=== THE PROBLEM ===

A research-only task has no lifecycle terminus. A task whose deliverable is a verdict or a report reaches [RESEARCHED] and stops there: it cannot reach [COMPLETED], because the house pattern requires report + plan + summary via an implement phase, and abandoning it is wrong, because the finding was real. The task rests forever in a state no command will advance and no archival will honour.

Four such tasks accumulated in one consumer repository (BimodalLogic) and could be cleared only by hand-rewriting each description into a library-landing task -- one at a time, by an operator, with no mechanism involved.

The harm compounds on archival. /todo archives into specs/archive/, which is gitignored, so an uncleared research task's report and probes leave version control entirely at the moment they are archived. The BimodalLogic repository's own scripts/check-evidence-probes.sh records this exact loss having already happened, in its "WHY THE PROBES DO NOT LIVE IN A TASK DIRECTORY" header section: four probes lived under a task directory, /todo archived that task, and the probes "left version control entirely (absent from a fresh clone)" while the guard that cited them failed against a path that no longer existed -- "Nothing announced either fact at the time". Re-read that header before citing it; its wording is the evidence, not this paraphrase. A stranded research task is therefore not merely untidy -- it is scheduled data loss.

=== DELIVERABLE: TWO HALVES OF ONE CONCEPT ===

Both halves turn on one definition, which this task must state once and both halves must reference rather than restate.

DEFINE "LANDING TARGET": a durable artifact OUTSIDE specs/ that a task's findings will end up in. The admissible kinds:
  - a library module (source code that ships),
  - a theorem-index or ledger row (a tracked record in a maintained index),
  - a document or manual section,
  - a tracked probe under specs/evidence/ -- which is tracked and CI-guarded, unlike specs/archive/, and is the one in-specs/ location that qualifies precisely because archival cannot silently remove it.
A report under specs/{NNN}_{SLUG}/reports/ is NOT a landing target. That is the whole point: a report is the vehicle, not the destination.

--- HALF 1: CREATION-TIME GATE ---

At task creation, refuse or re-prompt on a description whose only declared deliverable is a report, a verdict, an investigation, or a recommendation with no landing target named.

Provide exactly ONE explicit escape hatch: a task may instead be marked, ON THE TASK ITSELF, as one that will be ABANDONED on completion. That records the no-durable-output case as a declared intent rather than letting it be discovered months later by an operator doing backlog archaeology. Decide and record where that mark lives (a state.json field is the obvious candidate) and what the lifecycle does with it -- in particular whether such a task's archival should warn about what it is discarding.

GUARDRAIL, load-bearing: the gate must not become a bureaucratic field nobody reads. Prefer a REFUSAL CARRYING A CONCRETE SUGGESTED LANDING TARGET over a free-text box. A gate that accepts any non-empty string teaches the operator to type "a report" and moves the failure one layer later. Where the creation surface can infer a plausible target from the description's own file scope or subject matter, it should propose that target and let the operator accept or correct it.

--- HALF 2: RESEARCH-PHASE COMPLETION CONTRACT ---

Require a research dispatch to name, in BOTH its report and its return metadata, where its findings will land. Two consumers, two reasons: the report names it so a human reader can audit the claim, and the return metadata names it so the planner has a machine-readable target to plan toward and the task has a reachable terminus.

A research round that genuinely cannot name a landing target records that as a LOCATED OBSTRUCTION -- which is itself a complete outcome, not a failure and not a stranding. Specify what a located obstruction must contain for the task to rest honestly rather than merely rest.

=== MOTIVATING EVIDENCE: A LIVE INSTANCE IN THIS SAME BACKLOG ===

The task cross_task_traceability_and_changelog_verdict in this same backlog is itself classified, in its own description, as "RESEARCH-AND-DESIGN, VERDICT-FIRST... The deliverable is a recorded design with a verdict, not a landed mechanism". That is a live instance of exactly the pattern this task abolishes.

It must therefore be CHECKED AGAINST the new gate, not silently invalidated by it. Two acceptable outcomes, and the implementer must pick one in writing: either the new definition of a landing target admits a recorded design-with-verdict (in which case say precisely which admissible kind it lands in and why that is durable), or it does not (in which case that task needs either a named landing target or the declared-abandonment mark, and this task's deliverable must say so rather than leaving a known-nonconforming record behind the new gate). A gate whose first act is to invalidate a record nobody intends to abandon is a gate that will be switched off.

=== FILE SCOPE (re-derive before editing; the planner may narrow or widen with cause) ===

  agent-system/extensions/core/context/standards/task-creation-standard.md   -- the definition and the creation-time gate
  agent-system/extensions/core/commands/task.md                              -- the /task creation surface
  agent-system/extensions/core/context/workflows/meta-interview.md           -- the /meta creation surface
  agent-system/extensions/core/context/formats/report-format.md              -- the report-side half of the completion contract
  agent-system/extensions/core/context/contracts/phase-closure.md            -- the return-metadata half and the terminus rule

=== DEPENDENCY NOTE ===

An edge on validate_carried_unverified_names_a_source is declared because that task also edits context/formats/report-format.md. The edge exists so the two SERIALIZE rather than collide on one prose file; it is not a content dependency, and neither task blocks the other on substance.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

- "Landing target" is defined ONCE, in task-creation-standard.md, with its four admissible kinds and the explicit statement that a report is not one; every other touched file points at that definition rather than restating it.
- A creation attempt whose only deliverable is a report/verdict/investigation/recommendation is refused or re-prompted, and the refusal names a concrete suggested target rather than opening a free-text box.
- The declared-abandonment escape hatch is recorded on the task itself, and its archival behaviour is specified.
- A research dispatch's report and its return metadata both carry the landing target; the located-obstruction outcome is specified as complete.
- cross_task_traceability_and_changelog_verdict is ruled on explicitly, in writing, against the new gate.
- No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/**; verify by running the repo's own lints before redeploy, then redeploy and confirm the deploy is no longer STALE.
