### 477. Normalize issue-log class vocabulary and reject the aggregate's malformed repeated-flag call
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: orchestrator
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: The issue-log `class` vocabulary is fragmenting, which defeats the cross-task grouping that is the entire purpose of conclusion-stage aggregation. Separately, orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh accepts a malformed call shape and answers it with a confident, plausible-looking empty result. Fix both.

CHANNEL: /orchestrate conclusion channel (b), agent-system change request, session sess_1793802705_e3f98a.

CONSOLIDATION NOTE (Multi-Task Creation Standard, Component 0): these arrived as two separate change requests but share a single narrow edit target, scripts/orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh, and a single acceptance gate. Under Component 0's default they are therefore ONE task. Split into two, each would have declared that script in file_scope, Component 4a's overlap check would have added a serializing dependency edge between them, and the pair could never have been dispatched independently -- the split would have been self-defeating rather than merely cosmetic.

=== PART 1: CLASS VOCABULARY FRAGMENTATION ===

EVIDENCE, from the aggregate over tasks 275, 274, 378, 387 and 407 in session sess_1793802705_e3f98a: 22 distinct classes appear OUTSIDE the 15-class seed enum documented in context/formats/issue-log.md. Among them are near-duplicate pairs and triples that did NOT group and should have:

  `COVERAGE_GAP` vs `coverage-gap`
      -- a pure case-and-separator difference; these are the same class by any reading.
  `design-gap` vs `design-ambiguity`
      -- a semantic near-duplicate.
  `scope` vs `scope deviation` vs `scope excursion` vs `scope-correction`
      vs `scope/file_scope omission discovered pre-emptively`
      -- FIVE spellings of one concept, which between them hold 13 of the invocation's entries
         while grouping as five separate proposals.

Three further entries put a free-text SENTENCE in the class field rather than a class at all:
  `research-confirmed-hypothesis`
  `reuse-not-reimplement confirmed by direct source read`
  `reuse_of_existing_machinery`

1a. PROMOTE `scope excursion` INTO THE SEED ENUM in context/formats/issue-log.md. At 8 occurrences across five tasks in a single invocation it is the highest-frequency class in the evidence base and the strongest promotion candidate. Choose ONE canonical spelling and state it explicitly -- the five-way variant spread above exists precisely because no canonical spelling was ever written down.

1b. NORMALIZE AT GROUPING TIME in scripts/orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh so that `COVERAGE_GAP` and `coverage-gap` group together. Case folding plus separator folding (hyphen, underscore, space) is the minimum.

    HARD CONSTRAINT: normalization applies at GROUPING time only. Do NOT rewrite the stored `class` value in any issues.jsonl, ever. Those files are append-only evidence, and a retroactive rewrite would destroy the provenance that makes a citation checkable by a future reader -- which is the property the whole conclusion-stage mechanism rests on.

1c. Decide, and record with reasons, how far normalization should go. Case and separator folding is mechanical and safe. Merging `design-gap` with `design-ambiguity`, or collapsing the five scope variants into one, is a SEMANTIC judgment that a grouping function probably should not make silently: an over-eager normalizer that merges two genuinely distinct classes destroys signal exactly as thoroughly as a fragmented vocabulary does, and does it invisibly. State where the line falls and why. One shape worth evaluating explicitly: mechanical folding applied automatically, PLUS a curated alias table for semantic merges, PLUS a report of unrecognized classes so the seed enum grows deliberately rather than by drift. Record the rejected alternatives on their own merits.

1d. Address the free-text-sentence entries. Whatever the remedy -- validation at append time, a warning, or a documented convention with a lint -- the three examples above must be caught by it. A class field that accepts an arbitrary sentence cannot group, and never will.

=== PART 2: A MALFORMED CALL SHAPE ANSWERED WITH A CONFIDENT EMPTY RESULT ===

This defect was observed DIRECTLY during the conclusion stage of the cited session, not harvested from any issue log, so it carries no citation. It is reproducible on demand.

`orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh --aggregate` accepts REPEATED `--tasks N` flags without any error and silently honours only the LAST one. The correct call shape is a single space-separated string:

  CORRECT:   --tasks "275 274 378 387 407"
  ACCEPTED, WRONG, AND SILENT:   --tasks 275 --tasks 274 --tasks 378 ...

The repeated-flag form returns `proposals: []`, `wins: []`, and the single last task listed under `no_contribution_tasks`. Nothing in that output signals that the caller's request was misunderstood. It reads exactly like a legitimate "these tasks logged nothing" answer.

WHY THIS IS WORSE THAN A CRASH: a wrong-but-accepted call shape that yields a confident empty answer gives the caller no error to notice. The caller draws a false conclusion about the invocation it just ran and may skip channels (b) and (c) entirely on the strength of it -- discarding the whole evidence base of a multi-task run. A rejected call costs one retry; this costs the entire conclusion stage, silently.

2a. Either REJECT repeated `--tasks` flags with a usage error that names the correct single-string form, or ACCEPT them as a UNION of all occurrences. Pick one, implement it, and document it in the script's usage header. Rejection is the safer default; union is defensible if it matches the convention other flags in the same script already follow. What is NOT acceptable is the current silent last-wins behaviour.

2b. While in the flag parser, audit the script's OTHER list-valued flags for the same last-wins hazard. The defect is a flag-parsing idiom, and an idiom used once is usually used more than once. Report what was found even if nothing else needs changing.

=== VERIFICATION ===

scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh already exists and is the regression home for all of this. Required cases:
  (a) `COVERAGE_GAP` in one task's log and `coverage-gap` in another's group into ONE proposal.
  (b) A repeated-`--tasks` call produces the chosen behaviour -- a non-zero usage exit, or a union result covering every named task -- and NEVER a silent empty result.
  (c) The canonical `scope excursion` spelling validates against the seed enum.
  (d) Stored `class` values in fixture issues.jsonl files are BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after an aggregate run, proving no retroactive rewrite (the 1b constraint).
  (e) If 1c produced an alias table, an unrecognized class still appears in the output rather than being dropped -- the aggregate must never silently discard an entry it cannot classify.

CONCURRENT TERRITORY NOTES, verified against the open backlog at creation time; none warrants a dependency edge, but a batch must serialize:
  - Record 402 ("Conclusion stage Step 4c allocates no task number and creates no task directories") claims scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-conclusion-aggregate.sh. Different defect, same regression home.
  - Record 448 ("contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations") also claims that test file.
  - Record 435 ("reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class") nominally claims all three of this task's files as part of a broad prose sweep.
  - Record 459 ("Design cross-record traceability; verdict on CHANGE_LOG retirement") is adjacent doctrine on how records cite each other; worth reading at plan time but not an edge.
  - The issue-log contract itself was established by a now-closed record ("per_task_issue_log_contract_writer_and_threading"); this task extends that contract's enum rather than redefining it, and should not re-litigate its threading design.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/. Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. NEVER hand-author under .claude/**, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- an edit there appears to succeed and is silently wiped by the next regeneration (rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout raised to 600000) to RESULT=landed_verify_clean. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description.

### 476. Make the seven untracked red Gate 8 shell suites green or declared-expected
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 423, Task 445
- **Parent**: Task 275

**Description**: Seven shell suites fail in Gate 8 of verify-deploy.sh with no open task tracking them. Make each one green, or make its remaining failure an explicitly declared and attributed baseline expectation, so that a full Gate 8 run regains its value as a binary signal.

CHANNEL: /orchestrate conclusion channel (c), follow-up task, session sess_1793802705_e3f98a.
CITATIONS: {task 275, iss_1793804490147_c845y4} and {task 275, iss_1793807807729_tsGu6f}, class "pre-existing failure".

THE SEVEN SUITES IN SCOPE:
  scripts/tests/test-gate-cache-equivalence.sh
  scripts/tests/test-gate-lock.sh
  scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh
  scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-predispatch-review.sh
  scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-unwind-dispatch.sh
  scripts/tests/test-postflight-deploy-gate.sh
  scripts/tests/test-redeploy-checkpoint-promotion.sh

AN EIGHTH SUITE IS DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED. The originating observation named eight suites, including scripts/tests/test-verify-deploy-context-budget.sh. That file is ALREADY OWNED by open record 423 ("Resolve test-verify-deploy-context-budget.sh's eager-load baseline drift, measured 67,088 B vs. baseline_bytes 66,100 B"), which is itself spawned from the ambient-host-state-isolation audit's corpus triage ledger at specs/170_isolate_shell_suites_from_host_state/reports/02_corpus-triage-ledger.md. It is excluded from this task's file_scope rather than re-claimed. This task takes a dependency edge on 423 instead, because this task's acceptance criterion (a Gate 8 run with zero unexpected failures) cannot be met while that suite is still red.

HAND THIS OBSERVATION TO RECORD 423 RATHER THAN ACTING ON IT HERE -- it is new information relative to that record's current diagnosis, and it should not be lost. test-verify-deploy-context-budget.sh was re-run in isolation during this invocation's conclusion stage: 14 passed, 1 failed, and the single failure was a FIXTURE-SETUP SELF-CHECK reporting "baseline fixture is not clean (rc=1, gate20 finding lines=0)". That reads as a host-state/repo-state leak into the suite's own fixture, NOT a Gate 20 context-budget ceiling problem and not obviously the byte-count drift record 423 describes. Record 423's own framing, inherited from the triage ledger, is that its failure is "a deterministic, load-independent content-size regression check" and explicitly NOT the ambient-host-state defect class. Those two readings may describe two different failures in the same suite, or the same failure diagnosed differently. Resolving that is record 423's business; this task's obligation is only to pass the observation along rather than silently drop it.

PROVENANCE ALREADY ESTABLISHED -- DO NOT RE-DERIVE IT. Task 275's agent proved five of these suites (cycle-postflight, predispatch-review, unwind-dispatch, postflight-deploy-gate, redeploy-checkpoint-promotion) fail IDENTICALLY on unmodified master, by using `git stash push` to strip that task's own changes and re-running. Two of the originally-named eight (gate-cache-equivalence, and the now-excluded verify-deploy-context-budget) are already marked EXPECTED by run-all.sh's own baseline. Re-running the stash experiment is wasted effort. Start from the finding, not from the question of whether these failures are task 275's fault. They are not.

WHY THIS MATTERS, and it belongs in the plan's own motivation: a standing red Gate 8 destroys the signal value of the full gate for every future task. Every subsequent task running a full verify-deploy must either hand-diff its failures against an unwritten mental baseline or ignore Gate 8 entirely. Both outcomes mean a real regression introduced by a future task passes unnoticed. This task restores the gate as a usable binary signal.

--- REQUIRED OUTCOMES ---

1. For each of the seven suites, classify its failure into exactly one of: (a) ambient-host-state leak into the suite's own fixture setup, (b) repo-state leak -- the suite reads the live working tree or live specs/state.json instead of a sealed fixture, (c) a genuine defect in the code under test, (d) a legitimately-expected failure that should be declared in run-all.sh's baseline with an attributed reason. Record the classification and the evidence for it, per suite. A classification without evidence is not acceptable: the whole point is that a future reader can check it.

2. Fix every (a) and (b) by sealing the fixture -- the suite must CONSTRUCT the state it asserts over rather than inheriting it from the host or the live repo. Established prior art exists and MUST be read before inventing a new isolation technique: task 170, "Audit and isolate shell test suites from ambient host state (memory and timing axes), and record the convention", is COMPLETED, and its plan at specs/170_isolate_shell_suites_from_host_state/plans/01_ambient-host-state-isolation.md plus its corpus triage ledger at specs/170_isolate_shell_suites_from_host_state/reports/02_corpus-triage-ledger.md are the reference. This residue is what that audit did not cover; reuse its convention rather than designing a second one.

3. For any (c), do NOT widen this task to fix the code under test if that code lies outside the declared file_scope. Record it and hand it off. A scope excursion stranding uncommitted files is itself a recorded defect of this same invocation and is separately tracked; committing one while fixing the test corpus would be self-defeating.

4. For any (d), declare it with a one-line attributed reason naming WHY it is expected. An undeclared expected failure is indistinguishable from a regression, which is the exact condition this task exists to end. NOTE THE DEPENDENCY: open record 445 ("Give verify-deploy.sh's Gate 8 an expected-versus-new failure distinction instead of collapsing any non-zero run-all.sh exit to FAIL") is building the mechanism this outcome consumes, and it also claims scripts/tests/run-all.sh. Use the distinction 445 delivers rather than inventing a parallel baseline format, and serialize behind it.

5. Verification is the acceptance criterion and must be literal: a full Gate 8 run (scripts/tests/run-all.sh, or verify-deploy.sh scoped to gate 8) reports ZERO UNEXPECTED failures, with every remaining failure matched to a declared baseline entry. Run it TWICE from a clean tree to catch order- and concurrency-dependent flakes -- run-all.sh parallelizes up to its JOBS_CAP of 4, so a suite that passes alone may still fail in a parallel run, and a suite fixed for host state may still be fighting a sibling.

CONCURRENT TERRITORY NOTES -- all verified against the open backlog at creation time, none of them requiring a dependency edge beyond the two declared:
  - Record 328 ("systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency", planned) declares the whole of scripts/tests/ as a directory-root scope and so nominally contains every file here. Its goal is net corpus DELETION driven by script-inventory.sh rankings, which is a different objective; per the Multi-Task Creation Standard's narrowness qualifier, a directory-root scope does not by itself force consolidation. A batch must still serialize the two.
  - Record 428 ("enforce_planner_agent_terminal_write") claims scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh for a different purpose (adding a case, not repairing fixture setup).
  - Record 448 ("contain_deliberate_break_source_mutations") claims scripts/tests/test-postflight-deploy-gate.sh.
  - Record 415 ("exact-pid process checking as a parallelism-safety suite-authoring standard") claims run-all.sh and is directly relevant to outcome 5's concurrency re-run; read it before designing the parallel-run check.
  - Record 452 ("Require a verification criterion over a repo-wide gate with standing pre-existing findings to be baseline-relative, not absolute") is the doctrine counterpart to this task's mechanics; this task supplies the clean baseline that doctrine presumes.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/. Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. NEVER hand-author under .claude/**, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- an edit there appears to succeed and is silently wiped by the next regeneration (rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout raised to 600000) to RESULT=landed_verify_clean. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description.

--- MEASURED EVIDENCE ADDED BY A LATER CONCLUSION STAGE (two of the seven suites) ---

Two suites already in this record's file_scope were re-measured during a later /orchestrate
conclusion stage. A follow-up record was drafted for them and then NOT created, because the
proposal audit correctly returned duplicate-of-open against this record, which already owns both
files and already states the right acceptance criterion. The evidence is handed here instead of
being lost or duplicated.

CITATION: {task 418, iss_1794025742468_254t2U}, class "test-resolution-quirk".

FAILURE COUNTS, MEASURED BOTH SIDES OF AN UNRELATED MIGRATION. Each suite's own pre-migration
version was extracted from git and run against the same tree as the current version, to establish
that an unrelated subject-resolution migration did not cause these failures:

    scripts/tests/test-postflight-deploy-gate.sh         pre-migration: 17 passed, 12 failed
                                                         current:                    5 failed
    scripts/tests/test-redeploy-checkpoint-promotion.sh  pre-migration: 10 passed,  7 failed
                                                         current:                    3 failed

Both were already red before that migration touched them, and the migration measurably REDUCED
each one's failure count. The remaining failures are this record's to resolve.

THE SURVIVING ASSERTIONS, AND WHAT THEIR SHAPE SUGGESTS. In test-postflight-deploy-gate.sh, the
refusal-then-recovery and marker-clearing cases fail like this:

    expected exit 0 on the second (fresh) call, got 2
    expected status 'completed' after the second call, got 'implementing'
    expected both keys absent, got deploy_pending='true'
      deploy_pending_reason='postflight completion-deploy gate refused (exit 6): modified_files
      overlap agent-system/extensions/** and the deploy is stale'

The FIRST call behaves correctly: it refuses with exit 6, leaves the task at implementing, and sets
deploy_pending. Those assertions pass. The SECOND call, against a tree the fixture has freshened,
should exit 0, promote to completed, and clear both marker keys; instead it exits 2 and changes
nothing. Exit 2 is a script-error code, not the gate's own refusal code 6, so the recovery call
appears to fail before it ever evaluates freshness.

In test-redeploy-checkpoint-promotion.sh the survivors are Cases B, C and E, of which Case C is the
informative one: it asserts a refused / exit_code=6 notice is recorded for a stale tree, and none
is. Taken with the exit-2 above, the shared cause may be that the refusal path's bookkeeping is not
running at all rather than running and reaching a wrong verdict. That is a hypothesis, not a
finding, and should be confirmed against the .err files each fixture already writes rather than
inferred from assertion text.

TWO POSSIBILITIES NOT YET ELIMINATED. Whether the exit-2 has one shared cause across both suites or
two independent ones is unsettled; do not assume one. And whether the fixtures themselves construct
a "fresh" tree correctly is unsettled: a fixture that does not would produce exactly this symptom
with no defect in the gate at all, and that must be ruled out before any production script changes.

ONE SPECULATION DELIBERATELY NOT CARRIED FORWARD. The originating entry speculated the breakage
might be a concurrent sibling writer's in-flight edit to update-task-status.sh or its dependency
chain in the shared working tree. That is UNVERIFIED. Confirm or discard it against the committed
tree; it is not evidence.

### 475. Close the audit-evidence gate internal --argjson hole: a large verdict dies with Argument list too long
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: general
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 435

**Description**: The audit-evidence gate's own internal jq invocations pass the whole audit verdict as a single --argjson argument, bypassing the transparent spill the same script implements for caller-supplied values. A large verdict therefore kills the write with "Argument list too long", and no task can be created.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/state-write.sh, never a deployed .claude/** tree (a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Redeploy afterwards via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/**; both are permitted in this description.

--- THE DEFECT, verified by reading the source store ---

state-write.sh implements a transparent spill for oversized --argjson values. Its own header states the intent as universal: any --argjson value at or under SPILL_THRESHOLD bytes is forwarded to jq exactly as before, and no existing or future --argjson call site has to change.

That spill covers CALLER-SUPPLIED --arg/--argjson passthrough. It does NOT cover the script's own internal jq invocations. The audit-evidence gate makes two, and both pass a potentially very large script-constructed value directly:

- the live branch: jq -c -n --argjson newnums "$AUDIT_NEW_NUMBERS" --slurpfile stagearr "$STAGE_FILE" --argjson verdicts "$AUDIT_VERDICT_JSON" ...
- the dry-run branch: the same --argjson verdicts "$AUDIT_VERDICT_JSON" pattern

AUDIT_VERDICT_JSON is built earlier by slurping the whole --audit-verdict FILE into a shell variable (jq -c -s normalizing NDJSON to a JSON array), then handed to jq as one argument.

WHY THE CEILING BITES HERE AND NOT AT LAUNCH. MAX_ARG_STRLEN (131,072 bytes) applies to each OS-level execve, as the large-payload test suite already records for the caller-facing case. Passing --audit-verdict FILE is safe at launch because a path is a short argv token. The failure is one level in: the gate's own internal jq is a fresh execve, and the verdict arrives there as a single oversized argument.

OBSERVED, not hypothesized: a meta-builder dispatch hit this with a 150KB verdict file produced by audit-task-proposals.sh over 11 declared file_scope paths. It could only file its task by trimming the verdict to 135 high-signal rows, retaining all discriminating rows and recording the trim in the stored audit_verdict. The perverse incentive is the point: the louder and more thorough the audit evidence, the likelier the write fails. The gate penalizes exactly the thorough audits it exists to require.

--- THE FIX IS ALREADY PRESENT IN THE SAME EXPRESSION ---

That same jq call reads the staged state via --slurpfile stagearr "$STAGE_FILE". The verdict should be read the same way -- from the --audit-verdict file, or from a normalized temp file derived from it, since the script already does the NDJSON normalization -- bound with --slurpfile instead of materialized into a shell variable and passed as --argjson. The filter body currently references $verdicts and would instead read the slurped array's first element, in the same shape $stagearr[0] is already used.

--- SCOPE ---

Fix BOTH call sites. They are the dry-run and live halves of one gate; fixing only the live one leaves --dry-run failing on exactly the inputs the real write now accepts, which is worse than the current symmetric failure.

A sweep for other internal jq invocations in this script passing large script-constructed values as --argjson is in scope. A blind refactor of unrelated call sites is not. The two named above are the confirmed ones.

Do NOT weaken or remove the audit-evidence gate. The gate is correct; only its argument-passing mechanism is wrong. Trimming verdicts to fit is a workaround and should stop being necessary.

--- VERIFICATION ---

Neither existing spill suite covers this path: a grep for audit-verdict in test-state-write-large-payload.sh and test-state-write-spill-names.sh returns zero hits in both. The regression test belongs in the large-payload suite, which already establishes the isolated-temp-root pattern (throwaway TMPROOT, state-write.sh copied byte-for-byte and never told it is under test, no testability hooks in production code).

Construct a verdict file comfortably over the ceiling -- the observed failure was at 150KB against a ~131KB per-argument limit -- and confirm that BOTH a live write and a --dry-run succeed with it, failing before the change and passing after.

--- DEPENDENCY EDGE (serializing only, not a logical prerequisite) ---

dependencies: [435] exists purely to serialize file territory. Task 435
(reword_dead_lifecycle_command_prose_per_class) is a prose pass whose declared file_scope
includes agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/, the directory holding BOTH files this task
edits (state-write.sh and test-state-write-large-payload.sh). The backlog audit classified the
overlap subset-of-open (mech=subset, both declared paths shared, semantic score 0.32): shared
territory, unsafe to edit concurrently, but no content dependency. Nothing 435 produces is
needed by this fix. Once 435 is complete, or its file_scope no longer covers
core/scripts/, this edge can be dropped safely without re-planning this task.

CITATION: {task 468, iss_1793770694906_KA3RHa}, class "research_gap": planning re-derivation found that agent-system/extensions/core/docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md:948-955 and :1393-1397 carry record-versioning prose ("used to", "is retired", "unchanged from before this design existed"). Task 468's plan then recorded a declared boundary adjustment: a corpus-wide sweep is out of scope because roughly 56 forbidden-vocabulary occurrences exist, roughly 53 of them inside architecture files whose territory is owned by other open records, where remediation means restating the protected assertions rather than deleting a phrase. 468 narrowed itself to touched hunks plus three named sites and recorded the remainder for follow-up. This task is that follow-up.

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/. Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. NEVER hand-author under .claude/**, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- an edit there appears to succeed and is silently wiped by the next regeneration (rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout raised to 600000) to RESULT=landed_verify_clean.

--- WHY THIS IS NOT A FIND-AND-REPLACE ---

The forbidden vocabulary in rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md ("no longer", "previously", "formerly", "used to", "supersedes", "was retired", "were deleted") is forbidden because it phrases a deliverable relative to an unstated past a future reader cannot verify. In these architecture files the offending phrases are frequently load-bearing: they are attached to a real assertion about how the engine behaves, and deleting the phrase deletes the assertion. Each occurrence therefore needs a rewrite that states the CURRENT design on its own terms, keeping whatever the sentence actually asserts.

Three durable, PERMITTED version axes must not be swept: software/toolchain pins (Lean v4.31.0), file-format and schema versions (schema = 2, book-cert-v2.md), and CI cache-key epoch segments (pnpm-v1-, lake-recheck-v3-). A dated cross-document amendment note on a decision a record OWNS is also permitted. Load context/standards/record-version-exemptions.md for the full exemption taxonomy and the version-ok marker convention BEFORE editing, and use scripts/lib/record-version-patterns.sh as the mechanical source of truth for detection and durable-axis discrimination rather than writing a fresh grep.

--- REQUIRED OUTCOMES ---

1. Re-enumerate the occurrence set first and work from what the enumeration returns, not from the counts above. The "roughly 56 / roughly 53" figures are a research-time snapshot taken before task 468's own edits landed and before three later tasks were created; treat them as an order-of-magnitude expectation only. Report the real current count.

2. Rewrite each in-scope occurrence so the surrounding assertion survives intact. Where a sentence's only content IS the draft history ("this replaces an earlier version"), delete it; where the phrase qualifies a live assertion, restate the assertion in the present tense. Do not introduce a supersession table, a disposition mapping, or a "(v1 Decision N)" attribution while fixing -- those are forbidden by the same rule.

3. Resolve the territory question explicitly before editing. The architecture files carrying the bulk of the occurrences are owned by other open records. Establish which occurrences this task may edit and which must be deferred to the owning record, and record the verdict per file as a boundary register in the plan, the way task 468 did. A deferred occurrence is recorded with its owning record number, never silently skipped.

4. Verify by re-running the mechanical detection over the touched files and showing it clean for every occurrence this task claimed, with each deferral named and attributed. A reduced-but-nonzero count is an acceptable outcome ONLY when every remaining occurrence is an explicit, attributed deferral or a permitted durable axis.

CONCURRENT TERRITORY NOTE: orchestrate-state-machine.md and the surrounding architecture/ docs are claimed by several open records (378, 387, 420, 428, 431, 438, 450 were the boundary-owned set at 468's planning time, and 274 claims batch-admit-schema.md). No dependency edge was added at creation time. A batch co-scheduling any of those with this task must serialize them rather than run them as parallel siblings, and the territory verdict in outcome 3 should be re-derived against the record set that is actually open when this task runs.

### 472. Stop validate-handoff.sh aborting on a string-shaped blockers array, and stop the defect recorder refusing every consumer-repo attribution
- **Effort**: 4 hours
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: orchestrator
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 378, Task 428, Task 435

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/ and agent-system/extensions/lean/ (never .claude/**, a gitignored disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/**; both are permitted in this description.

## Goal

A partial-status handoff carrying a non-empty `blockers` array must not crash the handoff validator, must not be reported as a required-field failure it is not, and must not be attributed to the wrong owner. Separately, the system-defect recorder must not refuse every handoff-validation defect raised from a consumer repository.

## Provenance: one dispatch, two coupled defects

Both sub-problems below were observed together in a single dispatch: session sess_1793770123_ca991a, task 761 in the BimodalLogic consumer repo, dispatch_seq 26, agent lean-implementation-agent, phase implement, 2026-11-04. The run recorded defect_class HANDOFF_VALIDATION_FAILED with attributed_path agent-system/extensions/core/skills/skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md and detecting_site skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md:cycle-postflight-handoff-validation. Postflight recovered and persisted `partial` correctly, so no task status was lost.

Sub-problem 2 is what makes sub-problem 1 hard to study after the fact: because the durable append failed, the only record of the firing was this run's in-memory multi-task state, and specs/events.jsonl carries no entry for it. The diagnosis below therefore had to be reconstructed by re-running the validator by hand against the surviving handoff rather than read out of the durable record, and that reconstruction is the only reason the original premise (below) could be corrected at all.

## PREMISE CORRECTION -- read before planning

The change request that produced this record stated that the validator's `partial`-without-continuation WARNING escalated into the postflight ERROR. THAT IS NOT WHAT HAPPENED, and planning against it would send the implementer after a non-defect. Verified by re-running the deployed validator against the surviving handoff at specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/.orchestrator-handoff.json:

  - validate-handoff.sh exit code was 5, not 0 and not 1.
  - Its output stops after `[PASS] Blockers array present with 4 entry(s)`. The Validation Summary block never printed, so neither of the script's own `exit 0` / `exit 1` summary paths was ever reached.
  - The deployed copy at .claude/scripts/validate-handoff.sh is byte-identical to the source-store copy (diff clean), so a stale deploy tree is excluded as a cause.
  - The warning path is confirmed non-escalating on its own merits: Check 5 calls log_warn, and the summary block maps warnings-only to `exit 0` with `HANDOFF VALIDATION PASSED WITH WARNINGS`.

## Sub-problem 1 (verified root cause): Check 7 aborts under `set -euo pipefail`

The handoff's `blockers` is an array of four PLAIN STRINGS. The schema requires objects. context/schemas/orchestrator-handoff-schema.json defines `blockers.items` as `{"type":"object","required":["phase","target"]}`, and docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md's `blockers` field definition says each entry uses the canonical object shape.

validate-handoff.sh Check 7 iterates the array and evaluates `jq -r ".blockers[$i].phase // \"__MISSING__\""`. Against a string element jq fails with `Cannot index string with string ("phase")` and exits 5. The call suppresses the message with `2>/dev/null` but NOT the exit status, and with `set -euo pipefail` in force (validate-handoff.sh:19) the failed command substitution aborts the whole script with jq's 5. Note the contrast one line earlier: the `blockers_count` assignment is guarded with `|| echo "0"`; the two per-element assignments inside the loop carry no such guard.

Consequences, all three to be addressed:

  (a) A real schema violation is reported as something it is not. orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh's handoff-validation block treats any nonzero exit as `ERROR: HANDOFF VALIDATION FAILED -- ... fails validate-handoff.sh's required-field checks`. No required-field check failed; every one passed. The block then derives `failing_fields` by grepping the output for `^[FAIL]` lines, finds none, and omits the flag -- so the recorded defect carries no detail naming the actual problem.
  (b) The defect is attributed to the wrong owner. The postflight attributes to skill-orchestrate/SKILL.md (or, via its local resolver, the dispatched agent's file), when the mechanical fault is validate-handoff.sh's own unguarded indexing.
  (c) The authoring site never told the agent the shape. agent-system/extensions/lean/agents/lean-implementation-agent.md's handoff block (around line 216) states explicitly that `artifacts[]` entries "MUST use that schema's `{type, path, summary}` object shape, never a bare path string", and for `blockers` says only that it "is a JSON array, and `[]` is normal and expected". An agent writing a NON-EMPTY blockers array is given no element shape at all, which is exactly the hole that produced a string array here. The same gap should be checked in lean-implementation-hard-agent.md and core/agents/general-implementation-agent.md.

Also determine why the write-time gate did not catch it: hooks/validate-handoff-location.sh is documented as rejecting a write whose `blockers` key is absent, but it evidently does not check element shape, so a string-shaped array passes the write-time gate and only detonates in postflight.

Required outcome for sub-problem 1: a string-shaped (or otherwise non-object) blockers element yields a clean, deterministic FAIL naming the offending index and the expected shape, with the summary block reached and the exit code being the validator's own, never jq's. The authoring instruction and the validator must agree on the element shape, and the authoring site must state it at the point of authoring.

INHERITED NON-GOAL, carried forward from the closed record handoff_required_fields_and_dropped_failure and binding here: DO NOT resolve this by relaxing or deleting Check 7 so the failure stops appearing. A string-shaped blockers array is a real schema violation and must be reported as one. The defect is that it aborts instead of reporting, not that it is checked.

REUSE THE EXISTING FIX PATTERN, DO NOT INVENT ONE. The closed record pin_handoff_artifacts_element_shape solved the identical problem one field over: research dispatches were writing `artifacts` as an array of bare path strings, the consumer's indexing raised the same "Cannot index string" class of error, and the remedy was to pin the element shape unambiguously in the agent contracts. That record's scope covered the research agents and the `artifacts[]` field only. It is the direct reason the implementation-agent instruction carries an explicit `artifacts[]` object-shape sentence and no `blockers[]` equivalent: the earlier work pinned one field at the research sites and stopped there. Follow its shape for `blockers[]` at the implementation sites.

Separately, and NOT the cause of this firing: Check 5's `partial`-without-continuation inconsistency. START BY READING WHAT WAS ALREADY RULED, rather than re-opening it blind. The closed record handoff_required_fields_and_dropped_failure explicitly put this in scope -- "Rule on `sorry_inventory` and `continuation_path` in the same pass: either they are genuinely optional and the WARN should go, or they are expected and belong in whichever mechanism (i)/(ii) establishes." The WARN is still present and still non-escalating, which is consistent with a deliberate ruling that the field is optional and the warning stays advisory. Establish which it was. If it was settled, record that the contract is already coherent and close this item without a change; if the ruling never landed, settle it so the validator, the schema document and the authoring instruction state the same thing. Do not treat it as the explanation for the ERROR under any outcome.

## Sub-problem 2 (verified root cause): the recorder refuses in every consumer repo

The postflight printed `Note: system-defect recording failed (non-fatal)` and the durable specs/events.jsonl record was never written. Reproduced directly: invoking system-defect-record.sh from BimodalLogic with exactly the arguments the postflight call site passes exits 3 with

  [SYSTEM-DEFECT RECORDER] REFUSING: Signal B attribution unresolvable (attributed-path='' dispatched-agent='lean-implementation-agent' did not resolve to a path under agent-system/extensions/**). Log only, no record.

The mechanism: system-defect-record.sh resolves `--dispatched-agent` by globbing "$PROJECT_ROOT"/agent-system/extensions/*/agents/NAME.md. That tree exists only in the source-store repo, never in a consumer repo, so the glob cannot match and the path-prefix case refuses. The handoff-validation call site (orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh around line 795) passes `--dispatched-agent "$agent_name"` and NO `--attributed-path`, so there is nothing else to resolve from. THIS IS STRUCTURAL, NOT INCIDENTAL: from any consumer repository this call site can never produce a durable record.

Compounding it, the refusal is loud by design but silenced in practice. The recorder writes its REFUSING line to stderr, the call site redirects `2>/dev/null`, and only the generic non-fatal note survives. The operator sees that recording failed but never why.

Note the near-miss in the same block: the postflight DOES compute an `hv_agent_path` fallback to `$attributed_path`, using the same glob -- but it computes it AFTER the recorder call, and passes it only to the in-memory append, never to the recorder.

Scope the fix across call sites rather than patching one. orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh makes 15 system-defect-record.sh invocations; three pass only `--dispatched-agent` with no `--attributed-path` (around lines 552, 795, 1087) and are therefore subject to the same structural refusal, while the remaining sites do pass `--attributed-path`.

Required outcome for sub-problem 2: either the attribution is resolvable from a consumer repo (e.g. hoist the existing fallback above the call and pass `--attributed-path`), or the refusal is surfaced loudly enough that an operator can act on it. A defect-recording path that fails silently-but-non-fatally defeats the durable-record guarantee the System Defects Detected output section promises, namely that the durable record of each firing is already in specs/events.jsonl. Whichever direction is chosen, the `2>/dev/null` that swallows the recorder's own reason must be reconsidered.

## Acceptance

  1. A fixture handoff whose `blockers` is an array of strings produces a reported FAIL (not an abort, not exit 5), reaches the Validation Summary, and names the offending index -- added to scripts/tests/test-validate-handoff.sh.
  2. A postflight test asserts that a handoff-validation defect raised with only a dispatched-agent name, from a repo with no agent-system/ tree, either records durably or emits a reason the operator can read -- added to scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh.
  3. The blockers element shape is stated at the authoring site(s), alongside the existing `artifacts[]` object-shape sentence.
  4. The Check 5 partial/continuation question has an explicit recorded verdict, and the validator, the schema document and the authoring instruction state the same thing.

## Backlog reconciliation

Audited against the full backlog corpus via audit-task-proposals.sh (11 declared file_scope paths) before creation, and checked by title and description text. The audit is recall-biased by design and returned 182 covered-by-closed, 24 duplicate-of-open, 6 overlaps-open and 37 scope-blind verdicts; the discriminations that actually matter are below.

TWO CLOSED RECORDS IN THE SAME FAMILY, both checked and both rejected as covering this:

  - pin_handoff_artifacts_element_shape (closed; semantic 0.49) -- REJECTED as covering, ACCEPTED as the pattern to follow. It pinned the `artifacts[]` element shape at the RESEARCH agent sites. This record pins the `blockers[]` element shape at the IMPLEMENTATION agent sites, and additionally fixes the validator's abort, which that record did not touch. A legitimate recurrence of one class at a different field and different sites, not re-litigation.
  - handoff_required_fields_and_dropped_failure (closed; semantic 0.50; shares nearly this record's whole file_scope) -- REJECTED as covering, on two discriminating facts. (1) It addressed a required key being ABSENT; this record addresses a required key being PRESENT with the wrong element type, which is a different failure mode and, unlike absence, aborts the validator rather than failing it. (2) It established the durable-trace requirement that a failed handoff validation leave an events.jsonl row and a detected_defects entry. Sub-problem 2 is that very guarantee not being delivered from a consumer repo -- a shortfall in the mechanism that record built, discovered by exercising it, rather than a new feature request. Any plan here should read that record's (i)/(ii) ruling before touching the validator or the recorder.

Nearest open misses, each checked and rejected as a duplicate:

  - 378 (reviser-agent handoff contract contradiction) -- REJECTED: a different defect class, HANDOFF_STALE_OR_ABSENT, about a writer emitting no handoff at all on a plan dispatch. This record is about a handoff that WAS written and then crashed the validator. Shares docs/architecture/handoff-schema.md, hence the dependency edge.
  - 428 (planner-agent terminal return-meta write) -- REJECTED on intent: return-meta enforcement, not handoff element shape or defect-recorder attribution. Shares three paths (orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh, its test, handoff-schema.md), hence the dependency edge.
  - 410 (lake-build-guard result-sharing replay) and 430/446 (--file-scope-add restriction) -- unrelated; named here only because they were the two candidates the dispatching stage dropped as already covered.
  - 402 proposes converting the orchestration conclusion stage's channels (b) and (c) from task-creating to evidence-contributing. If that lands first, a record of this shape would arrive as evidence rather than as a task. It does not make this record a duplicate, and this record was created under the contract in force at the time.

Dependency edges are SERIALIZING ONLY, on shared file territory, with no logical dependency in either direction -- nothing here blocks on any of their findings, and an operator who wants this sooner can drop any or all of them:

  - 428 shares three paths (orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh, its test, handoff-schema.md).
  - 435 shares ten paths; it is a prose-rewording pass across the agent and script corpus, so concurrent edits to the same files are a live conflict risk even though the intents do not overlap.
  - 328 shares seven paths and declares scripts/tests/ and docs/ wholesale, matching the precedent set by the other recently filed lint-defect records.
  - 378 shares handoff-schema.md and is the sibling handoff-contract record.

## Why this is worth fixing despite the clean recovery

Nothing was lost in the observed run, and that is precisely the risk: the failure mode is a correct-outcome run that emits a misattributed defect and then fails to record it. Every future instance costs an operator the same hand reconstruction performed to write this record, and in a consumer repo -- which is where orchestration normally runs -- the durable record will be absent every time.

### 463. State that lean_goal's empty goals_after is not evidence a declaration elaborates, and point step verification at that statement
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: lean-extension
- **Dependencies**: Task 412

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: resolve `source_dir` from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. Never hand-author under a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description, which lives under specs/**.

CROSS-REPO CITATION CAVEAT: the issue identifier cited below is an entry in a DIFFERENT repository's issue log -- BimodalLogic, at specs/849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/issues.jsonl. The number 849 is a BimodalLogic task number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json; do not try to look it up here. This record is filed here, not in BimodalLogic, because an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store. Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today; that gap is itself tracked by this repo's cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness task.

CITATION: BimodalLogic:849:iss_1793752926544_AfZmP9 (class `tooling`, severity minor, implement phase).

=== THE DEFECT ===
An empty `goals_after` from `lean_goal` means "no goals remain at this position". It is not the claim "this declaration elaborates". Nothing in the lean extension says so, while the shared flow document instructs the agent to verify STEP completion with exactly that tool.

=== MEASURED INCIDENT (carried from the originating repo's record; CARRIED-UNVERIFIED in this repository) ===
`lean_goal` returned an empty `goals_after` at a closing `exact` line whose term referenced an unknown constant (`Bool.true_ne_false`). The elaboration error surfaced only in the full `lake build`.

=== THE GAP, RE-VERIFIED IN THE SOURCE STORE AT FILING (line numbers drift; re-derive before editing) ===
  - `context/project/lean4/tools/mcp-tools-guide.md` ~L83 is the whole of what the guide says about this output: "- Omit `column` to see `goals_before` (line start) and `goals_after` (line end)". No trust-surface caveat.
  - `context/project/lean4/agents/lean-implementation-flow.md` ~L117: "**Verify step completion** with `lean_goal` (proof state) and `lean_verify` (axiom/sorry check); do NOT run `lake build` per-step". ~L151 restates it ("Per-step verification uses `lean_goal` + `lean_verify`"), and ~L113 leans on it again after each edit.
  - A grep of the whole lean extension for an empty-goals / not-evidence statement returns nothing. The only "not evidence" statements present concern other surfaces entirely: `operations/long-builds.md` on an unfinished build, and `domain/metadata-trust-surfaces.md` on an empty `meta_imports`.
So the agent is told to verify step completion with a tool whose trust surface is undocumented, and has no stated reason to distrust an empty goal list between phase boundaries.

=== WHAT THIS DOES NOT REVISIT (verified, not assumed) ===
The mitigation already exists: the completed task `lean_phase_commit_full_build_and_plan_anchor` gated a phase COMMIT on the full library build rather than a module-scoped one. That ruling stands and is not reopened. This task adds the missing statement about the tool itself, which is what makes the per-step instruction safe to follow BETWEEN those commit boundaries.

=== SUGGESTED SHAPE (research may refine) ===
State the trust surface ONCE and have consumers point at it. Two siting candidates:
  (a) Alongside the existing `goals_after` bullet in `mcp-tools-guide.md`.
  (b) In `context/project/lean4/domain/metadata-trust-surfaces.md`, which already exists for exactly this genre of "what this metadata is not checked by" statement and already carries a structurally identical sentence about an empty `meta_imports`; the guide would then need a pointer to it.
Pick one. Do not write it twice. The flow document's step-verification line should POINT at the statement rather than restate it.

=== A SUBSTANTIVE QUESTION WORTH ANSWERING, NOT JUST A CAVEAT ===
Say what the agent should do INSTEAD at step granularity, given that a per-step `lake build` is forbidden by the same line that names `lean_goal`. Candidates include `lean_diagnostic_messages` for the edited file (which reports elaboration errors, where `lean_goal` reports only goal state) and `lean_verify` on the fully qualified name. State the cheapest check that actually covers the unknown-constant case. A caveat with no replacement check leaves the agent with nothing to do differently.

=== DEPENDENCY AND WHY (serialization against shared file territory, NOT absorption) ===
`lean_tier_vocabulary_to_build_scope_mapping` shares `context/project/lean4/agents/lean-implementation-flow.md` and is the task that will write INTO THAT SAME FILE a mapping from core's `prose`/`local`/`interface`/`full` tiers to concrete Lean verification actions. Its own description names "which tiers are satisfied by lean_goal plus lean_verify" as an example of what it will state. Writing a tier mapping that rests on `lean_goal`, and a trust-surface caveat about `lean_goal`, into one file from two uncoordinated dispatches is the avoidable failure. Ordering: this task runs AFTER that one, so the caveat and the pointer are applied to the mapping as it then stands. That task's own single dependency is already completed, so it is not itself blocked.
THE REVERSE ORDER WAS CONSIDERED: authoring the tier mapping with this caveat already in hand is the better outcome, and was declined here only because it would require mutating an existing task's dependency chain. If that task is still unstarted when this one is planned, merging the two into one dispatch is legitimate and preferable.

=== CONDITIONAL SCOPE ===
If the chosen siting changes a summary already recorded in `agent-system/extensions/lean/index-entries.json`, that file JOINS the scope -- widen file_scope before the edit rather than writing outside it. Note that two other unstarted lean tasks (`lean_research_compile_verify_recommendation`, `lean4_context_three_measured_gotchas`) also name `index-entries.json`, so coordinate rather than racing. Editing only existing, already-indexed prose needs no index change.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===
One statement, in one place, saying that an empty `goals_after` means no goals remain at that position and is not evidence that the declaration elaborates; a named cheaper-than-full-build check that does cover the unknown-constant case; the flow document's step-verification line pointing at that statement without restating it; no contradiction introduced with the landed phase-commit ruling.

=== NON-GOALS ===
No change to the phase-commit gating ruling. No general reconciliation of `mcp-tools-guide.md` against the live MCP tool set -- that is already recorded elsewhere as separate, larger work. No new scripts, hooks or linters: this adds context, not enforcement. Do not touch a deployed `.claude/` tree.

### 462. Give lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check a scope limit so a research prototype under specs/ stops forcing a false ambiguity failure
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: lean-extension
- **Dependencies**: Task 385, Task 451

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: resolve `source_dir` from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. Never hand-author under a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store (rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description, which lives under specs/**.

CROSS-REPO CITATION CAVEAT: the issue identifier cited below is an entry in a DIFFERENT repository's issue log -- BimodalLogic, at specs/849_forward_slack_discharge_and_exact_shift_iteration/issues.jsonl. The number 849 is a BimodalLogic task number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json; do not try to look it up here. This record is filed here, not in BimodalLogic, because an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store. Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today; that gap is itself tracked by this repo's cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness task.

CITATION: BimodalLogic:849:iss_1793754709802_bWVZnZ (class `tooling`, severity minor, implement phase).

=== THE DEFECT ===
`lean-challenge-snapshot.sh --check` has no scope limit, so a compiled research prototype parked under `specs/**` -- the sanctioned place for one -- forces a false ambiguity failure and exit 71 on a tree that is in fact correct.

=== ROOT CAUSE, RE-DERIVED BY DIRECT READ OF THE SOURCE STORE AT FILING (line numbers drift; re-derive before editing) ===
`do_check()` begins ~L198. It resolves each pinned identifier by walking the entire project root:
  - `find_lean_files(root)` (~L273-279) is `os.walk(root)` with `dirnames` filtered to exclude only the directory names ".git", ".lake" and "lake-packages".
  - The comparison loop (~L282-294) iterates that walk over `project_root` and skips exactly one further path, the pinned Challenge artifact itself (`challenge_abs`).
Any other `.lean` file anywhere in the tree is therefore a comparison target, including `specs/{NNN}_{slug}/prototype/*.lean`. When such a prototype declares a pinned name, `ambiguous` (~L297) is non-empty and the branch at ~L306-310 writes "ERROR: '{n}' resolves to more than one declaration in the current tree: {files}" and exits 71.

=== A SECOND ENUMERATION SITE -- DERIVED HERE, DO NOT FIX ONLY ONE ===
The R2 fallback path has its own independent enumeration and its own ambiguity hard-exit:
  - ~L602: `tree = git("ls-tree", "-r", "--name-only", commit)`; ~L605: `lean_files = [p for p in tree.stdout.splitlines() if p.endswith(".lean")]`. That is every `.lean` path in the repo tree at that commit with NO exclusions at all -- not `.lake`, not the Challenge.
  - ~L652-660: `ambiguous` non-empty writes "ERROR: R2 fallback found more than one declaration named '{n}' at commit {commit[:12]}: {files}. Extraction ambiguity is a hard error, never a best-effort guess." and exits 71.
So a COMMITTED prototype under `specs/` reaches the same defect by the second path. If a scope notion is introduced, both sites should consume it rather than diverging; if only `--check` is fixed, the record must say why. The R2 site's "hard error, never a best-effort guess" stance is deliberate and must be preserved for genuine ambiguity.

=== MEASURED INCIDENT (carried from the originating repo's record; CARRIED-UNVERIFIED in this repository -- re-derive or re-cite at plan time) ===
Five of seven pinned names resolved to two declarations each: the landed declaration in `FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderChain.lean` and the research prototype `specs/849_.../prototype/helpers-compiled.lean`. The implementer checked drift independently (whitespace-normalized header diff of all seven against Challenge.lean: MATCH 7/7) and did NOT edit the prototype, correctly treating it as the research record. The verdict is advisory by the script's own header, so nothing was blocked. The loss is that a mechanical check was silently replaced by a manual one.

=== DESIGN QUESTION -- DELIBERATELY NOT PRE-DECIDED ===
Candidate mechanisms, each with a real cost to weigh:
  (a) Exclude `specs/` in the enumeration. Cheapest, but hard-codes a repository-layout assumption and would also hide a genuine duplicate that some task legitimately parks under `specs/`.
  (b) An explicit `--scope` / `--include` flag. Pushes the decision out to every call site, and the call sites include a hook.
  (c) Read the task's own `file_scope`. Couples the script to state.json's `file_scope`, whose lifecycle is owned elsewhere in this repo (see the file-scope-lifecycle topic).
Research picks one and says why the others lose.

=== INVARIANT TO PRESERVE ===
Two declarations of a pinned name in the LIBRARY is a real finding and must remain a hard failure. The deliverable narrows WHERE the script looks, never WHETHER genuine ambiguity fails.

=== EXIT-CODE CAUTION ===
Prefer not to overload 71 further. This script's exit-code vocabulary is already being revised by the zero-goal task named in dependencies, which observes that 71 is documented as "Config error" in the script's own header while carrying several distinct conditions. Adopt whatever that task settles rather than inventing a parallel code.

=== DEPENDENCIES AND WHY (serialization against shared file territory, NOT absorption) ===
Both descriptions were read in full at filing; neither covers `--check`'s identifier resolution or its tree-walk scope, so this is a sibling record, not a duplicate.
  - `lean4_plan_with_zero_lean_goals`: same two lean files. Owns the exit-71 vocabulary for the EXTRACT step and the zero-theorem plan shape. A new scope mechanism must not reintroduce a 71 collision that task is in the middle of untangling.
  - `snapshot_lean_challenge_at_plan_time`: same two lean files plus the test suite. Owns WHEN the snapshot is invoked (moving it to plan time). Changing where `--check` looks while the invocation point is moving would land two uncoordinated edits in one script.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===
(a) A `--check` run in a tree containing a `.lean` prototype under `specs/{NNN}_{slug}/` that declares a pinned name resolves to the library declaration and exits 0.
(b) Two declarations of a pinned name in library source still fails hard.
(c) Both enumeration sites either share the scope notion, or the record states why only one is in scope.
(d) The chosen mechanism documented in `challenge-snapshot.md` and in the script's own exit-code header.
(e) Test coverage in `scripts/tests/test-lean-challenge-snapshot.sh` for both (a) and (b).

=== NON-GOALS ===
Do not relitigate the advisory-vs-blocking status of the `--check` verdict. Do not change drift detection (the whitespace-normalized header comparison) itself. Do not touch a deployed `.claude/` tree.

### 461. Rule on and close the V7 refusal of a positive directory pathspec for a git rm --cached untracking whose content is still on disk
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 430, Task 435, Task 446, Task 448

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation in the Logos/Vision consumer repository, user-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN VISION: an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store. Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today (see the open record on cross-repo source-store gate blindness); where it matters below it is stated in prose.

CITATION: the sole evidence base is Vision:#234:iss_1793752881502_Y429oi, in the ORIGINATING repository's log at Vision:specs/234_resolve_standing_verify_deploy_gate_failures/issues.jsonl. The identifier #234 is a Vision number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json. No suggested_channel hint was recorded on that entry; routing it to the agent-system channel is the orchestrator's own classification. Severity: minor -- a workaround exists and nothing is blocked.

PROVENANCE: every file, line number, and quoted string in the ROOT CAUSE section below was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-11-03), not copied from the originating report. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narrative and the exit code are CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited entry, which states they were reproduced in a dedicated scratch reproduction rather than observed once in situ; that reproduction is not re-runnable from this repository.

LIVE SYMPTOM. In the Vision consumer repo, `git rm -r --cached .opencode` was run to untrack a stale deploy tree (500 files; the index records deletions, every file remains on disk because `--cached` by definition leaves the working tree alone). The subsequent `git-commit-scoped.sh` invocation naming `.opencode` as a positive pathspec exited 2 with the V7 refusal.

ROOT CAUSE, VERIFIED AGAINST THE SCRIPT (not inferred). In scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh, the post-add verification loop over `add_positive_pathspecs` (around lines 844-878) first tests `git ls-files --error-unmatch -- "$p"`. For an untracked-by-`--cached` directory this fails, because no file under it remains in the index. Control then reaches the `staged_deletion_adds` carve-out, whose acceptance predicate is the three-way conjunction

    [ ! -e "$p" ] && [ "$staged_deletion_rc" -eq 1 ] && git rev-parse --verify -q HEAD

The second and third conjuncts hold: `git diff --cached --quiet HEAD -- .opencode` exits 1 (a staged change exists) and HEAD exists. The FIRST conjunct fails -- `.opencode` is still on disk. The path therefore falls through to `genuinely_failed_adds+=("${p} (not present in the index at all)")` and the refusal at the `genuinely_failed_adds` block below it. The carve-out's own comment states its premise explicitly: it exists for "the source half of a directory move", where the content is GONE from the working tree. A `--cached` untracking inverts exactly that premise -- index deletions WITH content present -- so it matches neither the normal staged-path branch nor the sanctioned fully-staged-deletion branch.

WORKAROUND ACTUALLY USED, AND WHY IT IS NOT A FIX. The implementing agent temporarily relocated `.opencode/` out of the repository, invoked `git-commit-scoped.sh` directly with the same pathspec set, then restored the content immediately after the commit landed. It deliberately did not use `git-commit-phase.sh`, whose post-commit `files_touched` completeness check would have misreported the restored untracked content as dirty. No forbidden git operation was used at any point. This manoeuvre is undocumented and fragile, which is the entire cost being reported: an untracking commit currently requires relocating files out of the working tree.

PRIOR ART -- DO NOT RE-LITIGATE ANY OF IT. (a) The completed record fixing the V2 gate dropping staged deletions addressed a DIFFERENT gate and a different mechanism, and its premise is again "content gone from both working tree and index"; it does not cover this case. (b) The settled bare-directory-pathspec policy and its lint (scripts/lint/lint-directory-pathspec-boundary.sh and its test) are closed; do not reopen the policy itself. (c) Both specs/state.json and specs/archive/state.json were scanned at creation time for `--cached`, `staged_deletion`, and untracking subjects: no open or closed record covers the `--cached`-with-content-present case. (d) No open record names scripts/git-commit-scoped.sh or scripts/tests/test-git-commit-scoped.sh in its file_scope.

THE TENSION THE PLANNER MUST RULE ON EXPLICITLY, NOT ASSUME AWAY. rules/git-workflow.md's Git Safety section (around lines 89-92) forbids a directory-or-glob `git add` pathspec outright and has that forbiddance enforced by hooks/guard-destructive-git.sh via exit 2 on a dirty tree. Yet a directory pathspec is ROUTINE inside git-commit-scoped.sh: context/standards/git-staging-scope.md (around line 423) records that the sanctioned path for staging `${task_dir}/` is precisely to invoke git-commit-scoped.sh, "because its own internal `git add` runs as a subprocess, never as the literal top-level `tool_input.command` the hook observes", and V7's own carve-out sanctions a directory pathspec for the source half of a directory move. So "a directory pathspec was used, therefore the refusal is correct" is NOT an adequate disposition of this report. Rule on which of these the untracking case is, and record the ruling with its reasoning:
  (1) it belongs INSIDE the existing fully-staged-deletion carve-out (widen the predicate's first conjunct);
  (2) it needs its OWN narrower carve-out, kept distinct from the directory-move case so neither's premise is blurred; or
  (3) it should not be served by a carve-out at all, and instead by a documented explicit-file-list recipe for untracking.
A defensible outcome here is (3) -- "no widening, and here is the recipe instead". Do not widen by default.

REQUIRED OUTCOME REGARDLESS OF THE RULING. An agent must be left with a sanctioned, documented way to commit an untracking WITHOUT relocating files out of the repository. If the ruling is (3), the recipe is the deliverable and the script may be left unchanged apart from a refusal message that points at it.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT weaken V7 into a boolean. The script's own comment documents that the `git diff --cached HEAD` exit code is read EXACTLY -- 1 means "a staged change exists", 0 means "nothing staged here" (a genuine drop), and anything above 1 (notably 128) is a git error such as an out-of-repo pathspec. Only 1 is accepted, specifically so the out-of-repo hard failure keeps refusing. Any predicate change must preserve that discrimination; a `! git diff ...` boolean test would wrongly accept the 128 case.
- Do NOT let a widened predicate accept a genuinely-unstaged directory. The whole point of V7 is that a path present on disk and differing from the index is a real failure. If the first conjunct is relaxed, something else must establish that every tracked file under the pathspec is staged as deleted -- content presence alone must not become sufficient.
- Keep the two deletion shapes distinguishable in the NOTE output. The existing note names its paths as "the source half of a directory move"; an untracking is not that, and a note that conflates them makes a future reader mis-diagnose.
- Preserve the existing gitignore-advisory tolerance and the `add_exclude_pathspecs` handling in the same loop; neither is in scope to change.

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-git-commit-scoped.sh with a regression case that FAILS against the current script and passes after the fix: a `git rm -r --cached <dir>` whose content remains on disk, committed via a positive directory pathspec.
- Pin the existing fully-staged-deletion directory-move case in the same suite, so the fix is demonstrated not to regress the carve-out that already works.
- Pin the two cases the predicate must keep refusing: a path present on disk with unstaged content differences, and an out-of-repo pathspec (the 128 case).
- Use the scoped narrowing (scripts/tests/run-all.sh --only SUITE), not the full aggregator.

TERRITORY EDGES (labelled rationale rather than bare numbers):
- 430 (SEMANTIC and SERIALIZATION): owns scripts/git-commit-phase.sh, scripts/tests/test-git-commit-phase.sh, and context/standards/git-staging-scope.md. Semantic because the workaround narrative above turns on git-commit-phase.sh's post-commit `files_touched` completeness check misreading restored untracked content as dirty -- if the ruling produces a recipe that routes through git-commit-phase.sh, that check is the thing it must not trip. Re-read this record against what 430 lands.
- 446 (SERIALIZATION, with a doc-territory edge): owns context/standards/git-staging-scope.md and scripts/git-commit-phase.sh as part of the plan-to-file_scope reconciliation work. Any recipe this record documents in git-staging-scope.md is contested territory with it; land after it and merge rather than overwrite.
- 328 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/ at directory level as part of a net-deletion corpus consolidation, covering test-git-commit-scoped.sh.
- 435 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/ at directory level, covering both of this record's script files.
- 448 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/ at directory level, covering test-git-commit-scoped.sh.
The three edges marked OPERATOR-LIFTABLE exist because the OTHER record declares a coarse directory, not because this record's targets are contested; an operator who confirms the predecessor does not touch this record's named files may lift any of them with update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. Nearest neighbours are 430 and 446, both of which own git-commit-phase.sh and git-staging-scope.md. Both were considered as absorption targets and rejected: neither names git-commit-scoped.sh, and neither's subject is the V7 post-add verification predicate. The completed V2 staged-deletion record and the closed directory-pathspec policy records are ruled disjoint above with reasons.
