### 459. Design cross-record traceability; verdict on CHANGE_LOG retirement
- **Status**: [PLANNED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None
- **Research**: [459_cross_task_traceability_and_changelog_verdict/reports/01_traceability-design-and-changelog-verdict.md]
- **Plan**: [459_cross_task_traceability_and_changelog_verdict/plans/01_decision-citation-convention.md]

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/ (never .claude/**, a disposable deploy artifact -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Redeploy afterwards via deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout raised to 600000), never a hand-patch of a deployed file. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (both are permitted in this description).

CLASSIFICATION: RESEARCH-AND-DESIGN, VERDICT-FIRST. NO IMPLEMENTATION BEFORE THE DESIGN IS RECORDED AND ACCEPTED. The deliverable is a recorded design with a verdict, not a landed mechanism. A pull toward "just build the obvious thing" is the main way this record goes wrong.

GOAL, in the terms it was asked for: improve the dependencies and references between open, closed and archived records so that CHANGES ARE EASY TO TRACE, referencing the DECISIONS MADE in each record -- and retire `specs/CHANGE_LOG.md` only IF APPROPRIATE, as an OUTCOME of that design rather than a premise. Do not pre-decide retirement. Do not pre-decide a mechanism.

=== THE PROBLEM ===
Work is traceable today only by reading scattered artifacts by hand. There is no path from "this changed" to "which record changed it and on what grounds", and no path from a recorded decision to the places it governs.

RAW MATERIAL THAT ALREADY EXISTS. The research must INVENTORY each of these against the live tree rather than assume its shape or its contents:
  - `dependencies[]` on each `active_projects[]` entry -- ordering edges, consumed by the Kahn-algorithm wave computation.
  - `parent_task` -- a LINEAGE pointer, explicitly NOT a dependency edge. `context/reference/state-management-schema.md`'s "Parent Task Field" section states that a child's dispatch eligibility never consults its parent and that `parent_task` is absent from the wave computation entirely; lineage and ordering "may point in opposite directions for the same pair without contradiction". This is the closest thing the system already has to a traceability edge, and it is deliberately inert.
  - `artifacts[]` on each entry.
  - Per-record `issues.jsonl` and `metrics.jsonl`, `handoffs/`, `records/`, `reports/`, `plans/`, `summaries/`, and `.return-meta.json`. All seven of these were confirmed present in live record directories on 2026-11-02; `prototype/` appears in at least one. Do not assume every record has every one.
  - `specs/events.jsonl` -- a single append-only repository-wide log (2.2 MB in one consumer repository at measurement).
  - `specs/archive/state.json` and its TWO sibling arrays, `completed_projects[]` and `archived_projects[]`.
  - Git history, including the `task {N}: {action}` commit convention.
  - `specs/CHANGE_LOG.md` itself.

=== THE CENTRAL DESIGN TENSION: TASK NUMBERS ARE NOT DURABLE ===
Any proposal must confront this head-on rather than route around it.

A vault operation renumbers active records by SUBTRACTING 1000 and resets `next_project_number`. `rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md` exists precisely because of this: it states that "task numbers are renumbered during vault operations" and are meaningless to a future reader, which is why deliverables must cite durable anchors instead. `context/reference/state-management-schema.md`'s "Renumber survival" section works through which fields the transform rewrites and which it strands -- and documents an actual casualty, a top-level `.artifacts[0].path` pointing at a directory name that is orphaned residue from a recycled number and exists in neither the working tree nor git history. That is this record's problem in miniature, already observed.

Worse, there are AT LEAST TWO INDEPENDENT COUNTERS -- a consumer repository's and this source store's -- and records in this task space already warn that the other repository renumbers on vault operations and that a number must be RE-RESOLVED BY DESCRIPTION rather than trusted. So any traceability scheme keyed on record numbers is broken by construction, in two ways at once: numbers are reused over time, and the same number denotes different work in different repositories.

SHARPEST INSTANCE, WHICH THE RESEARCH MUST ADDRESS EXPLICITLY. The git commit convention is `task {N}: {action}` (`rules/git-workflow.md`, with the standard action table). This bakes a MUTABLE number into IMMUTABLE history. After a vault operation, historical commit messages point at numbers that now denote different records, and nothing in history records which epoch a message belongs to. Say what, if anything, to do about it -- INCLUDING the legitimate answer that it is accepted and worked around rather than fixed. Rewriting history is not an option; changing the convention going forward has a cost; adding an epoch marker is a third option. Pick one and give the grounds.

=== PRECEDENT ALREADY IN THE SYSTEM -- BUILD ON IT, DO NOT REINVENT ===
  - MINTED STABLE IDS ALREADY EXIST AND ARE ALREADY CITED. `issues.jsonl` entries carry `entry_id` of the form `iss_{timestamp_ms}_{random6}` -- verified live, e.g. an entry beginning `iss_1791533278944_` with a six-character suffix. `context/formats/issue-log.md` specifies the form and states that it mirrors `events.jsonl`'s `event_id` and `errors.json`'s `err_{timestamp}` conventions; `context/formats/dispatch-metrics.md` specifies `met_{timestamp_ms}_{random6}` on the same pattern. These ids are IMMUNE TO RENUMBERING and are already cited across records. The convention is settled; the question is what else deserves one.
  - SESSION IDS have the same shape, `sess_{timestamp}_{random}`, minted at GATE IN and carried into commit bodies -- an existing durable join between a commit and the dispatch that produced it. Evaluate whether this already answers more of the problem than it appears to.
  - NAMED ARCHITECTURE DOCUMENTS under `docs/architecture/` are durable named anchors. Measured 2026-11-02, core ships six: `batch-admit-schema.md`, `extension-system.md`, `handoff-schema.md`, `orchestrate-cycle-postflight.md`, `orchestrate-state-machine.md`, `system-overview.md`. They are named, not numbered -- so cite them by name; there is no numeric decision-record id scheme here to extend.
  - Records in this system ALREADY cite decisions by durable name: route labels, numbered decisions inside a specific plan, document names, section headings.
THE DESIGN QUESTION IS WHETHER DECISIONS DESERVE FIRST-CLASS DURABLE IDS OF THAT KIND -- and if so, where they live, who mints them, and what cites them.

=== QUESTIONS THE RESEARCH MUST ANSWER, VERDICT-FIRST ===
(1) What is the MINIMAL durable identifier for a record, and for a DECISION made inside a record, given renumbering and two independent counters? "The existing minted-id convention, applied to X" is a better answer than a new scheme.
(2) Which existing fields ALREADY carry traceability, which are REDUNDANT, and what is GENUINELY MISSING? Prefer strengthening an existing field over minting a new one. `parent_task` is the obvious candidate to strengthen, since it already encodes lineage and is already validated.
(3) Does `specs/CHANGE_LOG.md` carry ANYTHING not recoverable from `specs/archive/` plus git history plus per-record artifacts? Evidence at filing suggests not: it is an archival log that stopped being written for a month across four archival runs with nobody noticing, which is itself evidence about how load-bearing it is. But VERIFY rather than assume.
  MEASURED 2026-11-02, exactly three files in the source store reference CHANGE_LOG, and nothing else does: `context/patterns/todo-archival-reference.md`, `skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md`, and the memory extension's `context/project/memory/knowledge-capture-usage.md`. Re-verify that set; it is small enough that retirement is cheap IF the verdict goes that way.
  RETIREMENT REQUIRES UPDATING A POINTER OUTSIDE THE SOURCE STORE. A consumer repository's `specs/ROADMAP.md` has a `## Maintenance` section stating that "the durable record is `specs/CHANGE_LOG.md` and `specs/archive/`" -- verified live. Retiring the file without fixing that sentence points readers at a durable record that no longer exists. Note that this pointer lives in a consumer repository, so it cannot be fixed by an edit under `agent-system/**`; the design must say how it is handled.
(4) WHO WRITES THE TRACEABILITY DATA, AND WHEN? A scheme that depends on an agent remembering to record a decision WILL DEGRADE EXACTLY AS CHANGE_LOG DID -- that degradation is the best-evidenced fact available here, so treat it as the primary design constraint rather than a caveat. Prefer a mechanism that FALLS OUT OF WORK ALREADY BEING DONE: something already written at a gate, already minted at dispatch, already committed.
(5) WHAT CONSUMES IT? Name the consumers concretely; a traceability scheme with no named reader is a cost with no benefit. The `/orient` record (`orient_command_with_scout_agent_retire_review`) reconstructs "what happened and why" BY HAND across precisely this scattered state -- its own description enumerates the same spread -- and is the obvious first consumer. RELATE THE DESIGN TO IT rather than duplicating it: /orient is read-only by construction and renders a brief to the user, so it is a reader of traceability data, never a writer of it.

=== HARD CONSTRAINTS ===
- THE ACCEPTANCE CRITERION IS A RECORDED DESIGN WITH A VERDICT ON CHANGE_LOG, NOT A LANDED MECHANISM.
- RESPECT THE ADDITIVE-TO-REDUCTIVE RATIO. `systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency` measured roughly SIXTEEN non-terminal records that add machinery against roughly THREE that reduce it, and concluded that working the backlog to completion makes the system larger. A design that adds a new file format, a new script AND a new gate to track work that git already tracks is THE WRONG ANSWER. "Strengthen two existing fields and retire one file" is a better outcome than any new subsystem.
- IF THE HONEST VERDICT IS THAT CURRENT MECHANISMS SUFFICE and only CHANGE_LOG's pointer needs fixing, THAT IS A COMPLETE AND SUCCESSFUL OUTCOME. Say so and stop. A refutation of the premise is a result, not a failure to deliver.
- Every existing-mechanism claim must be RE-VERIFIED against the live tree. Several figures in this description were measured on 2026-11-02 and will drift.

=== SEQUENCING: RECOMMENDATION ONLY, DELIBERATELY NOT A DEPENDENCY EDGE ===
No `dependencies[]` edge is declared onto the /todo consolidation record (`consolidate_duplicated_todo_implementation`), following this task space's own precedent that a recommendation is preferable to an edge that would overconstrain.

THE RECOMMENDATION: IF this design concludes that CHANGE_LOG should be KEPT AND WRITTEN, that writing behavior MUST NOT LAND IN /todo until the consolidation record has settled which /todo implementation is authoritative. The abandoned `todo_consolidate_and_optimize` record's phase-ordering rationale applies verbatim -- remove the divergence surface BEFORE new behavior lands on it. Landing a CHANGE_LOG writer into one of two divergent copies is how the current defect was created in the first place: the never-dispatched skill carries CHANGE_LOG stages that never run.

IF THE VERDICT IS RETIREMENT, there is no /todo behavior to land and NO INTERACTION with that record at all, which is why this is a recommendation rather than an edge.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===
- A recorded design answering (1) through (5), verdict-first.
- An EXPLICIT KEEP-OR-RETIRE VERDICT on `specs/CHANGE_LOG.md`, with the grounds that settled it stated plainly enough that a reader can disagree with the grounds rather than only with the conclusion.
- An explicit answer on the `task {N}:` commit convention, including "accepted and worked around" as a permitted answer with grounds.
- Every existing-mechanism claim re-verified against the live tree, with the measurement stated.
- NO IMPLEMENTATION in this record beyond what the design itself requires -- and if the design requires none, none.

=== DECLARED file_scope IS THE ARTIFACT DIRECTORY ONLY, DELIBERATELY ===
This record's file_scope names only its own `specs/` artifact directory. Every file named above --
`context/reference/state-management-schema.md`, `rules/git-workflow.md`,
`rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md`, `context/patterns/todo-archival-reference.md` and the
rest -- is an INPUT TO BE READ, not an edit target. A design record that declares its reading list
as territory manufactures collisions with every record that genuinely edits those files, and
`context/reference/state-management-schema.md` in particular is also in the /todo consolidation
record's scope, which would turn a deliberate non-edge into a shared-territory batching obligation
for no reason. IF the accepted design turns out to require edits under `agent-system/**`, WIDEN
file_scope at that point via `update-task-status.sh` and re-run the collision check -- do not edit
outside the declared scope silently.

### 458. Consolidate the duplicated /todo implementation
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 459

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/ (never .claude/**, a disposable deploy artifact -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Redeploy afterwards via deploy-headless.sh using the bounded-wait idiom (timeout 540, Bash tool timeout raised to 600000), never a hand-patch of a deployed file. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (both are permitted in this description).

GOAL. Collapse the two divergent implementations of /todo into one authoritative implementation, and fix an `archived_projects`-blind detection bug once in the survivor rather than twice in the copies. NET DELETION.

=== THIS IS A REVIVAL, AND 328 CANNOT ABSORB IT ===
This work is the first phase of the abandoned record `todo_consolidate_and_optimize`, which was abandoned into `systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency` (planned). That record cannot deliver it. Its acceptance criterion is explicitly behavior-preserving -- "complete on a measured net reduction in corpus size, with every gate's finding set and every suite's assertion set provably unchanged" (verified verbatim in its description, 2026-11-02). Consolidating /todo requires DECIDING WHICH IMPLEMENTATION IS AUTHORITATIVE, which changes behavior wherever the two diverge, so it fails that criterion by construction. Corroborating measurement, 2026-11-02: that record's live plan `plans/03_markdown-corpus-consolidation.md` mentions `skill-todo` ZERO times and `CHANGE_LOG` ZERO times -- the work is not in its plan and was not silently inherited. Revive it here; do not re-abandon it into that record.

=== THE DUPLICATION (all figures re-measured 2026-11-02; re-measure again before quoting) ===
`skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md` hand-implements the same archival stages that `commands/todo.md` implements, and `commands/todo.md` NEVER DISPATCHES IT.
  - `commands/todo.md`: 886 lines. The string `skill-todo` appears ZERO times in it. Its allowed-tools line (line 3) is `Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep, Bash(git:*), Bash(mv:*), Bash(mkdir:*), Bash(ls:*), Bash(find:*), Bash(jq:*), TaskCreate, TaskUpdate, AskUserQuestion` -- NO Skill entry, so it could not dispatch the skill even if it named it.
  - `skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md`: 1120 lines (the abandoned record measured ~1,170; the live figure is 1120). Combined /todo footprint 2006 lines, of which the never-dispatched skill is roughly 56%.
  - Every out-of-directory reference to `skill-todo` is documentation, manifest registration, a merge source, a lint registry or a test -- none is a dispatch. Measured: 21 files outside `skills/skill-todo/` mention it, concentrated in `scripts/tests/test-todo-move-pair-staging.sh` (18 mentions, a test), plus `manifest.json`, `merge-sources/claudemd.md`, `context/reference/skill-agent-mapping.md`, `context/architecture/system-overview.md`, `scripts/lint/lint-postflight-boundary.sh`, `scripts/lint/lint-state-writer-boundary.sh`, `scripts/validate-state.sh`, `scripts/skill-base.sh`, `scripts/memory-harvest.sh` and others. Re-enumerate before acting: that set determines the blast radius of either direction below.

=== FRAME AS CONSOLIDATION, NOT DELETION ===
`skill-todo` is manifest-registered and deployed, so it appears in the harness's own Skill listing and IS invocable as `Skill(skill-todo)` even though `/todo` never calls it. DELETING THE FILE ALONE BREAKS THAT PATH. Pick ONE source of truth, then either:
  (a) make the other a thin delegator to the survivor, or
  (b) remove it TOGETHER WITH its `manifest.json` registration, its `index-entries.json` entry if present, and every registry/lint reference that would otherwise dangle, in the same change.
Then reconcile the documentation. Do not leave a registered-but-absent skill or an unregistered-but-present one; `validate-index.sh` and the agent/skill contract lints are the mechanical check on that.

=== ALSO CORRECT context/architecture/system-overview.md ===
Line 417 reads `| /todo | Direct | skill-todo | (no agent) | B |`, which places `skill-todo` in /todo's execution path. It is not in that path. The abandoned record identified this misleading row as the reason the duplication survived unnoticed -- a reader checking how /todo works is told the skill is involved. Re-verify the line number and the row's current text before editing it, and correct it to whatever the consolidation makes true.

=== WHAT THE DIVERGENCE HAS ALREADY COST (measured live, 2026-11-02 -- concrete, not hypothetical) ===
`skill-todo/SKILL.md` advertises automated CHANGE_LOG maintenance in its own frontmatter description ("Archive completed, abandoned, and expanded tasks with CHANGE_LOG.md updates and memory harvest suggestions") and in its `<role>` line, and carries CHANGE_LOG stages. `commands/todo.md` has NO CHANGE_LOG step at all -- the string `CHANGE_LOG` appears ZERO times in its 886 lines. Because the skill is never dispatched, those stages never run.

Consequence in a consumer repository, measured: `specs/CHANGE_LOG.md`'s newest entry is dated 2026-10-04, while FOUR archival runs have landed since -- 2026-10-09 (17 tasks), 2026-10-13 (19), 2026-10-15 (18) and 2026-11-02 (19), each a `todo: archive N completed tasks` commit. That is 73 archived tasks absent from a log whose own entries are titled "Archive N completed tasks". Nobody noticed for a month.

DO NOT FIX CHANGE_LOG HERE. The companion record `cross_task_traceability_and_changelog_verdict` owns the question of whether CHANGE_LOG should exist at all, and fixing it here would prejudge that. Cite this only as evidence of what two divergence surfaces cost.

=== FOLD IN: ORPHAN AND MISPLACED-DIRECTORY DETECTION IGNORES archived_projects (correctness bug) ===
Both implementations' orphan and misplaced-directory scans query ONLY `.completed_projects[]` in `specs/archive/state.json`. Abandoned tasks route to the SIBLING `.archived_projects[]` array, so every directory belonging to an abandoned-then-archived task reads as UNTRACKED -- an orphan that is not one. The archive write itself is correct (it populates both arrays); only the read-back is blind.

RE-COUNTED 2026-11-02: SIX occurrences of the defective lookup, exactly as the abandoned record measured. `commands/todo.md` lines 71, 88 and 127 (orphans-in-specs, orphans-in-archive, and the misplaced-directory scan at section 2.6); `skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md` lines 246, 263 and 294 (the same three scans). Re-derive these line numbers before editing -- they shift with any other change to either file.

THIS IS WHY THE BUG BELONGS IN THIS RECORD RATHER THAN ITS OWN. Fixing it before consolidating means fixing it twice and leaving the two copies free to diverge again on the fix. Fix it ONCE, in the survivor.

Confirmed still live: a /todo run in a consumer repository on 2026-11-02 used the defective query. It happened to find zero orphans, so nothing was mis-archived, but the scan was not trustworthy and a clean result from it proves nothing.

=== SECOND DUPLICATION SURFACE, NEWLY MEASURED: THE VAULT OPERATION ===
The vault operation -- the single riskiest state transform in the system, which renumbers active tasks by subtracting 1000 and resets `next_project_number` -- is ALSO implemented twice. `commands/todo.md` section 5.7 ("Vault Operation (when next_project_number > 1000)", with the renumber at step 5.7.7) and `skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md` stage 10 both carry it, including both AskUserQuestion confirmation prompts and both dependency-array rewrites. `context/reference/state-management-schema.md`'s "Renumber survival" discussion names `commands/todo.md`'s step as THE live renumbering path, and separately quarantines `scripts/deprecated/vault-operation.sh` as an older copy that is NOT the live path. A third live copy inside the never-dispatched skill is the same hazard that quarantine exists to prevent. Treat the vault operation as a first-class part of the consolidation, not an incidental block: whichever implementation survives must be the one the schema document names, or that document must be corrected in the same change.

=== COLLISION FIELD IS CLEAR (verified 2026-11-02) ===
The two records the abandoned task named as competing editors of `commands/todo.md` -- a scoped-commit pathspec change and the `commands/task.md` slimming record -- are both terminal and archived. Those overlap notes are STALE; do not carry them forward as live.
Current measurement: NO non-terminal record's file_scope names `commands/todo.md` or `skill-todo`. One record does name `skills/skill-todo/SKILL.md` in its file_scope -- `archival_silently_untracks_evidence_gate_targets` -- but its status is `completed`, awaiting archival only. Re-run the check at dispatch time; it is one jq query over `active_projects[].file_scope`.

=== WHY THIS SHAPE IS RIGHT ===
`systematic_script_and_test_corpus_efficiency` measured the backlog's additive-to-reductive ratio: roughly SIXTEEN non-terminal records add machinery (a lint, gate, guard, warning, chokepoint or enforcement) against roughly THREE that reduce it, and concluded that working the backlog to completion makes the system larger. This record is net deletion AND a correctness fix at once, so it runs WITH that doctrine rather than against it. Declare a net-line figure and report it; a consolidation that does not reduce total lines has probably become a rewrite and should be re-scoped.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===
- ONE authoritative /todo implementation. The other is a thin delegator to it, or is removed together with its manifest registration and every registry/lint reference that would otherwise dangle.
- `Skill(skill-todo)` either still resolves (delegator kept) or is deliberately and completely deregistered -- never registered-but-absent.
- `context/architecture/system-overview.md`'s /todo routing row states what is actually true after the change.
- The `archived_projects` detection bug is fixed ONCE, in the surviving copy, across all three scans (orphans-in-specs, orphans-in-archive, misplaced directories). A fixture or test demonstrates that an abandoned-then-archived task directory is no longer reported as an orphan.
- The vault operation exists in exactly one live place, and `context/reference/state-management-schema.md`'s "Renumber survival" section names that place correctly.
- `validate-index.sh`, `validate-state.sh`, `lint-agent-contracts.sh`, `lint-postflight-boundary.sh` and `lint-state-writer-boundary.sh` are all green; `scripts/tests/test-todo-move-pair-staging.sh` and `test-validate-state.sh` still pass or are updated with their assertions' intent preserved.
- A REPORTED NET-LINE FIGURE (lines before, lines after, delta).
- Redeployed via deploy-headless.sh to a clean verify-deploy.sh.

### 457. Repo-specific content has leaked into the shared agent-system source store
- **Effort**: medium
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: Task 446, Task 455

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/{formal,lean}/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh to RESULT=landed_verify_clean. No task-number references in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md) and no record-versioning language (rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

GOAL. Repository-specific content from one consuming Lean project has leaked into the SHARED agent-system source store, so every other repository deploying the formal or lean extension receives context files asserting facts about a Lean tree it does not have. Resolve the four affected files, choosing PER FILE between relocation and de-specialization, and record the grounds that settled each choice.

THE ROUTE DECISION IS DELIBERATELY NOT PRE-MADE. See the PER-FILE ROUTE DECISION section below. A dispatch that quietly defaults to one route for all files has not done the work this record asks for.

STANDING INSTRUCTION: re-verify every path, line number and hit count below against the tree before citing it again. They were measured at filing and they drift.

=== WHAT WAS MEASURED (2026-11-02, at HEAD 86b8fc759) ===

Pattern `FormalSystem|BimodalLogic|BimodalTools|ProofChecker|PlusLanguage`, counted over TRACKED files in the source store (`git ls-files agent-system/`). Hit counts are matching LINES, not occurrences.

The four substantive files:

- agent-system/extensions/formal/context/project/logic/domain/frame-constraint-landscape.md -- 37 hits. Line 5 states the coupling outright, verbatim: "Every Lean name below is fully qualified and lives in the ProofChecker (BimodalLogic) tree."
- agent-system/extensions/formal/context/project/logic/domain/ztime-no-finite-carrier-fmp.md -- 26 hits. The same coupling is stated at line 16: "qualified and lives in the ProofChecker (BimodalLogic) tree or in the named probe file."
- agent-system/extensions/lean/context/project/lean4/domain/sentence-translation-contract.md -- 17 hits. Hardcodes FormalSystem/SourceLanguage/Sentence.lean, BimodalTools/SentenceExport.lean and Tests/BimodalTest/ paths.
- agent-system/extensions/lean/context/project/lean4/domain/ladder-counter-observables.md -- 1 hit, at line 10, naming FormalSystem/Metalogic/Decidability/RaySummary/LadderModel.lean. Likely a trivial de-specialization.

CONSEQUENCE: the formal and lean extensions ship these files to every consumer. A repository with no such Lean tree receives domain context whose named declarations and paths do not exist for it, and nothing currently detects this.

=== PER-FILE ROUTE DECISION -- NOT PRE-DECIDED, BY EXPLICIT RULING AT FILING ===

Two routes are on the table for each of the three substantive files. The implementer must choose PER FILE and record the grounds that settled it. The choice may legitimately differ per file.

ROUTE A -- RELOCATE. Move the file out of the shared store into the consuming repository's own per-repo context location, context/repo/, which is never overwritten by a deploy and is protected by that repository's .syncprotect (which already protects context/repo/project-overview.md). COST: whatever generic value the file carries for other Lean projects is lost to them.

ROUTE B -- DE-SPECIALIZE IN PLACE. Keep the file in the shared store, strip repository names and hardcoded paths, rewrite as generic guidance. COST: more work, and it risks discarding the concrete grounding that makes the file useful at all. A file whose every claim is a specific Lean declaration may not survive de-specialization as anything a reader can use.

Neither route is pre-selected and neither is the default. For frame-constraint-landscape.md and ztime-no-finite-carrier-fmp.md in particular, note that their own opening lines ANNOUNCE the coupling rather than hiding it -- weigh whether that is a file honestly declaring its scope (favouring Route A) or a fixable framing (favouring Route B).

=== TRIAGE INVENTORY -- THE FULL SWEEP, NOT JUST THE FOUR ===

The same sweep found 24 tracked files with at least one hit. The acceptance clause below is stated over the whole sweep, so the rest must be triaged, not ignored. Measured counts at filing:

EXPLICITLY LEGITIMATE, DO NOT TOUCH:
- agent-system/extensions/lean/scripts/tests/test-lean-src-roots.sh -- 11 hits. Lines 63-95 build a deliberate "BimodalLogic-shaped lakefile.toml" fixture (srcDir = "Tests" on one lib, several libs) and are correctly repo-named: the fixture's whole purpose is to exercise that shape.
- agent-system/extensions/core/context/reference/known-consumer-repos.json -- 2 hits, lines 18-19. A registry of consumer repositories; naming them is the file's function.

COSMETIC, DECIDE SEPARATELY WHETHER IT IS WORTH TOUCHING AT ALL:
- agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh -- 7 hits, including lines 85, 88, 309 and 325, which embed FormalSystem/, Tests/ and BimodalTools/ in explanatory comments only. No behavioral coupling: the script does not branch on these names.
- agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-plan-file-scope-harvest.sh -- 36 hits, the single largest count in the sweep. This is the harvest script's test, so its fixtures mirror those same comments; it is listed here rather than above only because its disposition follows whatever is decided for the script, and a 36-hit file should not be discovered unmentioned.

PLAUSIBLY LEGITIMATE, CONFIRM RATHER THAN ASSUME (spot-checked at filing; each looked like illustrative-example or provenance-comment use, which is the "consciously retained" class the acceptance clause allows):
- agent-system/extensions/literature/context/guides/literature-organization.md -- 8 hits, example filenames (Brastmckie_2024_BimodalLogic.pdf/.md) in a directory-layout illustration.
- agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/validate-state.sh -- 4 hits, lines 167, 1015, 1025 and 1032: measurement provenance in comments (where a historical count or a one-off repair was observed).
- agent-system/extensions/core/context/contracts/territory.md -- 2 hits, lines 66 and 70, an illustrative file_scope example.
- agent-system/extensions/typst/context/project/typst/standards/notation-conventions.md -- 1 hit, line 92, an illustrative repo-root-relative path.
- The remainder, each 1-4 hits: agent-system/extensions/literature/agents/literature-agent.md (4); agent-system/extensions/literature/context/project/literature/patterns/literature-rebuild-mode.md (3); agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh (3); agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-orchestrate-batch-admit.sh (2); agent-system/extensions/core/context/patterns/mcp-server-ownership.md (2); agent-system/extensions/core/context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md (2); agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/test-roadmap-generation.sh (1); agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/check-extension-docs.sh (1); agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/backfill-file-scope.sh (1); agent-system/extensions/core/context/guides/hard-mode-routing.md (1); agent-system/extensions/core/context/contracts/anti-analysis.md (1); agent-system/extensions/literature/scripts/literature-audit.sh (1).

=== NOT A BOUNDARY-VIOLATION RECORD -- VERIFIED CLEAN IN THE OTHER DIRECTION ===

This is pollution of the shared store by repo-specific CONTENT. It is NOT an instance of hand-authoring under a deploy tree. Verified at filing: diff -rq found byte-exact matches between the BimodalLogic deploy tree and the source store for .claude/context/project/lean4/, .claude/context/project/logic/, and all six deployed skill-lean-*/skill-lake-repair skills. There is no hand-authored local content under that repository's .claude/**. Do not re-frame this record as a source-store/deploy boundary violation.

=== DETECTION GAP -- RECORDED HERE, OWNED ELSEWHERE ===

Nothing currently catches repo-specific content entering the shared store. A second task for a detection gate was explicitly DECLINED at filing, so this record does not own one and must not grow one.

The adjacent open record is cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness (numbered 401 in this task space at filing; re-resolve it by description rather than trusting the number). That record covers the cross-repo overlap/dependency gate being blind when two repositories file work against ONE shared store -- which is DUPLICATE-WORK detection, a different problem from content pollution. Any gate proposal belongs there, after that record's own owner-adjudication escalation is answered, and not as a third overlapping record.

=== DO NOT DUPLICATE -- EACH CHECKED AT FILING ===

- cross_repo_source_store_gate_blindness (401) -- cross-repo duplicate-work detection, as above.
- migrate_mis_sited_agent_system_tasks (390) -- owns moving mis-sited RECORDS into this task space. A data migration of task records, not of content.
- agent_system_task_siting_and_target_root (391) -- owns record siting and target-root resolution.

None of the three addresses repo-coupled CONTENT inside a shared store. NO DEPENDENCY EDGES were added to any of them, deliberately: they are adjacent, not blocking, and none of them has to land before this one can.

=== NOTE AT FILING -- DIRTY WORKING TREE ===

The source store's working tree was dirty at filing, including agent-system/extensions/lean/context/project/lean4/domain/ladder-counter-observables.md -- one of the four files above -- as well as agent-system/extensions/lean/manifest.json, agent-system/extensions/lean/index-entries.json, agent-system/extensions/lean/scripts/lean-challenge-snapshot.sh and two of its tests, plus agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/lake-build-guard.sh and its test. Re-check that file's state before editing it; the 1-hit measurement above was taken against the working tree, not against HEAD.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

1. Each of the three substantive files (frame-constraint-landscape.md, ztime-no-finite-carrier-fmp.md, sentence-translation-contract.md) has a RECORDED route decision with the grounds that settled it, and has been relocated or de-specialized accordingly.
2. The 1-hit file (ladder-counter-observables.md) is resolved.
3. A grep for the five repo identifiers over tracked shared-store content returns only the self-labelled fixture, the consumer registry, and whatever comments were consciously retained -- each retention being a decision someone made, not a file that was never looked at.
4. The store is redeployed to a clean verify.

=== PROVENANCE ===

specs/reviews/review-2026-11-02.md in ~/Projects/BimodalLogic, High Priority Issues section. That review separately confirmed that BimodalLogic's own agent-system defects are already tracked by its frozen-record tracker, which is why this record belongs in the source store's task space and not in that repository's.

### 456. Tag version gate unpassable for private manifest
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: None

**Description**: /tag's version-consistency gate is unpassable when a manifest version intentionally does not track release tags

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never .claude/**, a disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh.

ORIGIN: hit live on 2026-10-19 while tagging v0.4.32 in the PersonalWebsite repo. Duplicate check performed at creation time: a TODO.md + state.json scan for /tag and version-consistency returned only false positives (the substring "tag" inside "stage"). No existing task covers this.

== PROBLEM (reproducible on EVERY /tag invocation in PersonalWebsite) ==

skill-tag Step 3.5 "Validate Version Consistency" (SKILL.md line ~184) discovers any
pyproject.toml / setup.cfg / setup.py / package.json / Cargo.toml within maxdepth 3 of the repo
root, extracts its declared `version`, and HARD-FAILS when any one of them differs from the
computed tag. The gate has NO notion of a manifest whose version field is not the release version.

In PersonalWebsite, package.json is
  {"name":"benbrastmckie-website","version":"0.1.0","private":true}
-- an unpublished Astro site deployed to Cloudflare Pages. Under npm's own semantics a
`private: true` package is never published, so its `version` is inert: no consumer reads it.
Meanwhile that repo's vX.Y.Z tags are DEPLOYMENT markers (v0.4.32 reached today, 32 patch
deploys).

Evidence the two axes were never coupled (measured, re-verify before planning): iterating
`git show $c:package.json | jq -r .version` over every commit returned by
`git log --format=%h -- package.json` yields 0.1.0 for EVERY such commit. The declared version has
never once equaled a tag.

Consequence: the gate can never pass there, and BOTH resolutions it prints are wrong.
  - "Update the mismatched manifest(s) to 0.4.32, commit, then re-run /tag" invents a
    version-tracking convention for a field no consumer reads, and demands a dummy commit before
    every single deploy.
  - "--skip-version-check" must then be passed on every tag forever. A gate that permanently
    requires its own override carries zero signal and trains the user to pass the override
    reflexively -- which also disarms it in the repos where it WOULD be a real catch (a genuinely
    published package whose manifest drifted).

== ROOT FIXES TO EVALUATE -- research/plan must RULE on these, not assume ==

1. PREFERRED: treat `"private": true` in package.json as "this manifest declares no release
   version" and exclude it from the gate. Principled (it is npm's own semantics), needs zero
   configuration, and fixes the whole class of private-site repos at once. Handle the analogous
   idiom in the same pass: Cargo.toml `publish = false`.
   DO NOT "FIX" THESE -- already correct: pyproject.toml `dynamic = ["version"]` and Cargo
   `version.workspace = true` ALREADY yield empty from the existing extractors and are therefore
   already handled; SKILL.md's own inline comments say so explicitly.

2. GENERAL ESCAPE HATCH, complementary to 1 (NOT a substitute): an explicit per-repo opt-out for a
   manifest that intentionally diverges, since a NON-private manifest can still legitimately not
   track tags. Follow the existing `.syncprotect` precedent documented in CLAUDE.md (project root,
   one relative path per line, `#` comments, blank lines ignored) rather than inventing a new
   config shape.

3. WEAKER -- record as considered-and-rejected unless 1 and 2 both prove insufficient: infer intent
   from history, i.e. if the declared version has never equaled any existing tag, downgrade the
   gate from fatal to a warning. Rejected by default because it is implicit and silently weakens a
   real gate.

HARD CONSTRAINT on any chosen design: a genuinely published (non-private) manifest whose version
really HAS drifted from the computed tag MUST still hard-fail. Do not soften the gate into a
universal warning.

== SECONDARY DEFECT (same file, folded into this task deliberately -- shares one plan) ==

Step 3.6's changelog extraction (SKILL.md line ~344) is
  changelog_section=$(awk -v ver="$new_version_bare" '$0 ~ "^## \\[" ver "\\]" {found=1; next} ...')
The on-disk source store is CORRECT (verified: it reads `$0 ~ ...`). But when the skill body is
loaded WITH invocation arguments, the bare `$0` token is interpolated with those args -- the copy
actually executed on 2026-10-19 literally read
  --patch ~ "^## \\[" ver "\\]"
a corrupt awk program. `$*` and `$1` elsewhere in the same file survived intact, so the hazard is
specific to bare `$0`.

Any agent executing that snippet verbatim in a repo that DOES have a CHANGELOG.md would
mis-extract the section or error outright. PersonalWebsite only dodged it because it has no
CHANGELOG.md, so that branch never ran.

Fix direction: rewrite so no bare `$0` appears in skill-embedded bash. Note `index($0,...)` and
`match($0,...)` are equally exposed -- prefer a grep+sed formulation, or `{line=$0}` ONLY if that
form is itself verified safe under argument interpolation. Then audit the rest of the core
extension's skill-embedded bash for the same `$0` interpolation hazard.

Why this is one task, not two: both defects live in the same file (skill-tag/SKILL.md), both are in
the same /tag invocation path, both were found in one diagnosis, and both require the same
redeploy + the same single /tag verification run. Splitting them would mean two plans touching the
same file -- a serialized pair with no parallelism gained.

== EDIT TARGETS (source-store-deploy-boundary rule applies) ==

  agent-system/extensions/core/skills/skill-tag/SKILL.md
      Step 3.5 (line ~184), Step 3.6 (line ~344), and the Error Handling examples that mirror
      their output verbatim
  agent-system/extensions/core/commands/tag.md
      flag table + Requirements section, kept in sync with the skill

Confirmed at creation time: `grep -rln "skip-version-check"` over the source store matches exactly
these two files. The gate is inline bash in SKILL.md, not a separate script, so there is NO
scripts/ file to change.

== ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ==

- `/tag --patch` in PersonalWebsite passes the version-consistency gate with NO override flag and
  NO package.json edit.
- A mismatch in a genuinely published (non-private) manifest still fails fatally.
- The gate's informational "no declared version found" path and both `--skip-*` disclosure paths
  keep working, including under `--dry-run` (all three sit before the dry-run exit).
- commands/tag.md documents whatever new opt-out mechanism lands.
- No bare `$0` remains in skill-embedded bash in skill-tag/SKILL.md; the rest of the core
  extension's skills are audited for the same hazard and findings recorded.

Severity: blocking-in-practice for /tag in any private-manifest repo (the gate cannot pass without
a permanent override), plus a latent correctness bug in Step 3.6 for any repo with a CHANGELOG.md.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- the first `check-module-invariants.sh` run after wiring a new module reported FOUR mechanical failures, all fixed in one pass, all predictable consequences of adding a module rather than defects in the work:
  INV -- 4 stale generated inventory blocks, fixed by re-running with --emit-inventory.
  C16 -- two env_linter unusedArguments findings needing two entries in the repository's scripts/nolints.json.
  C23 -- one Uppercase_x identifier flagged by the naming check, fixed by a rename.
  C29 -- a linter suppression carrying no reason comment, fixed by adding a reason block naming the linter and the deletion trial.
No work was wrong; the cost is one avoidable fix-up pass per newly wired module.

WHY THIS HAS AN AGENT-SYSTEM HOME AT ALL. The core extension ALREADY encodes knowledge of this exact gate, for exactly this reason. Verified in the source store at creation time (around these lines; re-derive before editing): scripts/plan-file-scope-harvest.sh carries a `Generated-surface appending` stage documented in its own header, which states that any harvested `.lean` path "mechanically forces regeneration of a fixed set of derived surfaces in this repository's generator toolchain (`scripts/check-module-invariants.sh` and `scripts/typst-sync-check.sh`)" -- the status-table typst file, every markdown README whose `<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: inventory -->` block covers a directory on the edited file's ancestor chain, and narrowly the automation module map and the machine-readable axiom appendix. Its stated rationale is that a plan harvesting a `.lean` path but omitting these "either blocks on the pre-commit typst-sync-check.sh gate or fails check-module-invariants.sh's INV check mid-implementation -- this has happened twice independently". A `generated_surfaces_available()` predicate gates the whole stage on both repository scripts existing, so the behaviour is inert in a repository that carries neither generator, and `generated_inventory_readmes()` deliberately mirrors the gate's own BEGIN_RE/fence/dir= parsing.
So the INV half of the cited incident is a MISS BY AN EXISTING MECHANISM, not a new concern: the stage exists precisely to make stale inventory blocks a declared consequence. Determine FIRST why it did not cover this case -- whether the wiring edit harvested no `.lean` path, whether the ancestor-chain walk missed the new module's READMEs, or whether the stage simply does not run on the plan shape used -- and fix that. That finding is the primary deliverable.

SCOPE -- narrow, and bounded by what belongs in the agent system:
(1) Establish and record why the existing generated-surface appending did not cover the four stale inventory blocks, and close that gap if it is a gap. If the stage behaved correctly and the plan simply harvested no `.lean` path, say so and stop -- do not manufacture a fix.
(2) Rule on whether the OTHER three failure classes (a lint-suppression allowlist entry, an identifier-naming check, a suppression-needs-a-reason check) belong in this stage at all. ARGUE IT BOTH WAYS AND RECORD THE RULING. Against: the stage's single documented job is appending GENERATED SURFACES a `.lean` edit forces regeneration of, and a nolints.json entry or a rename is not a generated surface -- widening the stage to 'things the gate will probably complain about' would blur a crisp contract and invite repository-specific guesswork into core. For: all four arise from the same act (wiring a module), the stage already carries repository-specific knowledge of this very gate, and a one-line pre-flag costs far less than a fix-up pass. A defensible outcome here is 'no, and here is why' -- record it rather than widening by default.
(3) THE REPOSITORY-LOCAL HALF IS OUT OF SCOPE FOR THIS RECORD. The actual remedies for C16, C23 and C29 -- scripts/nolints.json entries, the rename, the reason block -- and the human-facing wiring directive that would warn about them live in the BimodalLogic repository (its scripts/check-module-invariants.sh, scripts/nolints.json, docs/project-info/MAINTENANCE.md and ORGANISATION.md all reference this gate). Those are not agent-system files and must not be edited from this record. If ruling (2) concludes the warning belongs in a human-facing wiring directive rather than in core's harvest stage, the correct outcome is to say so and have a BimodalLogic-local record created for it -- not to reach across the boundary.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Preserve `generated_surfaces_available()`'s gating exactly. Anything added must stay INERT in a repository (or fixture) that does not carry both generator scripts. Core must not acquire a hard dependency on one repository's toolchain.
- Do NOT let `generated_inventory_readmes()` drift from the gate it mirrors. Its header states it mirrors check-module-invariants.sh's own BEGIN_RE/fence/dir= parsing and deliberately NOT its rows=/cols=/filter=/sort= rendering semantics. Keep that boundary and keep the comment accurate.
- Append only. The stage augments a plan's declared file_scope with surfaces the edit will force; it must never remove or narrow a planner's declared paths.
- No task-number references in anything landing under agent-system/**.
- Resist scope growth. This is the lowest-value item of its batch. If the honest answer to (1) and (2) is 'the mechanism is correct and the remaining friction is repository-local', that is a complete and successful outcome.

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-plan-file-scope-harvest.sh with a fixture reproducing the cited wiring shape: a newly wired module whose ancestor-chain READMEs carry generated inventory blocks. Pin whichever behaviour the ruling selects.
- Pin the inert case: a fixture repository carrying neither generator script gets no appended surfaces.
- Pin that the stage remains append-only (a planner's declared paths survive unchanged).
- Use the scoped narrowing (scripts/tests/run-all.sh --only SUITE), not the full aggregator.

TERRITORY EDGES:

SURFACE A -- typst/generated/status.typ. iss_1793832006386_cqjHI0, class gate-collision, severity

typst-sync-check.sh Check 2: the formalsystem-line-count stamp had drifted committed=377507

surface. The remedy taken was the hook's own documented one -- scripts/typst-sync-check.sh --fix,
staged with the phase commit -- NOT --no-verify, since the drift was caused by this task rather than

SURFACE B -- the repository-root README.md. iss_1793832388723_rMKo4p, class scope-drift, severity
minor, phase implement, dispatch_seq 3. scripts/check-module-invariants.sh --emit-inventory rewrote
TWO files, not the one the plan declared: FormalSystem/OpenLanguage/README.md (declared) and the
repository-root README.md, whose generated metrics block carries live lines-of-code and
comment-line counts that this task's +97 Lean lines move. Both are generator-owned and neither was
hand-edited; the root README was carried in the phase-3 commit as a generator output.
WHY THIS ONE IS A SHARP PROBE OF THE EXISTING MECHANISM: the root README is on the ancestor chain of
EVERY FormalSystem file, so if generated_inventory_readmes()'s ancestor-chain walk were working as
its header describes, this surface should already have been appended for any harvested
FormalSystem/**.lean path. Part (1) can therefore be answered by deciding between exactly two
hypotheses: the walk terminates before reaching the repository root, or the root README's block is
not recognised by the BEGIN_RE/fence/dir= parsing the function deliberately mirrors. Settle which,
by reading the function against the root README's actual block, before considering any wider change.

=== A FOURTH EXEMPLAR FOR PART (2)'s RULING ===
iss_1793830921417_rJPnwz, class scope, severity COSTLY, phase research, dispatch_seq 1.
check-module-invariants.sh's C15 SECOND assertion requires that every declaration named by a
docs/theorem-index.md row carry a "Paper:" line in its own /-- doc comment ("Paper: -- (reason)" for

makes it easy to miss: sibling declarations with NO ledger row (stab_of_box, ofut_of_stab,
opast_of_stab) correctly carry no such line, so the obligation is per-ROW, not per-declaration.
Weigh this as a fourth member of part (2)'s class, alongside the lint-suppression allowlist entry,
the identifier-naming check and the suppression-needs-a-reason check. It is the STRONGEST of the
four for inclusion and should be argued as such rather than lumped in: unlike a rename or an
allowlist entry, a ledger row MECHANICALLY determines the obligation, so it is derivable from a
declared surface (docs/theorem-index.md) by exactly the kind of parse the stage already performs --
which makes it much closer to a generated-surface consequence than to "things the gate will probably
complain about". If part (2)'s ruling is nonetheless "no", that argument is the one it has to answer.
PART (3)'s BOUNDARY STILL APPLIES: the repository-local remedy (the "Paper:" lines themselves, and

LIVE SYMPTOM -- a detached evidence-gate run COMPLETED SUCCESSFULLY (exit 0, 31 PASS) and its completion notification was not acted on before the turn ended. The team lead flagged a stall and the gate was re-run in the foreground, producing the identical result (exit 0, 31 PASS) at a MEASURED cost of about 5 minutes plus the stall triage. The implementer recorded the lesson in the entry itself: for a gate whose result is needed in the same turn, run it in the foreground within the tool timeout.

FIRST OBLIGATION OF THIS RECORD: DETERMINE WHETHER IT IS ALREADY FIXED, AND CLOSE AS NO-CHANGE IF SO. The incident is dated 2026-10-12. This repository's #376 -- "Lean implementation agents mandate the detach-then-await-notification path core forbids, stranding dispatches mid-phase" -- is COMPLETED, with a last_updated of 2026-10-17, and its file_scope includes context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md and all four lean agent definitions. So the cited incident PREDATES that fix by five days, and the agent that hit it was running the pre-fix lean contract. Research MUST first establish whether the landed wording already governs this case. If it does, close this record as no-change with the finding recorded -- that is a successful outcome here, not a failure.

WHAT IS NEVERTHELESS NOT COVERED, AS FAR AS A SOURCE-STORE READ AT CREATION TIME CAN TELL (re-derive; line numbers move):
- The existing foreground-preference rule is keyed on DURATION, not on when the result is needed. context/patterns/bounded-build-waiter.md's `## The Canonical Idiom` (around line 99) says "Prefer the simpler foreground form, `timeout 3000 cmd`, whenever the command fits within the" per-call ceiling, and goes on (around lines 103-106) to warn that preferring the foreground form for anything longer than two minutes REQUIRES raising that timeout, since otherwise it reproduces the very failure the file's idiom exists to avoid. That is a cost-and-fit test. The cited lesson is a DIFFERENT test -- do I need this answer before this turn ends -- and it can select the foreground form even for a command whose duration would otherwise argue for detaching.
- The forbidden path is stated as ending a turn on an UNRESOLVED wait. The canonical agent-contract bullet (around lines 201-207) forbids using run_in_background or a Monitor to watch a local gate from within a dispatched subagent, and forbids ever ending "the turn on an unresolved local background wait", on the ground that the harness's asynchronous detach-then-await-notification path hands the dispatch back unfinished. In the cited incident the wait RESOLVED and the notification ARRIVED -- the failure was that it was not CONSUMED before the turn ended. A strict reading of 'unresolved' does not cover that.
- bounded-build-waiter.md carries a `## Shape Taxonomy: Three Ways a Wait Goes Wrong, Distinguished by Trigger` section (around line 440) enumerating three shapes, the third being harness-initiated backgrounding of an ordinary foreground call. An arrived-but-unconsumed completion notification is a candidate FOURTH shape, distinguished by its own trigger, and the taxonomy is the natural home for it.
- context/patterns/external-process-wait.md's six Required Rules and its `## Local vs. Remote Waits` section (around line 162) govern long remote waits (its rule 3 already forbids run_in_background and Monitor for CI waits inside a subagent). A grep of that file for 'same turn' / 'this turn' returns nothing.

SCOPE -- deliberately small, and prose-only:
(1) State the result-needed-this-turn criterion where the foreground/detach choice is actually made, as an ADDITIONAL and independent reason to take the foreground form, alongside the existing fits-the-ceiling reason. Make clear it can override the duration-based preference, and say what to do when the command genuinely cannot fit the ceiling AND the result is needed this turn (the honest answer is probably: raise the timeout per the existing guidance, or restructure so the result is not needed this turn -- never detach and hope).
(2) Add the arrived-but-unconsumed notification to the Shape Taxonomy as a fourth shape with its own trigger, so the diagnosis is nameable. One sentence of remedy, pointing at (1).
(3) Cross-reference from external-process-wait.md's `## Local vs. Remote Waits` so a reader who arrives at the remote-wait file first is not told a local same-turn gate may be detached.
NOTHING IS BROKEN IN ANY SCRIPT and this is not a data-repair record. The deliverable is guidance prose in the core source store plus any index wiring it needs.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT weaken the livelock protection that detachment exists to provide. #376's own record is explicit that lean's underlying concern -- a killed build caches no artifact, so retries restart at the same module and livelock -- may well be sound, and that the correct shape keeps detachment while fixing the WAIT step. Adding a same-turn criterion must not become a blanket 'always foreground'.
- Preserve the canonical idiom verbatim: captured `pid=$!`, a `kill -0 "$pid"` liveness loop, an outer `timeout N`, all inside ONE Bash call that does not return control until the wait resolves, never `ps | grep` or `pgrep -f`, one waiter per log.
- bounded-build-waiter.md is a GENERATED-COPY SOURCE, not an @-import: it carries a `### Canonical Agent-Contract Bullet (Generated-Copy Source)` section with a `### Copy this exact text` block and a `### Forbidden-Shape Lint Anchor` with a NOTIFICATION_PHRASE and a Check K predicate in scripts/lint/lint-agent-contracts.sh. IF the copied bullet text changes, every consuming agent file must be updated in the same change and the lint must still pass -- and note the file documents a deliberate self-trip exemption for the bullet pair's authoritative home. If the change can be made WITHOUT touching the copied bullet, prefer that and say so; it keeps this record to two files.
- Respect the file's own scoping rulings rather than silently widening them: hard variants do not inherit, research agents are out of scope there, and rule 5 does not propagate. Each has a recorded ruling section; do not re-litigate them in passing.

VERIFY:
- scripts/lint/lint-agent-contracts.sh must still pass, including Check K and its self-trip exemption. If the copied bullet text is unchanged, pin that it is byte-identical.
- If a validator exists for this corpus (scripts/tests/test-validate-bounded-waiter.sh was observed in another record's scope), run it and keep it green.

### 453. Carry the degraded-retrieval signal into the zero-result case, where the briefing drops it and the agent is left to invent a fallback
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: literature
- **Dependencies**: Task 328

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/literature/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over BimodalLogic:#761 (ladder realization theorem), user-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN BIMODALLOGIC: an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store, per the mis-siting correction recorded under this repo's #390 and the authoring hole recorded under #391. All issue-log citations below are entry_ids in the ORIGINATING repository's log, BimodalLogic:specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl -- the identifier #761 is a BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json. Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today (#401); where it matters below it is stated in prose.

CITATION: BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1791766528048_mRmvAU -- class 'tooling bug or gap', severity minor, phase research, dispatch_seq 1, resolution 'worked_around'.

PROVENANCE OF THE FIGURES BELOW: every file, line number, and quoted string in the ROOT CAUSE sections was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-10-18), not copied from the originating report. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narratives and measured costs are CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited issues.jsonl entries; they are not re-measurable from this repository, since the incidents happened in BimodalLogic.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- `literature-search.sh` returned degraded=true with NO results for a quasimodel query, and the research agent fell back to grepping the corpus source directory directly for the specific theorem and definition numbers it needed. The workaround succeeded, so nothing was lost on this run; what the entry records is that the agent had to invent the fallback, because the tool told it retrieval had degraded and then told it nothing about what to do next.

ROOT CAUSE -- the degraded signal is produced honestly and then dropped in exactly the case where it is most actionable. Verified in the source store at creation time:
- scripts/literature-search.sh computes `degraded = fallback_tier != 'bm25'` and emits it in the result envelope alongside fallback_tier and query_error. Its own comment states the intent precisely: degraded is "the honest, never-silent signal that distinguishes 'retrieval degraded' from 'genuine zero-result', which literature-briefing.sh consumes downstream". The producer is correct and this record does not fault it.
- When the merged result set is EMPTY, that same code sets `fallback_tier = 'none'`, which makes `degraded` TRUE. So the zero-result case is reported as degraded by construction -- which is the honest reading (no tier answered), but it also means degraded=true with zero results is the single most common degraded shape, not an edge case.
- scripts/literature-briefing.sh is the consumer, and its degraded banner is emitted under the guard `if [ "$degraded" = "true" ] && [ "${#briefing_lines[@]}" -gt 0 ]`. The second conjunct is the defect: the banner requires that there BE results. It carries three well-written variants -- a trigram-fallback warning, a phrase-retry warning after an FTS5 syntax error, and a generic fallback_tier warning -- and each ends by telling the agent to verify relevance itself, which is advice that only makes sense when rows exist.
- Net: in the cited case (degraded=true, fallback_tier='none', zero rows) the briefing emits NO banner and NO directive at all. The signal the producer went to trouble to make never-silent is silent precisely when the agent has nothing else to go on.

SCOPE -- give the zero-result degraded case its own banner and a usable directive, in literature-briefing.sh. The directive should say what the agent in the cited incident worked out by itself, so the next agent does not have to:
- distinguish 'the corpus has nothing on this' from 'the index did not answer this query', and say which one the tool can and cannot tell apart;
- name the sanctioned next steps rather than leaving them to invention -- at minimum re-querying with narrower or alternative terms (the --multi mode exists for per-term recall in one process), consulting --toc / --doc to navigate by document structure instead of ranking, and, as the explicit last resort, reading the corpus source directly;
- state whether an index-health problem is indicated and where that is checked (`/literature --validate`, and the index-health mode of `/literature`).
Also rule on whether `fallback_tier = 'none'` on an empty result set should remain folded into `degraded`, or be reported as its own distinguishable state. Do not change the producer's semantics without recording the reasoning: downstream consumers read the field, and the current definition is deliberate.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Preserve the three existing banner variants verbatim in their current cases (trigram_fallback, phrase_retry, generic). This record ADDS a fourth case; it does not rewrite the working ones.
- Preserve backward compatibility of the envelope reader. literature-briefing.sh already accepts a legacy bare-array shape and two envelope shapes; whatever lands must keep all three readable and must not fail when degraded/fallback_tier/query_error are absent.
- `query_error` is a DIFFERENT condition from `degraded` -- the producer documents it as non-null only when every sub-query failed against every database that exists. A genuine query error must not be collapsed into the new zero-result banner; if anything it deserves a louder one.
- The banner is guidance, not a gate. Nothing here may make a research dispatch fail or block on degraded retrieval.
- No task-number references in the banner text or the context file -- it lands under agent-system/**.

VERIFY:
- Add a literature/scripts/tests/ case covering the cited shape: degraded=true, fallback_tier='none', zero rows -> a banner and a directive ARE emitted. Pin the negative too: a healthy bm25 answer with rows emits NO banner.
- Pin that each of the three existing variants still fires in its own case with its existing wording.
- Pin that all three accepted envelope shapes still parse, including the legacy bare array with no degraded field.
- Reproduce the cited case end-to-end if the corpus allows: a query that no tier answers should now produce actionable text rather than silence.

TERRITORY EDGES:
- 328 (SERIALIZATION, NOT liftable without a read): its file_scope names literature/scripts/literature-search.sh EXPLICITLY (not merely by directory) and also covers literature/scripts/tests/ at directory level, as part of a net-deletion corpus consolidation. This is a direct, named collision on one of this record's own files, so the edge stands until an operator reads 328's plan and confirms otherwise.
- 437 (NO EDGE, checked explicitly): also a literature-extension record, but its file_scope is the Zotero export/search path (zotero-generate-export.sh, zotero-search.sh, zotero-export-status.sh, zotero-export-freshness.sh, tools/zotero-scripts.md). Disjoint files and a disjoint mechanism -- a false-negative acquisition answer from a lossy sqlite reconstruction, not a dropped retrieval-health banner. Both concern honest reporting of a degraded literature answer, so keep the wording consistent, but neither blocks the other.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. No open task names literature-briefing.sh. 404 (completed, literature) was checked. 437 is the only adjacent open literature record and is explicitly ruled disjoint above. No absorption candidate found.

### 452. Require a verification criterion over a repo-wide gate with standing pre-existing findings to be baseline-relative, not absolute
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 360, Task 385, Task 435, Task 446, Task 448

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (primarily) and agent-system/extensions/lean/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over BimodalLogic:#761 (ladder realization theorem), user-approved at the lead level and dispatched to the meta builder for task creation only. FILED HERE, NOT IN BIMODALLOGIC: an agent-system change belongs in the repository that owns the source store, per the mis-siting correction recorded under this repo's #390 and the authoring hole recorded under #391. All issue-log citations below are entry_ids in the ORIGINATING repository's log, BimodalLogic:specs/761_ladder_realization_theorem/issues.jsonl -- the identifier #761 is a BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json. Cross-repo overlap has no mechanical representation today (#401); where it matters below it is stated in prose.

CITATIONS -- two independent cases, two different gates, six days apart:
  BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1791769990070_9Nl16r  (class gate-baseline, severity minor, implement, dispatch_seq 3) -- the repo-wide task-reference lint.
  BimodalLogic:#761:iss_1792364282512_jWKKvT  (class verification-criterion, severity minor, implement, dispatch_seq 6) -- the vacuous-definition grep.
A third entry in the same log (iss_1791770495965_TM78BN) records the vacuous-pattern case a second time from the earlier phase, so the grep case recurred across dispatches rather than being a one-off.

PROVENANCE OF THE FIGURES BELOW: every file, line number, and quoted string in the ROOT CAUSE sections was re-derived by direct read of the source store at creation time (2026-10-18), not copied from the originating report. Line numbers move -- re-derive before editing. The incident narratives and measured costs are CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from the cited issues.jsonl entries; they are not re-measurable from this repository, since the incidents happened in BimodalLogic.

LIVE SYMPTOM -- a plan demanded an absolute gate result the tree cannot produce, so a correctly-passing phase had to report a non-conforming verdict:
(a) "repo-wide check-task-references.sh --quiet exits 0". MEASURED: it exits 1 on 198 pre-existing occurrences in Boneyard/*/README.md (archived code), NONE in any file the task touched; every touched file passes individually and the per-file checks pass. The criterion is unattainable without an out-of-scope archive cleanup.
(b) "vacuous-definition grep count 0", asserted repeatedly across phases. MEASURED: the tree carries one PRE-EXISTING hit at HEAD -- FormalSystem/Examples/TemporalStructures.lean:495, int_domain_universal -- whose `:= trivial` closes a REAL goal (membership in the universal domain is True by definition). So the hit is not even a finding; it is a correct declaration that matches the pattern.
Both had to be reported as baseline-unchanged rather than as the stated criterion. The cost is not a wrong result -- it is that a conforming phase produces a verdict that does not match its own plan, which is indistinguishable on first read from a phase that failed its gate, and so costs a triage pass and erodes the verdict's meaning.

ROOT CAUSE -- the authoring standard has every adjacent rule except this one. Verified in the source store at creation time (around these lines; re-derive before editing):
- context/formats/plan-format.md's `### Naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria` (around line 498) is the closest existing rule and is strikingly near-miss. It MANDATES naming the repository's sanctioned entry point rather than a bare binary, and it MANDATES checking the invoked command's exit status rather than relying on a stdout diff against a baseline -- because "a tool broken identically on both sides of that diff makes 'no new findings' vacuously true". So the file already reasons about baselines, and already warns about one specific way a baseline-relative criterion goes wrong, while never stating when a criterion MUST be baseline-relative in the first place. Its declared enforcement level is advisory prose only, with no corresponding validate-artifact.sh check.
- `### Counts-are-hypotheses obligation` (around line 422) makes any count, file list, or scope estimate a hypothesis carrying a `**Scope Hypothesis**:` line stating how to confirm it at implementation time. That is the right shape for a FORWARD-LOOKING estimate; a standing pre-existing finding count is a different object -- a measurable property of HEAD that should be measured ONCE at plan time and then held fixed as the comparison point.
- `## Verification Tiers` (around line 260) defines how BROADLY verification must run (prose < local < interface < full) and requires the final phase to take the `full` tier, whose in-phase verification is "the complete gate set for the repository". So the format compels a repo-wide run at the final phase while saying nothing about how to phrase the criterion for a repo-wide gate that is not green at HEAD. The two halves do not meet.
- A grep of plan-format.md for baseline / pre-existing / unchanged finds only incidental uses: the stdout-diff warning above, and tier prose using 'unchanged' in an unrelated sense. There is no rule to amend here -- there is a rule to ADD, sited next to the entry-point rule it completes.

SCOPE -- add the baseline-relative criterion rule to plan-format.md, sited as a sibling of `### Naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria`, and propagate it to the planner's own contract. The rule must state at least:
- WHEN it binds: whenever a phase's verification criterion invokes a gate whose scope exceeds the phase's edit scope (a repo-wide lint, a tree-wide grep, an aggregate sweep) AND that gate is not green at the task's starting commit.
- WHAT the criterion must then say: a baseline-relative verdict -- 'no NEW findings relative to the recorded baseline', or 'baseline-unchanged at N' -- never an absolute 'exits 0' or 'count is 0'.
- WHERE the baseline comes from: measured at PLAN time against the task's starting commit and recorded IN the plan, so the implementer compares against a number the plan committed to rather than one it discovers mid-phase and could rationalize. Pair this with the existing counts-are-hypotheses and carried-figure obligations rather than restating them.
- The ESCAPE HATCH, stated explicitly: an absolute criterion remains correct and preferred when the gate IS green at the starting commit, and a plan may legitimately demand an absolute result as a deliberate in-scope cleanup -- in which case the cleanup is part of the task's scope and must appear in a phase's Files to modify.
Then rule on enforcement: whether validate-artifact.sh can mechanically flag an absolute criterion naming a known repo-wide gate, or whether this stays advisory prose like its sibling rule. Record the ruling either way; a mechanical check here is only worth having if it can avoid false-flagging the legitimate absolute case above.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Do NOT make this a license to lower a gate. 'No new findings' must mean the gate still RUNS at full repo scope and its exit status is still checked -- the sibling entry-point rule is explicit that a stdout-diff-only comparison makes 'no new findings' vacuously true when the tool is broken identically on both sides. Whatever wording lands must preserve that warning and must not be satisfiable by a diff alone.
- Do NOT restate the carried-figure or counts-are-hypotheses conventions. report-format.md's `## Carried-Figure Discipline` is the authoritative home for carried figures for BOTH reports and plans, and plan-format.md already POINTS there rather than restating, deliberately so the two cannot drift. A recorded baseline figure is a measurement this plan makes, so say how it interacts with those conventions and point; do not fork them.
- Keep every existing heading string in plan-format.md unchanged; several have consumers (validate-artifact.sh, plan-file-scope-harvest.sh reads `Files to modify`, and the lean-gated `## Lean Challenge Statements` section).
- If a mechanical check is added, it MUST fail open and advisory-first. A false flag on a legitimate absolute criterion would push planners toward vaguer criteria, which is worse than the defect being fixed.

VERIFY:
- If a validate-artifact.sh check lands: extend scripts/tests/test-validate-artifact.sh with both polarities -- an absolute criterion over a repo-wide gate is flagged; an absolute criterion over a gate that is green at HEAD, and one paired with an in-scope cleanup phase, are NOT flagged.
- Pin that the existing `### Naming a tool's entry point in verification criteria` and `### Counts-are-hypotheses obligation` sections are intact and that plan-format.md still points to report-format.md for carried figures rather than restating it.
- Use the two cited cases as the acceptance fixtures: a criterion phrased the new way must be satisfiable by the measured states described above (198 archived occurrences untouched; one pre-existing correct declaration matching the vacuous-definition pattern).
- Do NOT verify by running the full verify-deploy.sh aggregator while other sessions are active -- use the scoped narrowing.

TERRITORY EDGES:
- 447 (SERIALIZATION plus a SEMANTIC edge): also writes agents/planner-agent.md, scripts/validate-artifact.sh and its test, and it is the record that AMENDS the carried-figure convention this rule must point at rather than fork. Reconcile wording during research; it should land first so this record points at the amended convention.
- 446 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes context/formats/plan-format.md (and plan-file-scope-harvest.sh, which parses that file). Same file, disjoint section -- it works the Files-to-modify/file_scope reconciliation half.
- 385 (SERIALIZATION only): also writes context/formats/plan-format.md, in the lean-gated `## Lean Challenge Statements` section. Disjoint section, direct file collision.
- 328 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): covers core/context/, core/agents/ and lean/rules/ at DIRECTORY level as part of a net-deletion corpus consolidation. Liftable with update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove if an operator confirms it does not touch these files.
- 360 (SERIALIZATION only, LOW CONFIDENCE): also writes scripts/validate-artifact.sh, for an advisory CARRIED-UNVERIFIED well-formedness check. Only relevant if this record adds a validate-artifact.sh check; if the enforcement ruling is 'advisory prose only', lift it.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. 447 is the nearest neighbour and is flagged for reconciliation rather than treated as disjoint: it strengthens the discipline around a figure a plan REPEATS from another record, while this record governs how a criterion a plan AUTHORS must be phrased against a measured starting state. Related, complementary, not the same object. 420 (plan-size versus cycle-budget mismatch) and 313 (advisory lint for hand-authored batch proposals) are both plan-authoring adjacent but address sizing and batching, not criterion phrasing. No absorption candidate found.

ADDITIONAL TERRITORY EDGES, SURFACED MECHANICALLY BY validate-state.sh AFTER THIS RECORD WAS WRITTEN (declared with labelled rationale rather than as bare numbers, per the open concern that dependency edges conflate semantic and serialization rationale -- see #438):
- 435 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): declares agent-system/extensions/core/context/ and core/agents/ coarsely, covering this record's plan-format.md and planner-agent.md targets.
- 448 (SERIALIZATION only, OPERATOR-LIFTABLE): declares agent-system/extensions/core/scripts/tests/ coarsely, covering test-validate-artifact.sh.
