### 360. Evidence discipline for report and plan artifacts, in three phases: validate that a CARRIED-UNVERIFIED mark names a source, extend the carried-figure obligation to recommendations and dispatch premises, and require a self-derived enumeration to state and stress-test its search pattern
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 328, Task 358, Task 385, Task 407, Task 428, Task 446

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS. No task-number references in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md); they are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

GOAL. report-format.md's "## Carried-Figure Discipline" section now requires that a figure or mechanical claim carried from another record be re-derived at authoring time or marked inline as `{figure} [CARRIED-UNVERIFIED: {source path}]`, with the source MANDATORILY named. The mark is today a pure authoring-side obligation: nothing checks that a mark exists where one is owed, and nothing checks that a mark which does exist actually names a source. report-format.md's own "**Enforcement level**" paragraph records the source-naming validation as a named future item, deliberately not built. Build that narrow piece.

=== SCOPE: THE NARROW CHECK ONLY ===

Check exactly this: every `[CARRIED-UNVERIFIED:` occurrence in a report or plan artifact is followed by a non-empty source designator before the closing bracket. That is a well-formedness check on a fixed literal token the contract itself defines -- greppable, unambiguous, and with no natural-language judgment in it.

=== DO NOT REBUILD THE DETECTOR THAT ALREADY FAILED ITS GATE ===

This is the critical constraint. A DIFFERENT and much more ambitious check -- a general detector for carried-figure contradictions, specifically the assert-absence-of-a-mandated-literal shape -- was specified, measured, and REJECTED on evidence. Its decision gate failed all three pre-stated criteria (measured 2026-10-07, recorded in that task's progress/phase-4-progress.json):

  RECALL:      the specified ERE missed the one known positive, because markdown bold (`**not**`) splits the trigger phrase "does not contain". It flagged an unrelated non-contradictory duplicate instead.
  PRECISION:   0 of a 12-file hand-inspected sample of flagged files were genuine; all were ordinary prose quoting a short literal (a filename, a command name, a common word) more than once.
  BOUNDEDNESS: 92 files flagged across a 2711-file corpus, against a 5-flag threshold -- "an advisory nobody can triage is noise, not a check".

That rejection stands and is not this task's business to revisit. The reasoned-infeasibility finding plus the reviewer prompt already in report-format.md are the complete answer for the general case. This task builds ONLY the source-naming well-formedness check, which is a different shape entirely: a literal token's syntax, not a semantic contradiction.

HEED THE RECALL FAILURE MODE ANYWAY. The reason that detector missed its known positive -- markdown inline formatting interrupting a matched phrase -- applies to this check too, because a mark may be written inside emphasis, inside a table cell, or wrapped across lines. Test those shapes deliberately rather than assuming a naive pattern holds.

=== ALSO SETTLE ===

- Whether the check belongs in validate-artifact.sh (which already has an advisory-first precedent in its Verification Tier check) or elsewhere. Follow the existing advisory-first convention: ship as log_warn-only, document the promotion criterion, and state explicitly that promotion to blocking is NOT part of this change.
- Whether a mark whose named source does not RESOLVE (a path that does not exist) should warn too, or whether that over-reaches into the territory the failed gate occupied. Rule on it; do not silently pick one.
- Whether the complementary obligation -- a mark is OWED where a figure was carried without re-derivation -- is detectable at all. The strong prior is NO, for the same reasons the general detector failed. If that is the conclusion, record it as a reasoned exclusion rather than leaving the question open, and say so in report-format.md's Enforcement-level paragraph.

=== NON-GOALS ===

- No general contradiction detection. No re-litigating the rejected gate.
- No new documents: extend validate-artifact.sh and, if the Enforcement-level paragraph needs updating to describe what shipped, report-format.md.
- Do not change the contract's substance in report-format.md -- the trigger classes, the mark syntax and the reviewer prompt are settled. Only the enforcement-level description may change, and only to match what actually ships.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===

- A mark missing its source designator produces a named advisory; a well-formed mark produces none.
- Exit codes unchanged in default mode: an advisory must not fail an artifact.
- Fixtures cover the plain shape, a mark inside markdown emphasis, a mark inside a table cell, and a line-wrapped mark -- the recall failure mode that sank the rejected detector is tested, not assumed away.
- Run against the real corpus: report the flag count. If it exceeds a triageable handful, say so and treat that as a finding about the corpus rather than shipping an untriageable advisory.
- The does-the-source-resolve question and the is-a-mark-owed question are each ruled on in writing.
- validate-artifact.sh stays shellcheck clean per context/standards/shell-strict-mode.md. Redeployed.


========================================================================
PHASE 2 -- FOLDED IN 2026-11-03 (backlog redundancy pass, owner-approved).
Source record: "Extend the carried-figure obligation to a recommendation's mechanical claim and
to a dispatch's acted-on premise" (abandoned into this record, nothing dropped). Its full text
follows verbatim. Where it names a dependency or a file, that naming stands; where it describes
itself as a separate task, read it as this record's phase 2.
========================================================================

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Edit at the path mirroring the deployed one, then REDEPLOY via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

ORIGIN: Conclusion stage (channel b, agent-system changes) of an /orchestrate invocation over tasks 417, 424, 443 and 413, session sess_1792311788_aadaee. User-approved at the lead level.

CITATIONS -- two entries, two independent tasks:
  #443:iss_1792313063812_v6b92J  (severity: costly -- the highest in that batch)  -> PART A
  #417:iss_1792312345583_nHZI6w                                                   -> PART B

THE CONVENTION THIS RECORD AMENDS ALREADY EXISTS. Verified in the source store while creating this task (re-derive before editing; line numbers move): context/formats/report-format.md around lines 42-100 defines `## Carried-Figure Discipline` as the authoritative statement for BOTH report and plan artifacts, with context/formats/plan-format.md (around line 436) pointing here rather than restating, explicitly so the two cannot drift. Its rule: a figure or mechanical claim the artifact did not produce by its own measurement in this authoring session -- copied or closely paraphrased from a prior report, a prior plan version, another task's artifact, THE TASK DESCRIPTION, or a session transcript -- must be either re-derived at authoring time or marked with the literal fixed-case token `CARRIED-UNVERIFIED` naming its source. Six trigger shapes are enumerated (numeric count or measurement; command output or a characterization of it; a file/line citation presented as locating content; a pass/fail or exit-status or gate-outcome claim; an "N of M" ratio; a qualitative claim about mechanical or environmental state including a hard always/never/cannot/is-required). Its stated enforcement level is authoring-side only. Do NOT invent a second convention; amend this one.

LIVE SYMPTOM, PART A -- a research-stage recommendation is not checked for behaviour-preservation against the live corpus before being handed to planning:
- The research report recommended tightening strip_exempt_regions to a depth counter with a validity verdict. Plan-time source reads established that this is NOT behaviour-preserving on the current tree: five source-store test files end their marker parse at eof_depth>0 -- scripts/tests/test-check-task-references.sh, test-gate-cache-equivalence.sh, test-git-commit-phase.sh, test-git-commit-scoped.sh, test-orchestrate-record-decision.sh -- partly from redundant nested begins, and partly from PROSE that merely mentions the literal marker token and is parsed as a marker. Under the recommended rule those files' trailing regions become scannable and the repo-wide lint goes red.
- Consequence: the planner had to insert a marker-hygiene remediation phase ahead of the library change that research never anticipated.

ROOT CAUSE, PART A -- the gap is a named exclusion in the convention itself, not an oversight. `## Carried-Figure Discipline`'s **Out of scope** paragraph states that "a purely interpretive or evaluative claim carried from another record (a recommendation, a priority ranking, a design preference, a quality assessment) is not a carried figure -- it is not falsifiable by re-measurement, so re-derive-or-mark has nothing to act on." The cited evidence is a direct counterexample: a recommendation to change a parser's rule carries an IMPLICIT mechanical claim -- that the change is behaviour-preserving over the current corpus -- and that claim is straightforwardly falsifiable by running the proposed rule against the corpus, which is exactly what plan time did. The exclusion is correct about a priority ranking or a design preference and wrong about a recommendation whose adoption asserts a measurable property of the live tree. Note also that the convention binds what an artifact RESTATES; a recommendation is the artifact's own new claim, so even the six trigger shapes do not reach it.

LIVE SYMPTOM, PART B -- a task description's premise goes stale between creation and dispatch, with nothing re-validating it at the boundary that acts on it:
- The dispatch premise cited gate20 failing at current_eager=67088 B against baseline 66100 B, and the context-budget suite failing 2 of 13 cases.
- Measured live at plan time: eager total 66707 B against baseline 66800 B, gate20 PASSING 4/4, and the suite passing 15/15 -- it had grown past 13 cases. The overage had already been closed by an earlier commit.
- The planner correctly re-scoped from "close the overage" to "recover durable headroom", but only because it re-measured on its own initiative, not because anything required it.

ROOT CAUSE, PART B -- the obligation attaches to the wrong thing. `## Carried-Figure Discipline` names the task description as a carried-figure source and would have required re-derivation of those numbers IF the artifact restated them. It says nothing about the premise an agent ACTS ON without restating. An agent that silently accepts a stale description premise, re-scopes nothing, and never quotes the figure triggers no obligation at all -- yet that is the case where a stale premise does the most damage, because the resulting work is aimed at a condition that no longer holds. The dispatch file composer (scripts/orchestrate-build-dispatch.sh, which emits `## Description` as its third section, around line 533) passes the description through verbatim with no staleness signal and no re-measurement prompt.

SCOPE -- decide and implement ONE coherent amendment per part, with the reasoning recorded in the artifact:
PART A: extend the re-derive-or-mark obligation to a recommendation that asserts a measurable property of the live corpus. Decide and record: (1) the TRIGGER -- what distinguishes such a recommendation from the design preferences the Out-of-scope paragraph rightly excludes (a candidate shape, offered as a starting point and not a prescription: a recommendation whose adoption would change a mechanical rule applied over a corpus, where the recommendation's soundness depends on how the current corpus responds); (2) whether the obligation is satisfied by running the proposed rule against the corpus and reporting the result, or by marking the recommendation `CARRIED-UNVERIFIED`, or by a distinct mark since the claim is the artifact's own rather than carried; (3) that the Out-of-scope paragraph is REWRITTEN to draw the line in its new place rather than left contradicting the new rule.
PART B: extend the obligation from "a figure the artifact restates" to "the premise the dispatch acts on". Decide and record: whether the obligation sits on the receiving agent's first stage (re-measure the description's mechanical claims before planning or implementing, and state the result even when it confirms the premise), or on the dispatch composer (emit the description's mechanical claims as an explicit re-validation checklist), or both. Weigh the cost: a blanket re-measure-everything obligation is expensive and will be skipped; a narrow one keyed to the six existing trigger shapes already present in the description is cheap and mechanically identifiable.

HARD CONSTRAINTS:
- Keep `## Carried-Figure Discipline`'s stated purpose intact: "presenting a carried figure as freshly measured is the defect; carrying one openly is not." Neither part may turn the convention into a prohibition on carrying.
- `CARRIED-UNVERIFIED` is a literal, fixed-case, greppable token with a REQUIRED named source. Do not change its spelling, its case, or the required-source rule; anything new must be greppable on the same terms.
- Preserve the single-placement rule: report-format.md is authoritative and plan-format.md points to it. Do not restate the amendment in plan-format.md, and do not relocate the section -- its own Placement record documents two rejected alternatives (a standalone contract document, raising net document count and adding a third drift site) and that reasoning still holds.
- Both parts are authoring-side and reviewer-side obligations. Do NOT add a blocking gate in this record; validate-artifact.sh's existing per-artifact checks are the only mechanical surface in scope, and any addition there must be advisory.
- PART A is about the research stage's own verification obligation, NOT about fixing the cited instance: 443's existing plan already absorbs the marker-hygiene remediation. Do not duplicate or re-plan that work.
- PART B likewise: 423 is the open record for the eager-load baseline drift itself. Do not re-scope or absorb it.

VERIFY:
- Extend scripts/tests/test-validate-artifact.sh where that suite's existing report/plan-format coverage lives, pinning whatever advisory check is added, in both the fires and does-not-fire directions.
- Confirm by grep that the amended Out-of-scope paragraph does not contradict the new trigger anywhere in report-format.md, and that plan-format.md still only points rather than restating -- report-format.md's own "Internal consistency" reviewer prompt is the applicable check and should be run against the amended section.
- Confirm the six existing trigger shapes are unchanged in meaning, so no existing artifact becomes retroactively non-compliant.

TERRITORY EDGES (declared per context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md, each labelled with its rationale):
- 360 (SEMANTIC + file overlap): it owns scripts/validate-artifact.sh and context/formats/report-format.md, and it adds an advisory-only well-formedness check that every `CARRIED-UNVERIFIED` mark names a source -- which is the "named future item" report-format.md's own Enforcement level paragraph points at. It establishes the first mechanical surface for this convention; this record's additions must build on that surface rather than open a second one. Must land first.
- 328 (SERIALIZATION only): its file_scope covers agent-system/extensions/core/context/, agents/ and scripts/tests/ at DIRECTORY level and is a net-deletion corpus consolidation that would churn these same files. An operator who confirms it does not touch these specific files may lift this edge with update-task-status.sh --depends-on-remove.

DUPLICATE CHECK PERFORMED. 360 was identified as the nearest neighbour and rejected as an absorption target: it validates the WELL-FORMEDNESS of a mark that is already present (does it name a source?), whereas this record changes WHEN a mark or a re-derivation is owed at all. Disjoint questions on a shared file, so an edge was declared. 427 (completed) stopped the dispatch composer emitting a superseded Prior Decision as settled -- adjacent in spirit but confined to the `## Prior Decisions` section and to supersession, not to a description's measured premise.
PARTS A AND B WERE DELIBERATELY COMBINED, not split: both are gaps in the SAME named convention, both land in the same section of the same file, and both have the same shape -- a claim written at one time is consumed at a later time without re-measurement. Two serialized records rewriting one prose section would mean the second rewrites what the first just wrote. One record, two phases, one coherent ruling on where the convention's boundary now sits.


========================================================================
PHASE 3 -- FOLDED IN 2026-11-03 (backlog redundancy pass, owner-approved).
Source record: "A self-derived enumeration is presented as exhaustive with nothing requiring its
search pattern to be stated or stress-tested" (abandoned into this record, nothing dropped). Its
full text follows verbatim, with the same reading instruction as phase 2.
========================================================================

A self-derived enumeration is presented as exhaustive with nothing requiring its search pattern to be stated or stress-tested

SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never a deployed .claude/** tree, which is a gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). Resolve source_dir from <project-root>/.claude-extensions.json and edit at the path mirroring the deployed one. REDEPLOY AFTERWARDS via deploy-headless.sh. No task-number references and no record-versioning language in any file landing under agent-system/** (rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md, rules/no-record-versioning-in-deliverables.md); both are permitted in this description and elsewhere in specs/**.

STANDING INSTRUCTION -- RE-VERIFY BEFORE CITING. Re-run every grep and re-read every line number below before citing it; line numbers move. Do not inherit a name or figure from this text.

ORIGIN: conclusion-stage observation from an /orchestrate run in the BimodalLogic repository (the upward-edge allowlist count-drift task, number 828 THERE, not in this repo). The run's own issue log recorded the first instance as minor and the lead dismissed it as "the designed defense working"; a second, worse instance surfaced afterwards and refuted that reading. Filed only after confirming no open task in this repo covers it.

CITATION: BimodalLogic repo, specs/828_metalogic_upward_edge_allowlist_count_drift/issues.jsonl, entry_id iss_1793658711955_yYAZ8O (class research-gap, severity minor). The second instance below is NOT in that log -- it was found after the task closed, and is recorded only here.

THE CONVENTION THIS RECORD AMENDS ALREADY EXISTS. Verified in the source store while filing (core source_git_head 26f3102fa0b1cc83f9e97b62b3148634eee47ad8): context/formats/report-format.md, `## Carried-Figure Discipline`, around lines 42-102, with context/formats/plan-format.md pointing there rather than restating. Do NOT invent a second convention; amend this one.

WHAT THE CONVENTION ALREADY COVERS, AND WHY IT DOES NOT REACH THIS. The discipline governs the PROVENANCE OF EACH INDIVIDUAL CLAIM: a figure copied from another record must be re-derived or marked CARRIED-UNVERIFIED. Its "Out of scope" paragraph states plainly that "a figure the author measured in this session is not carried". That is exactly the case here. Every individual line citation in the offending artifact was freshly derived in-session and every one was correct. The defect was not in any claim's provenance -- it was in the COMPLETENESS OF THE SET. The re-derivation guidance's closest clause, "re-count rather than trusting a stated tally", does not reach it either: the author did count, and counted its own incomplete search output faithfully.

LIVE SYMPTOM, INSTANCE 1 (caught downstream, cost: one plan-time inventory addition).
The research report's "Exact Edit Sites" section enumerated the hand-typed count statements needing correction across four files. Its sweep patterns were `seven|7 line|7 recorded|upward`. Two live sites could not match any of them and were omitted: scripts/check-metalogic-cycles.sh:7 (`recorded 7-line allowlist` -- hyphenated, so `7 line` misses it) and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:54 (an ASCII diagram label `-- 7 lines --`, a bare-word 7). The planner's independent re-verification caught both before any edit was authored. This instance alone is consistent with "the designed defense working".

LIVE SYMPTOM, INSTANCE 2 (NOT caught -- survived research, planning, implementation AND the acceptance check).
The same report also stated that exactly two live "seven"/"7-line allowlist" mentions existed OUTSIDE the task's declared file scope, naming scripts/README.md and docs/development/PUBLICATION_REFACTOR.md. There were three. The miss was docs/development/MODULE_INVARIANTS.md:416 -- `recorded allowlist of 7 lines` -- a present-tense, undated claim in the reference document for the very script the task was about. It was found only afterwards, by a differently-shaped pattern (`allowlist` within ~30 characters of a bare `7`).

ROOT CAUSE -- A BLIND SPOT IN A SWEEP PATTERN PROPAGATES INTO THE VERIFICATION THAT REUSES IT.
This is the load-bearing finding and the reason instance 2 is not merely instance 1 again. The plan's final phase carried an explicit acceptance criterion of the form "re-grep and confirm no live count still says seven", and that check PASSED -- because it reused the same pattern family that produced the omission. A sweep and the sweep that verifies it are not independent evidence when they share a pattern; the second inherits the first's blind spot and converts an open gap into a false green. Nothing in the convention, in the research agent contract, or in plan-format.md's verification guidance currently distinguishes a verification sweep from the sweep it is meant to check.

A SECOND-ORDER NOTE WORTH DECIDING ON. The artifact's section was titled "Exact Edit Sites" -- a completeness claim in the heading itself -- while the patterns that produced it were not stated anywhere in the artifact. A reader could not have audited the enumeration's coverage without re-deriving it from scratch, which is precisely what the discipline elsewhere exists to prevent.

SCOPE -- decide and implement ONE coherent amendment, with the reasoning recorded in the artifact.
1. THE TRIGGER. Define when an enumeration carries an implicit completeness claim. Candidate discriminator: the artifact asserts or implies that a set is the COMPLETE set of occurrences ("every", "all", "exact", "the N sites", or a closed table a later phase will treat as its work list) as opposed to presenting examples. Decide whether a bare count of occurrences ("19 sites") is itself such a claim.
2. THE OBLIGATION. Decide between (a) stating the search patterns used, inline and verbatim, wherever a completeness claim is made, so coverage is auditable without re-derivation; (b) requiring a second, DIFFERENTLY-SHAPED pattern before a completeness claim may be asserted (e.g. a loose structural pattern alongside the literal one), with both recorded; (c) a `COVERAGE-UNVERIFIED` mark parallel to the existing CARRIED-UNVERIFIED, for an enumeration the author believes complete but did not cross-check. Record why the chosen option beats the others; (a) and (b) are complementary and may both be adopted.
3. THE VERIFICATION-INDEPENDENCE RULE. State that a verification sweep MUST NOT reuse the pattern family of the sweep it verifies, and say where that rule belongs -- report-format.md's own discipline section, plan-format.md's verification-criteria guidance, or both via a pointer. This is the part with no existing home; do not let it be dropped in favour of the easier items 1-2.
4. ENFORCEMENT LEVEL. Decide and record whether any of this is mechanically checkable. A literal `COVERAGE-UNVERIFIED` token would be greppable exactly as CARRIED-UNVERIFIED is; a missing-where-owed check is likely not feasible, for the same reason report-format.md already records for internal consistency. Do not over-claim: an authoring-side obligation honestly labelled as such is an acceptable outcome.

OUT OF SCOPE. Reworking the research agent's general search strategy, adding a new standalone contract document (report-format.md's own Placement record rejects that, and the reasoning still holds), and anything in the BimodalLogic repository -- the two documentation fixes there are already committed and the count drift is closed.

ACCEPTANCE.
- report-format.md's `## Carried-Figure Discipline` (or a successor section in the same file) states the completeness trigger, the chosen obligation, and the verification-independence rule, each with its reasoning.
- A reader can determine, from the convention alone, which of the two instances above would now have been caught and by which clause. State this explicitly in the artifact as a worked check against both instances -- including instance 2, the one that reached a false green.
- plan-format.md still points rather than restating, so the two cannot drift.
- bash scripts/lint-agent-contracts.sh and bash .claude/scripts/verify-deploy.sh both exit 0 after redeploy.

FILE SCOPE (re-derive before editing; the planner may narrow or widen with cause): agent-system/extensions/core/context/formats/report-format.md, agent-system/extensions/core/context/formats/plan-format.md, agent-system/extensions/core/agents/general-research-agent.md.

DEPENDENCY NOTE. Two open tasks already carry report-format.md in their file_scope -- the carried-unverified source-naming validation task and the research-recommendation verification task -- and the latter amends this very section and also touches general-research-agent.md. Both edges are recorded as dependencies so this serializes behind them rather than colliding at admission; neither blocks it on content grounds.

========================================================================
CONSOLIDATION RATIONALE (why these are one record and not three)
========================================================================
All three phases write the same two contract files -- context/formats/report-format.md and
context/formats/plan-format.md -- plus the same validator, scripts/validate-artifact.sh, and its
fixture suite. They were filed as a chain, each declaring the previous one as its dependency, so
no two could ever run concurrently; the chain serialized three dispatch rounds over one pair of
files. Settled decision 6 in specs/ROADMAP.md rules that case directly: a linear chain of small
tasks serializing on one file is one task with phases, applied at creation time. This record is
that application, taken late.
Consequence for ordering: the three edges that pointed at phase 2's former record now point here.
Phase order is phase 1 (the narrow well-formedness check on the CARRIED-UNVERIFIED token), then
phase 2 (widening the obligation to a recommendation's mechanical claim and a dispatch's acted-on
premise), then phase 3 (requiring a self-derived enumeration to state and stress-test its search
pattern). That is the order the folded records' own edges already encoded; keep it.

========================================================================
FURTHER PART B CASE FOLDED IN 2026-11-04 (backlog reconciliation at proposal time). The conclusion
stage of an /orchestrate invocation over BimodalLogic:#833 (stab definable from open modals) audited
a proposed agent-system task against this backlog via scripts/audit-task-proposals.sh, found PART B
already owns the obligation, and folded the evidence here instead of filing a duplicate. Citation is
an entry_id in BimodalLogic:specs/833_stab_definable_from_open_modals/issues.jsonl -- #833 is a
BimodalLogic number and does NOT resolve in this repository's state.json. CARRIED-UNVERIFIED from
that log.

CITATION: iss_1793831541081_EKWSJ7, class planning, severity minor, phase plan, dispatch_seq 2
(first two of the entry's three items; the third is folded into the settled-route record instead).

LIVE SYMPTOM -- the premise did not merely go stale, it arrived ALREADY CONTRADICTED, and the
contradiction reached the PLANNER to adjudicate rather than the boundary that acted on it:
(1) The dispatch asserted that check-metalogic-cycles.sh currently exits 1 for a pre-existing
    eighth-upward-edge reason. Research re-measured it at exit 0. The plan had to INVERT the
    dispatch's guidance, requiring exit 0 and treating any nonzero as a NEW failure.
(2) The dispatch asserted that Frame class is Base for all six ledger rows. Research scoped that
    claim more narrowly, to rows carrying OpenValid or [F.IsRegular] -- which excludes the one
    class-generic declaration, stab_to_ofut_opast. The plan kept Base for all six per the dispatch
    (true of all six, and uniform across one contiguous block) and put the sharper "needs no frame
    axiom" fact in that row's Statement cell.
Both were adjudicated correctly and nothing was damaged. The cost is that the adjudication happened
in the plan, one stage after the conflict was already knowable, and that in case (1) the resolution
was a straight INVERSION of something the dispatch presented as measured fact.

WHY THIS SHARPENS PART B's OPEN QUESTION RATHER THAN RESTATING IT. PART B weighs two candidate
owners: the receiving agent's first stage (re-measure the description's mechanical claims) or the
dispatch composer (emit those claims as a re-validation checklist). Neither fits this case, because
the receiving agent HAD already re-measured -- research recorded both contradictions before the plan
was composed. The gap was that nothing CONSUMED that recorded re-measurement at compose time; the
composer passed the description through verbatim while a prior dispatch's artifact in the same task
directory already refuted two of its claims. Weigh a third shape alongside the existing two: when a
prior dispatch's research artifact contradicts a mechanical claim the description still asserts, the
composer has both documents in hand and can surface the conflict (or suppress the refuted claim)
without any new measurement at all. That is strictly cheaper than a blanket re-measure obligation and
catches the conflict a full stage earlier than the receiving-agent option. It also bears on PART B's
cost objection: this shape is keyed to claims a LATER artifact already disputes, so it does no work
in the common case where nothing disputes them.

### 342. Refactor the books extension's context corpus against the settled convention: role-scoped loading, agent-set review, version-pinned files
- **Effort**: 2-3 days
- **Status**: [HOLD]
- **Held**: 2026-10-05
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: extensions
- **Dependencies**: Task 340, Task 341, Task 346
- **Research**: [342_refactor_books_context_corpus_role_scoped/reports/01_seed-books-context-engineering.md]

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET (agent-system/extensions/books/..., never .claude/**). `.claude/` is a
gitignored, disposable deploy artifact regenerated from the source store; a file hand-authored there
is silently wiped by the next deploy. See rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md.

No task-number references in any file written into the source store
(rules/no-task-references-in-deliverables.md). Cite durable anchors -- filenames, section headings,
decision numbers -- instead. Task numbers are permitted in this description and in specs/**.

== PROSE PRECONDITION (not a dependency edge) ==

This task refactors against the SETTLED record, not the one in flight. Before it runs, the consuming
repository ~/Projects/Logos/Verification must have landed its alpha review of the book convention WITH
THE VERSION STAMPED. If the review has not landed, this task waits -- refactoring the corpus against a
record still being revised buys a second refactor.

== GOAL ==

The books context corpus is 21 files, about 4,450 lines, WRITTEN BEFORE THE SPLIT AND BEFORE THE ALPHA
REVIEW. Review it against the settled record and refactor it so each books agent loads EXACTLY THE CONTEXT
ITS JOB NEEDS, with the AGENT SET ITSELF reviewed and EVERY FILE PINNED to the convention version.

== THE MEASURED STARTING POINT ==

  - `index-entries.json` (538 lines, 21 entries): only `project/books/README.md` and
    `patterns/signal-tagging.md` carry `load_when.task_types: ["books"]` -- AND NO REAL CONSUMING-REPO TASK
    CARRIES TYPE `books` (measured: fourteen books-topic tasks are `lean4`, two `general`, one `typst`).
    The other 19 entries are on-demand with empty `task_types`. NOTHING IS KEYED BY ROLE.
  - Corpus line counts: `README.md` 88; `domain/` known-gap-register 275, layer-vocabulary-and-matrix 230,
    book-toml-v2 187, certificate-ledger-and-records 275, identity-and-versioning 237,
    status-and-trust-vocabularies 163, gate-tiers 194; `patterns/` authoring-workflow 213,
    warning-driven-convergence 174, gate-collision-ledger 198, signal-tagging 204, books-review-submode 219,
    books-revise-submode 288; `standards/` metadata-split 180, forgery-probe-discipline 171,
    reconciliation-contract 231, observation-record 249; `tools/` tooling-inventory 213,
    typst-template-contract 244, certify-guide 214.
  - FIVE domain files restate Decisions 2, 3, 7, 8, 9, 11 and 12, which after the split are SINGLE FILES OF
    70 TO 330 LINES WITH STABLE ANCHORS. Before the split, restating Decision 7 saved an agent from
    ingesting a 359 KB file; after it, that decision is a short file at a stable path, so the
    restatement's REMAINING value is only whatever it ADDS.
  - FOUR agents split by LIFECYCLE PHASE (research 188, research-hard 295, implementation 294,
    implementation-hard 285 lines), NOT BY JOB.

== THE FIVE JOBS, MEASURED ==

| Job | Files needed today | Lines |
|---|---|---|
| Author a book | layer-vocabulary-and-matrix, metadata-split, book-toml-v2, authoring-workflow, warning-driven-convergence, rules/books.md | ~1,050 |
| Certify / diagnose | gate-tiers, gate-collision-ledger, certify-guide, tooling-inventory, forgery-probe-discipline | ~990 |
| Document a book | typst-template-contract, reconciliation-contract, status-and-trust-vocabularies | ~640 |
| Maintain the record | the record-editing guardrail rule and maintenance pointer, identity-and-versioning, known-gap-register | ~580 |
| Review / revise the convention | observation-record, signal-tagging, books-review-submode, books-revise-submode | ~960 |

Nothing in the extension selects by job. A research dispatch that reads the corpus README is pointed at all
of it.

== FIVE DELIVERABLES ==

(1) A PER-FILE REVIEW TABLE, all 21 files: what it RESTATES, what it ADDS BEYOND THE DECISION FILE MEASURED
IN LINES, which job needs it, and a disposition -- KEEP / TRIM TO THE DELTA / MERGE / RETIRE INTO THE RECORD
/ SPLIT BY ROLE. "Lines that are not in the decision file" is a measurement, not a judgement; make it.

(2) A LOADING MATRIX for the five jobs, giving each agent and skill an EAGER SET and an ON-DEMAND SET,
expressed in `index-entries.json` `load_when` keys and in each agent's context references, with a
PER-DISPATCH EAGER BUDGET MEASURED BEFORE AND AFTER.

(3) THE AGENT-SET RULING: which jobs need their own agent and which are served by EXISTING agents given a
role-specific context slice -- including whether the existing typst and planner agents already cover
documenting and planning -- with the frontmatter and routing-table changes that implement it.

(4) A `- **Reviewed against convention version**:` HEADER ON EVERY CORPUS FILE, matching the extension pin,
plus THE RULE THAT A BUMP OBLIGES RE-REVIEW of every file whose header is BELOW the pin. This turns "update
all the parts" from a search into a list.

(5) Registration (`README.md`, `EXTENSION.md`, `index-entries.json`, `manifest.json`), the gate suite green,
and A STATED MEASUREMENT THAT NO DISPATCH KIND'S EAGER CONTEXT GREW.

== BINDING PREFERENCES AND PROTECTED FILES ==

PREFER TRIMMING TO THE DELTA over retiring a file, unless the delta is empty. PREFER A ROLE-SPECIFIC
CONTEXT SLICE over a new agent, unless the slice would exceed the budget for an existing agent's other jobs.

These carry MEASURED FAILURE MODES THE RECORD DOES NOT HAVE and MUST SURVIVE: `gate-collision-ledger`
(the six gate collisions), `forgery-probe-discipline`, `warning-driven-convergence` (the `book_requires`
loop), and `certify-guide` (the certifier's economical operation) -- along with the `Books.+` glob failure
and the `certificate/` directory discovery corruption these files record.

Deleting the corpus and pointing agents at the record directly was considered; it is REJECTED AS AN
ASSUMPTION and must instead be MEASURED per file, precisely because of those failure modes.

== OWNER GATE ==

The owner looks at THE LOADING MATRIX DURING PLANNING, BEFORE the rewrite. Role separation is a design
choice, not a mechanical consequence of the measurements.

== SCOPE BOUNDARY ==

NO change to the record. NO change to `/books` sub-mode SEMANTICS beyond their context references. NO
repo-side files. Applying the same loading-matrix method to the lean4 and typst corpora is a possible later
task, NOT this one.

== ACCEPTANCE ==

The review table covers all 21 files with measured deltas and a disposition each; the loading matrix names
an eager and on-demand set per job with before/after budgets; the agent-set ruling is recorded with reasons;
every corpus file carries the version header and the bump rule is stated; the four protected files survive;
no dispatch kind's eager context grew, stated as a measurement. Gate: `verify-deploy.sh`,
`check-extension-docs.sh`, `check-task-references.sh`, and the books test suites.

== DEPENDENCIES ==

Depends on the books-extension split-retarget task (the retargeted anchors and the version pin every corpus
header must match) and on the record-editing guardrails task (the rule and maintenance pointer that the
"maintain the record" job's eager set loads).

Seed report: specs/342_refactor_books_context_corpus_role_scoped/reports/01_seed-books-context-engineering.md

### 338. Sweep task support file tracked or ignored
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 275

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/** (never .claude/**), per rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md.

Sweep every task-support file a producer writes under specs/ and classify each as tracked or ignored, so support artifacts stop dirtying the git tree for no reason

--- WHY ---

context/standards/orchestrator-runtime-files.md defines a two-class split (Ephemeral/gitignored vs. Durable provenance/tracked) and scripts/lib/runtime-file-patterns.sh renders the ephemeral half into each repo's specs/.gitignore. The split is sound. The ENUMERATION is not: it was grown file-by-file as each defect surfaced, never swept, so whole artifact families a producer writes today appear in neither class. The consequence is support files showing permanently in `git status` for no reason, which trains a reader to ignore the residue channel entirely.

MEASURED 2026-10-04 across the two consumer repos (~/.config/nvim and ~/Projects/Logos/Verification):

1. `progress/phase-N-progress.json` -- ZERO mentions in the standard, ZERO in runtime-file-patterns.sh, yet roughly 2,000 such files are TRACKED in nvim's history (391 `phase-1-progress.json`, 382 `phase-2`, 367 `phase-3`, 339 `phase-4`, 276 `phase-5`, 196 `phase-6`, 121 `phase-7`, 60 `phase-8`, and more), plus ~580 in Verification. This is the dominant volume in the whole question and is entirely unpoliced. By its own description it is crash-recovery scratch, which would put it in the ephemeral class -- but it is committed thousands of times over. Settle it.
2. `.blocker-research.json` -- ZERO mentions in the standard, ZERO in the lib, yet FOUR live scripts write or read it (orchestrate-build-aux-dispatch.sh, orchestrate-cycle-plan.sh, orchestrate-cycle-postflight.sh, lib/territory-contention-lib.sh) and three docs describe it. One instance (specs/187_prove_crc8_detection_and_record_limit/) has sat untracked and unignored in Verification across multiple sessions, reported as residue by every run's own end-of-batch check.
3. `tools/` under a task directory -- ZERO mentions in either. A task wrote specs/193_improve_docs_presentation/tools/{reflow.py,before-after.md,gates-after.txt} and the postflight's own excursion advisory listed them as out-of-scope writes.
4. Whole UNTRACKED TASK DIRECTORIES: `specs/335_gate_modified_files_excursion_at_staging/` is untracked in nvim right now because its producer never staged it. Adjacent to the existing vacated-source-never-staged task; coordinate rather than duplicate.

Already correctly covered, as evidence the mechanism works when a name is in it: metrics.jsonl, issues.jsonl, and the 42-pattern enumeration the lib already renders.

--- SCOPE ---

1. ENUMERATE, by grep over the source store rather than from this list, every file and directory any producer writes under `specs/` -- dot-prefixed and plain alike, per-task and specs/-root alike. The deliverable is the inventory itself: producer, reader, lifetime, current git disposition (tracked / ignored / neither).
2. CLASSIFY each against the two-class split, applying the standard's own freshness-gate test: a file a resume/read site trusts with NO freshness check is ephemeral, because a stale git-restored copy would silently corrupt live state. A per-dispatch audit trail a later reader needs is durable provenance.
3. RULE EXPLICITLY on the `progress/` family. It is the one case where the current behaviour (thousands of tracked files) and the likely correct class (scratch) disagree at scale, so it needs a stated decision with its reason, not a quiet pattern addition. If it becomes ignored, say what happens to the thousands already in history -- leaving them tracked while ignoring new ones is a legitimate answer, but it must be the recorded answer rather than an accident.
4. APPLY: add every ephemeral name to runtime-file-patterns.sh's enumeration (the canonical generator; specs/.gitignore is rendered from it by init-specs.sh and must never be hand-edited), and for every durable name confirm its producer actually stages and commits it rather than leaving it untracked.
5. RECORD the result in context/standards/orchestrator-runtime-files.md's per-file table, including any artifact deliberately left in neither class -- that document already carries two such carve-outs (the deploy ledger, and the stray-handoff/stale-guard trio), so a third is permitted but must be argued, not implied by omission.
6. Leave a CHECK so the enumeration cannot silently fall behind again: the recurring failure mode is a new producer writing a new artifact name with nothing noticing it belongs in neither class.

--- CONSTRAINTS ---

- Do not collapse the bare-vs-suffixed `.return-meta.json` distinction. The standard states explicitly that it must never be collapsed: bare `.return-meta.json` is tracked durable provenance, `.return-meta-*.json` is ignored.
- `.orchestrator-handoff.json` and `.return-meta.json` stay tracked. The managed block's own comment names them as deliberate exclusions from the ephemeral class.
- Verify every claim in the WHY section by grep before acting on it; the counts above were measured on one day in two repos and are a starting point, not the inventory.

### 313. Advisory lint for hand-authored /orchestrate batch proposals in ROADMAP.md phase blocks
- **Status**: [NOT STARTED]
- **Task Type**: meta
- **Topic**: core-agent-system
- **Dependencies**: Task 306, Task 328, Task 344, Task 387, Task 401

**Description**: SOURCE STORE IS THE EDIT TARGET: agent-system/extensions/core/ (never .claude/**, a disposable deploy artifact -- see rules/source-store-deploy-boundary.md). No task numbers in deliverable files outside specs/**.

GOAL. Add an ADVISORY lint that validates hand-authored /orchestrate batch proposals written into specs/ROADMAP.md phase blocks, so an under-inclusive batch is caught at authoring time instead of only at dispatch -- or never.

=== THE OBSERVED FAILURE (real, not hypothetical) ===
An agent hand-edited a ROADMAP.md phase batch and REMOVED a task from it because that task depended on another task already in the batch. That reasoning is wrong: /orchestrate performs dependency-aware wave dispatch, so an intra-batch dependency edge merely sequences the two tasks into successive waves. The two tasks also declared overlapping file_scope (three files under one component's certificate/ directory), which under the dominance rule makes batching them MANDATORY rather than optional. Nothing caught either error, because the proposed batch existed only as prose in a markdown file. The user corrected it by hand.

=== RULING ON THE SCOPE-NOTE OBJECTION (settled; do not re-litigate) ===
context/patterns/batch-orchestration-guardrails.md's scope note "Territory is a human judgment, not a machine derivation" does NOT forbid this lint. Its own first sentence draws the line: "This document's admission layers (below) derive collisions mechanically from `file_scope`; the selection criterion here is different". The note excludes machine derivation as a SELECTION input. A lint that reads a batch a human already typed and mechanically compares declared file_scope is an admission-layer act, which that same sentence sanctions.

Corroboration: docs/architecture/batch-admit-schema.md's `idle_overlap_advisory` (v5) ALREADY emits finding (a)'s fact at dispatch time -- it names a cross_batch overlapping task that is not in the batch, with `overlapping_path` and a remedy string. This lint is therefore the AUTHORING-TIME ANALOGUE of a mechanism that already ships. The gap it closes is WHEN the signal arrives, not whether the fact is derivable. Scope the novelty claim accordingly.

=== FINDINGS TO IMPLEMENT (all ADVISORY, never blocking) ===
(a) TERRITORY UNDER-INCLUSION -- the primary and only mandatory-defect finding. A non-terminal task whose declared file_scope overlaps an in-batch task but which is ABSENT from the batch. Rule 1 (shared file territory) is unconditional and does not depend on topic cohesion; this is the one case where an omitted task is a real defect.
(c) The batch exceeds MAX_TASKS=8 (commands/orchestrate.md:232; contract at docs/architecture/orchestrate-state-machine.md's "Batch Size Cap").
(d) The batch cites a task number that is terminal or does not exist in state.json.
(e) DECLARATIVE, and arguably the actual fix: when the batch block is specified in the format contract, state plainly that a batch MAY contain intra-batch dependency edges, that such edges sequence tasks into successive waves rather than disqualifying them, and that a dependent's presence or absence is the author's judgment. The failure above was an author's false BELIEF; a lint can only catch its symptom, so the declarative sentence carries real weight. This is a pointer plus one sentence -- NOT new doctrine. The guardrails document already classifies an unmet predecessor as an ORDERING CONSTRAINT in its Gate Catalogue; cite that rather than re-explaining it.

ALL findings are ADVISORY. They fail the Blocking-vs-Advisory criterion's condition 2: a prose markdown file is never dispatched, so an under-inclusive batch causes no silent concurrent write -- the real admission gate still fires at dispatch time regardless.

=== FINDING (b) WAS CONSIDERED AND DELIBERATELY REJECTED -- DO NOT RE-PROPOSE ===
A proposed finding (b) would have reported "a non-terminal dependent of an in-batch prerequisite that is absent from the batch" as rule-3 under-inclusion. It is dropped ENTIRELY, including its weaker informational variant, for two reasons:
1. It overstates the guardrails document on that document's own terms. In its worked example the dependent C joins as a topic-cohesive OPTIONAL add whose edge means it "cannot dispatch usefully apart from the same invocation" -- a width/fill preference under rule 3, not an unconditional mandate like rule 1's territory requirement. As a defect finding it would assert a rule that does not exist.
2. Even as information it would fire on nearly every batch, given a dense dependency graph and a cap of 8, and it would push batches to grow transitively against that cap. Noise on a never-actionable signal is precisely what trains operators to ignore advisories, degrading finding (a), which IS actionable.
The aim is to ALLOW intra-batch dependency edges where they make sense -- never to mandate pulling dependents in. A dependency edge must simply never be grounds for EXCLUDING a task from a batch. Finding (e) carries that positive half declaratively.

=== HARD CONSTRAINT: THIS IS A VALIDATOR, NEVER A PROPOSER ===
The lint validates a batch a human already wrote. It must NEVER author, suggest, or rank a batch. Machine-derived batch SELECTION is the territory of the next-admissible-batch suggestion task (topic orchestrator), which is gated behind several prerequisites precisely because it is the harder, riskier mechanism. Copy that task's binding constraint as precedent: a second copy of an admission predicate drifts from the real one, and a suggestion the real script then refuses is worse than no suggestion at all.

=== REUSE, DO NOT REBUILD ===
- SPLICE $FILE_SCOPE_OVERLAP_JQ_DEFS from scripts/lib/file-scope-overlap.sh (the `norm` / `scopes_overlap_first` / `edge_connected_nums` defs). NEVER fork, restate, or re-derive the overlap algorithm. Note that scopes_overlap_first is deliberately glob-free and symmetric; do not "improve" it locally.
- ADD this lint to the Consumers list in context/patterns/file-footprint-overlap.md, which names that algorithm's callers. That list currently says three active callers; this becomes a new one.
- REUSE generate-task-order.sh's compute_waves (Kahn's algorithm) for any wave reasoning. Do NOT modify generate-task-order.sh -- it is in another task's file_scope, and consuming rather than modifying is what keeps this task free of a dependency on it.
- RESPECT the truncation precedence rule in the guardrails document: a cap-driven trim to 8 can never split a territory-mandatory group off the end. Finding (c) must not recommend a trim that violates this.

=== OPEN RESEARCH QUESTION: WHICH SEAM ===
Research must settle the call site. Note first that verify-deploy.sh, through which all ten existing scripts/lint/*.sh are wired, is the WRONG seam here: it validates the source store and deploy, whereas this lint reads a CONSUMER repo's specs/ROADMAP.md against that repo's specs/state.json. The precedent does not transfer, which is why a roadmap-touching command is the only seam.
  SEAM A: a new mode on scripts/roadmap-integration.sh, which the /review rewiring already invokes. This may need no commands/review.md edit at all, dissolving the territory overlap noted below -- but it enters the roadmap-generation task's territory.
  SEAM B: a standalone scripts/lint/lint-roadmap-batch-proposals.sh called explicitly from commands/review.md, following the naming and test/manifest-registration precedent of the ten existing lint scripts.
LEAN: seam B for the script itself, with the call site decided in research. If seam A wins, narrow file_scope accordingly and say so.

=== EMPIRICAL FINDING THE ROADMAP-GENERATION TASK NEEDS ===
The batch-block convention is DIVERGENT ACROSS REPOSITORIES, so a lint written to one shape silently no-ops on the other:
  - The consumer repo uses a literal `**Run**:` marker followed by a fenced block containing `/orchestrate 120,121,...`.
  - The global agent-system repo uses NO such marker: it uses a `## Call N -- <description>` header followed by a bare fenced block that may contain SEVERAL `/orchestrate ...` lines, i.e. several distinct batches under one header.
This matters beyond this task: the roadmap-generation task's own description asserts that the live file uses a `**Run**` line, which is true only in the consumer repo. That task should reconcile the two shapes into one specified grammar before this lint can parse anything. This is FLAGGED HERE DELIBERATELY rather than by silently editing that task's description. Confirm the divergence still holds at research time; the two files drift.

=== DEPENDENCIES ===
- On the roadmap-generation task (hard, substantive): there is no parseable grammar to lint until the batch block is specified as a structure element in context/formats/roadmap-format.md, which today specifies only phase headers, checkboxes, status tables, priority markers and completion annotations -- it is 65 lines with zero occurrences of "Run". That task already owns both roadmap-format.md and roadmap-integration.sh and already has the batch-block spec in its scope, so DO NOT duplicate that work here; consume it.
- On the /review rewiring task (hard, footprint-corroborated): it rewrites the /review Step 2.5 call site this lint would hook. Landing this first would simply be overwritten. The edge is deliberately kept EXPLICIT alongside the roadmap-generation edge even though the latter is transitively implied through it, per the standing documented-redundant-edge convention: the two edges record different facts (grammar availability vs. call-site ordering) and a redundant edge costs only a wave.
- NOT a dependency, but note the absent-file_scope admission-posture task: it rules whether an absent file_scope is admission-relevant. For this lint an absent file_scope simply means "no evidence of overlap", so finding (a) cannot fire on it. Adopt the softer posture and RECORD in the script header which absent-scope posture was assumed, so the ruling can be reconciled later. Do not hold this task on it.

=== CROSS-REPO TERRITORY CONSTRAINT (not expressible as an edge) ===
A consumer repository has its own in-flight task that ALSO edits the /review command, to add a Book health section. That is a different repository's task number, so it CANNOT be expressed as a dependencies[] edge on this task. Whoever implements this MUST check for concurrent /review-command work in consumer repos before editing commands/review.md. Choosing seam A would avoid this hazard entirely.

=== ACCEPTANCE ===
- A fixture ROADMAP batch that omits a task whose file_scope overlaps an in-batch task produces finding (a); the real failure case above is reproduced as that fixture and the lint flags it.
- A fixture in which the only omitted task is a DEPENDENT with no territory overlap produces NO finding, demonstrating (b) is genuinely absent.
- Findings (c) and (d) each have a fixture.
- No copy of the overlap predicate exists anywhere in the new code: the lint splices scripts/lib/file-scope-overlap.sh.
- context/patterns/file-footprint-overlap.md's Consumers list names this lint.
- Every finding is advisory: the lint's exit code is unaffected by findings, and this is stated in its header.
- Registered in manifest.json with a scripts/tests/test-*.sh companion, matching the existing lint-script convention.
- shellcheck clean per context/standards/shell-strict-mode.md.

=== SEAM INVALIDATION: BOTH CANDIDATES IN "OPEN RESEARCH QUESTION" ARE DEAD (re-measured 2026-11-02) ===
Nothing else in this record changes. The settled scope ruling above and finding (b)'s explicit
rejection both stand as written. What follows replaces only the two seam candidates.

SEAM B's CALL SITE IS BEING DELETED. This record's own file_scope names
`agent-system/extensions/core/commands/review.md`. The /orient record (project_name
`orient_command_with_scout_agent_retire_review`, not_started) retires /review and removes that
command file outright, along with its command-reference and skill-mapping rows in
`merge-sources/claudemd.md`. A lint call wired into commands/review.md would be deleted with it.

SEAM A's PREMISE IS GONE. "A new mode on scripts/roadmap-integration.sh, which the /review
rewiring already invokes" refers to the record `review_roadmap_regen_and_optimize`, which was
ABANDONED (commit `a59f8f373`, "task: abandon tasks 307, 308, 270 into 328"). There is no /review
rewiring, so nothing "already invokes" roadmap-integration.sh from a command.

MEASURED, 2026-11-02: NO file under `agent-system/extensions/core/commands/` and NO file under
`agent-system/extensions/core/skills/` invokes `roadmap-integration.sh` at all -- zero matches in
either directory. Its only live invocation on a command path is /orchestrate's conclusion stage:
`context/contracts/conclusion-stage.md` calls `roadmap-integration.sh --regenerate`. Every other
occurrence in the source store is documentation, registry registration, a test, or the quarantined
copy under `scripts/deprecated/`.

RE-CHOOSE THE SEAM FROM LIVE OPTIONS. This stays an OPEN research decision, framed exactly as the
original seam question was; neither option below is pre-selected here.
  LIVE SEAM 1 -- the /orchestrate conclusion stage. Already the single live caller of
    roadmap-integration.sh and already touching ROADMAP.md, so the lint arrives at a moment when
    the file is being written rather than at authoring time. Weigh that timing against this
    record's stated purpose (catch an under-inclusive batch AT AUTHORING TIME): the conclusion
    stage may be late enough to weaken the gap this lint claims to close.
  LIVE SEAM 2 -- the new /orient command the /orient record introduces. /orient is read-only by
    construction (no Write, no Edit in the scout agent's frontmatter), which fits a lint that only
    reports, and /orient is an on-demand orientation read, which is closer to authoring time. But
    the seam does not exist until that record lands.
  NARROW file_scope once the seam is settled. `commands/review.md` must come out of this record's
  file_scope under EITHER option, because the file is being removed. The CROSS-REPO TERRITORY
  CONSTRAINT above (concurrent /review-command work in a consumer repo) dissolves with it and can
  be dropped.

TWO STALE CLAIMS IN THE DEPENDENCIES SECTION ABOVE, recorded for correction rather than acted on
here. (i) The "hard, footprint-corroborated" edge on the /review rewiring record describes an
abandoned record; dependencies[] does not carry that edge, so only the prose is stale. (ii) The
roadmap-generation edge is SATISFIED: `roadmap_generated_artifact` is completed, and
`context/formats/roadmap-format.md` is now 234 lines and DOES specify the batch block -- it defines
a Run-line grammar with an explicit regex (`^/orchestrate ([\d, ]+)(\s+(-\S.*?))?(\s{3}# (.+))?$`,
stated twice) and a fenced-block detection rule. The description's "65 lines with zero occurrences
of Run" no longer holds. Re-read that file before designing a parser, and re-check the
divergent-batch-block finding above against it rather than against the quoted figures.

=== NEW FINDING (f): UNRESOLVABLE CITED PATH (ADVISORY, like every other finding here) ===
A ROADMAP.md reference to a `specs/{NNN}_{slug}/...` artifact path, or to any cited repository
file, that does not resolve on disk.

GROUNDS, measured live in a consumer repository on 2026-11-02 -- a real defect, not a hypothetical.
An archival run moved 19 task directories into `specs/archive/`, and ROADMAP.md was left citing a
report under `specs/819_ladder_cell_presentation_design_decision/reports/` at its pre-archival
path, which had ceased to exist. It was repaired by hand; the citation now carries the
`specs/archive/` prefix and resolves. The repair is the point: nothing mechanical caught it, and
archival runs move directories in batches of ~20, so every run can strand every citation into the
moved set at once. This is the same defect class that previously propagated a phantom declaration
citation into downstream planning in that repository, which is why it is worth a mechanical check.

TWO FALSE-POSITIVE CLASSES THE IMPLEMENTER MUST HANDLE -- both were hit while finding the defect by
hand, so neither is speculative:
  1. BARE BASENAMES USED AS PROSE SHORTHAND are legitimate and must never be reported. A naive
     basename grep flags backticked names like `ci.yml` or `MintBound.lean` that are referring to a
     file by name rather than citing a path. Require enough path structure to constitute a citation
     before checking resolution.
  2. A PATH QUALIFIED FOR ANOTHER REPOSITORY must be resolved against that repository's root, not
     the local one. A citation beginning `~/.config/nvim/specs/...` is a cross-repo reference; it
     is not unresolvable merely because it is absent locally. Either resolve it against the named
     root or skip it explicitly -- never report it against the wrong root.

ADVISORY, on the same grounds as every finding above: a prose markdown file is never dispatched, so
an unresolvable citation causes no silent concurrent write. Finding (f) needs its own fixture pair
-- one genuinely unresolvable path that fires, and one file containing both false-positive classes
that produces no finding.
